# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## Commands

```bash
# Development
pnpm dev              # Build packages, then run api + web concurrently
pnpm dev:web          # Build packages, run web only
pnpm dev:api          # Build packages, run api only

# Build
pnpm build            # Build all packages and apps

# Quality
pnpm lint             # ESLint check (max-warnings 0)
pnpm lint:fix         # ESLint auto-fix
pnpm format           # Prettier write all files
pnpm typecheck        # Real `tsc --noEmit` across apps/web, apps/api, packages/ui (strict — reports all errors)
pnpm validate         # lint + typecheck (full check — run manually or in CI)

# Pre-commit hook runs `pnpm lint` only. `pnpm typecheck` is strict and is NOT
# in the pre-commit hook. The web app's ~370 "pre-existing TS errors" (and 130
# lint errors) were ONE environmental defect, fixed 2026-09-29 by
# `apps/web/src/emotion.d.ts` — see "Emotion Theme augmentation" below. What is
# left is 3 SVG `dominantBaseline` typings in packages/ui icons; once those are
# clean, restore `pnpm run validate` in `.husky/pre-commit`.

# Docker (PostgreSQL 16 + pgvector image, Redis 7, pgAdmin, amazon-scraper, Loki/Promtail/Grafana)
pnpm docker:up        # Start services
pnpm docker:down      # Stop services
pnpm docker:logs      # Follow logs
pnpm docker:clean     # Remove containers and volumes
```

Packages must be built before apps can run (`pnpm dev` handles this automatically).

**Tests.**
- `pnpm --filter api test` — Jest, ~150 `*.spec.ts` files under `apps/api/src` (config `apps/api/jest.config.js`: CJS + ts-jest, a `@repo/shared → dist/cjs` moduleNameMapper, so build `@repo/shared` first, and a `uuid` CJS shim for uuid@13 ESM). They cover pure helpers, services against fakes, and ~28 source-grep `*.guard.spec.ts` invariant guards that lock rules described throughout this file.
- `pnpm --filter api test:integration` — `*.integration-spec.ts` under `apps/api/test/integration/`; only an infrastructure smoke harness against a dedicated Postgres/Redis (`INTEGRATION_*` env). The DB/queue/NestJS layer is otherwise verified manually.
- `pnpm --filter web test` — Vitest; two trivial tests only.
- `services/amazon-scraper/tests/` — Python tests for the scraper service.

## Architecture

**pnpm workspace monorepo** with two apps and three packages:

| Path | Description |
|---|---|
| `apps/api` | NestJS 10.3+ backend. Raw PostgreSQL via `pg` (no ORM). BullMQ job queues (Redis). JWT auth (Passport). |
| `apps/web` | React 18 + Vite 5. Redux Toolkit + RTK Query. Emotion CSS-in-JS. i18n (16 languages: EN, TR, RU, HI, UR, AR, AZ, BN, DE, FR, ES, IT, RO, UK, ZH, PT — UR and AR are right-to-left). |
| `packages/shared` | Domain types, DTOs, Zod schemas, constants, i18n resources. Dual-format build (ESM + CJS). |
| `packages/ui` | Design system (Atomic Design). Theme tokens, styled components, contexts. Also hosts the ESLint plugin (`src/eslint-plugin/`). |
| `packages/mcp` | Stub — `package.json` + `tsconfig.json` only, no source. |

`services/amazon-scraper/` (Python) is a separate service outside the pnpm workspace — see "Scraper provider".

### Key Patterns

**Container/Component split** — Every page:
- `[Feature]Page.container.tsx` — Logic, RTK Query hooks, state management
- `[Feature]Page.component.tsx` — Presentation only (only `useTranslation` hook allowed)
- `[Feature]Page.style.ts` — Emotion styled components
- `[Feature]Page.types.ts` — Feature-specific types

**API calls** — All frontend API interactions use RTK Query via `baseApi.injectEndpoints`. No direct fetch/axios.

**Shared types** — All domain types/DTOs/interfaces in `packages/shared/src/domain/`. All Zod schemas in `packages/shared/src/schemas/`. No duplicates, no `any`.

**Database** — Raw `pg` driver via `DatabaseService` (query, getClient, transaction methods). Base schema in `docker/postgres/init.sql`, then numbered migrations in `apps/api/migrations/`. No ORM.

**i18n** — No hardcoded UI strings. Files in `packages/shared/src/i18n/resources/{en,tr,ru,hi,ur,ar,az,de,fr,es,it,ro,uk,zh,pt}/` (see "Supported languages" below). Key resolution rule:
- Domain namespace JSONs (`ebay.json`, `dashboard.json`, `orders.json`, etc.) have a **top-level key matching the namespace name** (e.g. `{ "ebay": { "connect": { "title": "..." } } }`).
- When calling `t()` with the namespace as default (via `useTranslation(['ebay', 'translation'])`), use **dot notation with the full path including the wrapper key**: `t('ebay.connect.title')`. Do NOT use colon syntax — `t('ebay:connect.title')` will show the raw key.
- Cross-namespace access to `translation` (which has no wrapper key) uses **colon syntax**: `t('translation:common.loading')`.

**Reference module** — `store-settings` is the canonical pattern:
- Backend: `apps/api/src/modules/store-settings/`
- Frontend: `apps/web/src/features/store-settings/`
- Shared: `packages/shared/src/domain/store-settings/`
- **`amazonTaxRate`** (`store_settings.amazon_tax_rate`, migration `035`): percent 0–100, default `0`, **resolved per store like every other store setting** (Store > Global). User-configurable in Settings → StoreSettingsDrawer. The price (`ListingStrategyService`), the provisional profit (`OrderSyncService.recomputeProfit`) and the auto-fulfill cost estimate all read the ORDER's / LISTING's store (2026-10-03: the last two read the global row, so profit assumed a different rate than the price was built with). An omitted value on save leaves the stored one unchanged. (An earlier note here said the save writes it to all rows; nothing ever did.)
- **Store settings rule (operator, 2026-10-03): every eBay store uses its own settings; a store with no setting of its own uses the global ("all stores") one.** Two mechanisms carry it: (1) **a store's FIRST row is a copy of the global row** — the store INSERT in `saveSettings` takes every field the caller did not send from the global row (`LEFT JOIN store_settings g … COALESCE($n, g.col, default)`), so a focused drawer (blacklist, buyer messaging) creating the row no longer writes auto-fulfill OFF, tax 0, the default blacklist or the tracking provider `'local'` (which pushed the raw Amazon number to eBay); (2) **NULL inherits at read** for location, `buyer_messaging`, the loss limit and the blocked-ASIN list (`getResolvedSettings`; listing rules moved to the settings group, migration `141`). The drawers prefill a store with no row from the global row. Every consumer resolves with the store the listing/order belongs to — never `getResolvedSettings(userId, null)` where a store is known. `store-settings-resolution.spec.ts` locks the INSERT seeding and the read inheritance.
- **Order sync reads each store with ITS OWN token** (`getAccountAccessToken(ebayAccountId)` in `syncOrdersForAccount`; the payment re-check and the suspension resume are scoped to that store). It used the user's "active account" token — an unordered `LIMIT 1` — so with two stores one store's orders were fetched by the other's run and filed under it (production, 2026-10-03: 11 orders). `order-sync-store-token.guard.spec.ts`.

### Supported languages: en, tr, ru, hi, ur, ar, az, bn, de, fr, es, it, ro, uk, zh, pt (2026-09-28; `bn` added 2026-10-01)

`SUPPORTED_LOCALES` (`packages/shared/src/domain/common/common.constants.ts`) is the ONE list — the `/:locale` route segment, `isValidLocale`, the API DTOs (`@IsIn(SUPPORTED_LOCALES)`), the language menus and `i18n.config.ts`'s `supportedLngs` all derive from it, and `i18nResources` (`packages/shared/src/i18n/index.ts`) is the one bundle map i18next reads. Adding a language is: a `resources/<code>/` folder, one entry in the constants + `LOCALE_DISPLAY_NAMES` + `SUPPORTED_LANGUAGES` (`packages/shared/src/i18n/types.ts`) + `i18nResources` + `getLocaleConfig`'s table (`packages/ui/src/utils/format.ts`), and a `languages.<code>` key in every existing locale's `translation.json` — nothing else. Formatting locales: Azerbaijani `az-AZ` (Latin script, LTR), Bengali `bn-BD-u-nu-latn` (Bangladesh; Western digits for the same reason as Urdu/Arabic — every ID and SKU beside a figure is Latin), Spanish `es-MX` (neutral Latin-American wording), Chinese `zh-CN` (Simplified), Portuguese `pt-BR`. Azerbaijani is written natively, not derived from the Turkish files (`sifariş`, `Yadda saxla` — not the Turkish spellings).
- **Arabic is Modern Standard Arabic, not Darija.** Moroccan Darija has no written UI standard, so `ar` is simple MSA with the locale `ar-MA` for formatting. French (Morocco's second working language) is NOT provided.
- **`admin` and `legal` exist for `en` + `tr` only, on purpose.** The operator console is staff-facing, and the Privacy Policy / Terms are supplied verbatim by the operator (see "Legal documents"), so they must never be machine-translated — a visitor in any other language gets the English document via `fallbackLng: 'en'`. If a real translation is ever supplied, add it as `resources/<code>/legal.json` + the `i18nResources` entry; nothing else changes. `OperatorLayout`'s language menu stays en/tr for the same reason.
- **Right-to-left (`ur`, `ar`; `RTL_LOCALES`, `isRtlLocale`).** `DirectionProvider` (`apps/web/src/components/`, mounted in `main.tsx`) sets `<html lang dir>` and swaps the Emotion cache for one running `stylis-plugin-rtl`, which mirrors every physical property (margin-left, left:, text-align, corner radii…) — the whole design system is written in physical properties, so this is what makes RTL work without a rewrite to logical ones. A rule that must NOT flip opts out with a `/* @noflip */` comment. Directional icons (chevrons, back/forward arrows, sidebar-collapse, log-out) mirror through `Icon`'s `MIRRORED_IN_RTL` set; artwork that is not directional (trend arrows, external-link) does not. Digits stay Western (0-9) everywhere: `getLocaleConfig` returns `ur-PK-u-nu-latn` / `ar-MA-u-nu-latn` because every ID, SKU and table beside a figure is Latin and mixed digit systems in one row read as a bug. Any NEW component that hard-positions something (an absolutely-placed badge, a transform: translateX) must be checked in `/ar`.
- **Plurals.** Russian and Ukrainian need `_one/_few/_many/_other`, Romanian `_one/_few/_other`, French/Spanish/Italian/Portuguese carry `_one/_many/_other` (`many` = millions, same wording as `other`), Arabic `_zero/_one/_two/_few/_many/_other`, Hindi/Urdu/Bengali `_one/_other`, Chinese only `_other`. English keeps `_one/_other` only; the fallback fills the rest per i18next's plural rules.
- **Fonts.** Lexend has no Cyrillic, Devanagari or Arabic, so the stacks in `designTokens.ts`, `index.css` and the landing's `FONT_*` fall through `Inter` (Cyrillic, Latin-extended) → `Noto Sans Devanagari` → `Noto Sans Arabic` → `Noto Sans Bengali` → `Noto Sans SC`. Google Fonts serves them by unicode-range, so an English visitor downloads none of them.
- **Stripe** renders Checkout/Portal in `en`, `tr`, `ru`, `de`, `fr`, `es`, `it`, `ro`, `zh` and `pt-BR` (`SUPPORTED_STRIPE_LOCALES` in `billing-helpers.ts`, checked against the SDK union `Checkout.SessionCreateParams.Locale`; the app's `pt` maps to `pt-BR` because Stripe's bare `pt` is European). Stripe has no Hindi/Urdu/Arabic/Azerbaijani/Bengali/Ukrainian Checkout, so those resolve to English rather than sending a code Stripe would reject and breaking checkout.
- **E-mail** templates are DB rows for `en`/`tr` only. `EmailService.getTemplate` falls back to the English row when the user's locale has none — without it a `ru` user's password-reset and verification mails were silently never sent.
- **Landing/auth screenshots** exist as `en`/`tr` sets only; every locale but `tr` shows the English screenshots (`screenSrc` maps anything that is not `tr` to `en`). Recapture per locale before marketing to those audiences.
- **Copy rules carry over to every locale**: never write "dropshipping" (or a transliteration), never name Aquiline or Keepa, no price superlatives, claims exactly as strong as the English. Translations are verified by parity (same key tree, same `{{placeholders}}`/tags; plural suffixes may differ).

### The seller address is ONE address, four fields (2026-09-02)

`StoreSettingsDrawer` collects one address in one `FieldGroup`: **Country, Region/State, City, Zip — all four required.** Continue is not disabled; clicking it on the address step with a gap sets `addressSubmitAttempted` and flags the empty fields red (the standard `submitAttempted` pattern). The drawer's inline completeness check mirrors `isStoreAddressComplete` (`packages/shared/src/domain/store-settings/store-settings.helpers.ts`, Jest-covered in `store-address.helpers.spec.ts`) — keep the two in step. The address feeds only the eBay `STORE` inventory location; there used to be a second "ship-from" card for the tracking provider, and sellers correctly read it as being asked the same thing twice.

- **Country is a PICKER validated as an ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 code — never re-introduce a free-text input for a field an external provider validates as an enum.** eBay reports an unmappable `country` as a **MISSING** one (`errorId 25801, "Missing field country."`), so a typed country NAME passed every "is it filled in?" check and still failed the publish. Four load-bearing pieces: `COUNTRY_CODES` / `normalizeCountryCode` / `isValidCountryCode` (`packages/shared/src/domain/common/country-codes.ts`, 249 codes) are the single value set; `getCountryOptions` (`apps/web/src/features/settings/utils/countryOptions.ts`) builds the picker with **`Intl.DisplayNames`** rather than i18n resources (the browser already ships the names; same approach as `PhoneInput`); `SaveStoreSettingsDto` re-validates server-side while still accepting a BLANK value (the focused drawers omit the field and the service reads blank as "leave unchanged"); and `ensureInventoryLocation` (`ebay.service.ts`) **normalizes at the last point before eBay**, rescuing rows that still hold a bad value. Country is checked for VALIDITY, not presence; the other three are free text on eBay's side.
- **The tracking provider needs NO address (verified live).** Aquiline `POST /v1/profiles` requires only `accountOrigin`, so `AquilineProfileService` sends no `storeAddress` and `fingerprintProfile` hashes none. Evidence (incl. that profile DELETE is unsupported) is in `docs/aquiline-open-questions.md` question 5.

| | eBay `STORE` location | Aquiline profile |
|---|---|---|
| addressLine1 | required (derived) | not sent |
| city | required | not sent |
| stateOrProvince | required | not sent |
| postalCode | required | not sent |
| country | required | not sent |
| name / phone / line2 | not used | not sent |

- **`ship_from_city` is reused as the location city** (migration `089`; wire name `shipFromCity` unchanged — see the comment on `StoreSettings.shipFromCity`). `ship_from_name`/`phone`/`address_line1`/`address_line2` are **dormant**: no UI writes them, nothing reads them, not dropped. Do not reintroduce inputs for them.
- **`inheritMissingStoreLocation` inherits `shipFromCity`** along with country/state/zip, so a store row created by a focused drawer (blacklist, buyer messaging) never publishes an eBay item location with an empty city while the global row has one.
- **No street is collected; one is DERIVED for eBay.** A seller with no warehouse has no true street to enter, so `buildStoreStreetLine` (pure, shared) returns `"Sheridan, WY"` from city + region. Do not reintroduce the old fallbacks it replaced: the literal `'Use Store Address'` as the street, or feeding `city` from `data.location` (which is the **state**, producing "WY, WY"). eBay shows buyers only city/region/country, so the derived line is never rendered on a listing.
- **Per-store address overrides are kept** — the eBay inventory location is per store.
- **Seller-facing copy must claim only what is verified.** Never tell a seller this address is shown on converted tracking (an unverified assumption that once reached i18n), and never say an incomplete address makes conversion fall back to the raw Amazon number — `mayPushToEbay` holds the order instead; see "THE RAW AMAZON TRACKING NUMBER IS NEVER PUSHED TO eBAY".
- **A deep-linked drawer adopts its config when the query resolves.** `StoreSettingsDrawer` is always mounted with `isOpen` as a prop, so `/settings?drawer=storeSettings` is open before the settings query returns. The container resyncs on the `null → loaded` transition of the resolved config (`configArrived`) — deliberately only that transition, so the new object a post-save refetch hands back cannot clobber edits.
- `Select`'s searchable dropdown box (`isSearchable`, used by the country picker and `PhoneInput`) needs `box-sizing: border-box` — there is no global reset, and without it the box renders wider than its wrapper and loses its left inset.

### Landing page (`apps/web/src/features/landing/`)

Public marketing page at `/` (no locale prefix), exempt from the container/component split (the component holds UI state such as FAQ/tab/reveal). `LandingPage.container.tsx` owns scroll detection, mobile menu, locale switching, navigation to `/{locale}/login|register`, entering the demo, and pricing-catalog shaping; `LandingPage.component.tsx` is presentation plus the scroll-reveal `IntersectionObserver`; `LandingPage.constants.ts` holds non-copy facts (`BUSINESS_CONTACT`, `SPEC_SAMPLES`, `SYNC_TIMES`). It mounts `TawkToWidget` with tawk.to's native bubble visible.

**Forced light theme, no dark mode, no theme toggle.** `LandingPageContainer` wraps its return in `EmotionThemeProvider theme={lightTheme}` (`@emotion/react` + `@repo/ui`), overriding the app-wide theme for every styled-component and `Icon` under it.

**Structure (rebuilt 2026-09-27 around the differentiators — argue what SellerHill does that the category does not, not the automation set every competitor lists).** Section order and anchors:
1. **Navbar** — logo, a "Features" `Dropdown` (`FEATURE_MENU`: why, groups, templates, Pending Actions, item specifics, mobile, trust — each scrolls to its anchor), links (templates, profit, pricing, FAQ, about), Demo, language `Dropdown`, Login, "Start free".
2. **Hero** (`#top`) — see below.
3. **Proof bar** — four verifiable facts that count up (`PROOF_KEYS`: 4× daily sync, 45 item specifics, 12 templates, 1-month trial), followed by a capability marquee reusing existing labels.
4. **Why SellerHill bento** (`#why`) — Setting Groups as the one dark "Only on SellerHill" card, 4×/day sync rail (`SYNC_TIMES`), item specifics "Does not apply" vs real values (`SPEC_SAMPLES`), Pending Actions, supplier concealment + blacklist, and a full-width "Affordable automation" strip.
5. **Setting Groups spotlight** (`#groups`) — five parameters (margin, template, stock/buffer, fees, title/content), seasonal/category examples, the real "All groups" drawer screenshot.
6. **Template gallery** (`#templates`) — live HTML previews (below).
7. **Showcase** (`#showcase`, rows `#showcase-<key>`) — Pending Actions, item specifics, order detail, listing detail with revision history.
8. **Profit** (`#profit`) — `TabNav` over overview / chart / P&L / per-order screenshots plus the confirmed / estimated / unknown points.
9. **Mobile** (`#mobile`) — navy band, three phone captures.
10. **Trust** (`#trust`) — card numbers never reach us (Stripe checkout); Amazon login/session details encrypted and never given to a third party; links to Privacy/Terms.
11. **Standard features** grid ("and everything you expect"), **how it works** (`#how-it-works`, five `STEPS`; `S.Steps` uses `minmax(12.5rem, 1fr)` so all five fit without orphaning one), **demo band**, **pricing** (`#pricing`), **FAQ** (`#faq`, `FAQ_KEYS` q1–q12), **about** (`#about`), final CTA, footer.

i18n lives under `landing.{navbar,hero,proof,why,groups,templates,showcase,profit,mobile,trust,about,standard,howItWorks,demo,pricing,faq,ctaBanner,footer}` in `translation.json`. `en` and `tr` are structurally identical (290 leaves each) and **Turkish is written natively**, not translated sentence-for-sentence. The `translation.json` files are CRLF in the working tree, so any scripted rewrite must round-trip byte-identically or it reformats the whole file and buries the real diff.

**Hero.** Flat navy band (`colors.sidebar.background`, matching the app sidebar) that cuts to the light page below with a hard edge, no gradient. Two-column grid (`S.HeroInner`, one column ≤980px): left — eyebrow (`landing.hero.eyebrow` "Amazon → eBay automation", with an amber live dot; `S.HeroEyebrow` uses `sidebar.*` + `accentAmber` because the light-surface `S.Eyebrow` vanishes on navy), headline with a solid amber second line (`headlineAccent`), subheading, amber `$accent` "Start free" CTA + light "Live demo" button, two `HeroNoteLine`s (`trialNoCard`, `trialCancelAnytime`). Right — the real dashboard screenshot in `S.HeroPreviewGlass` (translucent frame with a blue→amber hairline), the real cropped KPI card (`S.HeroFloatCard`) over its left edge, and **`S.HeroOfferCard`**: the amber-rimmed placard straddling the screenshot's top-right corner — trial pill (`landing.hero.trialTitle`, uppercase written into the string so Turkish caps are correct), `startingPriceDisplay` at 4rem + `priceBadge.per` (`/AY`, `/MO`), `priceBadge.caption`. Below 1080px the placard and KPI crop hide and `S.MobileOffer` (an inline pill under the CTAs) carries the offer. Motion is restrained: copy rises in once, placard and KPI crop drift a few pixels, two faint equaliser clusters (`HeroSpectrum`, `SPECTRUM_BARS`) breathe.

**Offer price and pricing grid.** `startingPriceDisplay` is the container's minimum over the catalog's paid monthly prices (`formatLandingPrice`), so the hero can never disagree with the pricing section. Its literal fallback must equal the cheapest paid tier — **`$19.99` since migration `137` (Mini, 2026-10-02)**, `$24.99` under `125`; a stale literal only shows when the catalog call fails, but then it advertises a price that does not exist. The grid shows the three cheapest paid tiers (`COLLAPSED_PLAN_COUNT`, derived from catalog prices, never a slug list) and a `showAllPlans` expander reveals the rest in place — an in-page expander because `/billing` is behind auth. `growth` carries the "most popular" badge (`HIGHLIGHTED_PLAN_SLUG`). **Every card LEADS with "Unlimited automatic orders and shipment tracking" in full ink and bold (`S.PlanFeature $strong`, operator request 2026-10-02 — it is the promise sellers compare tools on, and it was the fourth, unemphasised line), and the listings line reads "N active listings · add and remove without limit"** (the limit is a level: ending a listing frees its slot; never "up to N"). The billing page's plan cards carry the same two lines (`billing.plans.unlimitedOrders` / `billing.plans.activeListings`). `landing.pricing.included.autoOrder` was removed so the claim is not printed twice. Every card shows its own quota lines — listings, tracking conversions and Best Sellers products, the three metered dimensions, plus automatic orders rendered as "unlimited" (never a figure) since `125` — plus one shared `landing.pricing.included.*` list; the catalog gates no feature by tier, so there is no per-plan feature ladder. **Plan names and descriptions come from the `billing` namespace** (`billing:billing.plans.<slug>.name|description`), never landing copy. `landing.pricing.catalogFallback` (rendered for nano/starter/growth only when the public catalog call fails) must stay aligned with the real catalog — since `125` it carries the v3 prices (Nano $29.99 / Starter $44.99 / Growth $89.99, plus a Pro $164.99 entry) and "unlimited" automatic orders; a fallback still carrying the old `083` prices or order counts is wrong. Limits are formatted with a hardcoded `Intl.NumberFormat('en-US')` on the live path, so the TR fallback deliberately uses `en-US` grouping too — fix the container first if this should be locale-aware.

**The free trial is spelled out above the plan cards (2026-10-04, operator request).** `S.TrialStrip` (navy + amber, the hero's offer language) states the trial's length and what it includes, with its own "start free trial" button. Its figures come from `BillingCatalogDto.trial` (`{ days, limits }`): the trial plan is `is_active = FALSE`, so it is never in `plans`; `BillingRepositoryService.loadTrialLimits` reads it by `TRIAL_PLAN_SLUG` and `days` is the `billing.trialDays` setting. The lookup is fail-soft (`trial: null`), and the container then falls back to `FALLBACK_TRIAL`, which must equal the real trial (50 listings / 20 conversions / 500 Best Sellers products / unlimited automatic orders / 30 days) for the same reason the `$19.99` literal must. **A plan card's button starts the TRIAL, not that plan** — there is no plan-carrying sign-up flow (operator decision: not worth building now) — so the button reads "Try free" (`landing.pricing.planCta`) and `landing.pricing.trialPlanNote` under it says every trial starts on the trial plan and the seller can switch to this plan whenever they like. Never relabel it "Buy" / "Choose plan" without building that flow. The hero's "1 MONTH FREE" and the proof bar's "1 month" are still static copy — change them by hand if `billing.trialDays` ever moves.

**Template previews are live HTML, not screenshots.** `scripts/build-template-previews.mjs` renders all twelve catalog templates (migration `073`) with the production `renderListingTemplate` into `apps/web/public/landing-screens/templates/<slug>.html` (one English set — this is what buyers see on eBay.com) and writes `apps/web/public/template-samples/catalog.json` for the demo's template picker. Each template's sample product comes from migration `122` (`predefined_templates.sample_data`), also read by `predefined-templates.guard.spec.ts` and the in-app picker, so landing and app preview the same product; photos are in `apps/web/public/template-samples/` (`minimalist` renders none). The gallery embeds the active file in `S.TemplateFrame` (`<iframe sandbox="">`) so the template's own responsive CSS runs at the visitor's width; `S.TemplateOpenLink` opens it full-page; on one column `S.TemplateSide` becomes `display: contents` so the preview sits under its tabs. **Re-run the script after `pnpm --filter @repo/shared build` whenever the catalog, the renderer or the samples change** — the files go stale silently. (A Playwright `fullPage` screenshot shows the iframe blank — a capture artifact.)

**App screenshots** are real captures from the sign-up-free demo account, WebP, once per locale: `apps/web/public/landing-screens/{en,tr}/` hold the same twelve names (`hero-dashboard`, `hero-kpi-card`, `setting-groups`, `item-specifics`, `action-center`, `order-detail`, `listing-detail`, `dashboard-chart`, `dashboard-pnl`, `mobile-{dashboard,actions,orders}`). A Turkish visitor must see the Turkish UI, so **adding a screenshot means capturing it twice**. `AuthShowcase` reuses `hero-dashboard` + `mobile-dashboard`. **Recapture recipe** = `scripts/capture-screens.mjs` (Puppeteer): run the web app (`pnpm --filter web exec vite --port 5199`), then `node scripts/capture-screens.mjs`. It pins the locale via `localStorage.locale`, enters demo mode, hides the demo banner (`[role="status"]`), scrollbars and motion, collapses the sidebar, and captures at `deviceScaleFactor: 2` — hero at 1440×900, showcase/profit screens at 1180px cropped to `<main>` (`listing-detail` stays full-window because its drawer is portaled outside `<main>`), phones at 390×844. Screens go stale silently when the UI changes — recapture after material UI changes. **Never put a 3D transform (`rotateX/rotateY`) on a screenshot** — Chrome resamples it and it blurs. Below 640px `ScreenImage` shows a screen's top-left at a fixed height instead of the whole shrunken screen.

**Demo data shaped for these screens** (`apps/web/src/features/demo/demoData.ts`): Setting Groups "Everyday essentials" / "Mother's Day gifts" / "Electronics" / "Automotive" (listings assigned by category in `demoGroupFor`), three pinned products with full item-specifics tables (`DEMO_RICH_SPECS`), a Pending Actions listing-job failure broken down by `BLACKLISTED_KEYWORD`, and revision history on the 6-hour check grid (`REFRESH_STEP_HOURS`).

**Navbar and mobile menu.**
- **The navbar row must never get an `overflow` value** — any non-`visible` overflow also clips vertically, and the absolutely-positioned Features `Dropdown` opens to nothing. `S.NavLinks` children keep natural width (`&& > * { flex-shrink: 0; width: auto }` — doubled `&&` beats the Dropdown atom's `width: 100%`), and the row hands over to the hamburger at `NAV_COLLAPSE` (1280px, sized to the Turkish row incl. "Hakkımızda"). **Adding a nav link means re-measuring that breakpoint.**
- Navbar controls on navy use full `colors.sidebar.text`. Controls reused on light surfaces too (`LoginButton`, `LanguageTrigger`, `LanguageText`) take a `$onDark` prop at their dark call sites; omitted, they follow the `landing.*` theme colours. The navbar Login is a plain icon + text link.
- The language control is the app's own `Dropdown` pattern (8rem wide, same as `AppLayout`/`OperatorLayout`), showing the full language name via `t('translation:languages.<code>')` — never a 2-letter code.
- `S.MobileMenu` is a full-screen navy sheet (`inset: 0`, `sidebar.*` ink), full-width link rows, two equal full-width CTAs, bottom padding reserving `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)`.

**Footer** is navy (`sidebar.background`/`sidebar.divider`, `sidebar.text*` ink — required because the logo's "SELLER" is white) with Product, **Contact** and Legal columns. Contact shows the legal name, address, `tel:` phone and `mailto:` e-mail from `BUSINESS_CONTACT` — facts, not copy, and they **must match what the payment processor holds as the business's customer-facing information** (change both or neither). Legal links go to Privacy, Terms and `/privacy#cookies`.

**Positioning and copy rules.**
- **Compete IN the Amazon → eBay automation category** (AutoDS / Easync / Yaballe): a visitor who knows those tools must place the product in the first second. Use the category's vocabulary (automatic order fulfillment, price & stock monitoring, tracking automation, automated buyer messages, AI-powered listings); lead on the differentiators (Setting Groups, full item specifics, Pending Actions, real profit, supplier concealment) rather than on abstractions.
- **The word "dropshipping" is banned from every seller/visitor-readable string** (operator decision) — landing, auth, template descriptions, anything in `packages/shared/src/i18n/resources/**`. The category is carried by "Amazon → eBay automation". Internal prose and code comments may use it. Grep the i18n resources before adding marketing copy.
- **Price is argued as AFFORDABLE, never the market's lowest (operator decision).** No superlative and no mention of "the market" / "piyasa": hero accent "Uygun fiyatla." / "At an affordable price.", offer caption "uygun aylık fiyat" / "affordable monthly price", Why strip "Uygun fiyatlı otomasyon" / "Affordable automation". Never "cheap". Static copy names no figure — the only numbers are the catalog-driven offer card and pricing grid. Companion claim: "every feature in every plan".
- **Claims are bounded by what ships.** Sync is claimed as four times a day, matching the `keepa.refresh.intervalMinutes` default of 360 (`platform-settings.registry.ts`); it is scheduled, not real-time, and the FAQ says so. **If the cadence changes**, the proof bar, the Why sync card, `SYNC_TIMES`, pricing `included.sync`, FAQ q2, the hero subheading and the demo's `REFRESH_STEP_HOURS` all state it. Tracking conversion is scoped (default Amazon Logistics numbers only, switchable off) — never claim every number is converted. **The only discovery feature is the Amazon Best Sellers browser (2026-09-29, see "Amazon Best Sellers browsing")** — a seller browses Amazon's own ranking lists and sends ticked ASINs to the Add Listings drawer. There is still NO seller-tracking, keyword or competitor research (nothing reads other eBay sellers — see `docs/ebay-reference/README.md` for why); never imply otherwise, and if the landing ever mentions Best Sellers it says exactly that and nothing more.
- **The supplier-concealment card never names the source marketplace** (operator request) — it says "supplier". "Aquiline" and "Keepa" are never named anywhere seller-facing.
- **No fabricated social proof** — no user/order counts, uptime figures or invented testimonials (FTC and TR advertising-law exposure). Likewise no marketplace-coverage map (only eBay US + Amazon US exist), no multi-user roles section and no agency program section — none of those exist. **No internal mechanism in copy** — no Keepa, LLM, proxy pool, queue or column names; describe the outcome, never the machinery.
- **About (`#about`, `landing.about.*`)** is a software company's About (operator request — the 2btradingllc.com retail vision/mission text is not reused). Facts are operator-supplied only: a software product of 2B Trading, LLC, founded in Wyoming in 2022; the team sold on Amazon and eBay firsthand; 20 years of software engineering. The three principle cards (`experience` / `data` / `security`) describe verifiable product behaviour. **Past marketplace account history is deliberately NOT mentioned (operator decision)** — "sold on", never implying the stores are still live, never naming FBA.
- **No brands or unlicensed photos anywhere the landing shows (operator request).** Demo products (11) are all unbranded (no brand row is shown in a description — see "A description never shows the brand"), invented `B0SH…` ASINs, no model/MPN/UPC, with CC0 / Public Domain Mark photos in `apps/web/public/demo-products/` (see its `CREDITS.md`); no CC BY (the landing has no attribution line), no Unsplash. Template sample photos (`template-samples/`, operator decision) may be CC0 / public domain **or Pexels / Pixabay**, still no CC BY, no Unsplash, no AI-generated images; the product must look brand-new (studio shot, plain backdrop, not held, no wear) with no visible brand, and the copy must describe the item in the photo. Record source + license in `CREDITS.md`.

**Visual rules.**
- **No "AI-template" palette or iconography (operator request).** The palette is the product's own: navy, brand blue, amber, neutral ink. No violet/indigo/rose, no multi-hue gradients, no gradient-clipped text, no spinning conic borders; `colors.landing.accentViolet` / `accentRose` were deleted from the theme so they cannot creep back. Icons must be literal to their claim — never `sparkles`, `star`, `rocket`, `bolt`, `zap`.
- **Every keyframe/transition is guarded by `prefers-reduced-motion`** (`REDUCED` in `LandingPage.style.ts`; `CountUp` checks it too) — keep that on anything new that moves.
- **Type is landing-only: Plus Jakarta Sans for headlines (`FONT_HEADING`) + Inter for everything else (`FONT_BODY`)**, both loaded by the shared Google Fonts `<link>` in `apps/web/index.html`; the app's own Inter/Lexend system is untouched. Sizes come from the local `TYPE` scale / literals, not the app's `typographyTokens`.
- **Premium read**: hero background is layered radial washes (`S.HeroGlow`), **never a ruled grid** (it read as graph paper); elevation uses the brand-tinted `shadowSoft`/`shadowStrong` (neutral grey shadows read as a template); `heroBg` is `#FBFCFE` so `surface.primary` cards lift off the page.
- Section rhythm is one value: `SECTION_Y` 5.5rem / `SECTION_Y_SM` 3.5rem — larger values stacked into dead space at every section seam. Use `Section`'s `$tightTop` rather than a literal when a section needs less.
- **Ink tokens**: `colors.landing.heroText`/`heroTextMuted` follow the theme and must NOT be used on a brand fill; text on a brand fill uses `colors.landing.onAccent`. The auth-page aurora (`MeshBackground`) draws white particles and has its own `colors.landing.auroraBg` — never merge it with `heroBg` or the hero's `sidebar.background`.
- **New landing tokens are registered in all three places** — `theme.types.ts` (`ThemeColors.landing`), `themes.ts` (light + dark blocks) and `tkn.ts` (`ThemePath` union) — otherwise `tkn('colors.landing.x')` is a type error.
- **Emotion component selectors (`${Other}:hover &`) crash at runtime** (no babel plugin) — use plain element selectors.
- **A new file under `public/` is invisible to an already-running Vite dev server** (it answers the SPA fallback `text/html` with a 200, so `<img>` fails while HEAD looks fine). Restart the dev server, or verify on a second one (`npx vite --port 5199`), before concluding a new asset is broken. `vite build` copies `public/` wholesale, so this is dev-only.

**Logo and brand assets.**
- **`apps/web/public/logo.svg` is the canonical logo**, rendered centrally by `packages/ui/src/atoms/Logo/`. Do not recreate the mark as text, inline its markup in TypeScript, or move/rename the file. Sizing prop is `height` (`size` is deprecated); it keeps its ~4.72:1 ratio. The asset carries its own colours — **"SELLER" is hardcoded white, so every surface the logo renders on must be dark.** Landing sizes: navbar `height={38}`, mobile-menu head `32`, footer `34`, all `layout="full"`. Desktop collapsed sidebars hide `LogoArea`; there is no icon-only logo.
- **`layout="wordmark"` renders `apps/web/public/logo-wordmark.svg`** — the same letter paths as `logo.svg` with the mountain badge removed and the `viewBox` refitted (≈8.1:1). Used by the expanded seller/operator sidebars and the `AuthShowcase` caption (all dark surfaces). To regenerate after a `logo.svg` change, re-measure the letter bbox with a headless `getBBox` and refit the `viewBox` + `WORDMARK_WIDTH`/`WORDMARK_HEIGHT` in `Logo.component.tsx`.
- **Favicon**: `apps/web/public/favicon.svg` (a copy of `brand/icon-light.svg`) plus `favicon-32.png` and `apple-touch-icon.png` (180px, flattened on white), linked from `apps/web/index.html`. Regenerate the PNGs if the badge changes.
- **Auth screens (`AuthShowcase`)**: the logo links to `/` (`S.HomeLink = styled(Link)`); the desktop panel pairs `hero-dashboard.webp` with `mobile-dashboard.webp` in a phone frame; below `lg` the panel is hidden and `S.MobileBar` (a navy bar with the full logo) renders above the form, which is why the four auth page `LayoutWrapper`s switch to `flex-direction: column` below `lg`.

### Sign-up-free demo (`apps/web/src/features/demo/`)

A "no sign-up needed" demo that drops a visitor into a populated app, served **from browser fixtures instead of a seeded server account** — no shared mutable account whose edits leak between visitors, no extra auth surface, and no eBay/Keepa/LLM quota (metered per application and shared by every real seller) spent on someone who is only looking.

- **The flag is read ONCE at module load** (`demoMode.ts`, `sessionStorage` key `sellerhill_demo`), and entering/leaving is a **full document navigation, never a router push** — that is what stops the Redux store and RTK Query cache from mixing real and sample responses. `sessionStorage` scopes it per tab.
- `baseApi`'s `baseQueryWithReauth` short-circuits to `demoBaseQuery` before the network when the flag is set. `AuthBootstrap` needs no demo branch: `POST /auth/refresh` is answered with a sample user.
- **A write resolves as a benign success and persists nothing — it must NEVER reject** (`demoWrite`). Feature containers `.catch()` their mutations and raise a generic error modal, so a rejected write greeted visitors with a failure dialog. GETs that hand out live external surfaces answer with nothing usable: `/billing/portal` and `/ebay/connect-url` return an **empty URL** and `/billing/checkout` returns `checkoutUrl: null`, which every caller guards on.
- Every write broadcasts one `DEMO_READONLY_EVENT`; `DemoBanner` shows a single friendly **info** modal ("this is a demo, changes are not saved"), so no mutation site needs to know demo mode exists.
- **Unmapped GETs answer empty rather than erroring**, so an unpopulated screen renders its own empty state.
- **Nothing external is called.** Zero requests reach the API, so Keepa/eBay/Amazon/the LLM cannot be invoked. `TawkToWidget` returns `null` and injects nothing when `isDemoMode()` (a demo visitor must not land in a real agent queue). Only Google Fonts loads.
- **Coverage is the whole shell** — both what the business *did* (listings, orders, dashboard, action centre) and how it is *set up* (profile, two eBay stores, two Amazon buyer accounts, global + per-store settings with a blacklist, buyer-message templates, four listing strategy groups, business policies, listing-job history, an active Growth subscription). Every sidebar route renders real content.
- Fixtures are generated from a fixed seed and anchored to "now". Dashboard period totals are **aggregated from the sample order rows**, never typed in, so cards cannot contradict the order list; the first three orders are pinned to the last few hours and fully cost-captured so "Today" is never $0.
- Files: `demoMode.ts`, `demoData.ts` (**one** fixture file — do not split it), `demoBaseQuery.ts`, `DemoBanner/` (4-file split, mounted once in `App.tsx`, renders `null` outside demo mode). i18n under `demo.*`.

### Legal documents (`apps/web/src/features/legal/`, 2026-09-07)

Privacy Policy at `/:locale/privacy` and Terms of Service at `/:locale/terms`, public, EN + TR, plus locale-less `/privacy` and `/terms` redirects, linked from the landing footer. "Cookies" points at `/privacy#cookies` — section 13 of the privacy policy is the cookie disclosure.

- **THE TEXT IS THE OPERATOR'S, NOT OURS.** It is supplied verbatim and carries legal liability. Do not translate a missing section, fill a gap from the other locale, delete a clause that contradicts the code, or reword for precision. **Report a discrepancy and let the operator decide.**
- **Content is STRUCTURED BLOCKS in the i18n `legal` namespace**, not prose or raw HTML. `LegalBlockType` (`packages/shared/src/domain/legal/`) is `p | h | ul | ol | dl | address | email`. Raw HTML cannot be style-tokenised, and one-key-per-paragraph loses the list structure these documents rely on.
- **`parseLegalDocument` (`features/legal/utils/`) narrows the `returnObjects` payload and fails soft** — a malformed block or section is dropped; a document with no usable sections resolves to `null` and the page shows `legal.unavailable`. This keeps the renderer's switch total over `LegalBlockType`.
- **Parity is asserted at the SECTION level only**, never block-for-block — the Turkish is written natively and legitimately needs different block counts. Both documents carry the same section ids in the same order.
- **The URL locale outranks the stored preference.** `LegalDocumentPageContainer` syncs `i18n.changeLanguage` to the path segment, deliberately WITHOUT writing the preference (following one link is not a decision to switch the app language).
- **`Text` always sets its own colour and never inherits**, so a label on the dark header must name a `sidebar.*` token (`color="sidebar.textMuted"`).
- **Headings are `role="heading"` + `aria-level` on wrappers**, because `Text` renders a `<span>` and raw typography elements are forbidden — a screen reader needs the heading structure.
- `LocaleRedirect` has **`preserveHash`** so `/privacy#cookies` survives the redirect (off by default).
- **The retention clause ties orders/buyer data to the account lifetime plus the §20 deletion request** (operator-approved), because nothing purges `orders`. **If a real purge is ever implemented, this clause has to move back.**
- **Account deletion is a manual email process to `support@sellerhill.com` (operator decision).** `POST /auth/deactivate` is a soft delete (`status = 'inactive'`) that erases nothing and has **no UI entry point**; leaving is cancelling the subscription in the Stripe portal. **Do not reintroduce a deactivate/delete-account button** — it cut the seller's login while every listing, order and connected account stayed, which support then had to undo by hand; a self-service delete needs a real erasure path first.
- Verified against the code: IP and user-agent are logged, the top-up validity period is shown before purchase (`billing.addons.note`), audit logs 730 days, buyer-message logs 400, fulfillment evidence 7, dormant browser profiles 90. **Do not add disclaimers for features that do not exist** (a "no product sourcing" clause was removed from both Terms by operator decision — the landing rule is to not claim product research, not to deny it in a binding document).

### Custom ESLint Plugin (`eslint-plugin-design-system.js`)

Source: `packages/ui/src/eslint-plugin/index.js`, wired in the root `package.json` as the `eslint-plugin-design-system` devDependency and enabled in `.eslintrc.json` (landing and `apps/api` are exempted via overrides). Enabled rules:
- `no-hardcoded-colors` — Use theme tokens, not hex/rgb
- `no-hardcoded-spacing` — Use theme spacing tokens
- `no-inline-styles` — No style prop in JSX
- `no-styled-typography` — Use `<Text>` component, not styled.h1/h2/etc.
- `no-bare-text-in-button` — Wrap Button children in `<Text>`
- `styled-only-in-style-files`, `types-only-in-types-files`, `logic-only-in-container`, `no-styled-in-container` — the 4-file split (see Architectural Rule 6)

`no-implicit-i18n-namespaces` and `no-native-select` exist in the plugin but are commented out in `.eslintrc.json` (not enforced).

## Domain Concepts

- **Product**: Source of truth from Amazon (ASIN, images, price, features). Cached to save API costs. Shared **ASIN + marketplace**-keyed across all users (see "Multi-marketplace architecture" below).
- **Listing**: An active offer on eBay. Linked to a Listing Setting Group **and** an eBay account (`listings.ebay_account_id`, multi-store). Per-listing automation overrides live on the listing row (pause sales, fixed price/qty, margins).
- **Listing Setting Group**: Repricing strategy, stock buffer, fees, HTML templates, plus **content policy** (`content` JSONB: strip brand from title, optional AI title/description flags). Multiple listings share one group.
- **Order**: An eBay sale. Linked to a Listing (`listing_id`) and eBay account (`ebay_account_id`). Product info via listing→product JOIN. Financials on both eBay and Amazon sides.
- **Amazon Account**: Buyer Amazon account for order scraping. AES-256-GCM credentials. Multiple per user. Carries its own `marketplace` (immutable after creation — see "Multi-marketplace architecture").
- **eBay Account**: Connected store(s). Multi-store filtering on listings + orders by `ebayAccountId`. OAuth tokens are **encrypted at rest** (AES-256-GCM via `AMAZON_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, `enc:` prefix in `ebay_accounts.access_token`/`refresh_token`; `EbayService.onModuleInit` lazily re-encrypts legacy plaintext rows). All API callers must obtain tokens via `EbayService.getActiveAccountAccessToken(userId)` / `getAccountAccessToken(accountId)` — never read the raw columns.

## Multi-marketplace architecture (eBay + Amazon, 2026-08-13)

Only `eBay.com`/`amazon.com` (US) is live. This section is the seam that lets a second marketplace be enabled later **without a schema migration or a rewrite** — without building support for any specific undecided country now.

**One allowlist per integration gates what is selectable.** `SUPPORTED_EBAY_MARKETPLACES` / `SUPPORTED_AMAZON_MARKETPLACES` (`packages/shared/src/domain/{ebay,amazon}/*.constants.ts`) decide what a picker offers AND what the backend accepts (`EbayService.createConnectUrl` / `AmazonAccountsService.create` throw `BadRequestException` outside the allowlist). Enabling a second marketplace is one array entry + real config data — never a new code path.

- **eBay**: `EbayMarketplaceId` (US/UK/DE/FR/IT/ES) and `EBAY_MARKETPLACE_CONFIG` (currency, siteId, countryCode) carry verified data for all six; `ebay_accounts.marketplace_id` is threaded through `ebay.service.ts`/order sync/fulfillment and the OAuth backend accepts `marketplaceId`. Every eBay-connect call site (`EbayAccountGuard`, `OnboardingEbayPage`, `StoresPage`, `SettingsHubPage` → `EbayAccountsDrawer`) renders a `ModernSelect` (via `ConnectEbayPrompt`'s `marketplaceOptions`/`selectedMarketplace`/`onMarketplaceChange`, or directly in the drawer) sourced from `getEbayMarketplaceOptions(t)` (`apps/web/src/features/ebay/utils/ebayMarketplaceOptions.ts`) — today exactly one option (US). `getEbayMarketplaceLabel` is the single label resolver for the picker, `StoresPage` and the `EbayAccountCard` mapper (`ebay.connect.marketplace{US,UK,DE,FR,IT,ES}` keys).
- **Amazon**:
  - `AmazonMarketplace` enum (`packages/shared/src/domain/amazon/amazon.enums.ts`) **has a single member, `AMAZON_US`**, deliberately not pre-populated — a second Amazon marketplace's Keepa domain id/TLD are undecided product scope. `AMAZON_MARKETPLACE_CONFIG` (`amazon.constants.ts`) carries `countryCode`/`currency`/`domain` (TLD)/`keepaDomainId`, mirroring the eBay config shape.
  - Migration `081`: `amazon_accounts.marketplace VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'AMAZON_US'`, set **only at create** (`CreateAmazonAccountDto`/`createAmazonAccountSchema`) and **immutable after creation** — `UpdateAmazonAccountDto` has no such field. `AmazonAccountDrawer` shows the picker in create mode and a locked one (+ hint) in edit mode.
  - Migration `082`: `products.marketplace VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'AMAZON_US'` and a composite `products_asin_marketplace_key UNIQUE (asin, marketplace)` replacing the single-column ASIN key (the same ASIN can be a different product on another storefront). Every query that filters/upserts `products` by `asin` also takes a `marketplace` parameter defaulting to `AMAZON_US` (`ListingsService.getProductByAsin`/`findOrCreateProduct` incl. its `ON CONFLICT` target, `ListingProcessorService.resolveProductData` incl. its advisory-lock key, `ProductSyncService`, `ProductsService.getProductPriceAndImageByAsin`/`getProductPriceByAsin`).
  - `KeepaService.request()`/`getProducts()`/`getProductDetailsWithMeta()`/`getProductDetails()`/`normalizeProductData()` take a `marketplace` parameter (default `AMAZON_US`); `domain` reads `AMAZON_MARKETPLACE_CONFIG[marketplace].keepaDomainId` and currency reads the config. `RefreshProcessorService.refreshBatch` groups a claimed batch **by marketplace** before calling Keepa (one `domain` per request) — at one marketplace that is exactly one group.
  - Amazon URLs go through `buildAmazonSiteUrl(marketplace)` / `buildAmazonProductUrl(asin, marketplace)` (`packages/shared/src/domain/ebay/ebay.urls.ts` — the one Amazon-URL home despite the folder name), threaded through `AmazonCheckoutService` (`checkout()`, `ensureLoggedIn()`; `loadInputs` selects `a.marketplace`) and `AmazonScrapingService` (`performLogin`, `doScrapeOrder`, `doScrapeOrderStatus`, `doScrapeAccountOrders`, `ensureAuthenticatedPage`, `testLogin` — `AmazonAccountsService.getDecrypted` returns `marketplace`).
  - **Deliberately NOT made marketplace-aware**: `amazon-order-parser.service.ts`'s currency-symbol regexes and `keepa-normalizer.ts`'s imperial-unit weight formatting (only USD/imperial exist today); `amazon-checkout.service.ts`'s module-scope `CHECKOUT_SELECTORS.cartUrl` and `amazon-scraping.service.ts`'s module-level sign-in URL const (no account context at that scope); `browser-state-manager.service.ts`'s `isSessionValid` (no account/DB dependency there).
- **Not implemented, deliberately**: no "choose Amazon source marketplace" UI at listing creation (`resolveProductData` always resolves to `AMAZON_US` via the default), no scraping/checkout for a non-US Amazon domain, no currency-symbol-aware order parsing.
## Product Refresh Pipeline

Amazon product data (metadata + Buy Box price + stock) comes from the provider selected by the panel setting **`product.dataProvider`** (`/admin` → Settings → Scraper; env `PRODUCT_DATA_PROVIDER`; default **`scraper`**). `ProductSourceService` (`apps/api/src/modules/listings/product-source.service.ts`, exported by `ProductSourceModule`) is the single switch: the create path (`ListingProcessorService.resolveProductData`), the existing-listing import and the refresh worker (`RefreshProcessorService`) all ask it which provider is live and never call `KeepaService` or `ScraperClient` directly on the other branch. **Keepa is the whole-provider rollback** — one setting flips every call back, no redeploy — and nothing falls back to Keepa per ASIN (operator decision: the paid provider must never be re-engaged by accident; a missing barcode, a blocked page or a 404 is handled inside the scraper branch). Do not reintroduce ScraperAPI — its per-request credit model is 10–100× more expensive than Keepa's token pool under bulk-add/churn.

The scheduler, claim, lease, quarantine and change-detection rules below ("Stale-driven scheduler + batch worker" onwards) apply to **both** providers; only "Keepa provider", "Stock semantics" and "Token tracking" are Keepa-specific.

### Scraper provider (2026-09-26)

Design: [docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-amazon-scraper-provider-design.md](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-amazon-scraper-provider-design.md). A separate Python service in **`services/amazon-scraper/`** — vendored upstream `omkarcloud/amazon-scraper` (commit `ea5aefd`, `curl_cffi` browser-TLS impersonation, bottle/cheroot) with a `sellerhill/` layer on top: `egress.py` (proxy binding), `fetcher.py`, `signals.py` (commerce parse), `content.py` (full parse), `parsing.py` (process pool), `pool.py` (proxy pool), `verify.py` (proxy test), `app.py` (`POST /v1/products`, `GET /v1/stats`, `POST /v1/proxies/verify`, `/health`; `X-Scraper-Secret` auth via `hmac.compare_digest`; every data call answers **503 until `SCRAPER_SERVICE_SECRET` is set**, 401 without it). The api reaches it over `SCRAPER_SERVICE_URL` with `ScraperClient` (`scraper.client.ts`, 200 s timeout, never logs a body). `SCRAPER_SERVICE_URL` is env-only on purpose — a panel-editable address would let a compromised admin session redirect the secret.

- **Never scrape from the server's own IP — a proxy is mandatory (operator decision).** `ProductSourceService` returns `NO_PROXY` and makes **no HTTP call** when the proxy list is empty; the refresh claim **pauses before leasing any row** while the provider is `scraper` and no proxy is configured (a `debug` line, not a per-tick warning); the Python side raises `NoProxyError` unless a proxy is bound. `SCRAPER_ALLOW_DIRECT=1` is the developer-machine-only exception: it may exist in `docker-compose.yml` and must never appear in `docker-compose.test.yml` / `docker-compose.production.yml` — `scraper-egress.guard.spec.ts` fails the build if it does. Proxies are entered at **`/admin` → Settings → Scraper** (`scraper.proxies`, a write-only secret; `SCRAPER_PROXIES` in `apps/api/.env` for local dev) and sent per request; the URLs carry credentials and are never logged, returned or rendered (`ScraperStats` carries `host:port` ids only).
- **Deployment.** In both Coolify stacks the service sits on a private **`scraper_net`** with no published port and is not on `coolify`, so a test and a production stack on one host can never reach each other's scraper. The api's `depends_on` is `service_started`, deliberately not `service_healthy`: the api fails soft (admin warning `SCRAPER_UNREACHABLE`, products keep their last values), and a health gate would stop billing, orders and auth from booting whenever the scraper is down. The image runs as a non-root `scraper` user; `.dockerignore` keeps the Windows `.venv` out of the Linux image.
- **Stock is a lower bound whenever Amazon does not print a number** (`SourceStockStatus` in `packages/shared`, `products.stock_status`, migration `124`):

  | Page signal | Stored | Shown |
  |---|---|---|
  | "Only N left in stock" (N ≤ 20) | `EXACT` N | N |
  | "In Stock", order-quantity dropdown maxes at D < 20 | `AT_LEAST` D | **D+** |
  | "In Stock" otherwise | `AT_LEAST` 20 (`scraper.inStockFloor`) | **20+** |
  | "Currently unavailable" | `OUT_OF_STOCK` 0 | 0 |
  | No Buy Box ("See All Buying Options" only, `noFeaturedOffer`) | `OUT_OF_STOCK` 0, stored price kept — create refuses as `NO_BUY_BOX` | 0 |
  | HTTP 404, or Amazon's HTTP-200 "Sorry, we couldn't find that page" (`fetcher.py` `_is_soft_not_found`) | `not_found` → 0 + `products.source_removed_at` | 0, "unavailable on Amazon" |
  | found page, availability unreadable | `UNKNOWN` — **previous stock kept** | previous value |
  | blocked / captcha / proxy failure / deadline | skipped — **previous price and stock kept**, re-claimed after the lease | previous value |
  | parse failure (no `productTitle`, not a soft-404) | data failure: `consecutive_failures++`, escalating backoff, quarantine after `KEEPA_REFRESH_MAX_FAILURES`, then the Action Center item | previous value |

  Amazon never prints "Only N left" above 20, and a seller's per-order limit suppresses it entirely while stock is above that limit — which is why the dropdown max is read as a floor only when the page says "In Stock". The seller-facing wording for `not_found` is **"unavailable on Amazon" / "Amazon'da erişilemiyor"** — never "removed"/"kaldırıldı". Every surface renders an `AT_LEAST` value as `N+` through `formatSourceStock` (`packages/shared/src/utils/source-stock.ts`) — listing detail, table column, CSV export, the advanced-filter note — because a bare "20" reads as an exact count (`listing-source-stock.guard.spec.ts`). The dropdown max is stored as **`products.max_order_quantity`** and caps the quantity formula: `min(max(stock − buffer, 0), defaultQuantity, maxOrderQuantity ?? ∞)` (`ListingStrategyService.calculateQuantity`), so a listing never promises more than one Amazon order can buy. A create refused at quantity 0 shows the stock/buffer arithmetic (`zero_stock_detail`, `_at_least` variant) only when stock is actually above 0. The soft-404 is `not_found`, not `parse_failed`, so the seller sees "ASIN not found" and BullMQ does not retry it as a data failure.
- **A price is never invented.** A page that is NOT out of stock but whose price cannot be read ("see price in cart", a coupon layout, a price-only DOM change) is `unavailable`/`PARSE_FAILED` on create — never a product row, because a stored 0 would then serve every later seller of that ASIN. An OUT-OF-STOCK page with no price is saved at price 0 / stock 0: a live create is refused as `ZERO_STOCK` (the create worker checks quantity through `computePricing` first), a draft is kept. `assertSourcePricePublishable` refuses any live create or draft publish whose source price is ≤ 0 with `SourcePriceUnavailableError` → `ListingFailureCode.SOURCE_PRICE_UNAVAILABLE` (terminal, provider-neutral — it also closes Keepa's missing-Buy-Box `?? 0`; a single-draft publish maps it to a 409 with the localized reason). Both paths call it BEFORE the EPS upload and the LLM rewrite; `prepareListingData({ live: true })` re-checks.
  - **Such a draft never becomes publishable on its own** — the refresh only visits products with an ACTIVE listing. The seller deletes the draft and re-adds the ASIN once Amazon shows a price; `asUsableCache` treats a row with no price > 0 as a cache miss, so the re-add re-fetches.
  - **The fan-out never sends a price ≤ 0 but still syncs QUANTITY** (skipping the product would leave a listing live at its old quantity after Amazon ran out, and oversell). The bulk offer goes out with `availableQuantity` and `BulkPriceQuantityItem.price = null`, so eBay keeps its price, and `persistQuantityOnly` writes the quantity alone. One warn per product: "price unknown — quantity synced, price left as is".
- **Two fetch modes, one page.** `commerce` (refresh) soups only the price/availability/buy-box fragments; `full` (create) runs the whole parse plus **our own gallery extractor** (upstream mixed every variant's images) and never uses A+ text as the description. Specs are translated at input (`source-content-mapper.ts` `translateScraperSpecs` CANONICAL/DROP maps → the same names `extractProductAttributes` gives Keepa data) so there is **one eBay aspect matcher**, not a scraper-specific one. `buildScraperSpecs` adds what the page shows outside its spec table — Brand/Manufacturer, Model/MPN from validated identifiers, this ASIN's twister selection (`variationAttributes`: Color/Size/Scent…), and `Item Length/Width/Height` parsed from the one-cell `Item Dimensions` string in Keepa's `in`/`oz`/`lbs` spelling. The residual gap to Keepa (`Package Quantity`, `Unit Type`, `Type`) is catalogue data the page never renders. A barcode cell listing several codes yields the first check-digit-valid one rather than failing whole.
- **Parsing runs in worker PROCESSES; thread sizing and stats use NETWORK time, never wall time.** Parsing a 2–3.5 MB page costs ~1.5 s of CPU; run in the fetch threads it serialised under the GIL, wall-time "latency" read the queueing as slow proxies, and latency-driven sizing added threads that made it worse — slow batches are CPU-bound, do not "fix" them with threads. `sellerhill/parsing.py` `run_parse` uses a spawned `ProcessPoolExecutor` (`SCRAPER_PARSE_PROCESSES`, default `min(3, CPUs)`, 0 = inline, cap 8; workers recycled every 300 pages, ~80 MB peak each — mind `SCRAPER_MEMORY_LIMIT`), rebuilt with one retry on `BrokenProcessPool`. Spawn, never fork (the parent is multi-threaded). `fetch_one` reports `netMs` (measured before parsing) for stats and sizing; it is stripped from the wire. Thread-local sessions pay a warm-up once per thread, so **the first batch after a scraper deploy is the slowest** — never judge throughput on it.
- **Capacity is proxies × rate, and the batch size follows it.** `scraper.perIpRequestsPerSecond` (default 1, bounds 0.1–10) × proxy count × 60 × (1 − reserve) is the per-minute refresh batch (`resolveScraperRefreshBatchSize`, `scraper-refresh.ts`, pure) — the analogue of Keepa's refill-rate sizing. Worker threads are pinned to one proxy for life (curl sessions are thread-local, so an IP must never change under a session); their count is `threads_for_rate` in `pool.py`: `ceil(rate × max(4, measuredLatency × 1.5))`, floor `SCRAPER_THREADS_PER_PROXY`, cap 40, grow-only. Two lanes (interactive create ahead of background refresh), a per-proxy token bucket, a cooldown after 3 consecutive blocks or proxy errors, and a 150 s deadline enforced by the waiter. `/v1/stats` keeps causes apart — `expired` (deadline) and `proxyError` (dead exit, auth 407, repeated 5xx) are their own counters, never `blocked`, though both reach the API as `blocked` (transient, retry after the lease); a result is recorded only when it is the one the caller received. The `listings` queue uses **fair per-seller priority** (`fair-priority.ts`: `1 + ceil(queued/25) + chunkIndex`) so many sellers adding at once interleave.
- **The refresh claim's entitlement/plan-limit predicate is one builder, `buildRefreshEntitlementSql` (`refresh-entitlement-sql.ts`)**, shared by the real claim and the admin refresh-lag/count query and gated on the same enforcement flag. `entitlement-enforcement.guard.spec.ts` and `listing-plan-limit.guard.spec.ts` slice the claim's method body (comments stripped) and assert it CALLS the builder and interpolates its fragments.
- **Proxy editor + connectivity test.** `scraper.proxies` is ONE write-only secret string (comma-joined `scheme://[user:pass@]host:port`), edited through `ScraperProxiesEditor` (`apps/web/src/features/admin/components/ScraperProxiesEditor/`, 4-file split): one row per proxy (scheme, host, port, username, password), writing the joined string into the same draft the row's Save/Cancel use. A row contributes only once host AND port are filled. Each row has **Test**, the block has **Test saved configuration**: `POST /v1/admin/settings/scraper/proxies/verify` (`AdminService.verifyScraperProxies`, body `{ proxies? }` — a draft, or omitted to test the saved list decrypted by `ProductSourceService.proxies()`) → `ScraperClient.verifyProxies` → the service's `POST /v1/proxies/verify` (`sellerhill/verify.py`), which probes each proxy concurrently with one request to `https://www.google.com/generate_204` — deliberately **not Amazon** (operator decision: a click spends no scraping capacity and adds nothing to Amazon's view of our block rate). Results are `{ id: host:port, ok, errorKind (invalid|proxy|timeout|dns|unreachable|other), latencyMs }`; a 407 is `proxy`. **The credential is never returned, logged or rendered** (`scraper-proxy-verify.guard.spec.ts` greps all four hops; the service redacts via `egress.redact`). Max 50 proxies per call; `socks5://` is probed as `socks5h://`.
- **`scraper.proxies` has one grammar on all three sides** — `isValidProxyUrl` / `partitionProxyList` (`packages/shared/src/utils/proxy-url.ts`) and the same regex in `app.py`: `^(http|https|socks5|socks5h)://[^\s]+:\d{1,5}$`, port ≤ 65535. The panel flags the first bad entry by POSITION as it is typed, the API refuses to save a list containing one (`admin.errors.setting.notProxyUrl`), `ProductSourceService` drops bad entries before a request, and the service ignores individual bad entries (logging the count) rather than 400-ing the whole call. A list of only bad entries is "no proxy", never direct egress. `socks5://` is bound as `socks5h://` (`egress.remote_dns`) so Amazon's hostname resolves at the proxy. No message anywhere carries a proxy value.
- **Admin visibility** (`/admin` → Overview, `AdminWarningKind`): `SCRAPER_NO_PROXIES` (critical — nothing is fetched), `SCRAPER_UNREACHABLE`, `SCRAPER_BLOCK_RATE_HIGH` and `SCRAPER_PARSE_FAILURE_HIGH` (both on `scraper.blockRateWarnPercent`, default 10, critical at 2×; each a share of found+notFound+blocked+parseFailed over the last hour), `SCRAPER_PROXY_INVALID` (count of malformed entries, which are skipped), `SCRAPER_TRANSPORT_FAILURE_HIGH` (share of `proxyError` + `expired` over every outcome, same knob — the only alarm a well-formed but dead or wrong-password list trips), `SCRAPER_DIRECT_EGRESS` (critical — the service reports `directAllowed: true` on `/health` and `/v1/stats`, which a PaaS env panel can cause even though no compose file sets it; the service also logs a loud startup line), `REFRESH_LAG` (oldest overdue product vs the interval, either provider). With the scraper live the Keepa balance card becomes last-hour ok·blocked·unreadable·proxy error·timed out counts, `meanLatencyMs · workers/proxy`, and **achievable syncs/day** (`proxies × rps × 86 400 × (1 − keepa.refresh.reservePercent) / unique refreshed ASINs`). The lag query fails soft (renders empty rather than 500-ing the operations summary). The Action Center's `LISTING_SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE` item and the listings `sourceUnavailable` filter count `source_removed_at IS NOT NULL` alongside the quarantine threshold through one predicate (`source-unavailable.guard.spec.ts`); the item's two exclusive chips (`SourceUnavailableReason`: `removed` = not found, quantity already 0; `unreadable` = quarantine, usually OUR parser failing) never claim "still buyable" or "removed".
- **`pnpm --filter api provider:compare -- --asins B0…,B0…`** (`src/scripts/provider-compare.ts`, read-only, dotenv only) fetches the same ASINs from both providers and prints one row per differing field (incl. `stockStatus`/`stock`) plus totals. Proxies come only from `SCRAPER_PROXIES` — there is no `--proxies` flag, so credentials never land in shell history. Spends ~6–7 Keepa tokens per ASIN. Run it before trusting the scraper after an Amazon layout change. Price differences on Prime-priced items are expected — buyer accounts are Prime.
- **Rollback**: set `product.dataProvider` to `keepa`. The Keepa path writes `stock_status` (`keepaStockStatusToSource`) on both its changed and unchanged branches and, whenever its observation is not UNKNOWN, **clears the scraper-era `max_order_quantity` and `source_removed_at`** (`planKeepaRollback`), counting a lifted cap as a commerce change so eBay is repriced once — otherwise a stale dropdown cap would throttle that product's listings for ever (`scraper-refresh.guard.spec.ts`).
- **Parser maintenance is ours.** Upstream is vendored, not tracked; fixes land in `sellerhill/` and `source-content-mapper.ts`, each with a fixture (`tests/fixtures/*.html.gz` + `expected.json`, values derived by reading the HTML, never by a browser). Rules the fixtures lock:
  - `extract_gallery` tries `A.$.parseJSON('[...]')` → the plain JS array media pages (Blu-ray/DVD/books) write for `'colorImages': { 'initial': [...] }` (decoded with `json.JSONDecoder().raw_decode` from the bracket, because entries nest `[w, h]` arrays) → the rendered main image (`landingImage`'s `data-old-hires`, else the largest `data-a-dynamic-image`). Fixture `media_literal_initial`.
  - Commerce fragments are re-serialised per fragment in `signals.py` (raw HTML slices nested and contaminated the price). `_fragments` caps each element at `_FRAGMENT_CHARS` (60k), so when the lean pass leaves price/availability/seller missing it re-reads them from the full document — the full parse is the reference (`test_commerce_price_equals_full_parse`, fixture `buybox_beyond_window`).
  - **No Buy Box** emits `noFeaturedOffer`, keyed on the `buybox-see-all-buying-choices` element id — **never** on "No featured offers" text, which hidden variation templates of buyable pages also carry — and only when neither price nor availability was read, so it can never refuse a buyable page. On create, `ProductSourceService.fetchForCreate` maps it to `{ kind: 'no_buy_box' }` before the normalizer and `resolveProductData` throws `NoBuyBoxError` → `ListingFailureCode.NO_BUY_BOX` (terminal; `listings.jobs.failure.no_buy_box`). On refresh (operator decision) `normalizeScraperCommerce` reads it as an OUT_OF_STOCK 0 observation (stored price kept), so `planScraperRefresh` fans quantity 0 out to eBay — a listing must not stay live with nothing auto-fulfill could buy. Fixture `no_featured_offer`.
  - When Amazon changes its DOM the alarm is **`SCRAPER_PARSE_FAILURE_HIGH`** (the block rate actually falls, because parse failures inflate its denominator). A page with no readable title takes the data-failure path — backoff 5m → 15m → 60m → 240m, quarantine after `KEEPA_REFRESH_MAX_FAILURES` — so **a whole catalogue is quarantined within ~5.5 h** and surfaces in the Action Center as "product data could not be read". Prices and stock are never zeroed by it, but refresh stops for those products until the parser is fixed.

### Keepa provider

Keepa is the rollback provider — one query shape for every call (create + refresh alike): `history=0&stats=90&offers=20&only-live-offers=1&stock=1`. **Full technical reference (verified cost model, response ground truth, invariants, probe procedure): [docs/keepa-integration.md](docs/keepa-integration.md) — read it before touching any Keepa code.**

**Token cost model (verified against live responses):** charges are conditional — `offers` costs **6 tokens per FOUND page of 10 live offers** when Keepa refreshes its offer cache, **0 tokens** when that cache is <1h fresh, and `stock=1` adds **+2 only when the stock observation is fresh** (observed 0–6 tokens/ASIN for the same query). Therefore: (a) accounting always uses the response-reported `tokensConsumed`, **never** a locally-estimated per-ASIN count; (b) `history=0`/`stats`/`only-live-offers` are payload optimizations, **not** token savers. Real Buy Box stock requires `offers` — `stock=1` without `offers` does not return usable per-seller stock.

### Shared product cache
`products` is a shared ASIN + marketplace-keyed cache — one ASIN exists once regardless of how many users list it, and one fetch serves every customer listing it. There is **no per-user provider cost isolation**; Keepa cost is attributed via fair-split (see Token tracking).

### Stale-driven scheduler + batch worker
There is **no "refresh everything every N hours" cron**. The `keepa-refresh` BullMQ queue (the name predates the scraper; it serves both providers) has two roles:
- **Scheduler** (`RefreshSchedulerService` registers the tick): every tick runs an **atomic claim** — a `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` CTE that selects the most-overdue products **having ≥1 ACTIVE listing** (and an entitled, within-plan owner — see `buildRefreshEntitlementSql`) and pushes their `next_refresh_at` forward by a short lease (`KEEPA_REFRESH_CLAIM_LEASE_MINUTES`, default 15) in the same statement — then enqueues **one** `refresh-batch` job. Overlapping ticks/parallel workers can never double-claim; a crashed batch's rows become due again when the lease expires. Products whose listings are all draft/ended are **not** refreshed (a scope rule, NOT per-product sales-velocity tiering, which is explicitly not done).
- **Worker** (`RefreshProcessorService`): one bulk provider call per batch (Keepa `getProducts`, which dedupes and chunks at Keepa's 100-ASIN/request limit summing `tokensConsumed`; or the scraper's `fetchCommerce`, grouped by marketplace) → per-product compare + update + fan-out. Per-product failures are isolated; a transport failure (429/5xx/network) propagates so BullMQ retries the whole batch idempotently (rows stay claimed).

### Stock semantics (three-state, unknown-preserve)
Keepa branch: `KeepaStockStatus` (`known|out_of_stock|unknown` in `packages/shared`), mapped to `SourceStockStatus` by `keepaStockStatusToSource`. The normalizer (`keepa-normalizer.ts`, pure + fixture-tested) matches the **Buy Box offer by sellerId** in `liveOffersOrder` and reads the last `stockCSV` observation; falls back to `stats.stockBuyBox` / `stats.stockAmazon` (Amazon caps at 1000). `OUT_OF_STOCK` (stock 0) only when live-offer retrieval **succeeded** and no live offer + no price exists. Anything ambiguous → `UNKNOWN`, and the worker **preserves the previous price/stock** — failing to observe is never evidence of $0/qty-0. Price sentinels (`-1` no data, `-2` no Buy Box) are null, never real prices. `totalOfferCount` is **never** a stock quantity. The modern `images` array is preferred over legacy `imagesCSV` (live responses can null the CSV).

### Field change-detection
`commerceChanged` (price/stock) triggers the eBay fan-out (`ProductSyncService.updateAllListingsForProduct` — pushes only where a listing's own price/qty actually changed); `metadataChanged` (title/brand/description) persists to the products row **without** fan-out. eBay API volume stays proportional to real changes.

### Failure handling & poison-product quarantine
- **Transport failure**: BullMQ exponential backoff retries the batch; rows stay claimed (lease), then become due again if all attempts fail.
- **Data failure** (ASIN missing from an otherwise-successful response, or a scraper parse failure): `consecutive_failures++` with **escalating backoff 5m → 15m → 60m → 240m** (`refresh-backoff.ts`, tested) so a dead ASIN cannot burn capacity every tick; at/above `KEEPA_REFRESH_MAX_FAILURES` it is quarantined (`KEEPA_REFRESH_QUARANTINE_MINUTES`).

### Config-driven frequency (no rewrite to change cadence)
Panel-tunable at `/admin` → Settings unless noted (DB override → env → default); the `KEEPA_` names predate the scraper but the cadence knobs govern both providers:
- `KEEPA_REFRESH_ENABLED=true` — kill switch, checked by the processor on every tick (no restart needed); `RefreshSchedulerService` also reads it at boot to register the tick. Create-path calls are unaffected.
- `KEEPA_REFRESH_INTERVAL_MINUTES=360` (6h, 4×/day; bounds 15–10080) — governs BOTH providers; lowered by hand as the catalogue grows. The landing page's "4×/day" claim depends on it staying at 360.
- `KEEPA_REFRESH_BATCH_AUTO=true` / `KEEPA_REFRESH_RESERVE_PERCENT=20` — **the batch size is derived from capacity by default.** The scheduler ticks once a minute, so `batchSize` IS the per-minute throughput: too high outruns the provider and burns the retry budget on 429s; too low **silently stretches** the configured interval. Keepa: `resolveRefreshBatchSize` (`refresh-batch-size.ts`, pure + Jest-covered) = `floor(refill_rate × (1 − reserve) / 8 tokens)` from `keepa_balance.refill_rate`, clamped to registry bounds, always floored (overshooting costs real money in retries). Scraper: `resolveScraperRefreshBatchSize` (above). The reserve keeps background refresh from starving the create path.
- `KEEPA_REFRESH_BATCH_SIZE=50` — fallback only, when auto is off or no Keepa balance has been captured yet.
- `KEEPA_REFRESH_CLAIM_LEASE_MINUTES=15` — must exceed worst-case batch runtime incl. BullMQ retries.
- `KEEPA_REFRESH_SCHEDULER_CRON=* * * * *` (`requiresRestart`), `KEEPA_REFRESH_QUARANTINE_MINUTES=1440`, `KEEPA_REFRESH_MAX_FAILURES=5`; `KEEPA_REFRESH_WORKER_CONCURRENCY=1` (env-only).
- `KEEPA_UPDATE_HOURS` (env-only, unset by default) — Keepa-side cache tolerance: accept Keepa's cached offer data younger than N hours for ~0 tokens instead of forcing a live refresh (6 tokens/found page). Keep well below the refresh interval. `update=-1` (never refresh) is deliberately unsupported — niche ASINs would go permanently stale.

Keepa budgeting: the offers+stock query costs ~6–14 tokens/ASIN on offer-cache refresh (0 when cached). Sustainable throughput ≈ `refill_rate_per_min / avg_tokens_per_asin`; e.g. 20 tpm ÷ 8 ≈ 2.5 ASIN/min ≈ 3.6k ASIN/day. `keepa_balance.refill_rate` (migration `055`) records the plan tier from each response.

### Token tracking (cost attribution)
- `keepa_usage_log` — one row per **requested** ASIN per call (fair share = batch `tokensConsumed` / requested-ASIN count — missing ASINs still get their row, so their users are not subsidized by users of returned ASINs), `source ('refresh'|'create')`, `user_ids` (JSONB array of users actively listing the ASIN). Append-only; purged after 90 days by the `data-retention` job. Projected into `usage_events` (`keepa-projection.ts`, `UsageEventsService`) for the admin Costs/Users tabs.
- `keepa_balance` — `tokens_left`/`refill_in_ms`/`refill_rate` snapshots from **every** Keepa response (refresh + create).
- Ad-hoc per-user monthly cost (fair-split): `SELECT uid, SUM(tokens / jsonb_array_length(user_ids)) AS tokens FROM keepa_usage_log CROSS JOIN LATERAL jsonb_array_elements_text(user_ids) AS uid WHERE requested_at >= date_trunc('month', NOW()) GROUP BY uid ORDER BY tokens DESC;`
- Token-balance-driven admission control is deferred until real utilization data accumulates.

### Listing creation path
`ListingProcessorService` (the `listings` queue worker) resolves product data via `resolveProductData`:
- **Cache hit = valid title + ≥1 image + price > 0 + FRESH** → no provider call. Fresh means not `source_removed_at` and `updated_at` within the refresh interval (`ProductSourceService.createCacheMaxAgeMs()` = `keepa.refresh.intervalMinutes`; `isCreateCacheFresh`, `create-cache-freshness.ts`, pure + Jest-covered; `getProductByAsin` selects `updated_at` → `ProductData.updatedAt`). The refresh only visits products with an ACTIVE listing, so a row nobody lists can be arbitrarily old — do not relax this: stale rows once published LIVE listings for ASINs Amazon had removed, with image URLs eBay could no longer fetch. A row whose stored price is ≤ 0 is a miss by design, bounded at one fetch per create.
- **Cache miss** → a **pg advisory lock keyed on the ASIN** serializes concurrent creates (recheck-after-lock), so N parallel jobs for one uncached ASIN make exactly **one** provider call (`getProductDetailsWithMeta` on Keepa, the batch prefetch — `product-source-prefetch.helpers.ts` — on the scraper). Keepa usage + balance are logged from response meta **before** validation, so tokens charged for empty/invalid responses stay in accounting.
