- New product rows get `next_refresh_at = NOW() + <refresh interval>` on insert (`ListingsService.findOrCreateProduct` reads `KEEPA_REFRESH_INTERVAL_MINUTES` from `PlatformSettingsService` — never hardcode a first-refresh delay).
- On the create path only, `UNKNOWN` stock normalizes to 0 (no previous value to preserve; live publish blocks at qty 0, drafts allowed).
- **A job flips to PROCESSING when its first batch STARTS** (`ListingsService.markJobProcessing`, guarded on PENDING), not when the first item lands.

### Listing quality: description template, item specifics, identifiers

The published eBay listing is built from three independent pieces (description, item specifics, identifiers). Each had defects visible on live listings, so the rules below are load-bearing.

**1. Description — ONE renderer, shared by preview and publish.** `renderListingTemplate` + `buildListingTemplateContext` (`packages/shared/src/utils/listing-template.ts`) are used by BOTH `ListingStrategyService.processDescriptionTemplate` (what eBay receives) and the settings-drawer live preview. Never add a second renderer or a placeholder in one surface only — two implementations once meant chosen templates never shipped or shipped with raw `{{{…}}}` text.
- Supported syntax: `{{key}}` (escaped; an array renders as `<ul>`), `{{{key}}}` (raw), `{{#key}}…{{.}}…{{/key}}` (section/repeat), `{{^key}}…{{/key}}` (inverted). Canonical keys (`LISTING_TEMPLATE_PLACEHOLDERS`): `title`, `product_description`, `feature_bullets`, `product_details`, `main_image`, `brand`, `manufacturer`, `asin`, `category`, `condition`, `price`, `currency`, `quantity`. Legacy aliases (`description`, `product_title`, `features`, `specs`, `image`, `main_image_url`) still resolve. There is no `images` array key: a description renders at most one image (see "eBay Picture Services"), so `{{#images}}…{{/images}}` in an old custom template resolves to nothing.
- **Presence flags `has_features`, `has_details`** (`LISTING_TEMPLATE_PRESENCE_FLAGS`) exist because a section cannot nest inside a section of the **same** key (the closer is matched by backreference), so `{{#product_details}}` cannot both wrap a heading and repeat rows. They are scalars (`'1'` / `''`, never `'0'` — `isEmptyValue('0')` is false), used only as `{{#has_details}}…{{/has_details}}`, and deliberately **not** in `LISTING_TEMPLATE_PLACEHOLDERS` (inserting `{{has_features}}` would print a bare `1`).
- **A description never shows the brand (operator decision, 2026-10-03).** `buildListingTemplateContext` leaves `Brand` / `Brand Name` / `Manufacturer` rows out of `product_details` (`isBrandDetailRow`, shared) — the brand is either the supplier's or eBay's placeholder, and eBay's own item specifics carry it anyway. `brand` / `manufacturer` are in `UNSAFE_LISTING_TEMPLATE_PLACEHOLDERS`, so the drawer no longer offers them as chips (an old custom template that already uses `{{brand}}` still renders it). Migration `139` removed the brand rows from every stored `sample_data`, because the drawer preview passes it straight to the renderer; `build-template-previews.mjs` applies the same filter.
- `stripUnresolvedPlaceholders` runs last, so **template syntax can never reach a buyer**.
- **Never use `condition`, `quantity`, `price`, `currency` or `asin` in a template.** `condition`/`quantity` are never passed by `processDescriptionTemplate`; **`price`/`currency` carry the AMAZON SOURCE price** (the description renders before `calculatePrice`, from `product.price.current`), which would show the seller's cost to the buyer; `asin` is an Amazon identifier. `predefined-templates.guard.spec.ts` enforces this for the catalog; **rendering the description after pricing is still open**.
- **`{{title}}` renders the LISTING's title.** `prepareListingData` settles the title FIRST (deterministic build incl. brand strip → AI rewrite), then renders the template with it, then optionally rewrites the description — otherwise the description carried the raw Amazon title (and supplier brand) while the listing title did not. Safe because `rewriteTitle` reads the full source from `product.title`. `listing-strategy-title.spec.ts` locks brand-strip, AI title, plain, and that the AI description receives the final title.
- PREDEFINED templates resolve through `ListingSettingsGroupService.getPredefinedTemplateHtml(id)` (5-min in-process cache); a missing id degrades to `DEFAULT_LISTING_TEMPLATE_HTML`, never to an empty description.

**1a. The template catalog is DATABASE DATA, not code.** `predefined_templates` lives in migrations: `070` (structure: `slug`), `073` (the current catalog), `122` (each template's `sample_data`), `123` (Tech Gadgets image fit). There is no boot-time seed in `ListingSettingsGroupService` — **do not restore one** (`predefined-templates.guard.spec.ts` greps for it).
- **`slug` is the stable natural key** (`NOT NULL`, `UNIQUE`, migration `070`). `id` is a random UUID referenced from `listing_settings_groups.templates->>'predefinedTemplateId'` **inside JSONB, with no foreign key**, so a changed id silently detaches every user's template choice (it degrades to the default template while the UI keeps showing the chosen name). `070` repointed groups before deleting duplicates (with a `jsonb_typeof(...) = 'object'` guard, since `jsonb_set` on a scalar raises and would roll back the whole boot-time migration run), then built the UNIQUE index.
- **To change or add a template, write a NEW migration that upserts by slug** (`ON CONFLICT (slug) DO UPDATE SET …`) and **never puts `id` or `created_at` in the update list**. An applied migration never re-runs, so editing an old one is a silent no-op. Do not DELETE catalog rows — there is no FK to protect them; soft-retire with `is_active = false` (hidden from the picker by `getPredefinedTemplates`, still resolvable by `getPredefinedTemplateHtml`). Migration `073` broke this once (a wholesale `DELETE FROM predefined_templates` + insert of the new catalog), which reset every group's chosen template to the default — do not repeat it. `sort_order` drives picker order.
- **Catalog (12, all `ds-*` from `073`):** `ds-general-store`, `ds-apparel-fashion`, `ds-auto-parts`, `ds-beauty-health`, `ds-fitness-sports`, `ds-home-decor`, `ds-kitchen-dining`, `ds-minimalist` (**zero `<img>` — the option for a seller who wants no image in the description at all**), `ds-outdoor-survival`, `ds-pet-supplies`, `ds-tech-gadgets`, `ds-toys-kids`. Names/descriptions are localized off the slug via `apps/web/src/features/settings/utils/predefinedTemplateLabel.ts` (picker and group-card badge), falling back to the DB name. Sample products (unbranded, same-origin photos in `apps/web/public/template-samples/`) come from migration `122`.
- **Authoring rules, all enforced by `apps/api/src/modules/listings/predefined-templates.guard.spec.ts`** (parses `073` + `122`, renders each template with and without data): survives `sanitizeListingHtml` **untouched** (a Google Fonts `<link>` once shipped and was silently stripped); no `<link>`/`<script>`/`<iframe>`/`<form>`/`<meta>`/`<base>`/`<object>`/`<embed>`, no `on*=`, no `<a href>` (eBay forbids off-site links and the sanitizer does *not* strip them), no web fonts/`@import`/`data:`; no banned placeholder; no `{{` left in either render; balanced sections; **every CSS selector starts with a class** (the drawer preview injects this HTML into the app document, so a bare `h1 {}` would restyle the app) and no attribute selectors; every `{{#key}}` present in `sample_data`; images `max-width:100%` + `height:auto`. **The source marketplace name may occur ONLY inside a real product-image `src` URL** — never in visible text, class/style names, alt text or any other attribute (the guard removes only `src` values, then scans the rest).
- **Two eBay description fields, two limits**: `offer.listingDescription` (500,000 chars) is what buyers read; `inventoryItem.product.description` (4,000) is catalog metadata. Truncate with `truncateHtml` (tag-boundary safe) using `EBAY_DESCRIPTION_MAX_LENGTH` / `EBAY_INVENTORY_DESCRIPTION_MAX_LENGTH` — never `substring`, which cuts styled HTML mid-tag.
- `sanitizeListingHtml` strips XSS **and** eBay active content (`iframe`/`form`/`object`/`embed`/`meta`/`link`). `<style>` is deliberately kept. AI-rewritten descriptions go through the same sanitize + truncate path.
- **The store-settings blacklist checks buyer-VISIBLE text only** (`extractVisibleText` for the description), never raw markup — a keyword in an `<img src>`, class name or inline style must not fail a create over text no buyer reads.
- **A blacklist keyword matches WHOLE WORDS ONLY — never a substring** (`containsBlacklistedKeyword`, `listing-blacklist.ts`, pure + Jest-covered). Substring matching failed unrelated ASINs on `"gin"` in *packaging*, `"rum"` in *serum*, and named a word the seller could not find. The escaped keyword is wrapped in Unicode-aware negative lookarounds (`\p{L}\p{N}`, **not** `\w` — ASCII-only would make every accented/Turkish letter a boundary).
  - **`-`, `/` and `_` count as word characters (operator decision)**: `cross` does NOT block `cross-body`; typing `cross-body` still matches it. Ordinary punctuation (`.`, `'`, `,`, brackets) stays a boundary — `amazon` must still catch `amazon.com` and `Amazon's Choice`.
  - The rule applies to all four scans: `title`, `description`, `feature_specification` (raw `product.features` + `product.specs`) and `brand_manufacturer`.
  - `DEFAULT_BLACKLIST_KEYWORDS` seeds exactly one keyword, `Amazon`, on all four types for every new user. `BlacklistKeyword` carries no per-keyword match mode — whole-word is the only semantic.

**1b. eBay Picture Services — one upload path for BOTH image surfaces.** Every product image is uploaded once through the Media API's `createImageFromUrl`, and the returned `i.ebayimg.com` URL is used for both the gallery entry (`imageUrls`) and the description `<img>`. **eBay does NOT re-host `imageUrls` we hand it** — it serves back the exact URL — so sending Amazon URLs names the supplier on the surface a buyer sees first. (An `i.ebayimg.com` gallery URL seen on a listing created by hand on eBay's own site is not evidence otherwise.) Do not reintroduce a self-hosted mirror (the deleted R2 / `img.sellerhill.com` design): it advertised the tool to anyone reading page source and needed a garbage collector that could destroy live images; `eps-image-invariants.guard.spec.ts` greps for its identifiers.
- **Host:** `EBAY_EPS_IMAGE_BASE_URL` = `https://apim.ebay.com/commerce/media/v1_beta` (`packages/shared/src/utils/ebay-eps.ts`), measured with `pnpm --filter api ebay:media-probe` — eBay's docs name different hosts, and this app's usual REST base (`EBAY_REST_API_URL`) fails for image calls. Scope is `sell.inventory` (already held).
- **The 201 body is read first.** `EbayMediaService` reads `imageUrl` from the `createImageFromUrl` 201 body and calls `GET /image/{id}` only when it has none, **logging which path ran** — whether eBay actually populates the 201 body is settled by that log under real volume, not by the OpenAPI doc.
- **Cache key is `(product, eBay store)`, never product alone** — `product_ebay_images` (migration `119`): an EPS image belongs to the uploading seller's own eBay account, while `products` is shared across users. `image_urls` is an ordered, POSITIONAL JSONB array — entry N is the EPS URL for the Nth `products.image_urls` entry at upload time; `''` means that upload failed.
- **Nothing is ever deleted.** The Media API has **no delete method for images**; eBay expires unused images after 30 days. No GC ships and none is needed. `eps-image-invariants.guard.spec.ts` source-greps for a re-introduced delete call (it would pass mocked tests and only fail at runtime).
- **Rate: 50 POST requests per 5 seconds, per user.** Uploads run at bounded concurrency per PRODUCT (`EPS_UPLOADS_PER_PRODUCT` = 4, results written by index) and are paced per STORE by `EpsUploadPacer` (`eps-upload-pacer.ts`, pure + Jest-covered, `EPS_UPLOAD_MIN_INTERVAL_MS` = 125 ≈ 8/s) — the pacer, not any loop shape, bounds the aggregate rate because the batch worker prepares several products of one store concurrently. A 429 backs off clamped at `MAX_BACKOFF_MS` (10s), never trusting `Retry-After` unbounded — this sits on the synchronous create path. **eBay reports NO daily cap for the image resource** (`getRateLimits` lists `Image (commerce, v1_beta)` with no rates). **Never invent a daily figure for it in `EbayCallBudgetService` and never add an `EbayApiResource` member for it**; re-run `ebay:limits-probe --api-name image` once real volume exists.
- **The description never falls back to an Amazon URL; the gallery falls back per image; NEITHER is correctable afterwards.** `main_image` resolves to an EPS URL or `''`, never a source URL. The gallery may fall back per image to the source URL (`resolveGalleryUrls`), because the alternative is `EBAY_PLACEHOLDER_IMAGE` on a listing with no photos; the description does not (`resolveDescriptionUrl`), because the gallery already shows every photo. **Nothing ever re-sends `imageUrls` after create** — `buildInventoryItemPayload` is the only emitter and `EbayBulkService.createListings` its only caller; `updatePriceQuantity` carries only SKU/quantity/price. So a fallback is permanent for the listing's life — upload reliability matters. Do not believe (as an earlier revision of this file did) that a price/stock sync revises images.
- **A gallery picture under 500 px on its longest side is dropped before eBay sees it** — eBay's Picture Policy refuses the WHOLE listing for one small picture. `readEpsImageDimensions` decodes the size eBay encodes in an EPS URL (`/00/s/<base64 "WxH">/`) and `resolveGalleryUrls` drops EPS entries below `EBAY_MIN_IMAGE_LONGEST_SIDE` (500). A source URL's size is unknown, so it is never dropped; if EVERY picture is small the gallery is returned unfiltered (eBay's refusal beats a live listing with the placeholder). Covered in `ebay-eps.spec.ts`.
- **An image can never fail a listing.** `EbayMediaService.uploadFromUrl` and `EbayImageResolver.resolve` never throw; the worst case is an Amazon-hosted gallery image.
- **Failed uploads are always retried, by shape.** A TOTAL failure (every upload null) writes NO cache row — the missing row is the retry signal (an all-`''` row would read as a permanent hit, and a transient outage could clobber a good row). A PARTIAL failure is stored, and a cache hit containing any `''` is not served as a hit: it takes the same advisory lock and re-uploads **only** those positions, merging results. A non-empty stored entry is never downgraded to `''`; a row is written only when a run produced something new. Re-attempting a permanently dead image on every listing is the accepted cost — there is deliberately no retry-count column and no expiry.
- **Two invariants locked by `eps-image-invariants.guard.spec.ts` (both reverted silently once with every test green):** the `(product, store)` `pg_advisory_xact_lock` is taken BEFORE the upload loop (else concurrent jobs race the rate limit and the cache write); and **a DRAFT costs zero EPS uploads** — `attachEpsImages` is behind `if (!asDraft)` on the create path, while the draft-publish path calls it unconditionally.
- **Known limitation, accepted: the cache is validated on LENGTH alone.** `products.image_urls` is overwritten on every refresh and Amazon rotates photos, so a rotation that keeps the same COUNT pairs new source URLs with older EPS URLs. Staleness, never a leak (every value is still `i.ebayimg.com`). A fix needs a migration storing the source URLs (or a hash) beside the cache row.
- **Unverified / outstanding:** whether an EPS image in use on a live listing has its 30-day expiry extended (eBay never defines "used"); whether a daily figure for the image resource appears under real volume. The Cloudflare R2 bucket and the `img.sellerhill.com` custom domain still exist as infrastructure — removing them is the operator's call once EPS is verified on live listings.

**2. Item specifics — category-aware, always resolvable, never fabricated.** `apps/api/src/modules/ebay/aspect-builder.ts` (pure, Jest-covered) maps product attributes onto the aspects the **resolved category actually declares** (from `get_item_aspects_for_category`: required flag, `SELECTION_ONLY` allowed values, max length, cardinality; cached by `EbayTaxonomyService` — 15-min memory, DB snapshot fresh for `EBAY_CATEGORY_ASPECT_TTL_HOURS`, default 168). Rules:
- The category is resolved **before** the inventory item is built.
- Values are matched to allowed lists (exact → case-insensitive → containment); an unmatchable value is dropped, not forced. Amazon↔eBay naming differences go through the synonym table (`Part Number`→`MPN`, `Colour`→`Color`, …).
- **"Unknown" is never published, and a required aspect is never left empty.** `pickTerminalValue` (`aspect-priors.ts`, pure + matrix-tested) guarantees a value for every category shape: eBay's own non-value (`Does Not Apply`/`Unspecified`) → a catch-all (`Unisex Adult`, `Other`, `One Size`) → a deterministic allowed value that asserts no measurement. Free-text aspects AND Brand get `Does not apply`, and on a selection-only list eBay's own `Does Not Apply` is preferred over any other non-value (`Unbranded` only when the list offers nothing better) — operator decision, 2026-10-03: a missing brand reads "Does not apply", never "Unbranded", wherever the category allows. eBay accepts it on a live listing: item 318892192139 (Bicycle Tires, published by another tool) shows Brand = Does not apply beside UPC = Does not apply, seen 2026-10-03. A LEARNED Brand row is ignored and never written (`aspect-resolver.service.ts`, `BRAND_ASPECT_KEY`): a brand belongs to one product, and the learned rows were old `Unbranded` fallbacks that would outrank the new value; a curated Brand still applies. This is what ends the "eBay requires the item specific X" whack-a-mole. Turn it off per call with `allowTerminalFallback: false` (probe script and tests).
- **Custom specifics.** eBay accepts up to `EBAY_MAX_ITEM_SPECIFICS` (45) name/value pairs and does NOT restrict names to the declared list. Everything harvested that the category did not declare is emitted as free-text specifics, required aspects first so they can never be crowded out. Marketing headlines are filtered out of feature-bullet scraping (a key must be ≤3 words, digit-free, with a non-sentence value).
- **The LLM is a gap-filler, never a resolver.** Deterministic layers run first; only required aspects that product data, curated defaults, learned values and priors ALL failed to fill reach the LLM (capped per listing). Its pick is validated against eBay's allowed list (an invented value is discarded) and learned for the category. With it off, the terminal fallback still guarantees a publishable listing.
- Provenance is recorded per aspect (`AspectResolutionLayer`: `product_data | curated | learned | prior | llm | terminal_fallback`) and logged as a one-line `product_data=13 prior=2` summary on every create.

**3. Catalog identifiers.** `product.brand` / `mpn` / `upc` / `ean` are sent on the inventory item (this triggers eBay catalog matching). GTINs pass a GS1 check-digit test (`common/utils/gtin.ts`) first — a malformed UPC fails the entire publish. eBay validates Brand and MPN as a **pair** (`<BrandMPN>`), so when no usable part number survives, `product.mpn` falls back to eBay's documented non-value rather than being omitted. All bodies come from `ebay-listing-payload.ts`.
- **A product with no UPC still sends `product.upc` — the site's substitute text** (`EBAY_MARKETPLACE_CONFIG[...].identifierNotApplicable`: US/UK "Does not apply", DE "Nicht zutreffend", FR "Non applicable", IT "Non applicabile", ES "No aplicable"), per eBay's "Product Identifier Text" guidance. Omitting it fails publish with 25002 "The UPC field is missing", and it is the common case (product pages rarely render a UPC). Only `upc` is substituted; `ean` is sent only when real (`resolveCatalogIdentifiers`, `ebay-listing-payload.spec.ts`).
- **eBay `errorId` 25002 is a GENERIC user-error id**, carrying "The UPC field is missing", "Offer entity already exists", "System error" and the duplicate-item refusal alike. `classifyEbayErrors` claims `EBAY_DUPLICATE_ITEM` only when the message says so (`already have on eBay|identical|duplicate`); a missing-identifier message is `UNKNOWN` (`retryable: false`) — our payload gap, raw text only on `/admin` → Listing Failures. `extractMissingAspectName` checks the message before reading eBay's positional parameter "2" (parameters are positional per message template).
- **A barcode is never an MPN.** Amazon often fills `partNumber` with the UPC, which eBay rejects (`<BrandMPN> is invalid`). `asPartNumber` (keepa-normalizer) drops GTIN-shaped part numbers, `matchAspectValue` rejects them for MPN/Model-class aspects, and the eBay client omits `product.mpn` as a last check; the required-aspect fallback then supplies `Does not apply`.

**Attribute extraction.** For Keepa data, `extractProductAttributes` (`keepa-normalizer.ts`, pure + tested) is the source for 2 and 3; scraper specs are translated into the same names at input (`translateScraperSpecs`, above). Its field names MUST match the official Keepa schema (keepacom/api_backend `Product.java`) — **do not invent field names** (reading non-existent `flavor`/`department`/`genre`/`platform`/`variationAttributes` once left listings with three specifics). It harvests ~30 attributes: `color`, `size`, `pattern`, `style`, `scent`, `itemForm`, `itemTypeKeyword`, `targetAudienceKeyword`, `audienceRating`, `materials[]` (singular `material` is deprecated), `includedComponents`, `recommendedUsesForProduct`, `specificUsesForProduct[]`, `specialFeatures[]`, ingredients, `safetyWarning`, `productBenefit`, `itemHighlights`, battery flags, `unitCount{unitValue,unitType}`, counts, weights/dimensions (metric → US display units), `variations[].attributes[]` for THIS asin, `hazardousMaterials[]`, `upcList`/`eanList`/`gtinList`, `partNumber`, media fields. Sentinels (`-1`) and placeholder text (`Unknown`, `N/A`) are dropped.

**Persistence (migrations `060`/`061`)**: `products.specs`, `products.identifiers` (JSONB, grow-only — an empty new map never erases a richer stored one) and `products.manufacturer`. `getProductByAsin` selects `features`/`currency` and re-derives specs from stored `raw_keepa_data` for rows that predate extraction (zero extra tokens); the refresh worker treats "row has no specs" as a metadata change. `ListingsService.getListing()` (listing detail) selects `specs`/`identifiers`/`raw_keepa_data` and `buildSpecs()` prefers them via the shared `resolveCachedAttributes`, falling back to a brand+features regex only when neither exists.

**Titles**: `listing-title.ts` (pure + tested) normalizes whitespace/control chars and truncates at a **word boundary** with dangling separators removed — never `slice(0, 80)`.

**ASIN intake is per-item, never whole-batch.**
- `isValidAsinShape`/`ASIN_SHAPE_REGEX` (`packages/shared/src/schemas/listings/listings.schema.ts`) checks 10 alphanumeric characters only — **no leading-`'B'` requirement** (ISBN-10 book ASINs start with digits). The 10-char bound matches the `VARCHAR(10)` columns.
- `createListingsSchema` blocks submission only when **nothing** entered is usable; `parseAsins` splits on commas/semicolons/whitespace/newlines.
- `AddListingsDrawer.container.tsx` partitions tokens into valid/malformed, warns (`listings.errors.invalidAsins`, Continue/Cancel) and submits only the valid subset. `ListingsService.createJob` re-filters server-side (`bulk-create` has no body validation pipe).
- A well-formed ASIN with no provider data → `AsinNotFoundError` → `ListingFailureCode.ASIN_NOT_FOUND` (terminal) per item; the rest of the batch continues.
- **An ASIN already ACTIVE/DRAFT for the user is dropped by `createJob` before the job row is written** (no job item). `createJob` returns `skippedDuplicateCount` on `ListingJobDto` (only on its own response); `resolveListingJobQueuedSummary` (`packages/shared/src/utils/listing-job-summary.ts`, Jest-covered from `apps/api`) picks `ALL_QUEUED` / `QUEUED_WITH_SKIPPED_DUPLICATES` / `ALL_SKIPPED_DUPLICATES`, which `AddListingsDrawer.container.tsx` maps to one of five `listings.success.*` keys (all 16 locales) — so the toast never claims "0 queued" or hides skips. The separate `recordDuplicate`/`DUPLICATE_LISTING` job-item path in `listing-processor.service.ts` covers only a concurrent submission racing this filter.
- **"Duplicate" is per store, with one store setting (`store_settings.allow_cross_store_asins`, migration `140`, operator request 2026-10-03).** An ASIN ACTIVE/DRAFT on the TARGET store is always a duplicate; one that is only on the seller's OTHER stores is a duplicate unless the target store's RESOLVED `allowCrossStoreAsins` is on (Store > Global > off: a store row's NULL inherits the global value, a global NULL is off — on globally, every store may take any ASIN; on one store, only that store may take ASINs the others carry). One rule, `apps/api/src/modules/listings/cross-store-asin.ts` (`decideAsinDuplicate`, `findAsinStorePresence` — one cast query scoped by `user_id` + `ebay_account_id`), used by `createJob`'s filter (`resolveAsinStoreScope` once per job, so `skippedDuplicateCount` stays right), both `isAsinListed` checks of the batch worker, draft publish (ACTIVE rows only, excluding itself) and the existing-listing import (`decideImportAsinConflict`: another item of the same ASIN on the SAME store stays importable; an ASIN on another store is a terminal `DUPLICATE_LISTING` unless allowed). `getResolvedSettings` always carries a boolean; raw rows carry `boolean | null`. The drawer toggle sits on the StoreSettingsDrawer's last step and sends only a choice made on that scope, so an untouched store keeps inheriting. `cross-store-asin.spec.ts` source-greps every check site. The plan limit is unaffected.

**AI title rewrite — the model proposes, code enforces.** eBay ranks on the title, so all 80 characters are search surface.
- `rewriteTitle` receives `product.title` (the full source), never the already-stripped/truncated `baseTitle`, and is judged against it.
- Small models cannot count characters, so `expandTitleWithSourceKeywords` packs the unused budget with source content the model left out: **identifiers first** (`48mm`, `50g`, `G5`), then whole comma/pipe-delimited **phrases** (skipped when half their words are already present), then single words. Deduplication compares the whole title as an alphanumeric blob (`1.19oz (34g)` = `1.19oz(34g)`); tokens split on commas too; bare numbers are never appended. `ensureBrandPrefix` puts a dropped brand back at the FRONT.
- With `stripBrandFromTitle` on, the prompt forbids the brand AND `stripBrandFromTitle` (shared, pure) is re-applied to the output.
- A reply shorter than 8 characters is rejected **before** packing; `isTitleRewriteAcceptable` rejects a final title under 60% of the budget or one that dropped every source identifier — then the deterministic title wins. **The AI path can never publish a worse title than the deterministic one.**

**Conditions**: `listing-condition.ts` maps Renewed/Refurbished → `USED_EXCELLENT` and Open Box → `NEW_OTHER`. Never `LIKE_NEW` — it is valid only in a few media categories.

**Create-path integrity (each was a live bug; `listing-invariants.guard.spec.ts` source-greps all of them; typed failures live in `ebay.errors.ts`):**
- Running out of publish attempts is a **`ListingPublishExhaustedError`**, never an empty success — an ACTIVE row with empty `ebay_item_id` does not exist on eBay, never matches an order and blocks re-listing. A bulk per-entry failure is JSON, so `BulkListingOutcome.errorName` ferries the type and the caller reattaches it (the classifier keys on `Error.name`). Both write sites guard on `!outcome.ok || !outcome.listingId`.
- Category resolution **throws `CategoryResolutionError`** — never falls back to `categoryId: '1'` (eBay's root, not a leaf).
- Aspect metadata **serves a stale cached copy, then throws `CategoryAspectsUnavailableError`** — never returns `[]` (a Brand-only listing).
- Every create-path eBay call is wrapped in `withEbayRateLimitRetry` (429/5xx). Aspect retry budget is **3** (`MAX_CREATE_ATTEMPTS`) — with the terminal fallback a further attempt cannot succeed.

**4. The learning layer (migration `062`).** "Ask once per category":
- `ebay_category_aspects` — taxonomy metadata cache. On a taxonomy outage a **stale snapshot is served with a warning**; an empty aspect list is never a valid answer.
- `ebay_category_map` — category resolution per `asin | amazon_category | query` scope, plus operator pins (`is_locked`, never overwritten). A DB CHECK constraint refuses category `1`.
- `ebay_aspect_defaults` — `curated` (operator, at most one per aspect, may outrank product data) and `learned` (candidates ranked by publish success). `AspectResolverService` reads one row set per category, **revalidates every stored value against the category's current allowed list** (eBay retires values), writes back successes fire-and-forget, and demotes a rejected value (`confidence − 20`, stale at 0). A DB outage degrades to the pure layers — it never blocks a listing.
- `EbayTaxonomyService` owns both caches (memory → DB → eBay → stale → throw).
- Per-listing audit on `listings`: `ebay_category_id`, `aspect_resolution` (JSONB: layer counts + per-aspect decisions), `aspect_autofilled_count`.

**5. Failure taxonomy (migrations `063`/`064`).** `classifyListingFailure` (`listing-failure.ts`, pure, tested) maps our typed errors → eBay `errorId`/message shapes → HTTP status into `ListingFailureCode`, stored on `listing_job_items.failure_code` + `failure_details`. The seller UI shows ONLY the localized reason (`listings.jobs.failure.*`, all 16 locales); raw provider text is admin-only and there is no per-item Retry (see "eBay API call budget & bulk writes"). Migration `064` stores the settings group + policy ids on `listing_jobs`. A failed create is deliberately **not** parked as a DRAFT — `DRAFT` means "publish later", and `isAsinListed` would then block the user's own retry.

**6. Admin → Listing quality.** An admin write surface (besides runtime settings), because category defaults are shared across all customers: `GET /v1/admin/listing-quality/summary|defaults|categories`, `PUT /v1/admin/listing-quality/defaults`, `DELETE /v1/admin/listing-quality/defaults/:id`. A curated value for a `SELECTION_ONLY` aspect is validated against eBay's allowed list **before** storage (`listing-quality.helpers.ts`, Jest-covered). Deleting a curated row removes it; a learned row is marked stale instead (its publish history is evidence).

**Diagnosis without publishing:** `pnpm --filter api ebay:aspect-probe -- --asin B0XXXXXXX [--category 260988]` prints harvested attributes, the resolution table (aspect → value → layer → required) and the would-publish count, reading only the local product cache.

Known gap: listings already published with an old broken description/specifics are **not** retroactively fixed — that needs a dedicated eBay revise batch (see "Deferred").

### Listing rules, VeRO protection, clean-up and scheduled listing (2026-10-02, migration `138`)

Built after comparing a competitor's settings screens with ours (operator-approved list). Everything below is OFF or neutral by default except VeRO protection, contact-detail stripping and "don't send the brand to eBay" (`hideBrand`, ON by default since 2026-10-03 — operator decision; a seller who wants the brand on eBay switches it off).

- **Listing rules belong to the LISTING SETTINGS GROUP, not the store (migration `141`, 2026-10-04, operator decision — spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-03-ad-campaigns-and-listing-rules-design.md` Part A).** `listing_settings_groups.listing_rules` JSONB is ONE config (`ListingRulesConfig`, `packages/shared/src/domain/store-settings/listing-rules.ts`, pure + Jest-covered in `apps/api/src/modules/listings/listing-rules.spec.ts`): `veroProtectionEnabled` (default **true**), `hideBrand` (default **true**; anything but an explicit `false` reads as on), `minSourcePrice` / `maxSourcePrice`, `amazonShippedOnly`, `minRating` (typed by the seller, **1.0–5.0 at one decimal**, comma or point — `normalizeMinRating`; it was a 3/3.5/4/4.5 dropdown), `minReviewCount`, `outOfStockEndDays`, `coldListingDays`, `coldListingAutoEnd`. `normalizeListingRules` is total (NULL / garbage → defaults; out-of-range → off; a max below the min is dropped; auto-end never stays on without a window); every group DTO carries a normalized `listingRules`, a group that never saved any reads the defaults, create always writes the normalized value and update touches the column only when `listingRules` is sent. A new rule is a field, never a column. **Blocked ASINs are NOT a rule — they are the store's own list**, `store_settings.blocked_asins` JSONB (Store > Global: a store NULL inherits the global list, a store `[]` is its own empty list, a global NULL is none; `resolveBlockedAsins`; raw rows carry `string[] | null`, the resolved settings always an array), saved with the omitted-means-unchanged flag (`$22`/`$23` `::boolean`, the same placeholders the old column used), edited on a second card of the Blacklist drawer (`blockedAsinsDraft` / `toSaveBlockedAsins`: an inherited list left untouched is not sent, so the store keeps inheriting). `141` copied each user's GLOBAL rules (minus `blockedAsins` / `promotedAdRate`) into every one of their groups and each row's blocked list into its own column; `store_settings.listing_rules` is left in place, unread (production held one global row, `hideBrand: false`, and 4 groups — read 2026-10-04). UI: the **fourth step of the Listing Group drawer, "Listing rules"** (`ListingGroupDrawer/ListingRulesStep/`, its draft in the container — `listingRulesForm.ts`, Vitest-covered — validated on Continue and again on Save); the `ListingRulesDrawer` and its hub row are deleted and an old `?drawer=storeListingRules` link lands on `listingGroupsAll`.
- **Rules are judged in the create worker, before anything is spent** (`ListingProcessorService.assertListingRules`, right after `resolveProductData`; the rules are the batch's settings GROUP's, read once per batch — a group deleted while the job waited fails every item of the batch rather than leaving them unfinished — and the blocked list is the store's resolved `blockedAsins`; a blocked ASIN is refused before the fetch and left out of the scraper prefetch). Drafts are judged too; draft PUBLISH and the existing-listing import are not. A refusal is `ListingRuleBlockedError` → `ListingFailureCode.BLOCKED_BY_RULE` (terminal, in `SELLER_CAUSED_LISTING_FAILURE_CODES`) with `failureDetails.listingRule` (`ListingRuleKind`), `ruleActual` / `ruleLimit`, and for VeRO the matched brand in `blacklistedKeyword`. The job page renders `listings.jobs.failure.blocked_by_rule_<kind>` (16 locales) and counts it with the blacklist under "your own settings".
- **VeRO list is PLATFORM data, never shown to sellers (operator decision).** `vero_keywords` (unique on `LOWER(keyword)`), edited at `/admin` → Listing Quality → "VeRO brand list" (`GET/POST /v1/admin/vero`, `DELETE /v1/admin/vero/:id`, `VeroKeywordsPanel`) — the fourth admin write surface, same reasoning as curated aspect defaults. `VeroService` (`modules/vero/`, its own dependency-free module imported by both `ListingsModule` and `AdminModule`; 60 s cache; **fails OPEN**) matches **the product's brand and manufacturer only, whole words** (`findVeroMatch`, the blacklist's own `buildKeywordPattern`) — a brand name is an ordinary word in thousands of titles ("Apple" cider vinegar), and a list no seller can see must not refuse products for a reason no seller could guess. A seller sees the on/off switch and, on a refused product, the ONE brand that matched. **The list ships EMPTY**: until the operator enters brands, protection refuses nothing (nobody invented an "official" list).
- **A blacklist keyword has an ACTION** (`BlacklistAction`: `block` — the default and what every pre-existing entry means — or `remove`). `remove` strips the whole word from the fields it is scoped to and lists the product anyway; it never applies to the brand field. Stored inside the existing `blacklist` JSONB (no migration). `applyContentRules` (`listing-content-rules.ts`, pure + Jest-covered) builds the LISTABLE copy of the product once, at the top of `prepareListingData`, so title, template, AI rewrite and the eBay payload cannot disagree: keyword removal (only text BETWEEN tags is touched), **contact-detail stripping — always on** (e-mail, `http`/`www` addresses, bare hosts on `.com|.net|.org|.io` written in lowercase only, separator-formatted North-American phone numbers; a bare ten-digit run is a UPC and is left alone; Amazon copy that drops the space after a full stop — "leak-proof.Store upright" — is the reason the bare-host pattern is narrow and case-sensitive), and `hideBrand` (brand `''`, Brand / Manufacturer specifics dropped, **no UPC / EAN / MPN sent** — a barcode would let eBay's catalog put the brand back; the title strip is implied; the Brand aspect then resolves to `Does not apply` through the existing terminal fallback). The brand BLACKLIST check still reads the SOURCE brand.
- **Shipper and rating come from the scraper** (`sellerhill/signals.py` `extract_shipped_by_amazon`, `content.py` `rating` / `ratingCount` / `isPrime`; `SourceQuality` on `ProductData`, stored inside `raw_provider_data.quality` so a cache hit answers the rules with no new column). **Upstream's `_buybox` never reads `#fulfillerInfoFeature_feature_div`, where the current page states "Ships from Amazon" — it reported every FBA offer as not fulfilled by Amazon**; ours reads that block first (fixtures in `test_signals.py`). `shippedByAmazon` is `None` when the page names no shipper. Quality captured-and-null (no ratings yet, shipper unnamed) FAILS a rule that needs it; quality never captured (Keepa, an older service image, a cached row from before) PASSES — refusing every such product would stop listing altogether. The competitor's "shipping time" filter was NOT built: the delivery estimate depends on the proxy's IP location.
- **An automatic purchase that finds the item out of stock makes the product due for refresh at once** (`AmazonCheckoutService.requestSourceRecheck`: `next_refresh_at = NULL`, which sorts first in the claim). The listing is deliberately NOT forced to 0: `products` is shared by every seller, and "this buyer account could not add it to the cart" is not proof the product is out of stock for everyone. No setting.
- **Clean-up (`ListingCleanupService`, queue `listing-cleanup`, hourly at :23, kill switch `listing.cleanup.enabled`)** ends listings a seller's own rules name, each judged by the rule of its OWN settings group (`buildGroupRuleSql(alias, field)`, `listing-cleanup.helpers.ts`; the candidate SQL is `$1` user, `$2` store, `$3` limit, and the sweep visits only stores whose seller has a group with a clean-up rule on — the not-selling predicate and the Action Center's auto-end test read the group too, `listing-rules-source.guard.spec.ts`): quantity 0 for `outOfStockEndDays` days in a row (clock = `listings.quantity_zero_since`, kept by a **trigger** because four code paths write `quantity`; a listing the seller paused — `disable_ordering`, or a quantity locked at 0 — is never ended), and, only with `coldListingAutoEnd`, no sale for `coldListingDays` days (counted from creation when it never sold). **It withdraws the OFFER through the Inventory API (`EbayBulkService.withdrawOffer`, 2,000,000/day), never Trading `EndItem`** (5,000/day for the whole platform — the seller-triggered "End listing" still uses that); a listing with no `ebay_offer_id` is left alone. Caps: 200 per tick, 50 per store; a refused ending is not retried for a day (`auto_end_failed_at`); suspended accounts are skipped; `auto_ended_reason` / `auto_ended_at` record why. Nothing is ever relisted. Watching WITHOUT auto-end raises the Action Center's `LISTING_NOT_SELLING` (INFO) and the `notSelling=true` listings filter — one predicate, `buildNotSellingSql` (`listing-cleanup.helpers.ts`).
- **Scheduled ("drip") listing**: `CreateListingsRequest.schedule` (`perDay`, `startHour`, `endHour`, `utcOffsetMinutes`) → `planListingSchedule` (`packages/shared/src/domain/listings/listing-schedule.ts`, pure + Jest-covered): one slot every 30 minutes inside the seller-local window, `ceil(perDay / slots)` items per slot (≤ 25), today's past slots skipped, more than 30 days refused (`listings.errors.scheduleTooLong`). Each group is a DELAYED BullMQ batch job. **A scheduled job reserves no plan slot at creation** — the group reserves as it starts (`reserveAtRun`, first attempt only) and product data, the listing rules and the price are all read then, so next week's product is listed from next week's data. `listing_jobs.scheduled_until` feeds the job page and keeps a scheduled backlog out of `countQueuedItems`' fair-priority count. UI: a toggle + three fields in `AddListingsDrawer` (not remembered between sessions).
- **Promoted Listings: the automatic "advertise every new listing at one rate" step is GONE (2026-10-04).** `ListingPromotionService` was deleted with migration `141`; nothing promotes a listing after a create or a draft publish, and `promotedAdRate` is no longer a listing rule (`141` dropped it from the copied rules). Ad campaigns replace it (spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-03-ad-campaigns-and-listing-rules-design.md` Part B, not built yet). What stays: `EbayPromotedListingsService` (`modules/ebay/`, pure half in `ebay-promoted.helpers.ts` + spec) and `GET /v1/ebay/accounts/:accountId/advertising-eligibility` (cached 6 h per store); `ebay_accounts.promoted_campaign_id` / `listings.promoted_ad_rate` are left in place. Facts are from eBay's own Marketing OpenAPI and live answers, recorded in `docs/ebay-reference/README.md` "Promoted Listings facts" (incl. the campaign-performance report metadata captured 2026-10-04): quota `sell.marketing` 10,000/day + `sell.marketing.ads.campaign` 100,000/day; the `sell.marketing` scope is already granted; `bidPercentage` 2.0–100.0 at one decimal. **The production store was `INELIGIBLE` / `NOT_ENOUGH_ACTIVITY` (live, 2026-10-02)**, so `createCampaign` / the bulk ad call have NEVER run against eBay.
- **Gift option and address deletion at checkout are NOT built — their pages have never been recorded.** The saved evidence (`fulfillment-evidence/`) holds only the pages the checkout walks through: review, thank-you, order history. The review page does carry the real entry link (`a[id^="checkout-item-block-gift-options-link-"]` → `/checkout/p/<pipeline>/gift`), but the gift page BEHIND it, and the account's address book, appear in no capture. `AmazonCheckoutService.captureReferencePage` now saves them as evidence, in a separate tab that is closed again, never throwing: the address book (`address-book-*.html`) after every real placement and every dry run; the gift page (`gift-options-page-*.html`) **in dry runs only**, because opening a second page of the checkout pipeline seconds before a real Place Order click is not a risk worth a capture. Off with `AUTO_FULFILL_CAPTURE_REFERENCE_PAGES=false`. **Next step: read those two files, then write the gift step and the address removal against them** (the removal must match the buyer's address with `addressBlockMatchesBuyer` before clicking anything — deleting the account's own address would be the failure that matters). `/a/addresses` is the one path here not taken from a capture; it is used only to record the page.
- **Not built, on purpose**: AI reply drafts and reply signatures, "instant" tracking push, delivery-photo attachment, shipping-time filter, per-store duplicate-ASIN allowance.
- **Store-scoped eBay calls (fixed 2026-10-03)**: the seller-triggered End (`EbayService.withdrawOffer(userId, ebayAccountId, itemId)`), draft publish and `prepareListingDraft` (account id now required) go through the listing's own store, and `GET /ebay/business-policies?ebayAccountId=` reads ONE store's policies (ownership-checked; omitted = the oldest active store, never an unordered `LIMIT 1`). The Add Listings and import drawers read the selected store's policies and drop the policy choices on a store switch. A legacy row with no store resolves through `resolveListingAccountId`. `ebay-store-scoped.spec.ts` / `listing-end-store.spec.ts`. `getActiveAccount` / `getActiveAccountId` / `getActiveAccountAccessToken` (unordered `LIMIT 1`) must not be used for anything that belongs to one store.

### eBay Marketplace Account Deletion notification (2026-09-11)

**eBay disables every new PRODUCTION keyset until this endpoint is validated** — it is the GDPR "right to erasure" webhook eBay calls when one of its users closes their account. There is no way around it (the exemption applies only to apps that store zero eBay-user personal data; SellerHill stores buyer PII on `orders`).

- **Public endpoint, both verbs on one path**: `GET/POST /v1/ebay/marketplace-deletion-notification` (`ebay-account-deletion.controller.ts`). No `@UseGuards` (like the OAuth callback and the tracking webhook); `@SkipThrottle` so eBay's validation + retries cannot be rate-limited into "validation failed".
- **GET is the challenge**: eBay appends `?challenge_code=<random>`; the endpoint returns `{ challengeResponse: sha256(challengeCode + verificationToken + endpointUrl) }` as lowercase hex. The concatenation ORDER is fixed; a trailing-slash difference on `endpointUrl` is the usual "validation failed" cause, so `EBAY_DELETION_ENDPOINT_URL` must be byte-identical to the URL registered in the portal (falls back to `${FRONTEND_URL}/api/v1/ebay/marketplace-deletion-notification`). `computeChallengeResponse` / `isValidVerificationToken` / `parseAccountDeletionNotification` are pure + Jest-covered (`ebay-account-deletion.helpers.ts`).
- **POST is the erasure**: carries `{ username, userId, eiasToken }`. `EbayAccountDeletionService.eraseUserData` nulls `buyer_name`/`buyer_email`/`buyer_phone`/`shipping_address` on `orders WHERE buyer_username = $1` — the row itself is a financial/tax record and is kept. Idempotent, and writes an `EBAY_ACCOUNT_DELETION` `audit_logs` row (`user_id` NULL — the subject is an eBay user). Answers 200 once the body shape is understood (including when we hold no data), 400 for a malformed body.
- **An erasure is REMEMBERED, not just performed (`buyer_data_erased_at`, migration `130`, 2026-09-30).** Until order sync started re-reading modified orders nothing wrote buyer columns a second time. Now the order upsert would overwrite `shipping_address` with whatever eBay returns, and the return sweep rewrites `buyer_login_name` / `buyer_comment` on every pass — the next read would put erased data straight back. The erasure stamps `orders.buyer_data_erased_at` and `ebay_returns.buyer_data_erased_at` (and nulls the two buyer columns on the return rows matched by login name); the order upsert keeps the stored address on a marked row (`CASE WHEN orders.buyer_data_erased_at IS NOT NULL …`) and the return upsert keeps both buyer columns NULL. **Any new column or table that stores eBay buyer data must be added to the erasure AND guarded in whatever re-reads it.** `130` back-marks past erasures from their `EBAY_ACCOUNT_DELETION` audit rows. `ebay-order-changes.spec.ts` locks the order half.
- **Config (plain env, `ConfigService`)**: `EBAY_VERIFICATION_TOKEN` (32–80 chars `[A-Za-z0-9_-]`, also pasted into the eBay portal) + optional `EBAY_DELETION_ENDPOINT_URL`. Both optional in the compose files; until the token is set the endpoint returns `503` and the API still boots. Deploy sequence: set the token in Coolify → deploy → register URL + token in the eBay portal → eBay's GET validates → keyset enables.
- **Not implemented**: per-POST `X-EBAY-SIGNATURE` verification (eBay signs with a rotating public key). Recommended, not what enables the keyset — a hardening follow-up.

### Amazon Best Sellers browsing (`/:locale/best-sellers`, 2026-09-29)

**What it is.** A seller browses Amazon's five ranking lists (Best Sellers, New Releases, Movers & Shakers, Most Wished For, Most Gifted) by category inside the app, ticks products, and lands in the Add Listings drawer with those ASINs pre-filled (`/listings?drawer=add&asins=…`). Amazon-side only — nothing reads eBay, so the eBay License Agreement findings in `docs/ebay-reference/` do not apply to it. Included in every plan, with a **per-plan monthly product allowance** (`best_sellers_products_per_month`, see below) as the seller-facing meter.

**Why it costs almost nothing, and what is metered anyway (2026-09-29 — this paragraph supersedes the daily-fetch-limit design it replaced).** The lists are fetched by the SAME scraper service, through the SAME proxy pool the product refresh uses (`sellerhill/bestsellers.py` → upstream `amazon/rankings.py`; the upstream `/amazon/best-sellers` route is NOT mounted in the container and must stay unmounted — it has no auth and no proxy binding). One list page is ~4 HTTP requests (page + up to 3 ACP POSTs), well under a tenth of a cent through a proxy the platform already pays for, and the answer is the same for every seller, so `BestSellersService` caches each `(country, listType, category, page)` in Redis for `bestSellers.cacheTtlMinutes` (default 360). **The seller-facing meter is therefore NOT our cost — it is value delivered**: `BillingLimitKey.BEST_SELLERS_PRODUCTS_PER_MONTH` (`'best_sellers_products_per_month'`, unit = products, per billing period, the same quota window as the other meters) counts **products viewed**, one list page = up to 50, cached or live alike. Allowance per plan: Trial 500, Lite 1,500, Nano 2,500, Micro 5,000, Starter 7,500, Basic 10,000, Plus 12,500, Growth 15,000, Advanced 20,000, Pro 25,000, Elite 35,000, Business 50,000, Enterprise 75,000 (migration `125`). The same list page reopened on the same UTC day is not recounted: the views table (`best_sellers_views`, UNIQUE `(user_id, view_key, viewed_on)`) is written with one race-safe upsert, and the used figure is the sum of products over the rows in the current window. All Redis calls still fail OPEN (no cache — never a broken page), same as `EbayCallBudgetService`. `bestSellers.enabled` (default true) hides the feature with a 404.

- **Browsing never starves creates or the refresh.** The pool gained a third lane, `browse` (`ScraperLane.BROWSE`, `_LANE_PRIORITY` interactive 0 < browse 1 < background 2 < crawl 3 — the platform's own tree crawl, below), and a generic `submit_call(fn, lane, expired_result)` primitive next to the product-page `submit`; a best-sellers task takes ONE `wait_turn` token for its several requests — accepted, the hidden daily cache-miss brake (below) bounds it. The API sends the exact same proxy list and `perIpRequestsPerSecond` it sends to `/v1/products`, because `pool.ensure` retires any proxy not in the list it is given. **No proxy → no HTTP call**, guarded by `best-sellers-egress.guard.spec.ts` exactly like the product path.
- **Exhaustion never blocks — it truncates.** When the allowance is spent the API cuts `list.items` **server-side** to what the allowance still covers and returns `lockedCount`; the page renders that many blurred placeholder rows plus an upgrade / top-up prompt. A client-side blur over real data was rejected explicitly (devtools would show the rows). Enforcement off or no subscription → unmetered (fail open, same as every other gate); a suspended account → everything locked.
- **Top-up packs** on `billing_quota_addons`: `best-sellers-2500` $4.99, `best-sellers-10000` $14.99, `best-sellers-25000` $29.99 — monthly credits exactly like the conversion packs, offered only once the allowance is reached. See "Quota top-ups" for why this dimension is sellable while listings and automatic orders are not.
- **The old per-seller daily cache-MISS cap survives as a HIDDEN anti-abuse brake on proxy capacity, not as anything a seller sees.** `bestSellers.dailyFetchLimit` (`platform_settings`, Scraper category) now defaults to **1000** (was 100); only a cache miss counts, a miss is charged BEFORE the fetch and refunded when nothing was fetched (`no_proxy`, scraper unreachable), `blocked`/`parse_failed` keep the charge because the proxy capacity was spent, `0` means nobody fetches but the cache still serves. It protects pool contention with the refresh — the plan allowance protects nothing of ours, which is exactly why it can be generous. **`resolveDailyFetchLimit`'s former "monetization seam" note is superseded**: the seam was used, and the monetised dimension is the product allowance, not the daily fetch count.
- **Wire contract lives in ONE place**: `packages/shared/src/domain/best-sellers/best-sellers.types.ts` — the scraper's `POST /v1/best-sellers` request/response (camelCase, the Python side renames upstream's snake_case in `to_wire()`), the seller-facing `GET /v1/best-sellers` DTOs (incl. `lockedCount` and the plan `allowance`) and `BestSellersErrorKey` (the i18n keys the API returns as `message`: disabled → 404, dailyLimitReached → 429 with `allowance` in the body — the hidden cache-miss brake, not the plan meter —, unavailable → 503, unsupportedMarketplace → 400). Only `AMAZON_US` is selectable today (`SUPPORTED_AMAZON_MARKETPLACES`).
- **Web**: `apps/web/src/features/best-sellers/` (4-file split, `hooks/useBestSellersUrlState` for `?list=&category=&page=`, selection kept in memory across lists/pages), nav item after Drafts in the Inventory group, `routeMeta` entry, locale-less `/best-sellers` redirect, i18n namespace `bestSellers` in all 16 locales (`bestSellers:bestSellers.menu` for the label). A list's first load is the `DataTable`'s own skeleton grid (`loading`), and empty/error are `EmptyState`; the category tree is a REAL tree of any depth built from per-node answers (`utils/bestSellersCategoryTree.ts`, pure + Vitest-covered: an answer is the chain ABOVE the browsed node, the node itself — the one `isSelected` entry, which has no path — and its children, read by position around that entry; the earlier two-level cache stored every answer as the department's children, so visiting a sub-category replaced its siblings with its own children and dropped the node just pressed); a 429 renders the used/limit figures from the error body, never a raw message; a truncated page renders `lockedCount` blurred placeholder rows under the real ones with the upgrade / top-up prompt. `AddListingsDrawer` gained `initialAsins` — and the deep-link bug that came with it was fixed: a drawer mounted already open (`?drawer=add`) never ran its open-transition reset, so stored preferences were not loaded and were then overwritten with blanks. Demo mode answers `/best-sellers` from the unbranded demo catalogue.
- **The tree: the label opens, the chevron only expands (2026-10-03, operator request).** A chevron asks `GET /best-sellers/categories?category=<node>` for that node's sidebar alone — no products, so walking down to a deep branch takes nothing from the allowance; only the label the seller finally presses loads (and meters) a list. That endpoint reads a **14-day shared tree cache** (`best-sellers:tree:<country>:<list>:<node>`, `TREE_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS`; written by every live list fetch, never with an empty tree, stored without the per-row `link`) before the 6-hour list cache, and a miss fetches the node's list page, which then also serves the list from cache. **Every tree row carries its nesting `level`** (`sellerhill/bestsellers.py` `tree_levels`, wrapping upstream's flat parser): on a LEAF Amazon draws the leaf among its siblings (parent's children, leaf in bold), so document order made siblings read as ancestors and corrupted the tree (verified on amazon.com, fixtures `tests/fixtures/bestsellers_{mid_headphones,leaf_open_ear}`). `mergeCategoryAnswer` reads parents from levels when every entry has one (the selected node's children and, on a leaf, its parent's children are complete; a chain link never overwrites a child list), else the old flat reading for answers cached before `level`. A leaf ends up with `children: []` and loses its chevron. A row waiting on either answer shows a spinner (`isLoading`). Cache misses take 1.3–4.7 s in production (page + up to 3 ACP POSTs through the proxies); hits ~20 ms. The `Skeleton` atom draws in the canvas tint (`background.tertiary`/`primary`) — surface white on a white glass card was invisible.
- **The platform crawls the whole tree itself (2026-10-03, operator decision).** `BestSellersCrawlService` (queue `best-sellers-crawl`, a one-minute tick, concurrency 1) runs two resumable passes, each a Redis list frontier + a per-pass seen-set under `best-sellers:crawl:<pass>:*`: **TREE** — every node of all five lists, sidebar only (`treeOnly`: one GET, no ACP), re-read once older than `bestSellers.crawl.intervalDays` (default 7, ≤ 13 so nothing expires), `bestSellers.crawl.pagesPerMinute` (default 30) fetches a tick, a node read within the interval walked through without a fetch; **PRE-WARM** — the Best Sellers LIST (products) of every node down to `bestSellers.prewarm.depth` (default 2: root, departments, their children, ~900 lists) once per `cacheTtlMinutes`, refetching a list with under half its life left, `bestSellers.prewarm.pagesPerMinute` (default 3). Measured before building: ~50k nodes per list type (33-page sample, ±50%), ~250k over five lists; a five-proxy pool at 1 req/s per IP does ~108k requests per 6 h, so the whole tree every 6 h was refused (2.3× capacity, and the same IPs serve the price/stock refresh) — weekly tree ≈ 8–10% of the pool, pre-warm ≈ 3%. Both run on the scraper's fourth and lowest lane, **`crawl`** (`ScraperLane.CRAWL`, after `background`; `test_crawl_lane_only_runs_after_the_background_refresh`), charge no seller (no allowance, no per-seller fetch cap — `BestSellersService.crawlFetch`), stop with no proxy, and put a BLOCKED node back up to `CRAWL_MAX_ATTEMPTS`. Every scraper call goes through `BestSellersService.scraperFetch`, which refuses an empty proxy list before the HTTP call (`best-sellers-egress.guard.spec.ts`). Redis at ~250k nodes is a few hundred MB (no maxmemory on production, 125 GB host). Specs: `best-sellers-crawl.spec.ts`.
- Files: `services/amazon-scraper/sellerhill/bestsellers.py` (+ `tests/test_bestsellers.py`, `submit_call` tests in `tests/test_pool.py`), `apps/api/src/modules/best-sellers/` (`module`, `controller`, `dto`, `service`, `helpers` + spec, egress guard spec), `ScraperClient.fetchBestSellers`.

### Emotion `Theme` augmentation — `apps/web/src/emotion.d.ts` (2026-09-29)

`packages/ui/src/theme/emotion.d.ts` declares `declare module '@emotion/react' { interface Theme extends AppTheme {} }`, but a module augmentation binds to the `@emotion/react` instance the DECLARING package resolves. `pnpm-lock.yaml` carries two `@types/react` versions (18.3.12 from the root `pnpm.overrides`, 18.3.27 pinned by the packages), so pnpm installs two `@emotion/react` instances and `apps/web` resolves the other one: `Theme` stayed `{}` in the web program, every `theme.colors.*` access in a `.style.ts` was a type error (373 `tsc` errors, ~340 of them this) and the type-aware lint rules reported the same sites as unsafe `any` access (130 errors across 16 files — the "pre-existing" errors this file used to warn about). Declaring the augmentation again from inside the app binds it to the copy the app imports; `tsc` dropped to 3 errors (SVG `dominantBaseline` typings in three `packages/ui` icon files) and lint to 0. **Do not delete this file as "duplicate"**, and if the `@types/react` split is ever resolved in the lockfile the file becomes harmless, not wrong.

### eBay official documents live in `docs/ebay-reference/` (2026-09-29)

**Read the local copies before deciding anything about eBay, and never re-derive eBay facts from memory or third-party blogs.** `docs/ebay-reference/README.md` indexes what is there (Browse API v1.20.4 OpenAPI, the Buy field-filter reference, the Analytics `getRateLimits` OpenAPI, the Application Growth Check page, Data Handling Compliance, the **API License Agreement**, the **User Agreement**, `www.ebay.com/robots.txt`), each with its source URL and fetch date, plus the pages that could NOT be obtained (API Call Limits page: 403 from eBay's edge; Marketplace Insights docs: sign-in shell) so nobody spends another session retrying them. developer.ebay.com answers many non-browser requests with a 403 "Something went wrong on our end" page, and this environment's egress policy may block the host outright, which is why the copies exist. The README quotes the clauses that bear on this product verbatim: the License Agreement's RESTRICTED ACTIVITIES list names "seller arbitrage (for example, automatically repricing eBay listings in response to price changes on other third-party sites, automatically ordering sold items from other third-party sites, or posting tracking information to eBay when items purchased from other third-party sites are shipped)" as requiring eBay's prior written consent, requires "express prior written permission" for anything deriving "Data relating to the performance of sellers" or "Information relating to specific eBay Users", and the User Agreement §3 forbids "any robot, spider, scraper … or other automated means" against the site "except with the prior express permission of eBay". For THIS keyset's real call limits run `pnpm --filter api ebay:limits-probe -- --api-name <name>`; that is eBay's own figure and outranks any doc page. Refreshing a copy means updating the README's table and date too. Added 2026-09-30: the Fulfillment (v1.20.7), Notification (v1.6.7) and Finances (v1.19.0) OpenAPI files, the Post-Order reference pages (`post-order/`, incl. the documented enum types), the six notification-topic payload contracts with the live `getTopic` answers (`notification-topics/`), and a README section "Order, cancellation, refund and return facts" that records what was verified live and which enum pages could NOT be obtained (`CancelStateEnum`, `OrderPaymentStatusEnum`, `RefundStatusEnum`, `ReasonForRefundEnum`).

### eBay API call budget & bulk writes (2026-08-09)

**eBay meters API calls PER APPLICATION, not per seller.** Every customer draws from one daily pool, so call volume — not customer count — runs out, and exhausting it stops that operation for *everyone*. Measured against the production keyset (`ebay:limits-probe`): Inventory 2,000,000, Feed 100,000, Fulfillment 100,000, Account 25,000, Taxonomy 5,000, Message (`commerce.message`) 500,000, Notification (`commerce.notification`) 10,000 per day — the last two read from the admin eBay Limits tab, 2026-09-29. **Post-Order** (`post-order.return`, `.cancellation`, `.inquiry`, `.casemanagement`) is 5,000 per day EACH (`getRateLimits`, 2026-09-30) — the tightest pools after Trading; see "eBay Returns". **Trading is metered PER METHOD**: `AddItem` 100,000, `RelistItem` 50,000, `GetMyeBaySelling`/`GetItem`/`UploadSiteHostedPictures` and ~85 others 5,000 each — never cite "Trading 5,000" as one pool.

**The Feed API / LMS route is deliberately NOT taken FOR WRITES.** eBay forbids revising an Inventory-API-created listing through Trading, and all LMS inventory feeds are Trading XML — adopting it would strand the existing catalog, the aspect ladder, `bulk_migrate_listing` and migration `065`'s import. With bulk endpoints the Inventory quota sits at ~1% utilization at 1M listings. **This rejection covers WRITES only** — a report download moves nothing to the Trading model; do not cite it to avoid a Feed API read (see "Periodic listing reconciliation").

### Periodic listing reconciliation (eBay Feed API, 2026-09-22)

A listing the seller (or eBay) ended outside SellerHill would otherwise stay ACTIVE here for ever, drawing refresh capacity. Reconciliation reads **`LMS_ACTIVE_INVENTORY_REPORT`**: one report per seller whatever the catalogue size (~8–12 calls: create + polls + download) on the 100,000/day Feed ceiling (~6% at 500 sellers). Doing it with Trading `GetMyeBaySelling` (one call per 200 listings, 5,000/day for the whole application) would cost ~half that quota daily — the API choice, not volume, is the constraint.

- **Feed is additive to Trading, not a replacement — split by FREQUENCY.** Import discovery (`discoverActiveListings`, once per seller) stays on Trading because `processImport` needs **`api_model`** (whether a legacy Trading-model listing must go through `migrateLegacyListing`) and **`title`** (copied into `listings.title`), neither of which the report carries. The daily, forever reconciliation is what Feed takes. The import drawer renders no discovered listings; `ebay_listing_discoveries.image_url` is written and never read.
- **Report shape (confirmed from a real production capture):** XML (`<BulkDataExchangeResponses><ActiveInventoryReport><SKUDetails>…`) **despite eBay's `Content-Type: application/json` header** — parsing never trusts the header. Each `<SKUDetails>` carries exactly `SKU`, `Price` (`currencyID` attribute), `Quantity`, `ItemID` — no title, image or api model.
- **`<SKU>` is NOT ours — never join on it.** Listings published by another tool carry that tool's SKU (e.g. bare ASINs without our `-NEW` suffix from `EbayService.buildSku`). **`<ItemID>` is the only field guaranteed to match `listings.ebay_item_id`**; every join in `feed-report-parser.ts` / `EbayFeedSyncService` uses it.
- **There is no cheap source for watcher / favourite / view counts — do not promise them in UI or copy.** Not in the Feed report or the Inventory API (`getOffers` → `ListingDetails` carries only `listingId`, `listingStatus`, `listingOnHold`, `soldQuantity`). Watchers need Trading `GetMyeBaySelling` + `IncludeWatchCount` (the 5,000/day door migration `095` stays out of); traffic needs Analytics `getTrafficReport` (100/day for the whole application). `listings.sold_count` is incremented in real time from orders.
- **Gates:** `ebay.feedSync.enabled` (default **false**) and `ebay.feedSync.captureOnly` (default **true** — the remaining gate before reconciliation goes live). Also `ebay.feedSync.cron` (default `*/20 * * * *`), `ebay.feedSync.intervalHours` (default 24), `ebay.feedSync.maxAccountsPerRun` (default 5). Capture-only downloads the report, writes it **verbatim** (possibly gzipped) to `EBAY_FEED_CAPTURE_DIR` (default `logs/ebay-feed-captures/`, a mounted volume in both Coolify compose files; Promtail globs `*.log` so a `.raw` file is never shipped), logs a decompressed truncated preview (`previewReport`, ~2KB), and **changes nothing in the database**. Never ship a parser against guessed column names — capture a real report first.
- **`feed-report-parser.ts`** (pure, Jest-covered against the real captured excerpt): `decodeReportBody` (gzip-or-not, never throws) + `parseActiveInventoryReportXml` (regex over `<SKUDetails>` blocks, no new dependency). A record with no `<ItemID>` is dropped; malformed input yields `[]`, never throws.
- **With `captureOnly` off, `EbayFeedSyncService.reconcileAccount` retires ONE DIRECTION ONLY**: an ACTIVE `listings` row of that `ebay_account_id` with an `ebay_item_id` **absent** from the parsed `<ItemID>` set is flipped to `INACTIVE`. Nothing is ever created, adopted or reactivated from the report (adoption is the import flow's job).
- **Three independent refusals to act — "eBay's answer is unusable" is not evidence of an empty catalogue**: `report.status !== 'COMPLETED'` (`COMPLETED_WITH_ERROR` is untrusted wholesale); a parse yielding zero items (logged, not acted on); `fetchActiveInventoryReport` returning `null` (download/poll failure). **A failed report is NEVER read as "this seller has no listings"** — that would retire their whole catalogue.
- **Task pacing is a separate, unpublished limit.** eBay meters feed TASKS separately — **160024** (too many queued/processing) and **160025** (too many per hour/day) — with no published figure. So the sweep claims a few stores per tick (`maxAccountsPerRun`), runs them **sequentially**, with worker concurrency 1. Raise only against observed 160024/160025 behaviour.
- **`ebay_accounts.last_feed_sync_at` (migration `114`) is claimed and stamped in ONE statement** (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` + `UPDATE … RETURNING`), like the refresh claim: replicas cannot take the same store, a crashed run costs one interval. Only `status = 'active'` stores are claimed; NULL sorts first, so a newly connected store is swept on the next tick.
- **No automatic Trading discovery on eBay connect (operator decision).** Discovery fires only when the seller opens "Import existing eBay listings" and clicks Continue — a seller who connects and abandons should not have their catalogue pulled. Do not add an automatic trigger without revisiting this.
- **`LMS_ORDER_REPORT` was researched and deliberately NOT built — do not reopen it as "a simple optimisation".** Order sync is REST Fulfillment `getOrders`. The report (`POST /sell/feed/v1/order_task`) is **stateful** (only *unacknowledged* orders from 30 days, so it needs an `LMS_ORDER_ACK` upload loop), **likely carries the old Merchant Data XML shape** (losing the REST-only `totalMarketplaceFee` / `totalFeeBasisAmount` of migration `098` and `ebayCollectAndRemitTaxes`), and forfeits `getOrders`' two-year reach. Unverified against a real report — capture one before believing otherwise; revisit only if Fulfillment usage approaches its ceiling.
- Files: `apps/api/src/modules/ebay/ebay-feed.service.ts` (client + task lifecycle + `extractTaskIdFromLocation`); `apps/api/src/modules/listings/feed-report-parser.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`), `ebay-feed-sync.service.ts` (claim, sweep, capture, reconcile), `ebay-feed-sync.processor.ts` (the tick). `EbayApiResource.FEED`'s ceiling comes from eBay's `getRateLimits` (`sell.feed`).

**`getOrders` returns orders up to TWO YEARS old** (eBay reference), defaulting to 90 days when unfiltered — so a suspended or lapsed seller's backlog is recoverable via the preserved `last_ebay_sync_at` watermark; there is no 90-day cliff.

**Everything that writes to eBay in volume goes through `EbayBulkService`** (`src/modules/ebay/ebay-bulk.service.ts`), 25 items per call:
- `updatePriceQuantity` — replaces a 4-call-per-listing path (`GET /offer?sku=` → `PUT /offer` → `GET /inventory_item` → `PUT /inventory_item`): 400k updates/day is 16k calls instead of 1.6M.
- `createListings` — staged `bulk_create_or_replace_inventory_item` → `bulk_create_offer` → `bulk_publish_offer`, the failure set shrinking at each stage so one bad ASIN cannot cost the other 24 their listings. The **aspect self-heal loop** re-resolves only items eBay named a missing specific for (no eBay call — metadata is cached) and replays them, bounded by `MAX_CREATE_ATTEMPTS = 3`. A replay collides with the offer its first pass created, so `extractExistingOfferId` recovers the id.
- **Chunking is size-only, never time-based** — a 3-ASIN job ships one 3-entry call immediately.
- **Per-item preparation runs with bounded concurrency inside one batch.** EPS uploads, the LLM title rewrite and category + aspect resolution run through `runWithConcurrency` (`common/utils/concurrency.ts`, pure + Jest-covered) at `LISTING_BATCH_ITEM_CONCURRENCY` items at once (env, default 5, clamp 1–8 — each in-flight item can hold a pg client, and ~8 concurrent LLM calls is where a free tier starts 429ing). The eBay WRITE stays one bulk call. The first `EbayBudgetExhaustedError` stops NEW items starting, in-flight items finish, then the whole batch defers once. Cross-item safety is structural: per-ASIN advisory lock in `resolveProductData`, per-(product, store) lock in `EbayImageResolver`, per-store pacing via `EpsUploadPacer`. The `Batch timing for job …: prefetch=… prepare=… publish=…` log lines show where batch time goes.
- **Every eBay body comes from `ebay-listing-payload.ts`** (GS1 check digits, BrandMPN pair, tag-safe truncation, image floor — each broke live listings once). Never hand-roll one at a call site; `listing-invariants.guard.spec.ts` greps this.
- **`locale` is required on every bulk inventory item** (eBay `LocaleEnum`, underscore form `en_US`, unlike the hyphenated `Content-Language` header). Without it `bulk_create_or_replace_inventory_item` rejects the **whole batch**.
- **eBay's own per-entry system error is replayed at EVERY stage, and exhaustion keeps eBay's error.** `isBulkSystemError` (`ebay-bulk.helpers.ts`) matches 25001 ("A system error has occurred…"), or 25002 whose message says "system error" in any case. `EbayBulkService.retriesSystemError` replays such an entry at the inventory stage (idempotent), the offer stage (a replay finding the offer eBay created after all is recovered by the existing-offer branch) and the publish stage (orphaned offer deleted first) — only while `attempts < MAX_CREATE_ATTEMPTS`, so the last failure settles with eBay's own error (→ `EBAY_UNAVAILABLE`), not `ListingPublishExhaustedError` (which reads as an item-specifics problem). A rejection of the input itself is never replayed. `ebay-bulk.service.spec.ts` drives the real staging against faked eBay answers. **The classifier checks `/image|picture/` before `/policy/`** — eBay words the picture refusal "Picture Policy".
- **A missing response entry is a failure, never a success** (`correlateBulkResponses`) — treating it as OK writes an ACTIVE row for a listing that does not exist.
- **There is exactly ONE create implementation, and it is this one.** No kill switch, no per-item path, no fallback (a per-item fallback would cost 25× the quota for byte-identical output, and the old single-item loop bypassed the budget governor). `ebay.service.ts` keeps only the read half, `prepareListingDraft` (location check + cached category/aspect resolution). The store is **required** at enqueue (`400` when absent).
- **Draft publish is batched too.** `publishListing` / `publishListings` (`POST /listings/:id/publish`, `POST /listings/bulk-publish`) go through `EbayBulkService.createListings` (100 drafts = 12 calls, not 300). Preparation stays per listing (a draft carries seller edits — title, price/quantity locks — a shared write path must not flatten), and so does the quota gate: each draft reserves its own slot and every failure branch releases it. Drafts are grouped by eBay account first (a bulk call carries one seller token). Publish acquires at `EbayCallPriority.INTERACTIVE` (full ceiling); queue-driven creates stay `BACKGROUND` (against the reserve).
- **Drafts run through the same batch and stop before the writes** — zero eBay calls and zero EPS uploads until publish, so draft→publish costs exactly what a direct create does, and an abandoned draft costs nothing. The AI title generated at draft time is preserved at publish (`applyContentAi: false` there; the stored title wins).
- **A bulk publish run is written as a `listing_jobs` record (`kind = 'publish'`, migration `096`), synchronously, with no BullMQ worker.** After the synchronous publish, `recordPublishJob` inserts a `listing_jobs` row + one terminal `listing_job_items` row per draft (`ACTIVE`/`ERROR`, with `failure_code`/`failure_details`/`error_message` from `classifyPublishFailure` → `classifyListingFailure`), so a partial failure is visible in the jobs list and detail page. `bulkPublish` returns `{ count, failed, jobId }`; the drafts page's modal deep-links to `/listings/jobs/{jobId}` on failure. The record write is best-effort — bookkeeping must never turn a successful publish into a seller-visible error. `classifyPublishFailure` is the seam for future publish-specific codes.
- **The nav item + page is "Listing jobs" / "Liste İşleri"** (`translation:menu.listingJobs`, copy under `listings.jobs.*`): it holds create, existing-import AND publish jobs (`getJobs` does not filter by `kind`). The Action Center's `LISTING_JOB_FAILURES` item (`failed_count > 0`) covers failed publish runs too.

**Retry policy: only two retry layers can work.** `withEbayRateLimitRetry` (429/5xx, 4 attempts, `Retry-After`-aware) and the aspect self-heal (which *changes the input*). A blind job-level retry does not: by classification time, transient causes are exhausted and what remains is a rejection of the input.
- `failPreparedItems` honours `failure.details.retryable`: an unretryable failure is terminal on its **first** attempt (`isTerminal = !canRetry || lastAttempt`), otherwise a broken ASIN re-pays the provider, the LLM and the publish sequence three times. Batch items are otherwise terminal — the other 24 succeeded.
- **There is no seller-facing per-item Retry**, endpoint or button (removed) — the shared eBay quota must not be spent on the attempt least likely to succeed. `failure-visibility.guard.spec.ts` locks the removal and the retryable-only rule.

**A job item must always reach a terminal state.** `listing_job_items.status` defaults to `'DRAFT'`, so an unprocessed item looks like a draft and pins the job at "processing" (`updateJobCounts` finishes a job only when `success + failed >= total`).
- `processListingBatch` closes prepared items out on any write-phase throw via `failPreparedItems` (RETRYING while attempts remain **and** the failure is retryable, terminal ERROR otherwise, reservation released only when terminal).
- An outcome whose key is not in the batch context, and an item eBay never answered for, are recorded as failures — never silently skipped.
- The UI labels a job item's `draft` as **Queued** (`listings.jobs.items.itemStatus.*`) — it is a queue state, not a draft listing.

**Every failure carries a support reference.** `ListingFailureDetails.correlationId` is stamped by `ListingProcessorService.withTrace` from the ALS correlation context (the same id through HTTP → queue → Winston → `queue_observations`), shown under the failure reason. It is an opaque `req_<uuid>` — it identifies a request, not a user or a resource.

**A blacklist rejection is the seller's own setting, so say so.** `ListingStrategyService.validateListing`'s blacklist refusal classifies as `ListingFailureCode.BLACKLISTED_KEYWORD` with the matched keyword in `failureDetails.blacklistedKeyword` ("your blacklist blocked this (keyword: …)"), never the generic `UNKNOWN`. The blacklist matches buyer-visible text, whole words only (see the description-template notes).

**Job cancellation (`POST /v1/listings/jobs/:jobId/cancel`).** The job details page offers Cancel (it already polls every 3s, so there is no Refresh). **What went out, went out**: published ASINs keep their listings and ACTIVE job items; everything not yet terminal is closed out as `ListingFailureCode.CANCELLED` and its billing reservation released.
- The queue is stopped by a **flag the worker re-reads before each batch**, not by queue surgery (BullMQ jobs have no stable ids; already-queued jobs drain as one-DB-read no-ops).
- An **in-flight batch is allowed to finish** (its eBay calls are spent; abandoning it would leave listings on eBay with no row here). `updateJobCounts` still runs after a cancel and must not recompute the status away from CANCELLED — `ListingJobStatus.CANCELLED` is terminal and sticky (`CASE WHEN status = 'cancelled' THEN status ELSE …`).

**`listings.sku` + `listings.ebay_offer_id` (migration `067`)** let `updatePriceQuantity` address rows without a per-update offer lookup. Both are written at create (the batch worker passes `sku` + `outcome.offerId` to `createListing`) and by draft publish on the row it flips to ACTIVE; a draft has neither. Production SKUs were backfilled; pre-067 sandbox rows stay NULL (sandbox SKUs are timestamped, not derivable).
