- **A guessed SKU is never written back.** The fan-out falls back to `${asin}-NEW` for pre-067 rows, but `persistApplied` adopts it only when eBay resolved an offer from it (`sku = CASE WHEN v.offer_id IS NOT NULL THEN …`) — a wrong SKU in this column is permanent. `listing-invariants.guard.spec.ts` locks both rules.

**Taxonomy, not Inventory, is the per-ASIN bottleneck (5,000/day).** `persistCategoryMappings` writes the `AMAZON_CATEGORY` cache scope keyed on the **full Amazon category path** (`products.category_path`, migration `068`, backfilled from `raw_keepa_data`), so a niche pays Taxonomy once instead of once per ASIN (the `QUERY` key is a brand+title hash, unique per product). The leaf name alone is too weak ("Accessories" recurs under unrelated departments). Precedence: operator's locked pin → ASIN → Amazon category → query, so a bad generalization is always correctable.

**`EbayCallBudgetService`** (`src/common/ebay-budget/`) is a Redis Lua token bucket per resource, keyed on the UTC day. It is **`@Global` and lives in `common/`, not in `EbayModule`** — AdminModule must read it, and importing EbayModule there would close the `Ebay → Llm → Admin` cycle that `module-cycle.guard.spec.ts` (`modules/admin/`) prevents.
- Background work acquires against `limit − EBAY_BUDGET_RESERVE_PERCENT` (`ebay.budget.reservePercent`, default 5); interactive calls get the full ceiling, so a night of refreshing can never leave a seller unable to publish.
- Budget is charged **before every attempt** inside `withEbayRateLimitRetry` — a retry is a real call.
- **Exhaustion defers, it does not fail.** `EbayBudgetExhaustedError` → `ListingFailureCode.PROVIDER_BUDGET_EXHAUSTED` → the worker calls `job.moveToDelayed(resetAt)` so BullMQ does not burn a retry on a quota that refills at midnight.
- **Fails open.** An unreachable Redis allows the call; eBay's own 429 is the hard stop.
- **Ceilings come from eBay's `getRateLimits` and nowhere else** (migration `121`, `EbayAnalyticsService` + `EbayRateLimitStore`, refreshed at boot and hourly with an application token, last value persisted). There is no panel entry, env var or code default for a ceiling; with no snapshot ever stored the governor counts but does not gate. Only `ebay.budget.enabled` and `ebay.budget.reservePercent` are settings. **Trading is governed PER METHOD** — `EbayApiResource.TRADING_GET_MY_EBAY_SELLING` / `TRADING_END_ITEM`, each mapped to its exact resource name in `RESOURCE_SOURCE` (`ebay-rate-limits.ts`); adding a Trading call means adding a member and a row, and `trading-methods.guard.spec.ts` fails until both exist. **Every window eBay reports is enforced**: `governedWindows` gives the daily UTC-day counter plus one fixed-bucket counter per sub-daily window, checked all-or-nothing in one Lua call (a short-window refusal spends none of the daily allowance); `EbayBudgetExhaustedError.windowSeconds` says which ran out, and a short-window refusal defers by the 60s floor. The admin eBay Limits tab shows eBay's figures beside our Redis count (per resource, per UTC day, no per-user split) and never subtracts them — the two days reset at different times.

**Failure text is split by audience.** Sellers see ONLY the localized `failureCode` message; the provider's raw wording names internal fields and reads as a defect in their product. It is stripped from `ListingJobItemDto` and surfaced to operators at `GET /v1/admin/listing-failures` with ASIN + account context. `failure-visibility.guard.spec.ts` locks both halves.

**Other fan-out rules:** a listing is repriced through the store it was published on (`listings.ebay_account_id`), never an arbitrary active account; price comparison is numeric (`"12.30"` vs `12.3` is not a change).

**Capacity:** eBay is not the constraint (updates ≈ 47M/day against the Inventory quota). The ceiling is the product provider's throughput — scraper: proxies × `perIpRequestsPerSecond`; Keepa: the plan's refill rate — and the batch size is derived from it automatically, so there is no separate throughput cliff. Raising the refresh interval does *not* raise throughput: the scheduler is stale-driven and eBay's quota is a daily bucket.

### Listing content policy + shared LLM infra (B)
Configured per **listing settings group** (`content` JSONB on `listing_settings_groups`, migration `031`):
- `stripBrandFromTitle` — deterministic removal of Amazon brand tokens from the eBay title at **create**.
- `aiTitleEnabled` / `aiDescriptionEnabled` — optional one-time rewrite through the shared LLM client at **create only**.

**Create-only hard rule (100k+ listings):** AI must **never** run on product refresh, product-sync or sale-driven stock-sync. Only `ListingProcessorService` calls `ListingStrategyService.prepareListingData(..., { applyContentAi: true })`; refresh paths omit it and recompute price/quantity only. `create-only-ai.guard.spec.ts` locks this. AI failures always fall back to the deterministic title/description, so creation continues. For mass rewrites of existing listings, never re-queue create jobs — use deterministic strip-brand/templates or a future dedicated eBay revise batch.

**Shared transport:** `LlmModule` exports `LlmService` (`src/modules/llm/`), an OpenAI-compatible Chat Completions client with `chat()` and accumulated-delta `chatStream()` (`sse-parser.ts`): typed errors, bounded `Retry-After` handling for 429, per-call timeouts/abort signals, and fail-soft append-only usage logging in `llm_usage_log` (migration `040`), projected into `usage_events` (`llm-projection.ts`) for the admin Costs tab.

**One provider group.** `LlmService.resolveProvider` reads `LLM_PROVIDER` / `LLM_BASE_URL` / `LLM_API_KEY` / `LLM_CONTENT_MODEL` for both purposes (`LlmUsagePurpose.CONTENT`, `ASPECT`). It runs across thousands of products per bulk add, so use the **cheapest tier that does the job** ("compress a title to 80 chars", "pick one value from an allowed list").

**`LlmUsagePurpose.ASPECT`** chooses a required eBay item specific from the category's allowed-value list (see "Listing quality"). Gated by `ebay.aspects.llmEnabled` (default off) and capped by `ebay.aspects.llmMaxPerListing` (default 3). Its answer is validated against the allowed list and **written back as a learned default**, so each `(category, aspect)` is asked once.

**Provider/config.** The client posts plain Chat Completions to `{LLM_BASE_URL}/chat/completions` with a Bearer token, so switching providers is three values, identical in every environment including local dev (never test against a different model than production ships). `LLM_BASE_URL` and `LLM_CONTENT_MODEL` are env-only; the key can also be set at `/admin` → Settings (`llm.apiKey`).
- **Current default: Google Gemini** (`LLM_BASE_URL=https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com/v1beta/openai`, `LLM_CONTENT_MODEL=gemini-3.1-flash-lite`, key from `aistudio.google.com/api-keys`). OpenAI (`https://api.openai.com/v1`) and Groq (`https://api.groq.com/openai/v1`) are drop-in alternatives. **Anthropic is the exception** — `/v1/messages` with `x-api-key` would need an adapter in `LlmService`, not an env change.
- **Never a thinking model.** It spends the token budget reasoning and returns `finish_reason: length` or empty `content` at our caps — silently, because create falls back to the deterministic title (observed with `qwen3:1.7b` and `gemini-3.6-flash`). Use `-lite` / `-instruct` tiers. `ContentGenerationService.stripReasoning` also removes inline `<think>` blocks.
- **`pnpm --filter api llm:check`** (`src/scripts/llm-check.ts`, read-only) verifies a provider switch: one title-rewrite and one item-specific probe with production's request shape and token caps, failing on empty content, `finish_reason=length`, or an aspect answer not verbatim in the allowed list. Run it after ANY change to `LLM_BASE_URL` / `LLM_CONTENT_MODEL`.
- **Cost attribution needs a pricing row.** `buildLlmUsageEvents` computes NULL (rendered as an em dash, never `0`) when `llm_model_pricing` has no effective row for `(provider, model)` — `provider` is the literal `LLM_PROVIDER` value. Record it with `pnpm --filter api llm:pricing -- --provider google --model <id> --input-per-1m <usd> --output-per-1m <usd>` (`llm-pricing-set.ts`, effective-dated in one transaction). A genuinely free tier is recorded as a real `0`, distinct from unknown.
- `LLM_CONTENT_ENABLED` defaults to `false` (admin-togglable). On a free provider tier keep `LISTINGS_WORKER_CONCURRENCY` low (default 2; 1 on a free tier) — free tiers cap requests per minute and the 429s turn AI rewrites into deterministic fallbacks.

**No local LLM service.** `docker-compose.yml` ships no Ollama; dev, test and prod use the same hosted provider. At our prompt sizes (~300 in / ~30 out tokens per title) 100k listings cost single- to low-double-digit dollars on a flash/mini tier — not worth a second model and quality bar.

**The Zon Assistant chatbot ("Spec C") was removed and replaced by tawk.to** — see "Customer support widget — tawk.to". Its design docs (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-26-zon-assistant-backend-design.md`, `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-26-zon-assistant-backend.md`) are historical only; the code they describe is deleted.

Key files: `src/modules/llm/` (`llm.service.ts`, `llm-usage.service.ts`, `llm-projection.ts`, `sse-parser.ts`), `content-generation.service.ts`, `listing-strategy.service.ts`, and the listing-group `content` config in shared types/UI.

### Customer support widget — tawk.to

Customer-facing "contact us" support is a third-party embed (**tawk.to**, free), not custom code. The agent side (answering, routing, canned replies, history) lives entirely in tawk.to's own dashboard/app.

- `apps/web/src/features/support-widget/TawkToWidget/` (4-file split) injects the embed `<script>` (`https://embed.tawk.to/{propertyId}/{widgetId}`) into `document.body` on mount. It injects nothing when either ID is unset **or in demo mode** (`isDemoMode()`); injection is idempotent (DOM-id guarded).
- Mounted in two places: `LandingPage` keeps tawk.to's native floating bubble; `AppLayout` hides it (`Tawk_API.hideWidget()`) and renders a SellerHill "Contact us / Bize ulaşın" launcher in the sidebar footer — click calls `showWidget()` + `maximize()`, collapsed sidebar shows only the headset icon, unread counts appear on the launcher, mobile launch closes the sidebar first. **Deliberately NOT mounted in `OperatorLayout`** — staff at `/admin` should not see the customer bubble.
- **Env: exactly two names everywhere — `VITE_TAWKTO_PROPERTY_ID` + `VITE_TAWKTO_WIDGET_ID`** (`apps/web/.env.example`, `Dockerfile.web` `ARG`/`ENV`, both Coolify compose files' `web` build `args`). Never create non-`VITE_` `TAWKTO_*` variables. They are public identifiers, not secrets, and build-time — a change needs a web rebuild. Current IDs: `6a77cbcad1e0df1d455e2131` / `1jvhv843q` (local `.env`, and the defaults in `docker-compose.test.yml`).
- **Production is OFF by operator choice**: `docker-compose.production.yml` defaults both to empty on purpose, so the widget no-ops unless a Coolify variable is explicitly set. Never add a `:-<real-id>` fallback there — an explicitly-blank Coolify variable would silently re-enable it.
- Going live: create a tawk.to property, copy the two IDs from the widget's Direct Chat Link (`tawk.to/chat/<propertyId>/<widgetId>`), set them in Coolify, rebuild `web`; invite agents in tawk.to (free, unlimited seats). Pre-chat forms, canned replies, office hours, triggers are tawk.to-dashboard configuration. The widget receives no SellerHill user data; showing the seller's name/email would mean a `Tawk_API.setAttributes(...)` call in `TawkToWidget.container.tsx` after `onLoad` — not implemented.
- **Residue of the removed assistant:** migration `093` dropped the assistant/knowledge/support schema from `041`–`047` + `049b` while keeping the load-bearing objects those migrations also created (`user_role` + `users.role`/`session_version` + `auth_refresh_sessions`, `llm_model_pricing` and the live `llm_usage_log` cost columns, `usage_events`/`shared_cost_entries`). `LlmUsagePurpose` is `CONTENT`/`ASPECT` only. **`UserRole.SUPPORT` is vestigial** — kept out of the seller app (`isOperatorRole`) with no console to land on (`resolveHomePath` → `/login`); demote any SUPPORT account with `pnpm user:set-role` (to `customer` or `admin`). The `support` enum value is kept (dropping a value from a live column's type needs a type rebuild).

AdminModule imports `ProductSourceModule`, never `ListingsModule`.

## Account scope — seller app vs operator console (hard separation)

**An account is either a seller account or a staff account, never both.** SellerHill ships two products behind one login: the seller app (dashboard / listings / orders / settings) and the operator console (`/admin`).

| Role | May reach |
|---|---|
| `CUSTOMER` | Seller app only. No admin panel. |
| `SUPPORT` | **Vestigial** — no console exists (the `/support` console was removed when support moved to tawk.to, see "Customer support widget — tawk.to"). Still kept out of the seller app by `isOperatorRole`, so it lands nowhere; demote any existing SUPPORT account via `pnpm user:set-role`. |
| `ADMIN` | Admin panel only. |

- **Shared predicate**: `isOperatorRole` / `isCustomerRole` / `OPERATOR_ROLES` in `packages/shared/src/domain/auth/account-scope.ts`. Both sides of the wire use it — never re-derive the role set inline.
- **API enforcement** is in `JwtAuthGuard` (`apps/api/src/modules/auth/jwt-auth.guard.ts`): a staff role is refused (`403 auth.errors.customerAreaForbidden`) on any route not marked **`@OperatorSurface()`**. **Every authenticated route is a customer surface by default**, so a forgotten decorator fails closed. The decorator only widens access for staff; customers are kept out of operator routes by `RolesGuard` + `@Roles(...)`.
- **The operator surfaces are exactly three**: `admin.controller`, `auth.controller`, `profile.controller`. `account-scope.guard.spec.ts` unit-tests the guard **and** source-greps that list, so adding a fourth is a deliberate edit — marking a seller controller would hand staff a customer's data.
- **Web enforcement**: `apps/web/src/layouts/OperatorLayout/` is the staff shell for `/:locale/admin` — own sidebar, no seller nav, no store switcher, no customer widgets. `AppLayout` bounces staff to the console; `OperatorLayout` bounces sellers to `/dashboard`. Both re-export one set of styled components (`layouts/shell/AppShell.style.ts`) so the chrome cannot fork.
- **`apps/web/src/app/operatorRouting.ts` is the single routing authority** for staff: the console route table, `resolveHomePath(role, hasConnectedAccounts)` (used by login, register, Google auth and both layout guards) and `resolveOperatorBreadcrumbs`. `routeMeta.ts` describes the seller app only.
- **Role changes are CLI-only** (`pnpm user:set-role`, see "Role CLI" below). Promoting an account to ADMIN removes its seller-app access on the next request (the CLI revokes sessions). Keep at least one ADMIN account that is not someone's selling account.
- **Not implemented (deferred):** operator impersonation. It needs an impersonation token, a redaction layer and its own audit trail — none exists.

## Admin Panel — Observability, FinOps & Operations

The admin module lives in `apps/api/src/modules/admin/` and reuses the auth RBAC (`UserRole`, `Roles`, `RolesGuard`, `PrivilegedSessionGuard`, migration `041`) — no second role system. Every admin route is gated by `JwtAuthGuard` + `RolesGuard` + `PrivilegedSessionGuard` (per-request session revalidation) + `@Roles(UserRole.ADMIN)`.

**Read-only except for two deliberate write surfaces**, both shared platform data with no owning customer module: **runtime platform settings** (`PUT`/`DELETE /v1/admin/settings/:key`) and **listing-quality curated aspect defaults** (`PUT /v1/admin/listing-quality/defaults`, `DELETE .../defaults/:id`). Two further POSTs exist but write nothing: `settings/email/test` and `settings/scraper/proxies/verify`. Observability data is never mutated from here; any other admin-initiated change goes through the owning module's endpoint and guard chain. (The platform proxy-pool surface was retired — see below.)

**One panel, no duplicates.** `/:locale/admin` is the single admin surface, rendered in the **operator shell**, lazy-loaded and role-gated (backend guards remain authoritative). Tabs (`AdminTabId`): Overview / Queues / Costs / Listing Quality / Settings / Billing / Users / eBay Limits / Listing Failures. Do not add a second admin page — add a tab. (`/:locale/admin/assistant` redirects to `/admin`.)
- **eBay Limits** is read-only: per-resource shared daily quota (used / remaining / background ceiling / reset).
- **Listing Failures** is the ONLY surface carrying a provider's raw error text — sellers see only the localized `failureCode` message. **Seller-caused codes are excluded by default (operator decision)**: `SELLER_CAUSED_LISTING_FAILURE_CODES` (shared: `BLACKLISTED_KEYWORD`, `DUPLICATE_LISTING`, `ZERO_STOCK`, `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED`, `CANCELLED`) are left out of the list and the 30-day breakdown unless requested explicitly via `failureCode` — the operator can act on none of them and they buried the provider failures. Uncoded rows stay in; Amazon-side conditions (`ASIN_NOT_FOUND`, `NO_BUY_BOX`, `SOURCE_PRICE_UNAVAILABLE`) stay visible as a scraper-health signal.
- Frontend: container logic is split into per-tab hooks under `features/admin/hooks/*` (e.g. `useAdminSettings.ts`). Money renders via `formatMicroCurrency` (`@repo/ui`); an unknown (null) cost renders as an em dash, never `0`. Only redacted data is rendered — no prompts, credentials or raw provider errors outside Listing Failures. Destructive queue actions are intentionally absent.

Read APIs and data model:
- `GET /v1/admin/overview` (platform counts, current-period usage, BullMQ counts), `GET /v1/admin/usage/summaries` (`(source, metric)` aggregation), `GET /v1/admin/queues/health` (read-only; the admin module never enqueues), `GET /v1/admin/queues/observations[/:id]`, `GET /v1/admin/finops/users`, `GET /v1/admin/finops/providers`, `GET /v1/admin/operations/summary`, `GET /v1/admin/ebay/budget`, `GET /v1/admin/listing-failures`, `GET /v1/admin/listing-quality/*`, `GET /v1/admin/users`, `GET /v1/admin/settings`, `GET /v1/admin/billing/metrics`.
- Migration `049`: append-only `usage_events` + effective-dated `shared_cost_entries`. Costs are nullable micro-USD `BIGINT`; NULL means unknown and is never represented as zero; currency and estimated cost are pair-coupled. `finops-helpers.ts` (pure, tested) resolves effective pricing, token cost and exact-residue shared-cost allocation.
- Migration `050`: partial unique indexes for idempotent projection replay. `UsageEventsService` is the single fail-soft writer, called from `KeepaUsageService` (deterministic per-user fair share) and `LlmUsageService` (effective-dated `llm_model_pricing`). Source logs stay authoritative; a projection failure never breaks a provider call. `UsageBackfillService` can rebuild history idempotently but is deliberately not wired to startup or cron.
- Migration `051`: append-only `queue_observations` for BullMQ completed/failed events. `QueueEventsCollectorService` persists only allowlisted correlation plus a SHA-256 hash of allowlisted identifiers — never raw payloads. `QUEUE_OBSERVABILITY_ENABLED=false` disables collection; retention is the shared `data-retention` job.
- Correlation: `stampJobData` / `extractCorrelationId` / `generateCorrelationId` plus an AsyncLocalStorage context carry one trace id across HTTP and every operational queue (`RequestIdMiddleware` enters ALS; Winston adds correlation/queue/job fields). Job ids, retry, repeat, dedup and priority are unaffected.
- Follow-ups: provider invoice reconciliation, external alert delivery.

### Database retention (`data-retention` queue)

**Every append-only table is purged by ONE nightly job (`DataRetentionService`) driven by one manifest** (`data-retention.manifest.ts`). Adding a table is one manifest entry plus its `platform_settings` key — never another worker.

| Table | Age column | Default | Floor | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `queue_observations` | `recorded_at` | 7d | 1d | Diagnostic only |
| `keepa_usage_log` | `requested_at` | 90d | 7d | Fastest-growing table; cost survives in `usage_events` |
| `llm_usage_log` | `requested_at` | 90d | 7d | Same |
| `usage_events` | `recorded_at` | 400d | 30d | The FinOps projection the Costs tab reads |
| `buyer_message_log` | `created_at` | 400d | **180d** | **Correctness — see below** |
| `audit_logs` | `created_at` | 730d | **365d** | Compliance evidence |
| `listing_revisions` | `recorded_at` | 30d | 30d | Revisions drawer history only. Since 2026-09-30 every successful refresh also writes a previous = new "checked, unchanged" row per active listing it did not push (`ProductSyncService.recordUnchangedChecks`, called from `RefreshProcessorService` after `flushUpdates`) — ~4 rows/listing/day, which is why the default window is the 30-day floor (operator decision, 2026-09-30); the cross-listing Revision History page filters those rows out |
| `best_sellers_views` | `viewed_at` | 400d | **60d** | The Best Sellers allowance ledger (2026-09-29, `retention.bestSellersViewsDays`): `SUM(product_count)` over the quota window IS the seller's used figure, so a row must outlive one billing period plus the webhook grace (~31 days) before it may be purged |
| `ebay_notification_events` | `received_at` | 90d | 7d | Dedupe of retried NEW_MESSAGE deliveries only; eBay stops retrying after a few attempts |
| `ebay_notification_raw_captures` | `received_at` | 30d | 7d | Diagnostic verbatim capture — see "eBay Messages" |
| `auto_fulfill_events` | `created_at` | 400d | 90d | Audit trail of automatic Amazon purchases (migration `132`). Evidence only — nothing re-derives state from it — but evidence about spent money |

- **`buyer_message_log` is an idempotency ledger, not a log.** Its partial unique index on `(ebay_order_id, event_type) WHERE status = 'sent'` is the only thing stopping a buyer being messaged twice; deleting a row **re-arms** that event. Hence the 180-day code floor, enforced independently of the registry and locked by a spec.
- **Identifiers are interpolated, so they come from a frozen literal list and are re-validated** by `isSafeSqlIdentifier`; a failure **refuses to build the SQL**. The age window is a bound parameter. `clampRetentionDays` maps NaN/0/negative to the rule's **floor**, never to 0 — a mistyped override must not mean "delete everything".
- **Batched, resumable, fail-soft.** `ctid IN (SELECT … LIMIT 5000)` with a 400-batch-per-table cap (`DATA_RETENTION_BATCH_SIZE` / `DATA_RETENTION_MAX_BATCHES`), so a first run on a long-lived DB cannot hold locks or bloat WAL. One failing table never stops the others, and nothing throws into the API process.
- Windows are panel-tunable at `/admin` → Settings → **Data retention & disk**. The schedule (`DATA_RETENTION_CRON`, default `17 3 * * *`, falling back to `QUEUE_OBSERVABILITY_RETENTION_CRON`) is read once at boot and stays env-only.
- The old `queue-observability-retention` queue has no worker; clear its lingering repeatable entry once with `redis-cli --scan --pattern 'bull:queue-observability-retention:*' | xargs redis-cli DEL`.

### Log file retention

`apps/api/logs/*.log` is a **shipping buffer for Promtail, not the archive** — Loki owns queryable history (7 days). Rotated files are **gzipped**; combined logs are kept `LOG_COMBINED_RETENTION_DAYS` (default 7, matching Loki), error logs `LOG_ERROR_RETENTION_DAYS` (default 30, small and needed after the Loki window closes). Promtail globs `*.log`, so a rotated `*.log.gz` is correctly invisible to it.

### Proxy pool management — retired (2026-08-13)

The admin "Proxies" tab and its backend (`AdminProxiesService`, `POST`/`PATCH /v1/admin/proxies*`, `proxy-pool.helpers.ts`, `AdminWarningKind.PROXY_EXPIRING`/`PROXY_EXPIRED`/`PROXY_POOL_EXHAUSTED`) are deleted, and migration `094` dropped the `proxies` table. Amazon browser proxies are now a self-service per-`amazon_accounts` field — see "Amazon Scraping — Anti-Ban Strategy". (Unrelated: the scraper's `scraper.proxies` setting and its verify endpoint, see "Scraper provider".)

### User monitoring (`/admin` → Users)

`GET /v1/admin/users` returns one row per user joining operational footprint (active listings, Amazon buyer accounts, active eBay stores, orders in the last 30 days) with period usage/cost from the `usage_events` projection (Keepa fair-split tokens, LLM tokens, estimated micro-USD). Read-only; role changes stay CLI-only.

### Runtime platform settings (`/admin` → Settings)

Operational configuration lives in `platform_settings` (migration `059`: `key` PK, `value` TEXT, `updated_by`) and is edited from the panel instead of env + redeploy.

- **Resolution order is DB override → env var → code default.** Deleting an override returns the key to env/default — the escape hatch when a panel change makes things worse.
- **The registry is the single source of truth**: `apps/api/src/common/settings/platform-settings.registry.ts` declares each key's type, bounds, default, env var and restart requirement. Adding a knob = one registry entry + the key in `PlatformSettingKey` (`packages/shared`) + i18n (below). API, validation and UI all derive from it.
- **`PlatformSettingsService` is `@Global`** (`common/settings/settings.module.ts`), so any worker/scheduler/controller can consume it without import cycles. Overrides load in one query, cached 30s; writes invalidate locally and other replicas converge within the TTL. A missing table or DB error fails soft to env/defaults.
- **Env-only, never in the registry:** connection bootstrap (`DATABASE_*`, `REDIS_*`), crypto/auth secrets (`JWT_*`, `AMAZON_ENCRYPTION_KEY`), provider credentials (`KEEPA_API_KEY`, `EBAY_*`, `GOOGLE_*`, `STRIPE_*`), process identity (`NODE_ENV`, `PORT`, `CORS_ORIGINS`). They are read before the DB exists, rotated as an ops action, and must not be reachable over HTTP.
- **Secrets in the registry** (`scraper.proxies`, `SMTP_PASSWORD`, `llm.apiKey`, the Aquiline API key and webhook secret) are write-only: stored `enc:`-prefixed AES-256-GCM, never returned (DTO carries `value: null` + `hasValue`). A secret's fallback is still its env var.
  - **A secret's DESTINATION stays out of the panel when the panel could redirect it.** `LLM_BASE_URL` / `LLM_CONTENT_MODEL` are env-only: an editable address would let a compromised admin session point the next call at its own host and receive the key. `llm.service.spec.ts` asserts the service never asks the settings layer for the address. (`tracking.aquiline.baseUrl` IS editable and carries the Aquiline token — same exposure, not yet closed.)
- **`requiresRestart` is not cosmetic.** It marks values consumed once at boot (`KEEPA_REFRESH_SCHEDULER_CRON`, `amazon.orderSync.cron`) or baked into an already-running per-order scheduler (Amazon tracking intervals — a running order keeps its cadence until it reschedules or `reconcileSchedulers()` runs at boot; new schedules read the setting fresh). Worker concurrency (`*_WORKER_CONCURRENCY`, `*_QUEUE_CONCURRENCY`) is applied at decoration time and stays env-only. Every consumer must read through `PlatformSettingsService`, never raw `process.env`/`ConfigService` — a raw read silently ignores the panel override.
- **The Keepa kill switch is enforced at job execution**: `RefreshProcessorService.selectRefreshBatch` checks `KEEPA_REFRESH_ENABLED` via `PlatformSettingsService` every tick, so turning it off stops spend immediately. `RefreshSchedulerService` reads the same key from `ConfigService` at boot only to decide whether to register the tick — deliberately not the enforcement point.
- **Every setting needs a title AND a description in BOTH `en` and `tr`** (`admin.settings.keys.<key>` / `admin.settings.descriptions.<key>` in `admin.json`); `platform-settings-i18n.guard.spec.ts` enforces it. A missing translation renders the raw key with no error.
- Writes are validated/normalized by `platform-settings.helpers.ts` (`validateSettingValue`, `coerceBoolean`, `coerceNumber` — tested) and audit-logged in the same transaction (`PLATFORM_SETTING_CHANGE` / `PLATFORM_SETTING_RESET`, secrets redacted).
- `POST /v1/admin/settings/email/test` opens an SMTP connection with the effective settings and sends no mail. `EmailService` rebuilds its transporter whenever the resolved config fingerprint changes.

### Role CLI (operator-only, no HTTP path)

Role changes are a **server-console operation only**: `apps/api/src/scripts/user-set-role.ts` (`pnpm user:set-role --email <email> --role <customer|support|admin>`), pure logic in `user-set-role-helpers.ts` (tested).

- **Strict `--email`/`--role` parsing** against `UserRole` — no positional args, no env fallback. Unknown flags → `RoleCliArgError` → exit 2; `--role` must equal an enum value exactly (`admin`, not `Admin`).
- **Transactional user lock** — `SELECT … FROM users WHERE LOWER(email) = LOWER($1) FOR UPDATE`.
- **No-op writes are skipped** (`shouldChangeRole`); re-running with the same role exits 0.
- **Session invalidation** — all active `auth_refresh_sessions` are revoked explicitly; migration `041`'s `users_security_change_revoke_sessions` trigger also bumps `session_version` (defense in depth), which invalidates outstanding access tokens via `AuthSessionService.validateAccess`.
- **Durable redacted audit** in the same transaction: `audit_logs` `action='ROLE_CHANGE'`, `resource_type='user'`, `details` = `{ action, previousRole, newRole, targetUserId, actor|null, changedAt }` only — never email/password/session secrets.
- **Exit codes**: `0` changed or already that role, `2` bad args, `3` user not found, `1` DB failure.

The role decides which product the account can open (see "Account scope"): promoting a seller cuts it off from its own listings and orders; demoting an operator cuts it off from `/admin`. Do not put a `--` separator before the flags: pnpm forwards it literally and this strict parser rejects it (exit 2) — unlike `billing:reconcile`, which skips a bare `--`. Alternatively run `npx tsx src/scripts/user-set-role.ts --email … --role …` from `apps/api` (or `pnpm --filter api exec tsx …`). The CLI loads `apps/api/.env` like `migrate.ts`, does NOT bootstrap NestJS, and records the shell `$USER` as the audit `actor` when available.

## Billing & Packages

Database-driven, on Stripe. Canonical details: [docs/billing-and-packages.md](docs/billing-and-packages.md); keep this section aligned when billing behavior changes.

### Customer packages

Customers see three capacity meters — active listings, monthly tracking conversions and monthly Best Sellers products — and **automatic orders are unlimited on every plan (migration `125`, 2026-09-29)**. Internal scraper, Keepa, LLM, API, queue and proxy usage is never exposed as customer-facing tokens or credits.

**13 tiers, monthly billing only (migrations `083` + `085` + `125` + `137`).** **Mini (migration `137`, 2026-10-02, operator decision) sits below Lite: $19.99, 100 active listings, 20 tracking conversions (the trial's own figure, so the plan is never smaller than the trial), 1,000 Best Sellers products, unlimited automatic orders** — worst-case margin ~77% (20 × $0.14 + Stripe $0.88 + ~$1 infrastructure = $4.68). Its price was NOT checked against the competitor's 100-listing tier (the operator has been told). **Fixed monthly costs as of 2026-10-02 (operator figures): server $270 + Aquiline Starter $42 (300 shipments included) + scraper proxies $20 = $332** — break-even is roughly 13–18 paying sellers whichever tier they are on (worst case, everyone spending the whole conversion quota). Migration `125_billing_prices_v3_unlimited_ao_best_sellers.sql` (2026-09-29) set the current "v3" prices, made automatic orders unlimited and added the Best Sellers allowance. The listings / conversions / AO limits table is under "The priced meter is tracking conversions" below; the Best Sellers allowance (Trial 500) is here and in "Amazon Best Sellers browsing".

| Plan | Monthly price (v3) | was (`083`) | Best Sellers products / mo |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Mini | **$19.99** | — (new in `137`) | 1,000 |
| Lite | **$24.99** | $19.99 | 1,500 |
| Nano | **$29.99** | $24.99 | 2,500 |
| Micro | **$34.99** | $29.99 | 5,000 |
| Starter | **$44.99** | $44.99 | 7,500 |
| Basic | **$54.99** | $59.99 | 10,000 |
| Plus | **$64.99** | $84.99 | 12,500 |
| Growth | **$89.99** | $104.99 | 15,000 |
| Advanced | **$129.99** | $159.99 | 20,000 |
| Pro | **$164.99** | $179.99 | 25,000 |
| Elite | **$229.99** | $319.99 | 35,000 |
| Business | **$284.99** | $429.99 | 50,000 |
| Enterprise | **$339.99** | $529.99 | 75,000 |

**Why v3 (operator decisions, 2026-09-29).** The benchmark is AslDrop (19 tiers, 50 → 100,000 listings; a tracking allowance of ~2% of listings; 12-hourly sync; 3/10/unlimited store caps; unlimited automatic orders). **Rule: never price BELOW the competitor at a shared listing tier** — undercutting invites a price war — while equal or $1–2 above is fine, because at the same tier SellerHill gives 2.5–6× the tracking conversions, 4×/day sync, no store cap and a 30-day trial. Lite, Nano and Micro each rose $5, Starter is unchanged, every other tier got cheaper. Product data now comes from our own scraper (Keepa is the rollback only — see "Product Refresh Pipeline"), so the Keepa-token cost line no longer applies and the **binding unit cost is the Aquiline conversion at the Starter plan's $0.14/shipment**. Worst-case margin (the seller spends the WHOLE conversion quota at $0.14, Stripe 2.9% + $0.30, ~$1 infrastructure + proxy share): Lite 78%, Nano 69%, Micro 53%, Starter 47%, Basic 43%, Plus 40%, Growth 48%, Advanced 57%, Pro 53%, Elite 59%, Business 61%, Enterprise 62%. **Existing subscribers move to the new price from their next renewal automatically** (`PriceMigrationProcessor` + the `billing_price_change` e-mail, Stripe Prices minted by the hourly catalog sync — no operator step; see "Changing a plan's price"), so the three price rises are never applied mid-period.

**There is no annual interval.** Dropped for simplicity; `BillingInterval.ANNUAL` stays in code and schema, so reintroducing it is a migration inserting `annual` price rows, not a refactor. Migration `083` closed the `052` annual rows with `effective_to` rather than deleting them.

**Prices come from a cost model, with the competitor as a FLOOR, never a target (2026-09-29).** The 2026-08-21 model (history) weighed Aquiline tracking conversion (~$0.10/shipment then — ~65% of unit cost at the 10,000-listing tier), Keepa refresh tokens (~$0.010–0.015 per active product per month), Stripe fees, listing churn (negligible) and infrastructure, and verified every tier profitable from 5 users AND for a single user filling their quota (worst-case margin 18%; normal 59–75%); its prices landed a few percent below Easync's, with an AO quota of 10% of listings (Pro the one 7% exception) — both moot now that AO is unlimited and the rule is "never below the competitor at a shared tier". The v3 model drops the Keepa line and prices Aquiline at its real $0.14; the worst-case margins under "Customer packages" are the figures to update when either input changes. **Do not re-derive these numbers from a competitor's page without re-running the cost model** — the competitor sets the floor at a shared listing tier, the model sets the margin, and both have to hold; a competitor with a different cost structure (Easync's in-house tracking; AslDrop's ~2%-of-listings tracking allowance against our 2.5–6× larger one) can sustain figures we cannot, and vice versa.

Plan prices, limits, active state, ordering and Stripe price ids live in the billing catalog tables, not TypeScript constants or UI copy. The landing pricing reads the public catalog. Do not create a second plan model.

### Billing implementation

- Migrations `052` (catalog, subscriptions, usage periods, listing/AO reservations, webhook inbox) and `053` (quota enforcement). Backend `apps/api/src/modules/billing/`; frontend `apps/web/src/features/billing/`.
- **Billing is a routed page**, `BillingPage` at `/:locale/billing` with its own seller-sidebar entry and `routeMeta.ts` breadcrumb. `/:locale/settings/billing` and `/:locale/settings?drawer=billing` redirect there (the latter via the `SettingsRoute` wrapper in `App.tsx`). The admin Billing tab is unrelated.
- **Stripe is the ONLY payment provider (Billing / Invoicing / Tax).** `StripeBillingProvider` (`billing-provider.ts`) is the single `BillingProviderPort` implementation — no provider switch, no fake local provider; local dev and test run against Stripe test mode. Without `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` the same class reports `isConfigured() === false` and checkout/portal throw `billing.errors.providerNotConfigured` (→ 409) while catalog + summary keep working. The client is built lazily because `new Stripe('')` throws. (A Paddle implementation was deleted unused; recover from git if ever needed.)
  - **`BillingProvider.LOCAL` is NOT a provider.** It is the state of a `billing_customers` row that never reached checkout (every user starts `local` via `ensureLocalCustomer`/`startTrialOnce`), and what DTOs report when no key is configured so the FE can hide checkout. Existing rows carry it — do not remove the member.
  - **Checkout and portal are Stripe-hosted redirects.** `POST /billing/checkout` → Checkout Session (`mode: 'subscription'`, `automatic_tax: { enabled: true }`, **never** `payment_method_types` — omitting it enables dynamic payment methods); `GET /billing/portal` → `billingPortal.sessions.create`. Card data, SCA/3DS and PCI scope stay with Stripe.
  - **Checkout/Portal render in the seller's in-app language.** `baseApi.ts`'s `prepareHeaders` sends `Accept-Language` from `i18n.language`; `resolveStripeLocale` (`billing-helpers.ts`, pure) maps it onto `SUPPORTED_STRIPE_LOCALES` (`en tr ru de fr es it ro zh pt-BR`; app `pt` → `pt-BR`) and falls back to `'en'` — never `'auto'`, which reads the browser/OS locale. The `locale` is passed explicitly on `createCheckout`, `createAddonCheckout` and `createPortal`.
  - **The Stripe customer is created and linked BEFORE the Checkout Session opens** (`ensureCustomer` → `linkProviderCustomer`), not from a webhook. Webhook order is not guaranteed; linking on `checkout.session.completed` let a `customer.subscription.created` arrive first, find no customer and be dropped — the seller paid and got no subscription. Do not move linking back into a webhook.
  - **`customer_update: { address: 'auto', name: 'auto' }` is REQUIRED** because the customer exists up-front with no address, and `automatic_tax` makes Checkout reject the session outright without one. Alongside it: `billing_address_collection: 'required'` and `tax_id_collection: { enabled: true }` (sellers are businesses; the EU VAT id triggers reverse charge).
  - **Webhook: one endpoint, `POST /billing/webhooks/stripe`**, verified with the SDK's `stripe.webhooks.constructEvent` (never a hand-rolled HMAC) against `req.rawBody` (`main.ts` sets `rawBody: true`). Verification is the whole security boundary: a missing/invalid signature or secret is a 401. `BillingWebhookProcessor.process` runs insert → claim → stale-check → apply → mark, idempotent on `provider_event_id`. JWT is never the billing source of truth.
  - **Only `customer.subscription.created|updated|deleted` change subscription state**, and `stripe-event-applier.ts` (pure, tested) trusts the subscription object's own `status`, not the event type. The other handled events: `checkout.session.completed` in `mode: 'payment'` grants a top-up (see "Quota top-ups"); `invoice.payment_failed` sends an e-mail only; `invoice.paid` and `charge.dispute.*` are recorded (disputes logged at `error`) and change nothing.
  - **Billing failure keys map to HTTP statuses** in `billing.controller.ts` (`BILLING_ERROR_STATUS` + `rethrowBillingError`); seller strings live in `billing.json` under `billing.errors.*`. Without a mapping a configuration problem reaches the seller as a blank 500. A key with no mapping stays a 500 and a key with no i18n entry renders raw — add both halves together.
  - **Stripe Tax** carries from the Checkout Session onto the subscription, so renewals are taxed with no extra code. **It silently collects ZERO tax until a head-office address AND at least one registration exist in the Dashboard.** Registration is a legal decision for the owner — never infer or automate it. Products use tax code `txcd_10103001` (`SAAS_TAX_CODE`, `stripe-catalog-sync.ts`). Stripe is not a Merchant of Record.
  - **Catalog mirroring**: `billing_plans.provider_product_id` / `billing_plan_prices.provider_price_id` must hold real Stripe ids before checkout works. `syncStripeCatalog` (`stripe-catalog-sync.ts`) mints missing ones, runs hourly inside `PriceMigrationProcessor`, and on demand via `pnpm --filter api stripe:sync-catalog` (direct `pg` Pool, no NestJS). Rows with an id are not re-created (Stripe Prices are immutable; a price change is a new row), but each mirrored Price is retrieved and compared — a mismatch is reported and the CLI **exits non-zero** so a deploy fails instead of advertising one figure while charging another.
  - Env: `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`, `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` (both optional; absent = 409). `VITE_STRIPE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` is provisioned (`apps/web/.env.example`, `Dockerfile.web`, both Coolify compose files) but unused — the redirect flow needs no Stripe.js.
- **The free trial is rationed per eBay STORE, not per email (migration `084`).** Every data screen sits behind `EbayAccountGuard`, so the eBay store is the resource worth farming. `ebay_trial_ledger` is deliberately **not** FK'd to `ebay_accounts` (and `first_user_id` is `ON DELETE SET NULL`) so it survives an account deletion; its `(seller_id, marketplace_id)` primary key is the enforcement (`INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` in `claimEbayTrial`). `BillingService.assertEbayStoreMayConnect` runs in the eBay OAuth callback **before** the account row is written. **A paying customer is never blocked**, and the check **fails open** on a billing outage (it is a fraud control; failing closed would lock out payers).
  - **The original claimant reconnecting their own store is allowed**: `getEbayTrialClaimant` distinguishes "same user reconnecting" from "a different registration farming". A NULL `first_user_id` (original account deleted) still refuses — that is the farming case.
- **Disconnecting an eBay store is a STATE CHANGE, never a delete** (`POST /v1/ebay/accounts/:accountId/disconnect`, `EbayAccountStatus.DISCONNECTED`, migration `105`).
  - **The row must survive**: `orders.ebay_account_id` is `ON DELETE CASCADE` (would destroy order/tax history) and `listings.ebay_account_id` is `ON DELETE SET NULL` (would orphan listings).
  - **DISCONNECTED is distinct from REVOKED**, which the Action Center renders as a CRITICAL alarm; a deliberate disconnect must not raise an alarm the seller cannot clear.
  - Every account/token accessor in `ebay.service.ts` filters `status = 'active'`, so leaving ACTIVE stops order sync, auto-fulfill and tracking pushes structurally with no worker changes.
  - **Tokens are NULLed** (migration `105` dropped `NOT NULL` on the three token columns) so an unentitled credential never sits at rest. The grant is NOT revoked on eBay's side; the confirmation copy tells the seller to remove SellerHill under eBay's "Third-party app access".
  - **Reconnecting reactivates the SAME row** (`(seller_id, marketplace_id)` is UNIQUE; a new row would strand the store's history). A disconnected row owned by a **different** SellerHill account is refused (`ebay.errors.storeOwnedByAnotherAccount`).
  - `getAccountsByUserId` excludes disconnected rows, which keeps `EbayAccountGuard` correct (disconnecting the only store returns the seller to the connect prompt). Trade-off: a disconnected store disappears from store filters until reconnected.
  - **A disconnected (or revoked) store's listings stay ACTIVE but are outside every automation scope (operator decision, 2026-10-03):** they hold no plan slot and are not ranked for the plan limit (`countActiveListings`, `applyListingPlanLimit`, the reconcile candidates), their products are not claimed for refresh (`buildRefreshEntitlementSql().storeActiveFilter`, shared with the admin refresh-lag query), and they get no price/stock push and no "checked, unchanged" revision (`ProductSyncService`). ONE predicate, `buildListingStoreActiveSql(alias)` (`common/utils/listing-store-active-sql.ts`); `listing-store-active-sql.guard.spec.ts` checks every site. Nothing is written on disconnect, so reconnecting brings everything back by itself. A listing with NULL store (pre-030; none in production) stays in scope.
  - **A non-active store is never guessed or silently skipped elsewhere either:** a listing job for a store that is not owned + active is refused at enqueue (400 `listings.errors.storeUnavailable`) and a batch whose store went away closes its items terminally (reservations released); a shipped order on a non-active store is HELD (`tracking_held`), not marked shipped, and is pushed once the store is back (every SHIPPED tick with `ebay_tracking_pushed_at IS NULL` re-runs the push, which reads eBay first); the on-demand conversion refuses (`orders.errors.storeNotConnected`) before buying a number; `getAccountAccessToken` and discovery read ACTIVE rows only; the per-user "active account" helpers (`getActiveAccount*`, an unordered `LIMIT 1`) are deleted and `no-active-account-helper.guard.spec.ts` keeps them out; `resolveListingAccountId` guesses only when the user has exactly ONE active store.
- **The trial is 50 listings / 20 tracking conversions / 500 Best Sellers products / UNLIMITED automatic orders for 30 days** (AO was 20 until `125` made it unlimited on the trial too — its cost ceiling was always the 20 conversions). Duration is `billing.trialDays` (DB → `BILLING_TRIAL_DAYS` → default 30; bounds 1–90). It must stay smaller than the plans it converts into. Cost is ~$1.00–1.25 per trial user (hard ceiling ~$345 per 100 if every one fills the 20-conversion meter — orders themselves cost no third-party money, see "The priced meter").
- **`BILLING_ENFORCEMENT_ENABLED` (`billing.enforcementEnabled`, panel-editable at `/admin` → Settings → Billing) defaults to `true`.** It is the master switch for every quota gate, the trial's included — with it off the trial has no cost ceiling. Set it `false` only to stage a rollout. Always read it through `PlatformSettingsService.getBoolean` (as `QuotaEnforcementService.isEnabled()` and `BillingService.getCatalog()`/`getSummary()` do); **never from `resolveBillingConfig()`**, which is an env-only reader (defaulting to false) kept only for the Stripe key fields, which are not DB-overridable.

### Entitlement & quota enforcement (rewritten 2026-08-21)

This section was once documented before it was implemented (the gate ignored subscription status, meters never reset, slots were never released). `entitlement-enforcement.guard.spec.ts` source-greps every call below so that gap cannot silently reopen.

**Entitlement is one function.** `resolveEntitlementState(status)` (`packages/shared/src/domain/billing/entitlement.ts`, pure, tested): `ACTIVE`/`TRIALING` → `ACTIVE`; `PAST_DUE`/`CANCELED`/`ENDED` → `SUSPENDED`; `null` → `NONE`. `NONE` is **not** suspension (no billing row yet) and callers fail OPEN on it — collapsing the two would lock everyone out when enforcement turns on. Every enforcement point reads this function; never re-derive the status list.

**Suspension is immediate on the first failed charge** (operator decision — a grace window was declined), so `PAST_DUE` suspends. There is no "un-suspend" action: every gate is status-driven, so the next `customer.subscription.updated` reporting `active` reopens them all, and paused work resumes where it stopped.

**What stops while suspended** (each via `QuotaEnforcementService.isSuspended` or the entitlement-aware quota context):
- the quota gate — `resolveSubscriptionContext` returns `limitValue: 0`, so create / publish / import / AO reservations refuse through their existing paths;
- eBay order sync — skipped **before** the `last_ebay_sync_at` watermark is advanced, so orders placed during suspension are re-pulled after payment (`subscription-enforcement.guard.spec.ts` locks the ordering);
- Amazon cost-capture sync — same watermark-preserving early return;
- Amazon tracking scrapes — skipped with the per-order scheduler **kept**, so the next tick after payment resumes (removing it would need a restart to recover);
- auto-fulfill — `AutoFulfillBlockedReason.SUBSCRIPTION_SUSPENDED` (never folded into `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED`: "pay the invoice" and "upgrade the plan" are different screens), re-checked at enqueue AND at execution; `OrderSyncService.resumeSuspendedAutoFulfill` re-queues these once the account is entitled again;
- Aquiline conversion (falls back to pass-through) and buyer messages (parked and re-checked, never dropped);
- the product refresh claim — rule "at least one ACTIVE listing belongs to an entitled owner" (`buildRefreshEntitlementSql`), so a paying seller sharing the ASIN is unaffected; with enforcement off the statement is byte-identical to the unbilled one;
- the manual Amazon-link endpoint (explicit `isSuspended` refusal, `billing.errors.subscriptionSuspendedOrders`).
- Best Sellers browsing (2026-09-29) — every list page is served fully locked (`lockedCount` = the whole page), the same truncation exhaustion produces.

**Four metered dimensions, each counted from a different source — this distinction is load-bearing (Best Sellers joined 2026-09-29, and AO is now a key that is unlimited everywhere):**

| Dimension | Measure | Counted as | Exhaustion |
|---|---|---|---|
| `listings_per_month` | **level** | `COUNT(ACTIVE listings)` + in-flight reservations. No period. | blocks new listings |
| `amazon_orders_per_month` | per-window **flow** | reservations scoped to the open usage period — **unlimited (`-1`) on every plan and the trial since `125`**, so the gate never refuses; the key is KEPT and a finite value still enforces if it ever needs re-tightening | blocks new automatic orders (never, at `-1`) |
| `tracking_conversions_per_month` | per-window **flow** | `COUNT(orders with tracking_converted_at inside the quota window)` — no ledger | **degrades, never blocks** |
| `best_sellers_products_per_month` | per-window **flow** | `SUM(products)` over `best_sellers_views` rows in the quota window (one row per `(user, view_key, UTC day)`, so a page reopened the same day is not recounted) — no reservation | **truncates, never blocks** — `list.items` is cut server-side to what the allowance covers and `lockedCount` says how many rows were withheld |

- **Listings are a level, so ending one frees a slot.** A successful create/publish RELEASES its reservation — the ACTIVE row is the durable entitlement and the reservation only covers the minutes a create is in flight. A consume that left rows `reserved` turned the quota into a ratchet.
- **`BillingRepositoryService.reserveSlot` builds its INSERT column list PER TABLE** (`billing_listing_reservations` has `listing_id`, `billing_ao_reservations` has `ebay_order_id`). A shared column list broke every single-slot reserve (`reserveForPublish`, `reserveAmazonOrder`) with `column "…" does not exist`; mirror `reserveSlotsBulk` if you touch either statement.
- **`billing_usage_periods` is opened lazily on read** (`ensureOpenUsagePeriod`, guarded by the table's unique constraint) — no cron. A period that cannot be opened yields a used-count of **0**, never the unscoped count: bookkeeping trouble must not read as exhaustion.
- **Conversions have no reservation table.** A conversion is one synchronous act with a durable record (`orders.tracking_converted_at`); a failed one writes nothing and costs no quota (the provider is paid only on success).
- **Exhausting the conversion quota does NOT stop the shipment** — eBay gets the raw Amazon number and only supplier concealment stops. Logged at `error`, since nothing else fails.
- Downgrades never disable existing listings; they block new create/publish only (see "Listings over the plan limit"). Quota exhaustion uses shared enums/reasons, never hardcoded strings.

**The manual link path costs NO automatic-order quota (operator decision).** `POST /amazon/orders/:orderId/link-amazon` reserves nothing. **Browser-seconds, queue slots and RAM are capacity, not cost** — governed by `AMAZON_GLOBAL_CONCURRENCY`, worker concurrency and monitoring; a seller-facing quota is for money leaving the business, and the only third-party cost here (Aquiline) has its own meter. Do not reintroduce the reservation: it was idempotent on the eBay order id, so a failed manual link on an auto-fulfilled order released THAT order's legitimately spent slot. Suspension is still refused server-side (a frontend redirect is not a security boundary).

**Quota usage is computed live, never read from `billing_usage_periods.used_qty`** — nothing increments that column. `BillingSummaryDto.quotas` is derived from the same sources the gate refuses on, so a warning and a refusal can never disagree.

**Monthly quota windows follow the Stripe billing period, not the calendar month.** `resolveQuotaWindow` (`quota-helpers.ts`, pure, tested) returns the subscription's `currentPeriodStart`/`currentPeriodEnd`, falling back to `utcMonthBounds` only when there is no subscription or the row is malformed (never an unbounded window); no repository method calls `utcMonthBounds` directly. **`periodStart` never moves without a confirmed payment** — a roll-forward on a stale window would reset the quota with no evidence of payment and is guarded against. A **6-hour grace** (`billing.webhookGraceHours` → `BILLING_WEBHOOK_GRACE_HOURS` → `DEFAULT_WEBHOOK_GRACE_HOURS = 6`) extends only `periodEnd`, so a paying seller is not suspended at their own renewal instant. Past the grace the window resolves to `QuotaWindowOutcome.UNPAID`, and `resolveEffectiveEntitlement` maps that to `SUSPENDED` **at read time** — webhook silence is not evidence of payment. Nothing persists that inferred suspension; the next real `customer.subscription.updated` clears it, and the hourly reconcile / `billing:reconcile` recover a lost webhook.

### The priced meter is tracking conversions, not automatic orders (2026-08-21; AO unlimited since 2026-09-29)

**SUPERSEDED IN PART 2026-09-29 — automatic orders are UNLIMITED (`-1`) on every plan, trial included (migration `125`).** Four reasons, each sufficient: an order costs no third-party money (the priced unit is the conversion, which keeps its own meter); every automatic order is backed by a real eBay sale, so the count cannot be farmed; the old 2×-conversions ceiling hit the best-paying sellers first; and the competitor shows "unlimited". Capacity protection moves ENTIRELY to `AMAZON_GLOBAL_CONCURRENCY` + monitoring (see below). The `amazon_orders_per_month` key is KEPT — a finite value still works if it ever needs re-tightening — and the "AO = 2× conversions" rule from `085` is superseded. The history follows.

Migration `085`. The per-unit cash cost of an order is the Aquiline conversion, not the order — and with carrier scoping (see "Tracking conversion") order count stops predicting cost. So `tracking_conversions_per_month` is the priced meter and AO is an anti-abuse ceiling at **2× the conversion quota** on paid tiers. The trial runs 20/20 (migration `097` doubled only its conversions): its cost ceiling is the 20 conversions either way.

**SUPERSEDED 2026-09-29 — AO was an anti-abuse ceiling on the Playwright pool, never a capacity guarantee; it is gone, and the pool is protected by `AMAZON_GLOBAL_CONCURRENCY` alone.** Every order — converted or not — consumes the platform-wide Playwright pool; polling continues to delivery regardless of conversion (`tracking-webhook-coverage.guard.spec.ts`). That is capacity, not money (see the manual-link rule above), so it is governed by a variable, not a seller-facing quota — and since `085` capped only the conversion exposure, `125` could lift AO without touching it. **Order throughput scales with `AMAZON_GLOBAL_CONCURRENCY`**: at 5 it is ~13M browser-seconds/month (~432k/day, of which the `amazon-order-sync` tick takes ~4.2M/month), i.e. ~20,000–26,000 orders/month; at 20 it is ~4× that. **Unlimited AO moved the production defaults (2026-09-29): `docker-compose.production.yml` defaults `AMAZON_GLOBAL_CONCURRENCY` to 20 (was 5) and `API_MEMORY_LIMIT` to 12G (was 6G)** — each resident Chromium context is ~150–300 MB and the production host has 128 GB; `docker-compose.test.yml` stays at 5 / 4G (a handful of accounts). Protection is monitoring (BullMQ depth on `auto-fulfill`/`amazon-tracking`, API RSS); the lever stays this one variable.

| Plan | Listings | **Tracking conversions** | Automatic orders (since `125`) | AO under `085`–`124` (history) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Trial | 50 | 20 | unlimited | 20 |
| Mini | 100 | 20 | unlimited | — |
| Lite | 200 | 25 | unlimited | 50 |
| Nano | 500 | 50 | unlimited | 100 |
| Micro | 1,000 | 100 | unlimited | 200 |
| Starter | 2,000 | 150 | unlimited | 300 |
| Basic | 3,000 | 200 | unlimited | 400 |
| Plus | 4,000 | 250 | unlimited | 500 |
| Growth | 5,000 | 300 | unlimited | 600 |
| Advanced | 7,500 | 350 | unlimited | 700 |
| Pro | 10,000 | 500 | unlimited | 1,000 |
| Elite | 15,000 | 600 | unlimited | 1,200 |
| Business | 20,000 | 700 | unlimited | 1,400 |
| Enterprise | 25,000 | 800 | unlimited | 1,600 |

Migration `085` derives AO from the conversion row (`= conversions × 2`) rather than multiplying in place — an in-place multiply is **not idempotent** and a replay would silently produce a 4× ceiling. Any future limit change must follow the same rule. Migration `125` writes the literal `-1` (unlimited) to every `amazon_orders_per_month` row, which is idempotent by construction.

### Choosing a plan means two different things (2026-08-21)

**Stripe does NOT refuse a second subscription for an existing subscriber**, so checkout while subscribed would bill for two. The two paths are distinct, and `BillingSummaryDto.hasProviderSubscription` tells the FE which applies (plan-card label "Choose Growth" vs "Switch to Growth"):

- **No Stripe subscription** (local trial or nothing) → `POST /billing/checkout`, a real subscribe.
- **A live Stripe subscription** → `POST /billing/change-plan`, billed by DIRECTION. `resolvePlanChangeDirection` (pure, shared) compares catalog prices; `changePlan` and `previewPlanChange` call it identically so the preview can never disagree with the charge.
  - **Upgrade**: `stripe.subscriptions.update` with `proration_behavior: 'always_invoice'` + `payment_behavior: 'error_if_incomplete'` — the prorated difference is charged now and a declined card fails the whole update, leaving the seller on the plan they paid for. It first releases any pending downgrade schedule (`cancelScheduledChange`, best-effort) so a stale schedule cannot undo a paid upgrade. **Never use `create_prorations`**: Stripe bills in advance, so it handed over the higher quota immediately and billed up to 30 days later — a Lite seller could consume Enterprise quota and let the card fail.
  - **Downgrade**: `provider.scheduleDowngrade` creates or reuses a Subscription Schedule — current price to period end, new price after, no money moves today.
  - A 3-D Secure decline reports `billing.errors.paymentRequiresAction` (the change is rolled back; the fix is the card, not a retry).
- **`metadata.plan_id` moves with the price on every change** — the webhook applier resolves the local plan from it.
- **The plan is written locally as soon as Stripe accepts the reprice** (`BillingService.changePlan` → `repository.updateSubscriptionPlan`), because the FE refetches before the webhook lands. Safe because Stripe already confirmed the exact price; the webhook re-applies the same `plan_id`. Best-effort — a local write failure must not turn a completed upgrade into a seller-visible error.
- **The seller sees the real figure before confirming.** `POST /billing/plan-change/preview` calls `stripe.invoices.createPreview` (Stripe's own arithmetic incl. tax/discounts) and the billing page shows it in a confirm dialog; a downgrade preview short-circuits to `amountDueMicros: 0`. The prorated amount scales with time left in the period.
- **Switching plans never redirects to Stripe** — Checkout would create a second subscription, and the Billing Portal's own plan switcher was declined to keep the seller in-app. "Manage billing" is the Stripe portal (card, invoices, cancel), disabled without a Stripe subscription.

### Subscription integrity: three layers, and a live billing page (2026-08-23)

A local-state check (`hasProviderSubscription`) once let duplicate subscriptions through because local state itself was wrong (the subscription row was never persisted, and the trial row kept outranking the real one). A check against local state cannot be trusted once local state is what broke, so there are three independent layers:

- **A1 — close the trial the moment it's paid.** The `customer.subscription.created` handler calls `repository.endTrialSubscriptionsForUser(customer.userId)` (best-effort — never reject the webhook over it; scoped to `provider_subscription_id IS NULL`, so it cannot touch a paying row).
- **A2 — a provider-backed row always outranks a local trial row.** `findCurrentSubscription` orders by `(provider_subscription_id IS NULL)` before the status `CASE`.
- **A3 — ask Stripe, not our tables.** `createCheckout` calls `provider.hasActiveProviderSubscription`, which lists the customer's Stripe subscriptions and treats `active`/`trialing`/`past_due`/`unpaid`/`paused` as subscribed (`paused` can resume billing). A hit refuses with `billing.errors.alreadySubscribed` → 409 and the FE refetches the summary and routes to plan-change — never auto-retrying checkout. **A lookup failure fails CLOSED**: a refused checkout is recoverable, a duplicate subscription is a refund.

**Customer creation is serialized**: `withUserBillingLock` (Postgres advisory lock per user) wraps `createCheckout` and `createAddonCheckout`, re-reading the local customer INSIDE the lock, so concurrent checkouts cannot mint two Stripe customers and orphan a paid subscription. `subscription-integrity.guard.spec.ts` source-greps A1–A3 and the lock ordering.

**Invoices, the saved card and the next charge are read LIVE from Stripe — no local mirror** (`GET /billing/details`, `GET /billing/invoices` → `provider.getBillingDetails` / `listInvoices`). Two records of the same money is the failure that matters most on a billing page. The card shown is `invoice_settings.default_payment_method`, never the first attached one. Invoice amounts carry the currency Stripe actually charged (`mapStripeInvoice`, `stripe-invoice-mapper.ts`, uppercased — Adaptive Pricing can bill in TRY), and an unpaid invoice reports `amount_due`, not `amount_paid`.

**`GET /billing/summary` makes no Stripe call and must never gain one** — `AppLayout` calls it on every page load (it drives the suspended redirect), so provider latency there would slow the whole app. It reads only local tables; a Stripe outage degrades only the billing page.

**Checkout options:** top-up checkouts set `invoice_creation: { enabled: true }` (a `mode: 'payment'` session creates no invoice otherwise, so the purchase would be missing from invoice history). `allow_promotion_codes` is on the subscription checkout only — coupons (Stripe Dashboard, no local model) are for winning subscribers, not discounting a thin-margin consumable. An hourly idempotency key (`hourlyIdempotencyKey`) guards checkout-session creation and the subscription reprice, but deliberately NOT top-ups, where two purchases in an hour are legitimate. Checkout grants no Stripe-side trial (`trial_period_days`) on top of the local one.

### The first real Stripe subscription (2026-08-22)

Two seam defects surfaced by the first end-to-end sandbox subscribe; both rules stand:
- **`billing_subscriptions.provider_subscription_id` must have a UNIQUE index** (migration `088`) because `upsertSubscriptionByProvider` uses `ON CONFLICT (provider_subscription_id) WHERE provider_subscription_id IS NOT NULL`. With a plain index every `customer.subscription.created` webhook failed and the seller paid for nothing.
- **Stripe return URLs have no locale segment.** `success_url` / `cancel_url` / portal `return_url` are `${FRONTEND_URL}/billing?...` (`billing-provider.ts`), and `App.tsx` needs a `/billing` → `LocaleRedirect` entry with `preserveQuery` (to carry `checkout=success&session_id=…` / `topup=…`), otherwise `billing` is read as the `:locale` param. **Any new backend-built URL pointing at a seller page needs the same redirect.**

**The billing page is ONE plan card, and the plans live in a DRAWER.**
- The card shows name + status badge, one muted meta line, then the quota block under a divider. The meta line is assembled in the container by status (`trialEnds` / `trialEnded` / `nextRenewal` / `accessEnds`; `TRIAL_PLAN_SLUG` from shared decides trial vs paid) — a trial has no interval and does not renew.
- The twelve plans open in a `Drawer` from the card's one control, which also hosts the portal link; **that control is never disabled** (the seller without a subscription is the one who most needs it).
- A notice renders only for states the seller can act on — no "your subscription is active" alert.
- Quota renders as `ProgressRing`s (`packages/ui`, mirroring `ProgressBar` variants), one per row.
- **Both used and limit come from `summary.quotas`**, never the limit from the catalog plan: `quotas[].limitValue` is the EFFECTIVE ceiling (plan + credits) the gate enforces.

### Billing reliability: the four things that were missing (2026-09-17)

Each of these failed silently; all are closed.

- **Hourly subscription reconcile.** Webhook silence (wrong `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, disabled endpoint, Stripe incident) would make every renewing window stale past the grace and suspend everyone. `SubscriptionReconcileProcessor` (`billing-subscription-reconcile`, hourly) re-reads from Stripe **only** (a) provider-backed subscriptions at/past period end or already PAST_DUE, and (b) recent checkouts with no subscription row yet (see "Trial → paid"). It writes through the webhook's own mapper and repository writer (`subscription-reconcile.ts`: `buildReconcileEvent`, `resolveReconcilePlanId`, `applyProviderSubscription`; the CLI helper re-exports them). A healthy mid-period subscription costs no Stripe call. It runs with `allowPeriodRewind = false` — an unattended sweep may only move a period forward.
- **The Stripe API version is pinned**: `STRIPE_API_VERSION` (`2026-07-29.dahlia`, `billing-provider.ts`) is passed explicitly, so an SDK bump cannot reshape parsed payloads (this once moved `current_period_start` onto the SubscriptionItem and silently synthesised 30-day windows). Raising it is a deliberate migration. `maxNetworkRetries: 2` lets the SDK replay timeouts under its own idempotency key.
- **Catalog price drift is detected** — see "Catalog mirroring" above.
- **Importing existing eBay listings is quota-gated.** `ListingImportService` reserves through `reserveForBulkCreate` (after the job rows exist, since reservations key on `listing_job_item` ids), deletes the unstarted job when refused, consumes on success, releases on terminal failure; the endpoint returns a 409 with a reason like create/publish.

**No tax/VAT note in the in-app plan list, by operator decision** — tax treatment differs by country.

**Three billing e-mails exist, and only three** (EN+TR): `billing_payment_failed` and `billing_trial_ending` (migration `107`), `billing_price_change` (migration `109`, see "Trial → paid"). A failed payment stops automation while the seller is away, and an ending trial is the one moment they have no reason to log in; everything else is actionless (Stripe's own receipts) or already visible in the Action Center. The payment e-mail fires from `BillingWebhookProcessor` on `invoice.payment_failed` AFTER the state write and fully swallowed — a mail failure must never make Stripe redeliver an applied event. The trial reminder rides the hourly `billing-trial-expiry` tick and is claimed by the selecting query (`claimTrialsEndingSoon` stamps `trial_reminder_sent_at` in the same statement), so a failed send costs one reminder rather than risking a duplicate; it skips anyone who already has a live provider-backed subscription. Senders are optional narrow ports (`BillingNotificationSender` / `TrialReminderSender`) so processors construct without the mail stack.

**Stripe Dashboard settings are not in this repository and several change app behavior** — above all "after all retries fail" must be *cancel* or *mark unpaid*, never *pause collection* (a paused subscription keeps `status: active`, nothing reads `pause_collection`, and automation would run unpaid). Work through [docs/stripe-go-live-checklist.md](docs/stripe-go-live-checklist.md) when switching to live keys.

### Trial → paid, price changes, disputes (2026-09-17)

**A first subscription does not depend on its webhook alone.** Two independent recoveries, both writing through `applyProviderSubscription` (subscription first, trial close second):
- **The checkout return page confirms the session** — `POST /billing/checkout/confirm { sessionId }`, called once by `BillingPage.container` on `/billing?checkout=success&session_id=…` (params removed afterwards). **Ownership is verified** because the id comes from a browser-controlled URL: the session's `client_reference_id` must be this user AND its customer this user's Stripe customer, else nothing is written. Failures are silent — a successful payment must never produce an error dialog.
- **The hourly reconcile covers the tab-closed case**: `createCheckout` stamps `billing_customers.updated_at`, and `listRecentCheckoutsWithoutSubscription` finds Stripe-linked customers who opened a checkout in the last 72h (Stripe's webhook retry window) with no provider-backed row. An abandoned checkout writes nothing.

**A trial's still-`reserved` quota slots are released when the trial ends** (`endTrialSubscriptionsForUser` / `expireElapsedTrials`) — reservations are released against the CURRENT subscription, so trial-era slots otherwise stayed `reserved` for ever.

**Changing a plan's price moves existing subscribers automatically from their next renewal** (operator decision): a seller finishes the current period at the price paid, and the next period starts at the new price. **There is no operator step** — shipping the price change as a migration (close the `billing_plan_prices` row, insert the new one) is all it takes. Do not add a manual migrate-price command; one was rejected.
- **`PriceMigrationProcessor` (`billing-price-migration`, hourly)** first runs `syncStripeCatalog` (so the new row gets its Stripe Price), then reads only subscriptions not yet in line with their plan's current price (migration `110`: `price_evaluated_for_price_id` / `price_change_scheduled_price_id` / `price_change_notified_price_id`); an in-line subscriber costs no Stripe call. Hourly because the schedule must exist before the renewal.
- **The candidate query joins on `pp.interval::text = s.interval::text` — both casts are load-bearing.** `billing_plan_prices.interval` and `billing_subscriptions.interval` are two different enum types with the same labels, and a bare comparison is a Postgres type error (`42883`). It shipped bare and failed on every hourly run until 2026-09-30, so NO subscriber had ever been moved to a new price — and nothing noticed, because the specs run against fakes and a failed BullMQ job logs only the database error (the symptom was `billing-price-migration` showing 24 failed jobs on `/admin` → Queues). `price-migration-sql.guard.spec.ts` refuses a bare interval comparison anywhere in the billing module. **A SQL statement is not verified until a real Postgres has parsed it** — `PREPARE` it against the local database.
- `decidePriceMigration` (pure, tested) chooses **MIGRATE** (schedule the new price from period end via `scheduleDowngrade`, labelled `metadata.source = price_migration`), **ALREADY_SCHEDULED**, **ALREADY_ON_TARGET**, **SKIP_PENDING_CHANGE** (the seller's own pending change — source `plan_change`, or unlabelled — is never overwritten; re-checked until it lands), **SKIP_CANCELLING**, **SKIP_NOT_LIVE**. Our own schedule for an older price is replaced.
- **"Pending" means a schedule phase starting in the FUTURE** — Stripe leaves a schedule attached after its last phase begins, so "has a schedule" would skip everyone who ever downgraded.
- **The `billing_price_change` e-mail goes out only after Stripe accepts the schedule** (old price, new price, effective date). A failed send is retried next run; a sent one is never repeated; no notice is sent for a renewal already in the past.
- **The billing page never shows a price migration as a "scheduled change"** (`getBillingDetails` ignores `price_migration` schedules). Every schedule we write carries `metadata.source` (`ScheduleSource`), overwritten on each write.
- `price-migration.processor.spec.ts` asserts against fakes: labelled schedule carrying the REAL plan id (the renewal webhook resolves the plan from it), no notice before the schedule exists, failed notices retried, the seller's own pending change untouched.

**Disputes stay manual, by operator decision.** `charge.dispute.*` is recorded and logged at `error`; the account is NOT auto-suspended (a dispute may be a bank error or winnable). Revisit only if disputes start happening.

### eBay stores are keyed on eBay's immutable user id (migration `108`, 2026-09-17)

`ebay_accounts.seller_id` — and therefore the `(seller_id, marketplace_id)` unique key and the `ebay_trial_ledger` — holds eBay's **immutable `userId`**, never the changeable username (a rename allowed a second free trial and duplicated store rows).
- `getSellerInfo` returns `sellerId = identity.userId` only and **fails closed** (`ebay.errors.identityUnavailable`). Never add fallbacks (JWT `sub`, `privilege`, `'unknown'`) — a shared fallback id made one seller's trial claim block everyone else's.
- The username is display-only: `ebay_accounts.ebay_username` (refreshed on every reconnect) and `EbayAccountPublicDto.ebayUsername`. Store labels prefer `storeName || ebayUsername || sellerId`; `store_name` never falls back to the opaque id, and the buyer-message `{{store_name}}` query uses `COALESCE(NULLIF(store_name,''), ebay_username)` — the id must not reach a buyer.
- **No username-based adoption of pre-108 rows, deliberately** — a released username can be re-registered by a stranger. No SQL backfill exists (the id needs the seller's token).
- Every FK into `ebay_accounts` references its UUID `id`, never `seller_id`.
- Known edge: the ledger key includes `marketplace_id`, so one eBay account on a second marketplace would get a new trial. Revisit when a second marketplace is enabled.
- `ebay-identity.guard.spec.ts` locks all of the above.

### Listings over the plan limit are not automated (migration `106`, 2026-09-17)

A seller can hold more ACTIVE listings than their plan allows (a downgrade never ends listings). The rule (operator decision): **the OLDEST listings up to the effective listing limit stay tracked; newer ones are left alone.** Suspension is a separate, broader stop.

| Automation | Listing over the limit |
|---|---|
| Product refresh claim | not refreshed (`AND l.over_plan_limit = FALSE`, only with enforcement on) |
| Price/stock fan-out (`ProductSyncService.computePendingUpdates`) | skipped — eBay keeps what it last had |
| Order ingest | still ingested and visible |
| Auto-fulfill | `SKIPPED` + `AutoFulfillBlockedReason.LISTING_OVER_PLAN_LIMIT` — **not** `BLOCKED`, so no action-required alarm |
| Shipment tracking / tracking push / conversion | none; on-demand conversion refused with `orders.errors.listingOverPlanLimit` |

- **`listings.over_plan_limit` is a stored flag recomputed by `ListingPlanLimitProcessor`** (`billing-listing-plan-limit`, every 10 min), not a live rank in the per-minute refresh claim, and not recomputed at each call site (a missed site would leave flags wrong; a reconcile converges). Cost: ≤10 min lag.
- **Ranking is `ORDER BY created_at ASC NULLS FIRST, id ASC`** (`BillingRepositoryService.applyListingPlanLimit`); the `id` tie-break is load-bearing (bulk creates share a timestamp). Only changed flags are written; ending an old listing promotes the next.
- **`resolveListingPlanLimit` deliberately ignores suspension** — using the gate's limit 0 would flag every listing and outlive the suspension. `decideListingPlanLimitAction` (pure, tested) picks NONE / CLEAR / RANK; enforcement off clears all flags.
- **The order carries its own flag, `orders.listing_over_plan_limit`, decided ONCE at first ingest** (INSERT only, never in `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`), so an in-flight order never flips when a downgrade lands. The tracking processor checks it first and removes the scheduler; `reconcileSchedulers` excludes flagged orders.
- **The downgrade confirm dialog warns** when active listings exceed the target plan's limit (`billing.planChange.listingLimitWarning`), using `summary.quotas` for "active" and reading the target limit directly (not via `planLimitValue`, which maps a missing limit to 0).
- `listing-plan-limit.guard.spec.ts` source-greps every read site plus the ranking order — a forgotten predicate fails silently.

### A refused create leaves nothing behind (2026-08-21)

`reserveForBulkCreate` keys reservations on `listing_job_items` ids, so the job rows must exist before the gate runs.
- On refusal, `ListingsService.deleteUnstartedJob` rolls the job back — narrowly: `status = pending` **and** no item in a non-draft state, so it can never discard real work. Best-effort; it never masks the refusal.
- **The refusal is a 409 with a reason, not a 500.** The gate throws plain `Error`s with a `name` (the queue worker classifies by it via `classifyListingFailure`); `LISTING_REFUSAL_KEYS` in `listings.controller.ts` maps them at the HTTP boundary.
- **Suspension and a full quota are separate errors** (`SubscriptionSuspendedError` vs `QuotaExhaustedError`) — "50 in use (limit 0)" is wrong text and the wrong fix for an expired trial. Both classify to `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED` on a job item.

**A suspended account is redirected to `/billing`** (`AppLayout`, `SUSPENDED_ALLOWED_PATHS = ['/billing']`, only when `enforcementEnabled`). `/settings` is deliberately not allowed (operator decision) — it hosts connecting eBay and Amazon accounts.

### Quota top-ups (migrations `087`, `113`, `125`)

**Two meters are sold as top-ups — tracking conversions and Best Sellers products (the latter since `125`, 2026-09-29).** Listings are a LEVEL (more is a plan upgrade); automatic orders are unlimited since `125` (before that they were an anti-abuse guard on the Playwright pool, which money cannot buy more of) — either way nothing to sell; conversions are the priced meter with a real linear unit cost ($0.14); Best Sellers products are a monthly flow with a near-zero unit cost (a page is ~4 proxy requests, well under a tenth of a cent) whose allowance meters value delivered rather than our spend, so a pack is almost pure margin and cannot strain anything — the hidden daily cache-miss brake, not the allowance, protects the proxy pool. A new product line is a row in `billing_quota_addons`, never a new table — but it needs the same argument first.
