- **Credits are scoped to ONE quota window** (keyed on `window.periodStart`, the billing period — same value `grantQuotaCredit` stamps) and raise the ceiling; they are never decremented. `resolveEffectiveLimit` = plan limit + credits for the current window. A credit that outlived its window would need a second consumption ledger, and those drift. **Packs are offered ONLY to a seller who has reached the limit** (the summary returns `quotaAddons` only then), so nobody buys an allowance they cannot use.
- **`resolveEffectiveLimit` lives in the REPOSITORY, and that placement is load-bearing** — both the gate and the summary call it, so a seller who just topped up can never see headroom and still be refused. A failed credit lookup falls back to the PLAN limit, never unlimited.
- **This is the ONE `checkout.session.completed` we act on** (a one-time purchase creates no Subscription). It carries `metadata.addon_slug` and `client_reference_id` (user id); the handler returns early unless `mode === 'payment'` and requires `payment_status === 'paid'` (an async method may still be settling).
- **Granting is idempotent on the Stripe event id** (`billing_quota_credits.provider_event_id UNIQUE`), in addition to the inbox dedupe.
- Packs need Stripe **one-time** prices (`prices.create` WITHOUT `recurring`), minted by `syncStripeCatalog` (hourly or via the CLI). Until then `isPurchasable` is false and the FE shows the pack disabled.
- **Best Sellers packs (migration `125`):** `best-sellers-2500` $4.99, `best-sellers-10000` $14.99, `best-sellers-25000` $29.99 — credits on `best_sellers_products_per_month` through the same `checkout.session.completed` grant and `resolveEffectiveLimit` sum, offered only once the allowance is reached (the page shows the prompt beside its blurred locked rows). The addon Stripe Product name/description is chosen per dimension (`describeAddonProduct`, `stripe-catalog-sync.ts`).
- Seeded conversion packs: 50 / 100 / 250 / 500 conversions at $9.99 / $19.99 / $44.99 / $79.99 (the 50 pack is migration `113`). Margins after Stripe fees are ~49/46/41/34% at the modelled $0.10 unit cost, but ~31/26/19/9% at the Aquiline Starter plan's actual $0.14 — the 500 pack is nearly margin-free until a larger Aquiline tier lowers the unit cost. Prices left as-is by operator decision (2026-09-19); revisit when the Aquiline plan changes.

### Admin billing metrics

`GET /v1/admin/billing/metrics` (admin Billing tab, standard admin guard chain) reports: account-status distribution (from `users.status`), access-tier distribution (from `users.role`), per-user quota-pressure bands for **listings** and **Amazon buyer accounts**, and the period's total estimated cost from `usage_events` (nullable micro-USD — null when no cost rows, never faked as 0). It does not read the billing plan/subscription tables. Bands use soft thresholds that are panel settings (`ADMIN_LISTING_QUOTA_WARN_THRESHOLD` 25, `ADMIN_LISTING_QUOTA_CRITICAL_THRESHOLD` 100, `ADMIN_AMAZON_ACCOUNT_QUOTA_WARN_THRESHOLD` 3, `ADMIN_AMAZON_ACCOUNT_QUOTA_CRITICAL_THRESHOLD` 10) — not plan limits. Each section fails independently. Pure helpers in `billing-metrics.helpers.ts` (tested).

### Two operator CLIs (no HTTP path, like `user:set-role`)

Both load `apps/api/.env` for `DATABASE_URL` and construct only the repository classes they need — no NestJS bootstrap, so no processor, scheduler or boot-time migration runs as a side effect. Exit codes follow `user-set-role.ts` (`0` done/dry-run, `2` bad args, `3` no such user, `1` refused or DB failure). (`pnpm --filter api stripe:sync-catalog` is the third billing CLI — see "Catalog mirroring".)

- **`pnpm --filter api reset-users`** (`src/scripts/reset-users.ts`) wipes user accounts from a NON-production database to re-run signup → trial → onboarding. **Dry run by default** (prints row counts); `--yes` deletes, `--email <e>` scopes to one user, `--products` also clears the shared ASIN cache. It **refuses `NODE_ENV=production`** and a non-local `DATABASE_URL` unless `--allow-remote`, prints the target first, and runs as one transaction. The delete plan is a frozen list in `reset-users-helpers.ts`, asserted against a protected-table list so catalog/config data (plans, templates, `platform_settings`, learned eBay taxonomy) can never be caught. It explicitly deletes `ebay_trial_ledger`, `billing_customers` and `aquiline_profiles`, which are deliberately not FK-cascaded from `users` (the ledger must survive account deletion to stop trial farming; the Aquiline profile is permanent provider-side).
- **`pnpm --filter api billing:reconcile -- --email a@b.c`** (`src/scripts/billing-reconcile.ts`, `--dry-run` to preview) re-reads a customer's live subscription from Stripe and re-applies it through the **same** `extractStripeSubscriptionFields` + `BillingRepositoryService.upsertSubscriptionByProvider` the webhook uses — never its own SQL (two writers of that row drift). It writes the subscription BEFORE closing trial rows (the reverse order could leave an account suspended with nothing to retry) and runs with `allowPeriodRewind = true`, the one place a period may move backwards to repair a wrongly stamped start. It re-enqueues nothing: auto-fulfill resumes via `resumeSuspendedAutoFulfill` in `OrderSyncService.syncOrdersForAccount` on the next order-sync tick.
## Order Management

### Data Flow
```
eBay Order → Order Sync (BullMQ, every 20 min) → orders table
  ↕ (matched via lineItem.legacyItemId → listings.ebay_item_id)
orders.listing_id → listings → products (title, image, ASIN via JOIN)
  ↓ (NEW order only, xmax-detected insert)
Sale-Driven Stock Sync → products.stock decremented → stock-sync queue → recompute + push eBay
  ↓ (automatic, every 3 h per Amazon account — AMAZON_ORDER_SYNC_CRON)
Amazon Order Auto Cost-Capture (amazon-order-sync queue) → scrape account order list
  → strict-match ASIN+qty+amount+date to pending/provisional eBay orders → write real costs → LINKED
  ↓ (or user manually links an Amazon Order ID)
Amazon Account + Amazon Order ID → Playwright scraping → order costs updated → recomputeProfit
  ↓ (automatic, when store_settings.auto_fulfill_enabled + per-account enable+cap; A2)
Automated Amazon Fulfillment (`auto-fulfill` queue) → Playwright checkout on round-robin buyer account
  → review-step hard cap → place order → atomic write real costs + LINKED + schedule tracking
  ↓ (automatic)
Amazon Order Tracking (BullMQ, per-order scheduler) → Amazon status polling → tracking conversion → eBay status sync
```

### Order → Listing → Product Relationship
- **Matching**: eBay order's `lineItems[0].legacyItemId` matched against `listings.ebay_item_id` during sync
- **Product data** (title, ASIN, image, SKU): Resolved via `orders.listing_id → listings → products` JOIN in `findOne()`
- **Purchase price**: Stored in orders as snapshot (product prices change, order needs historical price)
- **Order table** only stores: eBay order data, buyer info, financials, shipping address
- **Columns**: `order_date` (eBay creationDate), `last_ebay_event_at` (eBay lastModifiedDate), `updated_at` (our updates), `last_synced_at` (sync tracking)

### Net Profit Formula
```
netProfit = ebayEarnings - purchasePrice - amazonTax - amazonShipping         (confirmed/linked)
netProfit = ebayEarnings - purchasePrice - (purchasePrice × amazonTaxRate/100) (provisional estimate)
```
- `ebayEarnings` = eBay's `totalDueSeller`, which is **net of eBay's marketplace fee** (verified 2026-09-10 on a real order: `total` 113.80 − `totalMarketplaceFee` 7.81 = `totalDueSeller` 105.99) and **excludes Collect & Remit sales tax** (verified 2026-09-20: item $11.47 − fee $1.84 = $9.63, the $1.06 tax never entered it). The formula needs no tax adjustment. Do not re-litigate either point.
- `transactionFee` and `adFee` are **estimates** stored for display only, NOT deducted again: `recomputeProfit` derives them from the seller's Listing Settings Group `ebayFeePercent` / `fixedFeeAmount` (a pricing input, not what eBay charged). The dashboard's fee rows still sum these estimates.
- **eBay's real fee figures (migration `098`)**: `orders.ebay_marketplace_fee` / `ebay_fee_basis_amount` / `ebay_collect_remit_tax`, mapped by `ebay-order-financials.ts` (pure + Jest-covered) from the `getOrders` payload we already download (zero extra API calls). The order detail page shows them, falling back to the estimate. No DEFAULT on purpose: NULL = eBay reported nothing, `0` = eBay reported zero.
- **Collect & Remit `sale_tax`/`sale_total` fix (2026-09-20)**: for C&R orders `pricingSummary.tax` reads `0` (and `pricingSummary.total` inherits the gap); the real tax is only on each line item's `ebayCollectAndRemitTaxes`. `EbayFulfillmentService.mapEbayOrderToEntity` falls back to `sumCollectAndRemitTax(lineItems)` when `pricingSummary.tax` is 0 and corrects `saleTotal` by the same delta, so dashboard revenue/tax aggregates are right at the source. Orders synced before the fix keep their wrong stored value until a dedicated backfill is written.
- **An order is re-read whenever eBay modifies it (2026-09-30).** Sync filters on `lastmodifieddate:[last_ebay_sync_at..]`, so a late fee, a payment, a shipment, a cancellation or a refund made on eBay (or by another tool) reaches us — see "Order change tracking" below. `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` uses `COALESCE(EXCLUDED.x, orders.x)` for every captured figure: a later read that lacks a field never blanks it.
- **eBay Finances API is the eventual source for what `getOrders` cannot give** (after-the-fact refunds, separately billed ad spend, payouts, disputes) — not needed for per-order fees. Its ceiling is **15,000 calls/day** (6.6× tighter than Fulfillment's 100,000) and `getTransactions` costs `accounts × ticks/day`, so it must run on a ~**4-hour** tick (500 accounts: 3,000/day), never the 20-minute order-sync cadence (36,000/day).
- **`net_profit` is NULLABLE** (migration `033`): `NULL` = unknown cost (never faked); `0` = a computed real zero. Aggregations `FILTER` out NULL rather than treating it as 0.
- **`cost_capture_status` enum** (`packages/shared` `OrderCostCaptureStatus`, DB type `order_cost_capture_status`) drives profit confidence:
  - `pending` — ingested, nothing captured, product cost unknown.
  - `linked` — Amazon costs scraped from a real Amazon order. **TRUSTED**: the only status that produces a headline `net_profit`.
  - `provisional` — listing matched so product cost is known, Amazon tax+shipping not yet captured.
  - `failed` — Amazon scrape returned no usable financials; prior values retained, `net_profit` stays NULL.
  - `untracked` — no listing match (`listing_id IS NULL`); cost can never be resolved (eBay's payload carries no ASIN). Stays NULL forever.
- **Provisional estimate**: `recomputeProfit` uses the pure `estimateProvisionalNetProfit({ ebayEarnings, purchasePrice, amazonTaxRatePct })` (`profit-calculation.ts`) with the product's last-known Amazon price and the per-user global `amazonTaxRate` store setting (migration `035`, default `0`, resolved via `StoreSettingsService.getResolvedSettings(userId, null)`; any settings failure falls back to `0%`). Amazon shipping is **not** estimated (disclosed in UI).
- **`profitBasis`** (`'confirmed' | 'estimated' | null`) on `OrderDto` + dashboard rows, via `deriveProfitBasis` (`linked→confirmed`, `provisional→estimated`, else `null`).
- **Single writer**: `OrderSyncService.recomputeProfit(ebayOrderId, { scrapeFailed? })` is the only method that sets `cost_capture_status`. Called on insert, on an `ebay_earnings` change during re-sync, and after every Amazon link / cost-capture path.
  - **Its `purchase_price` write is `COALESCE(NULLIF($4::numeric, 0), …)` — the cast is load-bearing.** A bare `NULLIF($4, 0)` makes Postgres type the parameter as INTEGER from the literal, and every real price fails (`invalid input syntax for type integer: "13.91"`, the first live order, 2026-09-29). The method only logs the failure, so the order silently stays `pending` with `net_profit` NULL. `recompute-profit-sql.guard.spec.ts` refuses a bare parameter next to a `0` literal; write `$n::numeric` whenever a parameter meets an integer literal.
- **Dashboard profit tiers** (`dashboard.service.ts`): `confirmed` = `SUM(net_profit)` over live `linked` rows — the headline; `provisional` = same over `provisional`, shown separately as "Estimated", never blended into the headline; `uncosted` = `SUM(sale_total)` over `pending|failed` — revenue only, no profit implied. **`untracked` orders are outside every dashboard figure** (next bullet).
- **The dashboard is scoped to TRACKED orders (`listing_id IS NOT NULL`), 2026-09-29 (operator decision).** The dashboard describes the business SellerHill manages, not the whole eBay store. An untracked order has no product cost and never can (eBay's payload carries no ASIN), so admitting it inflated Sales/Payout with revenue nothing could be earned on and — because its `purchase_price` is 0 — counted its whole payout as gross profit and ROI. The scope lives in ONE fragment, `periodSelect`'s `live` filter (shared by the period cards, the chart and the P&L); the dashboard's `OrderCarousel` sends `isTracked: true` and its "view all" link carries `tracking=tracked`; the demo's `aggregate` mirrors it. The one figure untracked orders contribute is `ordersUntracked`, the COUNT the period card renders as a neutral "N untracked orders excluded" chip (`dashboard.profit.untrackedExcluded*`) so the Orders page and the dashboard never look like they disagree. They stay visible on the Orders page ("Not tracked" filter) and in the Action Center's `ORDER_UNTRACKED` item, which is the path to importing the listing — after which `adoptUntrackedOrdersForListing` links them and they enter the dashboard as `provisional`. `dashboard-tracked-scope.guard.spec.ts` locks the fragment and the carousel filter.

### Order Sync (eBay → Local)
- **Queue**: `order-sync` (BullMQ), cron `*/20 * * * *`, one job per user, concurrency 3.
- **Manual refresh**: `POST /orders/sync` exists for operator/debug use only. **Do not wire a seller-facing Refresh button to it** — the Fulfillment API is metered per APPLICATION (20-min cron ≈ `72 × accounts` calls/day, ~36% of the 100,000 ceiling at 500 accounts), and exhausting it stops order sync for every seller.
- **Budget-governed**: `EbayFulfillmentService.fetchOrders` / `fetchOrderById` acquire `EbayApiResource.FULFILLMENT` from `EbayCallBudgetService` before every call; `EbayBudgetExhaustedError` defers that user's sync to the next tick. **Known gap**: `createShippingFulfillment` (the tracking push) still bypasses the governor.
- **Window**: `ebay_accounts.last_ebay_sync_at` — each tick fetches the orders MODIFIED since the last one (`lastmodifieddate`, never `creationdate`), paged by eBay's documented `limit` (200) + zero-based `offset` against the reported `total`. The watermark written afterwards is the run's START minus a 5-minute overlap (`ORDER_SYNC_OVERLAP_MS`), never "now": an order modified while its page was in flight would otherwise fall between two windows. See "Order change tracking".
- **Idempotency**: `upsertOrder()` uses `RETURNING id, (xmax = 0) AS inserted`. The sold counter fires on a genuine insert; the side effects that ACT on a sale (stock decrement, auto-fulfill, the `order_received` message) fire only for a **fresh sale** — inserted AND created after the store was connected AND within `FRESH_SALE_WINDOW_DAYS` (7) — via `decideIngest` (`ebay-order-changes.ts`). `inserted` alone stopped being enough once sync re-reads by modification date: it can surface an order we missed weeks ago, and buying it (or thanking the buyer) then would be acting on stale news.
- **A new order row is inserted with its OWN starting `auto_fulfill_status`, never the column default (2026-10-01, migration `133`).** `pending` means "a purchase job is queued" and reads as the stage `buying`. The column default (`038`) handed it to every row, so a sale the platform never queues — no SellerHill listing behind it, or first seen long after it was placed — said "buying on Amazon" for ever with no job anywhere (15 such rows on the first production store, three of them unshipped). `initialAutoFulfillStatus` (`ebay-order-changes.ts`, pure + tested) returns `pending` only when the purchase gate below is about to run for the row and `skipped` (no reason) otherwise; it is bound as `$39::auto_fulfill_status` in the INSERT and is absent from `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`. The column default is now `skipped`. If the enqueue itself throws, `failStrandedAutoFulfill` writes FAILED (scoped to a still-PENDING row with no click stamp) so the seller can restart it instead of watching "buying". `ebay-order-changes.spec.ts` source-greps all three. Consequence: an untracked, unshipped sale now reads `to_purchase`, which is what it is.
- **Auto-fulfill runs ONLY on a paid, unshipped order.** Without this a seller returning after a lapse would have their whole settled backlog re-purchased on Amazon in one tick.
  - `resolveAutoFulfillEligibility` (`auto-fulfill-helpers.ts`, pure + tested) is the single gate: only `WAITING_SHIPMENT` passes; `SHIPPED`/`COMPLETED`/`PROCESSING` → `AutoFulfillBlockedReason.ORDER_ALREADY_FULFILLED`; anything else → `ORDER_NOT_PAID`. Both are written as **SKIPPED**, not BLOCKED. Checked **before** the plan-limit branch.
  - **`PROCESSING` counts as fulfilled** — eBay's `IN_PROGRESS` means at least one line item shipped, and the platform only reads `lineItems[0]`.
  - **The unpaid skip is reversible** (the re-sync does see the payment, but automation starts only on a first insert, so this sweep is still what releases the order): `releaseOrdersAwaitingPayment` re-checks via `fetchOrderById` and releases them once paid. The bound is **per order**: `auto_fulfill_attempted_at` is stamped before the eBay call and gates re-checks to once an hour (a per-tick limit alone would scale to ~720k calls/day). Candidates are limited to a 7-day window and exclude any order with an `amazon_order_id`.
- **`order_received` buyer message follows the same rule** (a settled backlog must not get "thanks, we're getting it ready" weeks later). Both read one predicate, `isOrderAlreadyFulfilled` (`packages/shared/src/domain/orders/fulfillment-state.ts`). An UNPAID order is still messaged.
- **eBay's "no such offer" reply retires the listing.** The price/stock fan-out already writes to every active listing; eBay's absent-entity answer is now acted on instead of discarded.
  - `ended-listing.ts` (`apps/api/src/modules/listings/`, pure + tested) branches on eBay's **numeric error ids**, never message prose. `BulkPriceQuantityResult.errorIds` (via `extractBulkErrorIds`) carries them; `describeBulkErrors` stays the operator string.
  - **Only ids meaning ABSENT match** (`25701`, `25702`, `25710`, `25713`, `25725`). "Present but refused" codes — `25019`, `25097`/`25098`, `25026`, `25003`/`25004` — are deliberately excluded: each describes a live listing, and retiring it would silently stop repricing.
  - **An empty id list is never an ending** — a transport failure reports failures with no ids, and must not retire a whole catalogue.
  - Writes `ListingStatus.INACTIVE` (no new `ENDED` value), scoped to `status = ACTIVE`, so the Keepa claim stops spending on it.
- **Tracking-at-ingest invariant**: every eBay-account order is ingested and visible, but `listing_id` is assigned only at FIRST ingest, when the eBay Item ID matches one of the seller's listings — **whatever the listing's status** (operator decision, 2026-10-01: a buyer who paid for an item whose listing ended a minute later is still owed it, and the old `status = ACTIVE` filter left that order untracked for ever; `ebay_item_id` is UNIQUE and a draft has none, so the match is still at most one row). Stock sync and auto-fulfill require that initial match. `ON CONFLICT` never backfills `listing_id`, so importing a listing later can never retroactively auto-purchase an older order. `order-tracking-invariant.guard.spec.ts` locks all three gates.
- **A listing IMPORT adopts that item's past orders (migration `111`).** `orders.ebay_legacy_item_id` records the eBay item even when nothing matched; `OrderSyncService.adoptUntrackedOrdersForListing` links those orders when the existing-listing import creates the listing row.
  - **Linking is not buying**: adoption never enqueues auto-fulfill (purchasing fires only on a genuine INSERT). `order-adoption.guard.spec.ts` asserts that, `listing_id IS NULL` (a matched order is never moved), seller/store scoping, and that profit goes through `recomputeProfit`.
  - Adopted orders become `provisional`, not `linked`. Orders ingested before `111` carry no item id and cannot be adopted.
- **Re-sync cost recompute**: an `ebay_earnings` change > $0.001 re-invokes `recomputeProfit`; `cost_capture_status` is preserved/advanced, never reset to `pending`.
- **`listings.sold_count` is incremented in real time** on the genuine-insert gate (`inserted && listingId && quantity > 0`), in its own try/catch so it can never block stock sync or auto-fulfill. Watcher/view counts do not exist (columns dropped by migration `095`) — eBay only exposes them via Trading `GetItem`, which this platform does not use.

### Order change tracking — cancellations and refunds (2026-09-30)

Until this date an order was fetched exactly once (creation-date windows), so a store's real history looked wrong here: the first production store had one cancelled and two fully refunded orders the platform showed as ordinary open sales. Everything below follows the local eBay documents (`docs/ebay-reference/sell-fulfillment-v1-oas3.json`, README section "Order, cancellation, refund and return facts") — no field or enum value is assumed.

- **Filter and paging are eBay's documented ones.** `getOrders` with `filter=lastmodifieddate:[ts..]` — and ONLY that criterion, because "if creationdate and lastmodifieddate are both included, only creationdate is used". Paging is `limit` (max 200; a larger value fails the call) + zero-based `offset`; there is no continuation token (the old code passed the response's `next` URL as one, which would have looped on a window larger than a page). `ORDER_SYNC_MAX_PAGES` (50) is a runaway guard only. Same quota as before: one call per store per tick unless more than 200 orders changed.
- **The status merges forward-only** — `mergeSyncedOrderStatus` and its generated SQL twin `buildSyncedStatusSql` (one rank table, compared on every status pair by `ebay-order-changes.spec.ts`). eBay's view is coarser than ours (it never reports "delivered", and it keeps saying NOT_STARTED + PAID on an order we already marked shipped), so a re-read may only move an order FORWARD; a cancellation wins over everything and a cancelled order stays cancelled. **Never write a bare `status = EXCLUDED.status` in the upsert again** — it would rewind `shipped`/`completed` on every re-read and re-trigger the shipped transition.
- **A cancellation is read from `cancelStatus.cancelledDate`** (documented: "the date and time the order was cancelled, if applicable"), with the observed terminal `cancelState = CANCELED` as a second signal. A cancel REQUEST (`cancelState` anything else) is not a cancellation. `CancelStateEnum`'s value page could not be obtained, so nothing depends on an enum list. Stored in `orders.ebay_cancel_state` / `ebay_cancelled_at`; the order's `status` becomes `cancelled` and its stage `cancelled`.
- **Refunds are read from `paymentSummary.refunds[]`** — only entries carrying a `refundDate` ("not returned until the refund has been issued"), summed into `orders.ebay_refunded_amount` with the latest date in `ebay_refunded_at`. NULL = eBay reported no refund, `0` = a real zero. The amount is "the seller's net amount"; eBay-collected tax is not in it, so the buyer may have received more — the order detail page says so. `totalDueSeller` itself is "subject to change … if a partial or full refund occurs", so a refund also flows into `ebay_earnings` and `recomputeProfit` through the existing earnings-changed path.
- **An order from before the store was connected is not admitted** (`decideIngest.mayInsert`): re-reading by modification date surfaces old orders eBay happened to touch, and an arbitrary slice of pre-connection history would make the dashboard inconsistent. An order we already hold is always updated.
- **A cancelled sale is never bought.** `resolveAutoFulfillEligibility` answers `AutoFulfillBlockedReason.ORDER_CANCELLED` (SKIPPED), `selectResumableOrders` and its SQL exclude `cancelled`, and `AmazonCheckoutService.runForOrder` re-reads `orders.status` at EXECUTION — a cancellation can land while the job waits in the queue. The tracking processor already stops polling a cancelled order.
- **The platform never cancels an Amazon order.** When eBay cancels a sale that already has a real Amazon order, the Action Center raises `ORDER_CANCELLED_AMAZON_OPEN` (WARNING) and the order detail hero says to cancel or return it on Amazon. Because polling stops with the eBay cancellation, the platform cannot observe the seller doing so — the item is bounded to 7 days from the cancellation (`CANCELLED_AMAZON_OPEN_WINDOW_DAYS`) so it clears on its own.
- **Backfill is migration `129`**: each store's watermark is rewound to `GREATEST(created_at, NOW() - 90 days)`, so the first tick after deploy re-reads what eBay modified in that window through the normal path. It updates rows and buys nothing (fresh-sale gate).
- **Known gaps**: the dashboard excludes cancelled orders from revenue (and counts them as "refunds") but does not subtract a partial refund on a live order beyond what `totalDueSeller` already reflects; `listings.sold_count` is not decremented on a cancellation; cancel REQUESTS awaiting the seller's answer are not surfaced (`cancelRequests` is "always empty" on getOrders and the Post-Order cancellation search is not read yet).
- **Not built, on purpose**: refunding from the app. Order-level `issueRefund` needs the `sell.finances` scope (not granted) and `ReasonForRefundEnum`, whose page could not be obtained; it moves real money and cannot be tried in sandbox.
- Files: `apps/api/src/modules/orders/ebay-order-changes.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`, which also source-greps the filter, the paging, the status merge, the COALESCEs, the fresh-sale gates and the watermark), `ebay-fulfillment.service.ts`, `order-sync.service.ts`.

### eBay Returns (`/:locale/returns`, 2026-09-30; in-app actions 2026-10-01)

The return requests buyers opened on the seller's eBay stores, with eBay's own deadline for the seller's next step, and — since 2026-10-01 — a detail drawer that reads the return LIVE from eBay and runs the three actions eBay documents (approve, mark as received, issue refund) from inside the app, behind an operator switch. It exists because a return has a clock on it and nothing else in the app showed one.

- **Polling, not push.** The `ORDER_RETURN_ACTIVITY` notification topic needs the `sell.return` scope (live `getTopic`, saved in `docs/ebay-reference/notification-topics/`), which no connected store has — push would force every seller through re-consent. `GET /post-order/v2/return/search` answers with the scopes stores already hold (verified read-only on production, 2026-09-30).
- **Post-Order is a different API family**: host `api.ebay.com`, `Authorization: IAF <user token>` (NOT `Bearer`), `X-EBAY-C-MARKETPLACE-ID` required, and **not supported in Sandbox** — `PostOrderClient.isReturnSearchSupported()` makes the sweep a no-op (no claim, no call) when `EBAY_ENVIRONMENT` is sandbox, so local dev and the test stack never show real returns.
- **Quota is the design constraint, so the sweep interval is DERIVED from it (operator decision, 2026-09-30).** `post-order.return` is 5,000 calls/day for the whole application (`EbayApiResource.POST_ORDER_RETURN`, charged at BACKGROUND before every attempt) and a sweep is one call per store, so `stores × 24 / interval` must fit the share the sweep may spend. A fixed interval is right for exactly one store count: a fixed 1 h exhausts the quota at ~200 stores, a fixed 6 h makes a single store wait six times longer than it has to. `resolveReturnSweepInterval` (`return-sweep-interval.ts`, pure + Jest-covered) = `ceil(stores × 24 / (backgroundDailyCeiling × quotaPercent))`, clamped to 1–168 h and always rounded UP (overshooting stops the sweep for every seller until eBay's counter resets). With the defaults: 1 h up to 98 stores, 2 h at 100, 6 h at 500 (2,000 calls/day), 21 h at 2,000. `ReturnSweepScheduleService` resolves it (active `ebay_accounts` count + `EbayCallBudgetService.backgroundDailyCeiling` — eBay's own figure from `getRateLimits`, never typed in; cached 60 s; logs one line whenever the pace changes) and is the ONE source for the claim AND for the freshness horizon on the page and in the Action Center. Settings: `ebay.returnSync.intervalAuto` (default true), `ebay.returnSync.quotaPercent` (default 50, bounds 5–90 — THE knob while auto is on; the other half stays for retries, which are charged too), `ebay.returnSync.intervalHours` (default 6 — the manual value, used only with auto off or before eBay has reported a limit), `ebay.returnSync.enabled` (default true), `.cron` (default `*/10 * * * *`, `requiresRestart`), `.maxAccountsPerRun` (default 35 — a burst guard only: 144 ticks × 35 exceeds the whole daily quota, so it can never be what slows the sweep). The claim stamps `ebay_accounts.last_return_sync_at` in the same statement (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`), like the feed-sync claim; budget exhaustion stops the tick.
- **Each sweep asks for the last 90 days, newest first, first page only** (`creation_date_range_from`, `limit=200`, `sort=-FILING_DATE`). eBay documents a from-only range as covering "the following 90 days", so 90 is the widest window that reaches today. **`offset` is never sent**: the reference describes it both as entries to skip and as a page number, and its own sample answers `offset: 1` for a first page — paging on a guess could skip or repeat returns. A store with more than 200 returns in the window logs a warning and its oldest are unread; settle the semantics against a live response before adding paging.
- **Rows are overwritten with what eBay reports now, including back to NULL** (`sellerResponseDue` "might not be returned if there is currently no action due"). Enumerations are stored as sent; nothing depends on an enum list except the two documented values `CLOSED` and `ESCALATED`. A failed read never deletes or blanks rows. `order_id` links the return to our order by exact `ebay_order_id` for the same seller — eBay does not document that the two ids share a format, so an unmatched return simply shows without a product.
- **`ReturnBucket` is the one grouping** (`packages/shared/src/domain/returns/return-bucket.ts`, `deriveReturnBucket` + SQL twin `buildReturnBucketSql`, compared in `return-bucket.spec.ts`): `closed` → `unconfirmed` → `escalated` → `action_overdue` (a seller action is due and `respondByDate` has passed) → `action_due` → `in_progress`, first match wins. The page tabs (`ReturnTab`), `GET /v1/returns`, `GET /v1/returns/counts` and the Action Center's `RETURN_SELLER_ACTION_DUE` all read it; the Action Center item is CRITICAL once any deadline has passed.
- **A row is only as true as the last sweep that saw it — `unconfirmed` is the freshness rule (operator decision, 2026-09-30).** A return that dropped out of the search response (older than the 90-day window, beyond the first 200, a store whose token broke, the sweep switched off) keeps whatever it last said for ever, and "eBay is waiting for you, deadline passed" must not be claimed on stale data. An OPEN row whose `last_synced_at` is older than the freshness horizon derives as `unconfirmed` (grey, question mark, listed under the In progress tab, its last action and deadline not shown); a closed row stays closed. The horizon is `resolveReturnFreshnessHours(<the interval the sweep is actually running at>)` = two sweep intervals, never less than 24 h — taken from `ReturnSweepScheduleService`, so a longer derived interval cannot turn every return stale between sweeps — and it is interpolated into the SQL twin as a validated whole number (`buildReturnBucketSql(alias, hours)`). Because the rule lives INSIDE the bucket, the page's Needs-action tab, its count and the Action Center item can never disagree. The earlier guard (count only returns created in the last 90 days) was the wrong measure: a row's age says nothing about whether eBay still confirms it. Cost, accepted: if sweeps fail for longer than the horizon the actions disappear rather than being shown unverified.
- **Web** (`apps/web/src/features/returns/`, 4-file split, gated by `EbayAccountGuard`): tabs All · Needs action · In progress · Closed with counts from `/returns/counts`, URL state `?tab=&page=&store=&q=&r=` (`useReturnsUrlState`; `r` is the open return and never resets the page), opens on Needs action once when something is waiting and the URL chose nothing, an overdue deadline renders red. **Every row opens `ReturnDetailDrawer`** (the canonical 32rem drawer, not a split pane): status pane (bucket badge, what is due, deadline), "What you can do" (the in-app action buttons eBay lists right now, "Open on eBay" from `sellerAvailableOptions[].actionURL`, and a caption naming what eBay offers that the app does not — decline, message, label…), product + buyer facts with "View order" when we hold the order, reason + the buyer's words, the money (item price, estimated, refunded, close reason), the return shipment, and the journey from eBay's `responseHistory` (oldest first; buyer / seller / eBay markers by activity prefix). Each action goes through a `ConfirmModal` (the refund one names the amount); the API answers with an i18n key on refusal (`returns.errors.*`) and the drawer's query refetches on success. When the live read fails the drawer shows the stored row, says so, and offers no action. An activity, return type, close reason or delivery status with no translation falls back to a generic label rather than printing the raw enum (`resolveHistoryActivityKey` & co., `return-presentation.ts`). No sidebar badge (the Action Center item carries the alarm). Demo mode serves nine fixture returns and builds each detail (journey, shipment, options) from its state (`demoReturnDetail`).
- **Three writes, each a documented Post-Order call, behind a kill switch (2026-10-01).** `EbayReturnsActionsService.act` (`ebay-returns-actions.service.ts`) is the ONLY path to `PostOrderClient.decideReturn` (`POST …/return/{returnId}/decide`, body `{ decision: 'APPROVE' }` — the only decision sent; `DecisionEnum`'s page is not in the local reference, so DECLINE stays on eBay), `markReturnReceived` (`…/mark_as_received`, empty body) and `issueReturnRefund` (`…/issue_refund`, ONE `PURCHASE_PRICE` line whose amount is eBay's own `sellerTotalRefund.estimatedRefundAmount`, `totalAmount` equal — never a figure of ours; no amount reported → `refundAmountUnknown`, refund on eBay). Gates, in order: the row is the caller's → `ebay.returns.actionsEnabled` (platform setting, env `EBAY_RETURNS_ACTIONS_ENABLED`, **default `false`** — turned on only after the first real return has proven the calls in production, because Post-Order has no Sandbox) → not suspended → not Sandbox → a FRESH live `GET …/return/{returnId}?fieldgroups=FULL` → the action's `ActivityOptionEnum` value is in `sellerAvailableOptions[].actionType` (`RETURN_ACTION_EBAY_OPTION`, `resolveReturnActions` in `packages/shared/src/domain/returns/return-actions.ts`) → the one call → an `EBAY_RETURN_ACTION` `audit_logs` row (outcome `sent` / `rejected`, no body) → re-read and `upsertReturn` so the list row is current. **A write is sent ONCE, never inside `withEbayRateLimitRetry`** (a refund replayed after a timeout eBay had processed would be a second refund); eBay's 4xx is `PostOrderRejectedError` → 409 `returns.errors.ebayRejected` with the error ids logged at warn, never the body. Reads and writes are charged to `EbayApiResource.POST_ORDER_RETURN` at INTERACTIVE (the sweep stays BACKGROUND); the detail read is cached 60 s per return. Quota check at the 500-seller target: the sweep's share is capped by `ebay.returnSync.quotaPercent` (≤2,500/day at 50%), and a seller opens a return (1 call) or acts on it (1 read + 1 write + 1 re-read) only when a return exists — a few hundred calls a day at most, well inside the remaining half. `ebay-returns.guard.spec.ts` now locks exactly five Post-Order paths, one `axios.post` outside the retry wrapper, one POST route (`:id/actions/:action`), the gate order and the APPROVE-only decision; `ebay-returns-actions.service.spec.ts` drives the gates against fakes. **Still on eBay, by design**: decline, partial-refund offers, replacement, shipping labels, messages, escalation — the drawer names them and links to eBay's own page. `EbayReturnDetailDto` (`live`, `actionsEnabled`, `availableActions`, `ebayOptions`, `ebayUrl`, `history`, `shipments`, `returnType`, `itemPrice`, `closeReason`, `closedAt`) is the wire shape of `GET /v1/returns/:id/detail`; `return-detail-mapper.ts` is total over eBay's `detail` container.
- Files: `apps/api/src/modules/ebay-returns/` (`post-order.client.ts`, `return-mapper.ts`, `return-detail-mapper.ts`, `ebay-returns-sync.service.ts` + processor, `ebay-returns.service.ts`, `ebay-returns-actions.service.ts`, controller, module, specs), `packages/shared/src/domain/returns/` (`return-actions.ts`, `return-bucket.ts`, `returns.types.ts`), `apps/web/src/features/returns/` (`ReturnsPage/`, `ReturnDetailDrawer/`, `shared/`), i18n namespace `returns` (16 locales), `ebay_returns` (migration `128`). The queue `ebay-returns-sync` is in `ADMIN_QUEUE_NAMES` / `OBSERVED_QUEUE_NAMES`.

### Sale-Driven Stock Sync (between Keepa refresh cycles)
- **Why**: a confirmed eBay sale is real signal that Amazon stock dropped, so eBay quantities are corrected before the next refresh.
- **Queue**: `stock-sync` (BullMQ). Producer = `StockSyncQueueService` (orders module), consumer = `StockSyncProcessorService` (listings module, concurrency 3) — same Redis queue.
- **Flow on new matched order**: `ProductsService.decrementStock` (floors at 0) → enqueue `{ productId }` → `ProductSyncService.syncListingsForProduct(productId)` → the same fan-out as the refresh pipeline: recompute `quantity` per listing's own settings group, push via `EbayBulkService.updatePriceQuantity`, persist.
- **Price formula + price ending (2026-10-02).** `calculateListingPrice` (`packages/shared/src/utils/listing-pricing.ts`, Jest-covered in `apps/api/src/modules/listings/listing-pricing.spec.ts`) is the ONE price formula — the API's create/refresh path and the Listing Group drawer's calculator both call it. A settings group may set a price ending ("charm pricing"): `fees.priceRoundingEnabled` + `fees.priceEndingCents` (0–99, inside the `fees` JSONB — no migration; a group saved before the feature reads as off). `applyPriceEnding` rounds **UP** to the next amount with that ending, in integer cents, as the LAST step (after the $0.99 floor) — never to the nearest, so the target profit is never cut and at most 99 cents are added; the added cents count as profit net of the eBay percentage. `resolvePriceEndingCents` reads an unusable stored value as off, never as a wrong price. A per-listing fixed price (`price_override`) is not rounded. UI: a toggle + cents input on the drawer's pricing step (`listingSettingsGroup.priceRounding.*`, all 16 locales); the calculator shows a "Rounded up to .NN" row.
- **Quantity formula** (single source of truth, `ListingStrategyService.calculateQuantity`): `min(max(stock − buffer, 0), defaultQuantity, maxOrderQuantity ?? ∞)` — used identically by create, refresh and this queue.
- **Shared product stock**: `products.stock` is an ASIN-level cache across all customers, so one sale recomputes EVERY listing of that ASIN, each with its own group settings.
- **Best-effort & idempotent**: never fails order sync; the worker re-reads `products.stock` at execution time; the refresh pipeline resets it to ground truth.
- **Rate-limit hygiene**: `jobId` bucketed per 5s window per product collapses a burst of sales; `attempts: 3` + exponential backoff; bulk calls go through `withEbayRateLimitRetry` (honours `Retry-After` on 429/5xx).

### Amazon Order Linking (manual)
- User provides Amazon Order ID + selects an Amazon Account; `AmazonScrapingService` scrapes the order detail page via Playwright.
- Scraped purchase price, tax, shipping, tracking are written, `amazon_linked_at` set, then `recomputeProfit(ebayOrderId)` → `linked` + trusted `net_profit`.
- **An unreadable cost summary never refuses the link (operator decision, 2026-10-01).** The manual link is the seller's recovery after a failed automatic purchase and they NAME the order, so when the financial DOM is empty the controller still writes `amazon_order_id` / account / tracking fields and schedules tracking — with NO cost written (never zero-filled) and `amazon_linked_at` left NULL, so `recomputeProfit` keeps the order `provisional` and the 3-hourly cost capture fills the real figures. It used to write nothing and mark the order `failed`, which also dropped it out of the cost-capture candidates (`pending`/`provisional` only) — nothing links such a row any more; re-linking by hand recovers it. The one refusal is `orderIdOnPage === false` (`isOrderIdOnPage`: the page's visible text does not print the id — mistyped id, wrong account), which writes nothing and leaves the order's state alone. The manual scrape opens `/your-orders/order-details?orderID=…` (the address Amazon's own "View order details" link carries; the legacy `/gp/your-account/order-details` address returned a page with no cost summary on the first failed link) — the tracking scrapes keep the legacy address, verified live for them. Costs are read from `#od-subtotals` first (on that page it holds the five cost lines and nothing else; the wider `[data-component="orderSummary"]` block also carries the ship-to address and the payment widget's inline `<style>`/`<script>` text, so it is the fallback). An unreadable page is saved (URL, HTML, screenshot) under `fulfillment-evidence/link-<amazonOrderId>/`. `amazon-link-order.spec.ts` locks the three outcomes; `amazon-order-details-dom.spec.ts` drives `parseOrderPage` in a real Chromium over the page captured 2026-10-01 (`__fixtures__/checkout/order-details.html`, personal data fictitious). **Unverified live.**
- Tracking is scheduled immediately for the order.

**The Amazon order-details parser reads Amazon's own summary lines, and only visible text (first live link, 2026-09-29).** `amazon-order-parser.service.ts`: `parseFinancialsFromText` strips "Total before tax" BEFORE the tax lookup and reads "Estimated tax to be collected" (a bare `/tax/` once matched "Total before tax:$13.91" and linked the order with `amazon_tax = purchase_price`, net profit −11.91 instead of +1.03); `\btotal` so "Subtotal" never feeds the grand total; "Shipping & Handling" is the shipping line. `extractTrackingNumberFromText` (pure, tested) runs over `locator('body').innerText()` — never `textContent('body')`, which includes every `<script>` and produced the digitless "UPS" number `1ZAUXFMSEBKUFEFJRA` — and requires a carrier-shaped code (uppercase, ≥4 digits, word-bounded; UPS is `1Z` + 6 + 10 digits in `resolveTrackingCarrier` too). `normalizeAmazonStatus` (pure, tested) returns `pending` for anything unrecognised instead of echoing the text (the `#orderDetails .a-text-bold` fallback selector once yielded the status "grand total:") and reads an ETA line ("Arriving tomorrow") as `pending` — an ETA says nothing about shipment (see "The progress tracker's status card" below). The order detail's Amazon card uses Amazon's own line names (`itemSubtotal` / `shippingHandling` / `totalBeforeTax` / `estimatedTax` / `grandTotal`) so a seller can check it line by line; `OrderDto.buyerPhone` falls back to `shipping_address.phone`, which is where eBay actually puts the buyer's number (`buyer_phone` is filled from the registration address and is usually empty).

### Amazon Order Auto Cost-Capture (`amazon-order-sync` queue)
Links Amazon costs to pending/provisional eBay orders without manual entry: one job per Amazon buyer account scrapes its "Your Orders" list and strict-matches each Amazon order to an eBay candidate.

- **Queue**: `amazon-order-sync` (`AMAZON_ORDER_SYNC_QUEUE`), cron `amazon.orderSync.cron` / `AMAZON_ORDER_SYNC_CRON` (default `0 */3 * * *`, `requiresRestart`), concurrency `AMAZON_ORDER_SYNC_CONCURRENCY` (default 2).
- **Per-account jobs are collapsed with BullMQ `deduplication: { id: acct-<id> }`, NEVER a fixed `jobId` (2026-10-01).** BullMQ silently ignores an add whose `jobId` a KEPT job still holds, and `removeOnComplete: 100` keeps completed jobs — so with `jobId: acct-<id>` the first run's completed job swallowed every later tick. Production synced once (2026-09-23) and then never again, with no error anywhere (`amazon_accounts.last_orders_sync_at` frozen, the tick logging "enqueuing" with nothing after it). `amazon-order-sync-dedup.guard.spec.ts` locks it. Any other queue that wants "one job per X at a time" must use `deduplication` (or a time-bucketed id), never a bare entity id with kept completed jobs. After this fix the first run reads back to the frozen watermark.
- **The cadence is a capacity decision, not a latency one.** Each fire costs one Playwright scrape per ACCOUNT regardless of sales. The platform has `AMAZON_GLOBAL_CONCURRENCY × 86,400` browser-seconds/day (~432k at the code default of 5; production runs 20 since 2026-09-29); at 500 accounts × ~35s the old every-30-min default needed ~840k/day (a permanent backlog), every 3 hours ~140k. Nothing a seller waits on runs here — purchasing is triggered from ingest and tracking has its own schedulers — so tune this before sizing up hardware.
- **Per-account flow** (`AmazonOrderSyncService.runForAccount`):
  1. Candidates FIRST: the user's orders with `cost_capture_status IN ('pending','provisional')` and `order_date >= NOW() - 60 days` (an order that already names an Amazon order id but has no costs yet — a hand link with an unreadable cost summary — is one too). **None → no browser is opened and the watermark is left alone** (2026-10-01: every scan costs a session on the shared Playwright pool; scanning accounts with nothing waiting was pure cost).
  2. `AmazonScrapingService.scrapeAccountOrders(userId, accountId, since)` → `{ rows, suspect }`, with `since = resolveScanSince(...)` (`cost-capture-plan.ts`, pure + tested): the later of the watermark (first scan: 30 days) and the oldest waiting sale less the matcher's 2-day slack — reaching back to its own sale for a candidate that already names its Amazon order or that the automatic checkout clicked for (an unknown-outcome order is looked for again on EVERY scan). **The scrape compares each card against a DAY, never the raw instant** (`scanCutoffMs`, `your-orders-card.ts`: start of the `since` day in UTC, less 1 day): a card carries a date only, parsed as midnight, so `cardDate < since` dropped every order placed on the same calendar day as the previous scan and stopped paging — those orders were then never seen by any later scan (found 2026-10-01; it had been true since the scan was written). Re-reading an already-handled order is harmless because of the held-id rule below.
  3. `planCostCaptureLinks` (`cost-capture-plan.ts`, pure + tested) decides the links: an Amazon order id that an order row ALREADY HOLDS is never attributed again — it fills the costs of the candidate that holds it, or is skipped (one Amazon order pays for one eBay order; without this a repeat buyer's second sale matched the Amazon order that had fulfilled the first and read as purchased without being bought). Otherwise `pickBestMatch` / `scoreAmazonOrderMatch` picks among the candidates that do not yet name an Amazon order and were not given one earlier in the run. An unlinked Amazon order that has the ASIN of an order the automatic checkout clicked for, is dated within `CLICK_PROXIMITY_DAYS` of that click and was read from the click's account is a **suspect**: never linked (that would be a guess), but written to `orders.auto_fulfill_suspect_amazon_order_id`, where it blocks the seller's "not on Amazon" confirmation.
  4. On a link: write `purchase_price`/`amazon_tax`/`amazon_shipping`, `amazon_linked_at`, `linked`, `recomputeProfit`, and schedule tracking immediately. A linked order that carries the click stamp (`auto_fulfill_submitted_at`) was bought by the automatic checkout, so it also becomes `auto_fulfill_status = placed` — this is the reconciliation that settles a `purchase_unknown` order.
  5. Misses stay pending/provisional for the next tick or manual linking.
- **Matching is strict, and the RECIPIENT is the discriminator, not the amount (operator decision, 2026-10-01)** (`order-matcher.ts`): the gates are ASIN (+40), quantity (+20), **the Amazon "Ship to" recipient is the eBay buyer** (+20; `recipientMatchesBuyer` in `address-match.ts` — equal 5-digit postcode AND every name word of the shorter name present in the other, case/accents/punctuation/initials ignored; eBay side is `shipping_address.fullName` → `buyer_name` and `shipping_address.zipCode`) and a date that FITS (+≤20): from `AMAZON_ORDER_EARLY_SLACK_DAYS` (2) before the eBay sale to `AMAZON_ORDER_SYNC_MATCH_WINDOW_DAYS` (default 7) after it. The window is one-sided since 2026-10-01 — a purchase cannot precede the sale it fulfils, and the symmetric window let last week's Amazon order match this week's sale; the 2 days exist only because Amazon's list prints a date in the account's timezone while eBay carries an instant. An order with a click stamp must additionally be dated within `CLICK_PROXIMITY_DAYS` (2) of the click and come from the account the click was made on. Every Amazon order ships to the eBay buyer, so the recipient is what tells two same-ASIN orders in one week apart. An unreadable recipient on either side is NOT a match. **Product NAME is deliberately not compared** — the ASIN already is the product, and eBay titles are shortened, brand-stripped or AI-rewritten.
  - **The amount is a tie-break plus a sanity bound, never an equality gate.** It used to be a gate comparing Amazon's grand total with the eBay `sale_total` — cost against revenue, a whole margin apart, so no real order ever linked (17-15222-04697: $12.98 vs $9.47; 03-15243-67997: $20.19 vs $14.88). Now an Amazon total within `AMAZON_ORDER_SYNC_MATCH_TOLERANCE_PCT` (default raised 5 → 15, since the Amazon total includes tax) of the order's provisional `purchase_price` adds +10. Beyond `AMOUNT_SANITY_FACTOR` (3× above or below the expected cost) the automatic link is refused and the seller links by hand (2026-10-01) — except for an order with a click stamp, where the bound is not applied: refusing the genuine order over a price move would make it look "not found", which is what invites a second purchase.
  - **A tie is a refusal**: `pickBestMatch` returns null when two DIFFERENT eBay orders share the top score — the seller links that one by hand. Wrong attribution is worse than none — there is no force-link path.
  - The card's recipient comes from `.yohtmlc-recipient` (name = the `.a-popover-trigger` text, postcode = the hidden popover's address, read by `textContent`, not a visibility wait) through `parseOrderCardRecipient`, which accepts a postcode only right after a two-letter state (never a five-digit house number) and without a trailing word boundary (textContent runs "72764" into "United States"). **Unverified live**: the ship-to is filled in by Amazon's client-side decryption script; if Playwright's DOM ever holds it undecrypted, nothing links (never a wrong link).
- **The live "Your Orders" card (checked against a capture, 2026-10-01, `__fixtures__/checkout/your-orders.html`)** is `div.order-card.js-order-card`; its header is bare label/value rows ("Order placed · September 30, 2026 · Total · $9.47 · Ship to · Order #") with no `.order-date` / `.order-total` hooks. `parseOrderCardHeader` (`your-orders-card.ts`) reads date and total by label as the fallback — before it, every row came back dated "now" with a $0 total. The first live card embeds a ~100 KB script, so ids and ASINs are read only after `stripScriptBlocks`. The same capture confirmed that the thank-you page's `purchaseId` (106-…) is NOT the order number (113-…).
- **Watermark discipline**:
  - **Transport failure** → throws → BullMQ retries; watermark NOT advanced.
  - **Suspect 0-row scrape** (redirected off "Your Orders", or zero cards on page 1) → treated as a data failure: watermark held, run returns without throwing (next tick retries, no retry storm on a layout change).
  - **Legit empty** → watermark advances. The watermark is the moment the scan STARTED (2026-10-01), so an order placed while the list was being read is not skipped by the next scan, and "scanned after the click" is exact for `confirmNotPurchased`.
  - **Per-order failure** in the match/write loop → logged and skipped.

### Amazon Order Tracking (Amazon → eBay Status Sync)
- **Queue**: `amazon-tracking` (BullMQ, per-order schedulers).
- **Intervals** (`/admin` → Settings, hours, `requiresRestart`; read fresh per scheduling call): pre-ship `AMAZON_TRACKING_PRESHIP_INTERVAL_HOURS` (default **6**); shipped `AMAZON_TRACKING_SHIPPED_INTERVAL_HOURS` (default **24**).
- **No auto-stop**: tracking continues until a terminal state (delivered, cancelled, completed); the processor then removes the scheduler.
- **eBay sync**: Amazon `shipped` → tracking conversion → `POST /sell/fulfillment/v1/order/{id}/shipping_fulfillment` (subject to `mayPushToEbay`, see below). Amazon `delivered` → local status `completed`; if `shipped` was never observed, the fulfillment push runs first so eBay always gets tracking.
- **Hardened semantics** (`amazon-tracking-processor.service.ts`):
  - **Fresh eBay token**: always `EbayService.getAccountAccessToken(ebayAccountId)`, never the raw `ebay_accounts.access_token` column.
  - **No silent drop**: a failed eBay POST propagates out of `handleShipped`, the local status write is skipped, and the next tick retries. Only permanently unpushable conditions (account gone/inactive, no linked listing item) log-and-advance.
  - **Fresh tracking on transition**: the freshly scraped number/carrier are mirrored into the in-memory row before the push.
  - **Status regression guard** (`shouldApplyStatus`): no-op writes, transitions out of terminal states, and SHIPPED → pre-ship downgrades are blocked (a parser `'pending'` fallback must not rewind an order and cause a duplicate fulfillment).
  - **Interval downshift**: on the shipped transition the scheduler is re-upserted at the shipped interval.
  - **Amazon-cancel ≠ eBay-cancel**: an Amazon cancellation (migration `056`) stamps `orders.amazon_cancelled_at` and stops tracking but NEVER overwrites the eBay order status — the eBay sale is still owed. Surfaces as AMAZON_CANCELLED fulfillment state / "needs attention".
- **On startup**: `reconcileSchedulers()` removes orphaned schedulers and creates missing ones.

### Automated Amazon Fulfillment (A2)

When `store_settings.auto_fulfill_enabled` is on, a brand-new matched, paid eBay order triggers an Amazon purchase on a round-robin buyer account, shipped to the eBay buyer. Fires only from the `upsertOrder` genuine-insert seam — never on re-syncs or adoption.

- **Queue**: `auto-fulfill` (`AUTO_FULFILL_QUEUE`, registered in `OrdersModule`). Producer `AutoFulfillQueueService` (orders module) from `OrderSyncService.maybeEnqueueAutoFulfill`; processor `AutoFulfillProcessor` (amazon module). `jobId = fulfill-${ebayOrderId}`, `attempts: 3`, exponential backoff 60s, concurrency `AUTO_FULFILL_QUEUE_CONCURRENCY` (default 1).
- **Producer resolution** (`maybeEnqueueAutoFulfill`, pure helpers in `auto-fulfill-helpers.ts`), after the eligibility gate above:
  1. `StoreSettingsService.getResolvedSettings(userId, entity.ebayAccountId)` (Store > Global > Default) → `!autoFulfillEnabled` → `skipped`.
  2. Accounts with `auto_fulfill_enabled=TRUE AND auto_fulfill_cap_total IS NOT NULL` → none → `skipped`.
  3. `pickRoundRobinAccount`: oldest `last_used_at` first (NULL oldest), ties by id.
  4. `meetsCoarseCapGate(saleTotal, cap)`: `saleTotal > 0 && saleTotal <= cap`.
  5. Stamp `last_used_at = NOW`, enqueue.
- **Checkout** (`AmazonCheckoutService`, every browser action via `AmazonRateLimiter.schedule(accountId, …)`), `runForOrder → checkout`:
  1. **Idempotency re-check + THE CLICK STAMP (2026-10-01)** — `decideFulfillStart(status, submittedAt)` (`auto-fulfill-helpers.ts`, pure + tested). `orders.auto_fulfill_submitted_at` (migration `132`) is written, as a compare-and-set (`claimPlaceOrderClick`: `WHERE auto_fulfill_submitted_at IS NULL AND auto_fulfill_status = 'running'`), immediately BEFORE the Place Order click, so "was the click sent?" is a stored fact instead of an inference from the status: PLACED → SKIP; **stamp set and not PLACED → UNKNOWN_OUTCOME** (the checkout is never entered; `settleIfClickWasSent` blocks the row as `interrupted` unless it already carries `no_confirmation`, and queues a scan of the account's orders); BLOCKED/DRY_RUN/SKIPPED → SKIP; PENDING/FAILED → PROCEED; **RUNNING with NO stamp → PROCEED** — the previous process died before the click, so nothing was bought (this used to block every such order as `interrupted`, although almost all of them die long before the click). `AutoFulfillProcessor` asks `settleIfClickWasSent` before it writes anything after an error and, when the click was sent, ends the job WITHOUT a retry; its PENDING and FAILED writes also carry `AND auto_fulfill_submitted_at IS NULL` in SQL. A lost claim never clicks: lost to an existing stamp → blocked as unknown; lost because the order now carries a real Amazon order id → `AutoFulfillAlreadyPurchasedError` → SKIPPED; lost with neither (the stale-run sweep took the row) → a plain error and a retry. **An order somebody already bought is never bought again**: the manual link does not touch `auto_fulfill_status`, so a job that waited in the queue still finds PENDING — `runForOrder` reads `amazon_order_id` and settles a real id (not a `SIM-` placeholder) as SKIPPED, and the claim carries the same condition in SQL. **`setStatus` never moves a row out of PLACED** (`AND auto_fulfill_status <> 'placed'`), so a run that loses the claim cannot turn a real purchase back into "blocked". `OrderSyncService.settleStaleRunningAutoFulfill` (order-sync tick) settles a row RUNNING for more than `STALE_RUNNING_MINUTES` (60): stamped → BLOCKED/`interrupted`, unstamped → FAILED. Graceful shutdown still matters (a deploy lets an in-flight checkout finish: `main.ts` SIGTERM → `app.close()`, compose `stop_grace_period: 5m`; `graceful-shutdown.guard.spec.ts`). `click-boundary.guard.spec.ts` source-greps the ordering (claim before click, exactly one click, no throw / PENDING / FAILED / `setStatus` after the claim in `checkout()`, `parseConfirmation` can only stop as `no_confirmation`, `onPlaced` never throws, the stamp is cleared in exactly one place). Reconciling by re-buying was rejected and stays rejected: "not found" is not "not placed". Suspension is also re-checked here (blocks `SUBSCRIPTION_SUSPENDED`).
  1b. **Live eBay re-read** — `OrderSyncService.recheckBeforePurchase(ebayOrderId, manual)` reads the sale with `getOrder` (one Fulfillment call per automatic purchase) right before RUNNING is set, writes back what eBay says now (forward-only status, cancel state, line count) and applies `decidePrePurchase`: cancelled / already fulfilled / unpaid → SKIPPED with the ingest gate's reasons; more than one line item → SKIPPED `multi_item_order`; an open cancel request (`cancelState` other than the documented `NONE_REQUESTED`, or a non-empty `cancelRequests`, which `getOrder` populates) → BLOCKED `cancel_requested`. No enum list is relied on, and a request the seller REJECTED cannot be told apart from an open one by documented fields — so a MANUAL start (`manual: true` on the job) skips that one hold and nothing else. A read that fails THROWS: BullMQ retries, exhaustion is FAILED (restartable), never a purchase on stale data. Observed live 2026-10-01 (read-only `getOrder` on a production order): `cancelStatus` = `{ cancelState: "NONE_REQUESTED", cancelRequests: [] }`, one line item, `shipByDate` present. **Still unobserved**: what an actually open cancel request looks like.
  2. `loadInputs` — `no_asin` if no row, `cap` on non-finite cap, `address` if the buyer address lacks street/city/zip.
  3. `ensureLoggedIn` (proven auth, see below) → `captcha`/`otp`/`login`.
  3b. **Cart hygiene** — `clearCart` empties the cart first (Amazon checks out the WHOLE cart).
  4. Add to cart — `out_of_stock` if unavailable; a missing product page (404/410 or Amazon's "couldn't find that page", `isProductPageMissing`) is `no_asin`, not `out_of_stock` (a delisted ASIN never comes back).
  4b. **Cart verification (fail-closed)** — `verifyCartContents` requires exactly one line matching our ASIN (and quantity when readable), else `cart`. "Proceed to checkout" is mandatory (`clickFirstAvailable` miss → `cart`).
  5. `selectShipToAddress` (`address`), `selectDefaultPayment` (`payment`).
     **Ship-to must be the eBay BUYER, never the buyer account's default address.** Saved-address selection uses `addressBlockMatchesBuyer` (`address-match.ts`, pure + tested): street **and** zip (plus unit line when present) — zip-only picked the wrong entry when one household had two addresses under one zip. Name is corroborating only. No match → `addBuyerAddress`; if unavailable, fail closed.
  6. **Review-step HARD CAP** — the Place Order control must be visible before the total is accepted (the address step also shows an "Order total" sidebar). Total read from labelled selectors, then a label-anchored `/order total|grand total\s*:?\s*\$N/` fallback (never "first price on the page"). Non-finite/≤0 → `review_unreadable`; `> capTotal` (while `AUTO_FULFILL_REVIEW_CAP_HARD_STOP !== 'false'`, default ON) → `cap`. Aborts BEFORE the click.
  7. **Dry-run** (`amazon_accounts.auto_fulfill_dry_run`) → snap `dry_run_review`, `simulatePlacement`, return — NO click. It writes a `SIM-`-prefixed `amazon_order_id` (`SIMULATED_AMAZON_ORDER_PREFIX`), splits the real review total into costs, sets `linked` and runs the real `recomputeProfit`. Load-bearing: status stays **DRY_RUN** (never `placed`), `SIM-` makes the state SIMULATED, and **tracking is NOT scheduled** (the id is fake). Fail-soft.
  8. Else read the review costs, click Place Order (click errors swallowed at warn to avoid double-order races), `parseConfirmation` (`no_confirmation` only without any proof — see "The 2026-09 checkout layout"), the "Your Orders" id lookup when the page gave none, `onPlaced`.
- **Money safety — `onPlaced` (fail-soft layered)**: after a confirmed placement NO path rethrows into the failure branch. Layer 1: one atomic UPDATE (`placed` + `linked` + real costs + `amazon_order_id` + `amazon_linked_at`). Layer 2 (if 1 throws): minimal `amazon_order_id` + `placed`. Layer 3: `recomputeProfit` (best-effort). Layer 4: `AmazonTrackingQueueService.scheduleOrderTracking` (best-effort).
- **Fail-closed typed errors**: `AutoFulfillBlockedError` carries an `AutoFulfillBlockedReason`; `runForOrder` catches its own → `blocked` + reason, NO BullMQ retry. Anything escaping to `AutoFulfillProcessor.process` is transport: final attempt sets `failed`, then rethrows for backoff.
- **Manual start — "Start automatic order" (2026-10-01).** Nothing re-arms a blocked purchase on its own (only a `subscription_suspended` block is resumed), so the order detail page offers a button while `OrderDto.canStartAutoFulfill` is true: `POST /v1/amazon/orders/:orderId/start-auto-fulfill` → `OrderSyncService.startAutoFulfillManually`. ONE rule decides both: `canStartAutoFulfillManually` (`packages/shared/src/domain/orders/manual-auto-fulfill.ts`) — a paid, unshipped (`WAITING_SHIPMENT`), tracked order inside the plan limit with no real Amazon order, whose status is BLOCKED with a reason in the ALLOWLIST `MANUALLY_RETRYABLE_BLOCKED_REASONS` (every reason raised BEFORE the Place Order click), FAILED, DRY_RUN (its `SIM-` placeholder and simulated costs are cleared, then `recomputeProfit`), or SKIPPED with no reason. **`no_confirmation` and `interrupted` are never retryable** — the click may have gone out; a card declined AFTER the click lands in `no_confirmation` too, and the seller checks Amazon and links by hand. PENDING/RUNNING are refused (a job may be queued or mid-checkout). The store toggle is **not** read (operator decision: the toggle means "buy new sales on their own"; a click is an explicit request), the buyer-account gate is (enabled + cap), the account the order was last tried on is preferred, the coarse cap, suspension and AO quota are checked, and every refusal is a 409 with an `orders.errors.autoFulfill*` key instead of a SKIPPED write. The row is claimed with a compare-and-set on exactly the approved state (double click / second tab → `autoFulfillAlreadyStarted`), and the job gets its own id (`enqueueManual`, `fulfill-<id>-manual-<ts>`) because BullMQ silently drops an add whose id a kept completed/failed job still holds. A failed enqueue leaves the order FAILED (retryable) and releases the slot. Since 2026-10-01 the rule also refuses any row whose click stamp is set (whatever its status and reason) and any multi-item order, the claim carries `auto_fulfill_submitted_at IS NULL`, the allowlist gained `cancel_requested` and `loss_limit`, and the coarse cap compares the estimated Amazon cost (below). Specs: `manual-auto-fulfill.spec.ts`, `order-sync-manual-auto-fulfill.spec.ts`.

- **"It is not on Amazon" — the only thing that clears the click stamp (2026-10-01).** `POST /v1/amazon/orders/:orderId/confirm-not-purchased` → `OrderSyncService.confirmNotPurchased`, offered on the order detail page while the stage is `purchase_unknown`. `resolveConfirmNotPurchased` (shared, pure + tested) needs two independent facts: the click account's order list was scanned AFTER the click (`amazon_accounts.last_orders_sync_at > auto_fulfill_submitted_at`; every account of the seller when the row has no click account, i.e. one stamped by migration `132`) and linked nothing, AND the seller's own call. Before a scan has run the answer is 409 `orders.errors.purchaseNotYetChecked` and the controller queues that scan. **"Linked nothing" is not "saw nothing"**: while a suspect Amazon order stands (see cost capture; not yet linked to ANY sale) the answer is 409 `orders.errors.purchaseFoundOnAmazon` and the seller links by hand. Locked by `order-sync-confirm-not-purchased.spec.ts`. On success the row becomes FAILED with no reason (why the click produced no order is not known, so none is claimed) and "Start automatic order" is offered again. "Not found" alone never re-arms a purchase; neither does the seller alone.

- **Enqueue-time rules (2026-10-01).** The coarse cap gate compares the ESTIMATED AMAZON COST (`estimateAmazonOrderCost`: the product's current unit price × quantity, plus the seller's `amazonTaxRate`), not the eBay sale total — revenue above the cap said nothing about a cost below it; an unknown price passes to the review-step cap, which stays the real guard. `pickRoundRobinAccount` prefers accounts with `status = active` and falls back to the whole enabled pool only when none is (a `needs_reauth` account used to be picked while a working one sat idle). `AutoFulfillQueueService.enqueue` collapses per order with BullMQ `deduplication`, never a fixed `jobId` — an order blocked at execution and later re-armed by the suspension-resume or unpaid-recheck sweep reused the id of its kept completed job, so the add was silently dropped and the order sat at PENDING for ever (the same defect as the cost-capture queue); double-purchase safety rests on the click stamp, not on the id. The provisional `purchase_price` is unit price × quantity (`toOrderCost`) at ingest and in `recomputeProfit`'s fallback — it used to be the unit price alone, overstating the estimated profit of a multi-quantity order; existing rows are not backfilled. **A multi-item eBay order is never bought automatically**: `orders.ebay_line_item_count > 1` → SKIPPED `multi_item_order` (the platform reads only `lineItems[0]`, so it would buy one item of several and the order would read as handled); the order detail page says how many items the order holds. The line-item data model is a separate, unbuilt project.

- **Loss limit (2026-10-01, `store_settings.auto_fulfill_max_loss`, NULL = off, the default).** At the review step, after the cap: `exceedsLossLimit` — Amazon total minus `ebay_earnings` above the limit → BLOCKED `loss_limit` before the click (manually retryable; it applies to a manual start too, the override is the setting). The cap is one absolute figure per buyer account and says nothing about THIS sale. An unknown payout never blocks. `resolveAutoFulfillMaxLoss` is Store > Global with one difference from the other store settings: a store row with NO limit inherits the global one (a store row created by a focused drawer never mentions the field, and its NULL must not switch off a guard set globally — so a store cannot opt out of a global limit). `StoreSettingsDrawer` shows a toggle plus an amount; the request field has three states (omitted = unchanged, `null` = off, number = limit), which is why the upsert uses a separate provided-flag rather than COALESCE.

- **Audit trail (`auto_fulfill_events`, migration `132`).** `AutoFulfillEventLog.record` (orders module, exported) appends one row per step — `AutoFulfillEvent`: attempt started, eBay re-check, cart / address / payment, review total, cap and loss checks, `submit_claimed`, `place_order_clicked`, confirmation, order id, placed, `purchase_unknown`, blocked / skipped / failed, `reconciliation_linked`, `confirmed_not_purchased`, `manual_start`. NEVER throws (it must not change the outcome of a purchase) and carries figures and codes only — never a name or an address, so the eBay account-deletion erasure does not reach it. Nothing reads it to decide anything. Retention `retention.autoFulfillEventsDays` (400, floor 90). No screen; read with SQL.
- **Evidence screenshots**: `fulfillment-evidence/{ebayOrderId}/{stage}-{timestamp}.png` **plus the page's HTML under the same stem** (`.html`, since 2026-10-01 — a screenshot shows that a step failed, not which element Amazon renamed; same sweep, same PII rules) via `snap(...)`. A live placement also snaps `place-order-review` before the click and `after-place-order` once the confirmation parse has finished (success or `no_confirmation`) (`FULFILLMENT_EVIDENCE_DIR`, default `$CWD/fulfillment-evidence`); swept hourly past `FULFILLMENT_EVIDENCE_TTL_DAYS` (default 7, clamp 1–365). No admin-role gating in code — protect the directory with filesystem perms.
- **Guardrails**: store master toggle + per-account enable/cap/dry-run + **cap-required-to-enable** (`AmazonAccountsService.assertCanEnable` → `amazon.errors.autoFulfillCapRequired`) + coarse pre-filter + review-step hard cap + loss limit (opt-in) + live eBay re-read + cart quantity verified for every multi-unit order (an unreadable cart quantity blocks as `cart` when quantity > 1: the quantity select is best-effort, and the cap only stops OVER-buying) + dry-run + fail-closed errors + the click stamp. A proxy is **not** required to enable (see Anti-Ban below).
- **Config** (optional): `AUTO_FULFILL_QUEUE_CONCURRENCY=1` (keep at 1), `AUTO_FULFILL_CHECKOUT_MIN_TIME_MS=4500`, `AUTO_FULFILL_REVIEW_CAP_HARD_STOP=true`, `FULFILLMENT_EVIDENCE_DIR`, `FULFILLMENT_EVIDENCE_TTL_DAYS=7`.
- **Settings UI — two gates, both must be on**:
  1. **Store gate** — `autoFulfillEnabled` in `StoreSettingsDrawer` (global or per store). The same drawer carries the tracking-conversion controls: one on/off toggle (the seller never sees a vendor name), the carrier-scope Select, the convert-manual-orders toggle, and their exposure warnings.
  2. **Buyer-account gate** — `autoFulfillEnabled` + `autoFulfillCapTotal` in `AmazonAccountDrawer`, per buyer account. The drawer surfaces `autoFulfillCapRequired` from the backend. Proxy is a separate optional section of the same drawer.

  **`auto_fulfill_dry_run` is deliberately NOT in any customer UI** — it exists to validate `CHECKOUT_SELECTORS` against live DOM; operators set it directly on `amazon_accounts`. Credential verify is a per-card "Verify login" action in `AmazonAccountsDrawer`. **Account delete has no UI entry point** (known gap). While automatic orders are on for an account the drawer warns (`amazon.autoFulfill.cartOwnershipNote`) that the account must be used for SellerHill only: its cart is emptied before every automatic order.

  **A TOTP secret is MANDATORY on every Amazon buyer account** — it is the only challenge workers can answer without a human. `CreateAmazonAccountDto` / `createAmazonAccountSchema` require `twoFactorSecret`; `AmazonAccountsService.assertTwoFactorSecret` checks presence and base32/length via `isValidTotpSecret` (`amazon.errors.twoFactorSecretRequired` / `twoFactorSecretInvalid`). On edit it is required only when no secret is stored. App-level only (no DB constraint).
  - `normalizeTotpSecret` / `isValidTotpSecret` live in `@repo/shared` (`utils/totp-secret.ts`); `apps/api/src/modules/amazon/totp-secret.ts` is a re-export shim (its spec covers it). The drawer normalizes on every change (strip `\s`/`-`/`_`, uppercase) with the same helper, so a pasted space-grouped secret is never rejected.

  **A failed verification persists a CODE, not the raw provider message.** `AmazonVerifyProcessorService` runs `classifyAmazonVerificationFailure` (`amazon-verify-helpers.ts`, pure + tested) and stores an `AmazonVerificationFailureCode` (`invalid_credentials | captcha | two_factor_required | auth_not_proven | unexpected_login_page | transport | unknown`) in `amazon_accounts.last_verification_error`; the card localizes it via `settingsHub.sections.amazon.verificationError.<code>` (unknown values → `unknown`). The raw text stays in the Winston log only.

**`locator.isVisible({ timeout })` does NOT wait — never use it as one (first live order, 2026-09-29).** Playwright ignores the option and answers from the DOM as it is at that instant. The checkout's address step was read while Amazon's shell was still a spinner, so "no saved addresses and no add-address control" blocked a perfectly good order (evidence: `address-add-unavailable-*.png` showing an empty "Secure checkout" page). Every presence check in `amazon-checkout.service.ts`, `amazon-scraping.service.ts` and `amazon-order-parser.service.ts` is `locator.first().waitFor({ state: 'visible', timeout }).then(() => true).catch(() => false)`, and `selectShipToAddress` first waits up to `ADDRESS_STEP_READY_TIMEOUT_MS` (20s) for any address-step control before counting radios. `playwright-visibility.guard.spec.ts` refuses `isVisible({` in those three files. A blocked order is NOT retried automatically — recover by linking the Amazon order by hand.

**The 2026-09 checkout layout (order 17-15222-04697, blocked `address` on 2026-09-30).** Once a buyer account has a last-used address (typically the previous eBay buyer, added by hand for an earlier order), "Proceed to checkout" lands on a single-page checkout with the address step COLLAPSED: only "Delivering to <name>" + `#deliver-to-address-text` and a "Change" link (`a[data-csa-c-slot-id="checkout-change-shipaddressselect"]`). None of the old selectors matched it, so it read as "no saved addresses and no add-address control". `selectShipToAddress` now reads that card: it is accepted only if it is the eBay buyer's address, otherwise "Change" opens the list. The list's radios are `input[name="destinationSubmissionUrl"]` inside `[id^="select-destination-on-sasp-desktop-panel-id-"]` blocks (AUI keeps the input visually hidden — select with a forced check, trust only `isChecked()`), the add link is `#add-new-address-desktop-sasp-tango-link`. **Every continue button on these pages is an `<input>` labelled only through `aria-labelledby`, and "Deliver to this address", "Use this payment method" and the add-address dialog's "Use this address" all share the element id `checkout-primary-continue-button-id`** — the accessible name even resolves to the page's own label inside the dialog. Only `data-csa-c-slot-id` tells them apart (`checkout-primary-continue-shipaddressselect` / `…-payselect` / `address-ui-widgets-continue-address-btn-bottom`); never select these by id or by name. An add-address dialog that stays open after submit (Amazon's inline field error) is an `address` block naming that error (`assertAddressDialogClosed`) — the page behind it still carries "Deliver to this address" for the pre-selected address. The final recipient check reads `#deliver-to-address-text`; `address-match.ts` compares both sides through a symmetric USPS abbreviation table (Lane→LN, Court→CT, North→N, Apartment→APT…), digit/letter splitting (`121Daniel` → `121 DANIEL`) and whole-token containment, because Amazon standardises every address it saves. `amazon-checkout-dom.spec.ts` drives these helpers in a real Chromium against the captured pages in `__fixtures__/checkout/` (markup verbatim, personal data fictitious; skipped where no Playwright Chromium is installed) — the previous code failed 6 of its 7 cases with the production error. **Not yet captured, so not yet covered: the final "Place your order" control on this layout** (`placeOrderControl` adds an exact accessible-name fallback to the CSS list). **The thank-you page WAS captured (2026-10-01, a hand purchase of 17-15222-04697) and it carries NO Amazon order id**: `/gp/buy/thankyou/handlers/display.html?purchaseId=<3-7-7>`, heading "Order placed, thanks!" in `#widget-purchaseConfirmationStatus .a-alert-inline-success`, and none of `confirmationOrderId`'s selectors (`[data-testid="order-id"]`, `.confirmation-id`, `a[href*="orderID="]`) nor `confirmationSummary` exist on it. The old `parseConfirmation` would have ended a REAL placement as `no_confirmation` — money spent, order blocked, nothing linked. Now (`order-confirmation.ts`, pure + Jest-covered, plus DOM cases over `__fixtures__/checkout/thank-you.html`): **proof** is an order id on the page OR `isOrderPlacedPage` (the `/gp/buy/thankyou/` route or the "Order placed" heading) — `no_confirmation` only when neither; **costs** come from the review summary read just BEFORE the click (`parseReviewCostLines` over `#subtotals-marketplace-table`, items = total − shipping − tax when the items line is missing), because the thank-you page has none; **the id** is read from "Your Orders" on the same page and in the same rate-limiter slot (`resolveOrderIdFromHistory` — calling `AmazonScrapingService` there would queue behind this job on the per-account limiter and deadlock), taking only the newest 5 cards, only one linking our ASIN, only an id no other order holds (`pickOrderIdFromHistoryCards`), and snapping `order-history` as evidence because those card selectors are still unverified live. **`purchaseId` is never used as the order id** — its equality with the "Your Orders" number is unproven.
