With no id, `onPlaced` writes PLACED + the review costs but NOT `amazon_linked_at`/`linked` and schedules no tracking, so the order stays a pending/provisional cost-capture candidate and the 3-hourly Amazon order sync links the real id and starts tracking; **`deriveOrderStage` + `buildOrderStageSql` read PLACED-without-id as `purchased`** (it read as `to_purchase`, inviting a second purchase).

**Amazon authentication must be positively PROVEN, never inferred (`amazon-auth-state.ts`).** `probeAmazonAuth` + the pure `decideAmazonAuth` are the single source of truth for `performLogin`, `BrowserStateManager.isSessionValid` and `AmazonCheckoutService.ensureLoggedIn`. An auth route (`/ap/signin`, `/ax/claim/*`) or a visible credential/OTP control is a HARD negative; auth-gated account content or a populated nav greeting is a positive; `signedOutNav` alone is a WEAK negative that must NOT veto a positive (Amazon's CDN-cached header can say "Hello, sign in" on an authenticated page). Do not go back to URL-substring checks — they marked `/ax/claim/*` sessions ACTIVE and let signed-out checkouts silently no-op until the review read, which was misreported as `cap` (hence `review_unreadable` is distinct).

- **Order stages (2026-09-29) — `OrderStage` is the ONE seller-facing status.** `packages/shared/src/domain/orders/order-stage.ts` (pure `deriveOrderStage`) and its SQL twin `buildOrderStageSql` (`order-stage-sql.ts`) are one ordered decision, proven equal on all 2,304 input combinations by `order-stage-sql.guard.spec.ts`: `cancelled → amazon_cancelled → delivered → test_run → shipped → tracking_held → buying → purchased → purchase_unknown → purchase_blocked → awaiting_payment → to_purchase`, first match wins. The operator's own words for the problem it solves: "I linked the Amazon order by hand and it still said Waiting for shipment before and after — I cannot tell which orders I have to act on." The eBay status (`OrderStatus`) is still stored and synced unchanged, but it describes the SALE, not the WORK, so it is shown only as an info row on the detail page's eBay card. `OrderDto.stage` carries the stage (plus `shippedDetectedAt` / `ebayTrackingPushedAt`); `GET /orders?stage=a,b` filters through the same CASE; `GET /orders/stage-counts` feeds the list page's counted tabs (`OrderStageTab` / `ORDER_STAGE_TABS`: All · Needs action · To purchase · In progress · Done); with no `sortBy` the list puts `ACTIONABLE_ORDER_STAGES` (`amazon_cancelled`, `tracking_held`, `purchase_unknown`, `purchase_blocked`) first, then newest. Web: `OrderStageBadge` (icon + tooltip; `features/orders/shared/order-stage.ts` maps stage → badge variant + icon — **every stage its own colour since 2026-10-01**: cancelled grey · test run slate · delivered green · shipped teal · buying sky · purchased brand blue · awaiting payment navy · to purchase SOLID amber · tracking held amber then **red after `TRACKING_HELD_ALARM_HOURS` = 12** from `shippedDetectedAt` · blocked orange · Amazon cancelled red · not confirmed SOLID navy; `to_purchase` stays loud even when automation is off — operator decisions), `OrderStageLegend` (the info popover beside the tabs explaining every stage), and `?tab=` / `?stage=` URL state so an Action Center row lands filtered. Copy is `orders.stage.<stage>.{label,meaning,action}` + `orders.stageTabs.*` + `orders.stageLegend.*` in all 16 locales (`order-stage-i18n.guard.spec.ts`). Two rules from the post-build review: a dry run is a TEST only while no real Amazon order id exists (the manual link writes one without touching `auto_fulfill_status`), and eBay `PROCESSING` (IN_PROGRESS = a line shipped) is `shipped`, never `to_purchase`; the manual link-amazon UPDATE clears `amazon_cancelled_at` because that flag belonged to the replaced Amazon order. `cancelled` is live since 2026-09-30: order sync reads eBay cancellations (see "Order change tracking"), the rule sits FIRST (a void sale has no buyer to serve, so neither an Amazon cancellation nor a blocked purchase is an action any more), and the Status select and legend list every stage again (`SELLER_VISIBLE_ORDER_STAGES` is the whole list). The default sort leads with the stage CASE, so it sorts ONE seller's rows (the WHERE starts at `user_id`; `idx_orders_order_date` is global and was never the access path) — add a stored stage column only if a single seller reaches six figures of orders. **`purchase_unknown` (2026-10-01)** is derived, not stored: the click stamp is set and nothing above it in the order proves a purchase (no Amazon order id, not PLACED, not mid-run). Red with a question-mark icon — deliberately not the blocked stage's triangle, because the instruction is the opposite: check Amazon BEFORE anything is bought again. Its three exits are reconciliation (cost capture links it → `purchased`), a manual link, and the seller's "It is not on Amazon" confirmation (below). The auto-fulfill reason is shown under `purchase_blocked`, `purchase_unknown` and `to_purchase` (`orderStageShowsReason`). Adding a stage = enum member + both derivations + presentation map + i18n. Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-29-order-stages-design.md`.
- **Flags sit BESIDE the stage, never inside it (2026-10-02, operator decision after reviewing a competitor's status list).** A late shipment and a refund are not stages: an order is "purchased AND late", "delivered AND refunded", and one more stage would hide the other. `OrderShipByState` (`due_soon` | `late`, `packages/shared/src/domain/orders/order-ship-by.ts`: `deriveShipByState` + SQL twins `buildShipByStateSql` / `buildNeedsActionSql`, compared in `order-ship-by.spec.ts`) is derived at read time from `orders.ebay_ship_by_date` and applies only while eBay still awaits a shipment (`STAGES_AWAITING_EBAY_SHIPMENT` — never unpaid, shipped, delivered or cancelled); due soon = inside `SHIP_BY_DUE_SOON_HOURS` (24). Rows read before migration `132` carry no ship-by date and are never flagged. A refund is `ebay_refunded_amount > 0` — there is NO refund status: eBay reports a refund only once issued, so "pending" cannot be shown, and partial vs full is not derived (the amount is the seller's net, tax excluded). `OrderDto.shipByState`; `GET /orders?shipBy=late|due_soon`, `?refunded=true`, `?needsAction=true`. **"Needs action" = an actionable stage OR a missed ship-by date** — the tab sends `needsAction` (not a stage list), its count is `OrderStageCountsDto.needsAction`, and the default sort floats the same predicate, so the three cannot disagree. Web: ONE "Deadline & refund" select (`?flag=late|due_soon|refunded`) beside the Status select, and chips next to the stage badge on the card and the table row through one function (`features/orders/shared/order-flags.ts`). Action Center: `ORDER_LATE_TO_SHIP` (CRITICAL, tracked orders only, links to `/orders?flag=late`). Rejected on purpose: a `purchase_mode` column (`to_purchase` already means "left to the seller" — automation's orders read `buying`), a stored `refund_status`, renaming stage codes, and an eight-tab rail.
- **"Test" is not offered to sellers (operator decision, 2026-10-02).** `SELLER_VISIBLE_ORDER_STAGES` leaves `TEST_RUN` out of the Status select and the legend, and the demo no longer carries a dry-run order. The stage itself stays (a dry-run row must never look purchased); only an operator-set dry run can produce the badge.
- **The seller's own note (`orders.seller_note`, migration `136`, 2026-10-02).** Private free text, max `ORDER_NOTE_MAX_LENGTH` (1000): `PUT /v1/orders/:id/note` (blank clears), edited on the order detail page's "Your note" card, shown as one line on the card and under the order number in the table, and included in the list search. Nothing sends it anywhere; order sync never writes the column (`order-stage-api.guard.spec.ts` greps for it); the eBay account-deletion erasure clears it with the other buyer columns, because free text may name the buyer. There is no inline edit on the list — the card is one button.
- **`OrderFulfillmentState` stays as the automation vocabulary** (`packages/shared/src/domain/orders/fulfillment-state.ts`, pure `deriveFulfillmentState`, tested): `purchased | amazon_cancelled | action_required | in_progress | not_automated | manual | simulated`. Precedence is load-bearing: an Amazon cancellation outranks `placed`, and a `SIM-` id reports SIMULATED so a dry run can never look purchased. `OrderDto.fulfillmentState` and the API's `?fulfillmentState=` filter are kept for back-compat and the SQL twin still backs the Action Center's non-stage predicates; **the web no longer renders it** (the list column, the detail hero and the order card all show the stage) and `features/orders/shared/fulfillment-state.ts` was deleted. New seller-facing code reads the stage.
- **A blocked/failed purchase the seller then linked by hand is MANUAL, not action-required (2026-09-29).** `deriveFulfillmentState` and `buildFulfillmentStateSql` both check `amazonOrderId` inside the BLOCKED/FAILED branch: the first live order (blocked on `address`, bought and linked manually) otherwise stayed "Action required" in the list and the Action Center with nothing left to do.
- **A settled sale is never action-required.** `COMPLETED` (written locally on delivery; `cancelled` is the other terminal status, written from eBay's `cancelStatus` and handled by the stage model before fulfillment state is consulted) reports MANUAL even with `amazon_cancelled_at` set or auto-fulfill `blocked`/`failed` — otherwise the Action Center could never reach zero. `SHIPPED` is NOT settled (an Amazon cancel after the push is the worst case).
- **Fulfillment state has ONE definition on both sides of the wire.** `buildFulfillmentStateSql(alias)` (`packages/shared/src/domain/orders/fulfillment-state-sql.ts`) emits one ordered `CASE` mirroring `deriveFulfillmentState`; `OrdersService`'s filter and every Action Center order count use it. Do not reintroduce per-state WHERE predicates — seven such copies had already drifted. Only enum constants are interpolated.
  - `fulfillment-state-sql.guard.spec.ts` interprets the generated SQL and compares it to `deriveFulfillmentState` across all 288 input combinations, asserts every state is reachable, and throws on unrecognized SQL.
- **The orders list opens on TRACKED orders (operator decision, 2026-10-01).** `useOrdersFilters`: `?tracking=` is `tracked` (the default, and the only value left out of the URL), `all` or `untracked`. A store connected with years of history shows hundreds of sales the platform can neither cost nor fulfil, and they buried the handful it is working on. The stage tab counts follow the same filter. **Every Action Center order link except `ORDER_AWAITING_PURCHASE`'s (tracked-only, see "Action Center") carries `&tracking=all`**, because those counts are over every order of the seller — without it "3 need you" could land on a list of two; a new link into `/orders` that must show untracked sales has to say so too.
- Orders list shows ONE stage column (`OrderStageBadge` + blocked reason + Amazon order id + tracking number) and no eBay-status column; the toolbar has the counted stage tabs, one Status `Select` over the eleven stages, the store filter and the listing-link filter. The old eBay-status and fulfillment-state selects are gone (`status`, `fulfillmentState` and `autoFulfillNeedsAttention` are still accepted by the API for back-compat). The `orders.fulfillmentState.*` i18n keys are now unread by the web and can be pruned once the other in-flight edits to the 15 `orders.json` files land.
- **Shared enums** (`packages/shared/src/domain/orders/orders.types.ts`): `AutoFulfillStatus` (`pending|running|placed|blocked|failed|dry_run|skipped`); `AutoFulfillBlockedReason` (`no_asin`, `captcha`, `otp`, `login`, `out_of_stock`, `address`, `payment`, `cap`, `no_confirmation`, `proxy_required`, `quota_exhausted`, `subscription_suspended`, `cart`, `review_unreadable`, `interrupted`, `cancel_requested`, `loss_limit`, and — written with status `skipped` — `multi_item_order`, `listing_over_plan_limit`, `order_already_fulfilled`, `order_not_paid`, `order_cancelled`). `TrackingConversionProvider` (`local | aquiline`, plus deprecated alias `api`) in `packages/shared/src/domain/amazon/amazon.types.ts`.

### Tracking conversion (Aquiline) — what the eBay buyer sees

Decides the tracking number pushed to eBay at the shipped transition, for every shipped order (auto-fulfill, manual link, auto cost-capture alike), resolved per order from `store_settings.tracking_conversion_provider` (Store > Global > Default).

- **`AQUILINE` is the default** (migration `112`, operator decision; existing rows backfilled). `normalizeProvider` reads an absent/unrecognised stored value as `AQUILINE` — a held order is recoverable, an exposed supplier is not. `LOCAL` passes the Amazon number through under its real carrier (`Amazon_Logistics`, `UPS`, `USPS`, `FedEx`, `DHL_Express`) — honest, but reveals the supplier. `AQUILINE` converts it to an `AQUA…YQ` number under carrier `AQUILINE` (`AQUILINE_EBAY_CARRIER_CODE`).
- **eBay accepts carrier `AQUILINE`** — verified in a live eBay Add-Tracking dialog (2026-08-11). Do not reintroduce the "eBay deprecated Aquiline validation" claim without a live screenshot showing rejection.
- **A tracking number is NEVER fabricated.** Inventing a carrier number for a parcel that carrier never handled is fraud. Aquiline issues a number it actually tracks; `LocalTrackingConverter` is the honest pass-through.

**Which carriers get converted is the SELLER's setting** (`store_settings.tracking_conversion_scope`, migration `086`, `TrackingConversionScope`), never an implicit rule in the service.
- **Default `amazon_logistics_only`** — conversions are the priced meter and a TB* number is the loudest supplier tell; `all` is opt-in.
- **`isAmazonLogisticsTracking(number, carrier)`** (`tracking-converter.ts`, pure + tested) is the single predicate, shared with `LocalTrackingConverter`'s carrier mapping; a test asserts they agree. Never add a second `TB` regex.
- **`store_settings.tracking_convert_manual_orders`** (default true): whether hand-linked orders convert automatically. "Manual" = `auto_fulfill_status !== PLACED` (same rule as `deriveFulfillmentState`), derived inside the service so no caller passes a flag.
- **`POST /v1/amazon/orders/:orderId/convert-tracking`** (`convertOnDemand`) converts one order on demand. It goes THROUGH `resolveForOrder` (already-converted returned as-is, suspension refused, quota spent) and bypasses only the scope and manual-orders rules. After success it calls `AmazonTrackingQueueService.triggerImmediateTracking` so a held order pushes at once — via the processor, never a second push implementation. The UI offers it only with an Amazon tracking number and no conversion.
- **Bulk historical linking never triggers a conversion** — `AmazonOrderSyncService` does not call the conversion service; conversion happens only at the shipped transition in `AmazonTrackingProcessorService`.

**`TrackingConversionService` owns the decision, not the converter.** The `TrackingConverter` interface is async; `AquilineTrackingConverter` is a marker that **rejects** if called directly (the Aquiline path needs the buyer address, the seller profile and durable persistence), so a bypassing caller fails loudly.

- **Persistence is the correctness fix (migration `075`)**: `orders.converted_tracking_number` / `converted_tracking_carrier` / `tracking_provider_shipment_id` / `tracking_provider` / `tracking_converted_at`. `handleShipped` rethrows on an eBay push failure so the next tick retries; a stored conversion short-circuits before any provider call, so a retry never buys a second number and never hands eBay a different one. `idx_orders_converted_tracking_number` is UNIQUE (partial) — one AQUA number can never belong to two orders.
- **Failures never throw into the shipment flow.** Suspended account, exhausted conversion quota, missing API key, incomplete buyer address, missing Amazon order id / ship-track URL, no usable profile, exhausted provider plan, or a provider error all return a pass-through **outcome**; whether that is pushed is decided by `mayPushToEbay` (below). Quota/auth/plan exhaustion logs at **error**.

**Delivery detection is NEVER stopped by a converted tracking number.** The Aquiline Integration API has **no delivery webhook** (events are `tracking.html.*` and `tracking.problem.*` only). Do not reintroduce the deleted `hasWebhookDeliveryCoverage` gate — it would strand every converted order in SHIPPED; `tracking-webhook-coverage.guard.spec.ts` locks this. Polling continues at the shipped interval via `scrapeOrderStatusWithTrackingHtml` (order status + ship-track HTML in one rate-limiter slot).
  - **Every tick on an already-SHIPPED order re-uploads that HTML** (`TrackingConversionService.refreshTrackingHtml`) — the recurring feed the provider derives carrier/delivery context from. Gated on a stored conversion; pushes nothing to eBay, spends no quota, stamps `orders.tracking_html_uploaded_at`, best-effort. On the first shipped tick `handleShipped` uploads as part of the conversion instead, so nothing double-uploads.
  - **The ship-track URL is absolutized and persisted.** `resolveTrustedAmazonTrackingUrl` returns a trusted ABSOLUTE url or nothing (a relative one is rejected by the provider as `tracking_url_mismatch` after the conversion is paid for); the processor writes it to `orders.amazon_tracking_url`, which `convertOnDemand` reads when it has no live page.

**Pre-flight fixes before the first live conversion (2026-09-29; verified live 2026-09-30, see the status paragraph below).** Three defects found by reading the shipped-transition path against the first real order, each locked by a test in `tracking-conversion.spec.ts` / `amazon-order-parser.spec.ts`: (1) `TrackingConversionService.resolveSettings` filtered `store_settings` on a column that does not exist (the table's store key is `store_id`, which IS the eBay account id every `getResolvedSettings` caller passes) — the query threw on every call, the catch swallowed it, and the seller's real provider/scope were silently replaced by the defaults; (2) under `amazon_logistics_only` an EMPTY raw number/carrier was read as "a non-Amazon carrier the seller chose not to convert" and passed through — which marked the order shipped on eBay with NO tracking; it is now `PASSTHROUGH_RETRYABLE` (held until a number is read); (3) the tracking number and carrier are read from the **progress-tracker page's visible text** in `scrapeOrderStatusWithTrackingHtml` (that is where Amazon prints "Tracking ID"; order-details rarely does), and `detectAmazonStatusLine` reads the delivery heading ("Shipped", "Out for delivery", "Delivered …", "Arriving …") off either page's visible text when no status selector matches — anchored to the START of a short line so the "Cancel items" / "Return or replace items" buttons can never read as a status. Aquiline state at that time (read-only probe from the api container): token valid, Starter plan active with 300/300 remaining, one orphan profile (`dfdVN3dlo4tmtOiC`, 9 slots left), **no webhook registered** (optional — it only carries problem codes; register with `aquiline:probe --register-webhook <url>` if wanted).

**The progress tracker's status card is the shipped signal, and the ETA heading is NOT a status (first shipped order 03-15243-67997, 2026-09-30).** Three tracking ticks logged `Amazon status=pending` on an order Amazon had shipped, so `handleShipped` and the Aquiline conversion never ran and eBay was never marked shipped. Both Amazon pages print the ETA heading ("Arriving tomorrow") FIRST and keep it there after the parcel ships; `detectAmazonStatusLine` returned `pending` on that first line and never reached the "Shipped" heading under it. Verified on two saved tracker pages (a shipped and an unshipped order, visible text reproduced in `amazon-order-parser.spec.ts`): the layout is promise card `<h1 class="pt-promise-main-slot">Arriving …</h1>` → status card `<h1 class="pt-status-main-status">Shipped|Ordered</h1>` (+ `.pt-status-secondary-status`, "Package left the shipper facility") → the milestone list `.pt-status-milestones` whose four labels **Ordered · Shipped · Out for delivery · Delivered are printed on EVERY tracker page, reached or not**. Rules now: `AmazonOrderParserService.parseTrackerStatus` reads `.pt-status-main-status` on the tracker page and `doScrapeOrderStatusWithTrackingHtml` prefers it over the visible text (still only when order-details read `pending`); in `detectAmazonStatusLine` an "Arriving …"/"Now expected …" line is a DATE line that is remembered and skipped, and the first exact `Ordered` line ENDS the scan (returns `pending`) so a milestone label can never be read as a status — a false `shipped` is an irreversible eBay push plus a paid conversion. The page-state JSON in the tracker's `<script type="a-state">` (`shortStatus: IN_TRANSIT|ORDER_PLACED`, `lastReachedMilestone`) is a further structured source if the class names ever change. Saved Amazon pages carry the buyer's name and address — never commit one verbatim; reproduce the visible-text ORDER with the PII removed.

**THE RAW AMAZON TRACKING NUMBER IS NEVER PUSHED TO eBAY WHEN A CONVERSION WAS EXPECTED (operator decision, 2026-09-01).** eBay's Fulfillment API has no update endpoint, so the first `createShippingFulfillment` is what the buyer sees forever. `mayPushToEbay(outcome)` (`packages/shared/src/domain/amazon/tracking-provider.types.ts`) is the single rule over `ConversionOutcome`:
  - `CONVERTED` → push (a real AQUA number).
  - `PASSTHROUGH_NOT_REQUIRED` → push. The seller's own choice: provider `local`, carrier outside scope, or manual conversion off.
  - `PASSTHROUGH_FAILED` / `PASSTHROUGH_RETRYABLE` → **HOLD**. The order is not marked shipped on eBay at all.

  Nothing gives up: `DEFERRAL_WINDOW_HOURS` (12h from `orders.shipped_detected_at`, `tracking-deferral.ts`) only sets retry intensity — hourly inside it, then the normal shipped cadence. Genuine eBay API failures still propagate as before. The cost is deliberate (a late-shipment defect / INR case), so `ActionCenterItemKey.ORDER_TRACKING_CONVERSION_HELD` is **CRITICAL**, detected as `shipped_detected_at IS NOT NULL AND ebay_tracking_pushed_at IS NULL` past a 12h grace. There is deliberately **no** "push the raw number anyway" override (considered and declined by the operator). Recovery is the on-demand conversion. `raw-tracking-never-pushed.guard.spec.ts` locks all of it.

**THE FIRST REAL CONVERSION RAN END TO END ON 2026-09-30 (order 03-15243-67997, production).** "Takibi dönüştür" at 21:40 UTC: `upsertOrders` accepted, `assign` returned `AQUAA0359110926YQ` (`reused=false`, `planUsed=1/300`), persisted before eBay; the 23:34 UTC tick pushed it as carrier `AQUILINE` (`ebay_tracking_pushed_number` = the AQUA number, order `shipped`) after fetching the order line item id (see "eBay Fulfillment API"), and the next tick re-uploaded the tracking HTML (`tracking_html_uploaded_at` 23:34:50). The operator confirmed the AQUA number is what eBay shows. Steps 1–5 of the verification plan below are observed; 6 (delivery detection by polling) and 7 (webhook — none is registered) are not yet. The 2026-08-26 status that follows is kept as history of what was verified before any order existed.

**History (status as of 2026-08-26).** An Aquiline **Starter** plan was bought 2026-08-23 ($42/mo, 300 shipments, 10 seller profiles, $0.14/shipment). Verified live: base URL, `Bearer {tokenId}.{tokenSecret}` auth, `GET /v1/me` shape, the error body's machine `code`, and no 429/rate-limit headers on 15 rapid GETs. Confirmed in writing by support (2026-08-26): webhook envelope, billing definitions, no enforced rate limits, profiles are permanent. **Never observed**: a real `upsertOrders` → `uploadTrackingHtml` → `assign` sequence (there is no sandbox and no live eBay order has gone through the platform), so its response envelopes and every webhook path are written against a documented contract. **Do not describe any of it as working**; confirm with the operator before claiming otherwise.
- **Testable without the provider**: the decision layer — scope, manual switch, entitlement, monthly quota, `mayPushToEbay`, the on-demand endpoint.
- **Unproven**: whether `upsertOrders` accepts our body, whether the uploaded HTML parses, whether `assign` succeeds right after an `accepted` upload, and the exact AQUA number. All isolated behind `AquilineClient`; a failure is a held order, never a wrong number sent to a buyer.
- **Watch when live**: whether `assign` lags `accepted` HTML uploads long enough to push orders past eBay handling time (would show as `ORDER_TRACKING_CONVERSION_HELD`).

**Verification plan (first live order)** — checked in order; 1–5 observed on 2026-09-30 in production, 6–7 pending:
1. A profile appears in the Aquiline dashboard as `sh-{userId}-{marketplace}` and in `aquiline_profiles`.
2. The order upserts and its HTML is `accepted` (`orders.aquiline_order_synced_at` / `tracking_html_uploaded_at` stamped).
3. `assign` returns an `AQUA…YQ` number, `planUsed` +1, persisted to `orders.converted_tracking_number` BEFORE eBay is called.
4. eBay accepts carrier `AQUILINE`; `orders.ebay_tracking_pushed_number` records exactly what it received.
5. **Amazon polling CONTINUES** at the shipped interval, re-uploading HTML each tick — an order that stops being polled after conversion is a defect.
6. **Delivery is detected by that polling**, never by a push; only then is the scheduler removed.
7. A `tracking.html.applied` webhook lands in `tracking_webhook_events` with `outcome = 'applied'` and `tracking_webhook_raw_captures.parsed_ok = true`. A `parsed_ok = false` row means the envelope changed — read its `body` before touching the parser.

### The webhook receiver (payload shape CONFIRMED 2026-08-26, migration `091`)

The provider's OpenAPI is 3.0.3 (no `webhooks:` section), so support's answer is the sole authority for the envelope — do not "correct" it against the spec.

```json
{ "event": "tracking.html.applied", "createdAt": "…", "data": { "profileId": "…", "orderId": "…", "outcome": "applied" } }
```

- `data` always carries `profileId` + `orderId`, where **`orderId` is the Amazon order id** we sent to `upsertOrders` (`orders.amazon_order_id`) — the join key.
- **The AQUA number is never in a webhook.** Read `GET /v1/profiles/{profileId}/orders/{orderId}` → `aquilineNumber` if needed. Migration `091` therefore dropped `tracking_webhook_events.tracking_number`'s `NOT NULL` and re-keyed idempotency on `(provider, profile_id, marketplace_order_id, event_type, occurred_at)`.
- Headers: `X-Webhook-Event` and `X-Webhook-Signature: sha256=<hex>` (HMAC-SHA256 over the raw body, secret `AQUILINE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`).
- Delivery: **4 attempts max** (1s / 5s / 20s, 8s timeout each), then dropped permanently — the receiver must be idempotent AND fast.
- `problemCode` appears on `tracking.problem.opened`, `tracking.html.rejected`, and optionally on a degraded `tracking.html.accepted`; `tracking.problem.cleared` carries `previousProblemCode`.

**Only `tracking.html.applied` clears a problem, never a bare `accepted`** (`decideTrackingProblem`, tested against the provider's verbatim examples). **An unknown problem code sets the flag but stores NULL** — a raw provider code must never reach a seller. **Matching is scoped by profile** (`findOrder` joins `aquiline_profiles`), because two sellers can hold the same Amazon order id.

`orders.tracking_problem_code` feeds the Action Center's `ORDER_TRACKING_PROBLEM` (WARNING, by code) — while a problem stands the order cannot convert, and nothing else reports it.

`tracking_webhook_raw_captures` (migration `090`) stays: `TrackingWebhookController.captureRaw` stores every signature-verified body verbatim before parsing, so a shape change shows up as `parsed_ok = false`. Needs `AQUILINE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` and a public URL (`pnpm --filter api aquiline:probe --register-webhook <url>`) — not reachable from local dev.

**The v3 partner API is the wrong surface — do not bring back `buildConversionBody`, `POST /v3/shipments`, the v3 base URL, `X-API-Key` or `X-Partner-Id`.** v3 is a courier-booking API with no field for an inbound tracking number (confirmed by Aquiline support 2026-08-13). The real surface is the subscriber-only **Integration API** (`Bearer {tokenId}.{tokenSecret}`), where a conversion is four calls — profile → `orders/upsert` → `tracking-html` → `assign` — driven by the ship-track page URL + HTML. Contract: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-23-aquiline-integration-api-design.md`.

**Plan limits (confirmed by the provider 2026-08-26):**
- **Shipments** — the ONLY thing that consumes plan usage: one successful NEW `assign`. Our `tracking_conversions_per_month` quota exists to stay under it.
- **Trackings** — not our meter (lookups of shipments created by *other* users). `upsertOrders`, HTML uploads, status updates, webhooks and GETs on our own orders cost nothing, so the recurring HTML feed is free.
- **Seller profiles** — 10/25/50/100/250 by tier, permanent, counted only via `GET /v1/profiles` (what `AquilineProfileService` does).
- **A re-assign returning `reused: true` is FREE** (read defensively — the field is in no published schema), so retries and the deferral cannot double-bill.
- Capacity: at 500 users × ~50 orders/month (~25,000 assigns, ~500 profiles) the largest published tier (5,000 / 250) needs a custom arrangement.
- **The provider's usage window is its own subscription period** (`windowKey = currentPeriodStart`), offset from our Stripe-period quota window; `isPlanExhausted` therefore reads the provider's `planRemaining` from a TTL'd snapshot (`aquiline_plan_snapshot`), never infers it from our counters.
- **Rate limits: none enforced** (confirmed); pace at ~1–2 req/s sustained. `AquilineClient` treats 429 as retryable transport. No client-side limiter — uploads ride the Amazon scrape, capped by `AMAZON_GLOBAL_CONCURRENCY`.

`store_settings.tracking_provider_profile_id` is vestigial — the profile lives on `aquiline_profiles`, keyed by user × marketplace.

Key files (`apps/api/src/modules/amazon/`): `tracking-converter.ts` (interface, `isAmazonLogisticsTracking`, `LocalTrackingConverter`, `AquilineTrackingConverter` marker, `resolveConverter`), `tracking-conversion.service.ts` (orchestration, persistence), `tracking-deferral.ts`, `aquiline.client.ts` (HTTP, typed `AquilineErrorKind`, 4xx never retried), `aquiline-profile.service.ts` / `aquiline-profile.helpers.ts`, `aquiline-plan-snapshot.sql.ts`, `tracking-webhook.{controller,service,helpers}.ts` (+ `.helpers.spec.ts`).

### Amazon Scraping — Anti-Ban Strategy
- **Session isolation**: one persistent Chromium user-data-dir per account at `${BROWSER_STATE_DIR || $CWD/.browser-state}/profiles/{accountId}/` via `chromium.launchPersistentContext`, cached in-process by `BrowserStateManager.getContext`. Legacy `.browser-state/{accountId}.json` sessions are migrated on first launch (cookies only, errors swallowed).
- **Idle eviction (memory bound)**: each context is a Chromium process (~150–300 MB RSS). A sweep (`BROWSER_CONTEXT_SWEEP_INTERVAL_MS`, default 2 min) closes contexts with `context.pages().length === 0` **and** untouched longer than `BROWSER_CONTEXT_IDLE_TTL_MS` (default 10 min). The zero-page guard is non-negotiable — in-flight work always holds ≥1 page.
- **Profile disk GC** (`BrowserProfileGcService`, daily, first run 10 min after boot; policy in pure, tested `browser-profile-gc.ts`) bounds DISK (unbounded profiles were 50–250 GB at 500 accounts).
  - **A cache prune never costs a login.** Amazon's session is cookies in `Default/Network/Cookies` + the key in profile-root `Local State`. `CHROMIUM_DISPOSABLE_CACHE_PATHS` is an allowlist; the spec asserts no disposable entry **is or is an ancestor of** a `CHROMIUM_SESSION_CRITICAL_PATHS` entry (e.g. listing `Default/Network` would delete `Cookies`). **Never add a disposable path without re-reading that test.**
  - **Full removal only for an orphan** (account gone) **or a profile dormant past `BROWSER_PROFILE_GC_DORMANT_DAYS`** (default 90; `0` disables dormant eviction; unparseable → default, 7-day floor). A re-login can hit a captcha/OTP.
  - **Safety**: every mutation runs inside `AmazonRateLimiter.schedule(accountId, …)` with a liveness re-check inside the lock; a failed `amazon_accounts` read **aborts the sweep** (never treat a DB outage as "no accounts"); `hasLiveContext` outranks everything.
  - Panel-tunable at `/admin` → Settings → Data retention & disk (`amazon.browserProfileGc.*`).
- **Fingerprint isolation**: deterministic per-account user-agent/viewport/timezone from `hashCode(accountId)`.
- **Proxy: optional, self-service, per Amazon account** (migration `080`: `proxy_enabled`, `proxy_connection_type` (`ProxyConnectionType`: `http|https|socks5`), `proxy_host`, `proxy_port`, `proxy_username`, `proxy_password` AES-256-GCM `enc:`). Set in `AmazonAccountDrawer`'s Proxy section — never from admin or env. No proxy = bare-IP, the expected default; the platform pays for no proxies.
  - `BrowserStateManager.resolveProxy(accountId)` reads the account's own row and returns `null` (bare-IP) when disabled, incomplete, missing, or on DB error. There is no platform fallback.
  - **Auto-fulfill is not gated on a proxy.** The only proxy check (`proxy_required`) fires when the account has `proxy_enabled=true` but the context did not launch through it (`!browserState.isProxyActive(accountId)`) — it protects the seller's explicit choice from silently degrading to bare-IP.
  - The retired platform pool (`proxies` table, `PROXY_ENDPOINT`/`PROXY_USER`/`PROXY_PASS_TEMPLATE`/`PROXY_STRATEGY`, `proxy.service.ts`) is gone; the table was dropped by migration `094`. Do not reintroduce a platform-paid pool — its cost scaled with active users and could not be passed on.
- **Rate limiting** (Bottleneck via `AmazonRateLimiter.schedule(accountId, …)`):
  - Global: `AMAZON_GLOBAL_CONCURRENCY` (code default 5; **the production compose defaults it to 20 since 2026-09-29**, test stays at 5) — a resource guard and the platform throughput ceiling (`maxConcurrent × 86,400` browser-seconds/day: 5 ⇒ ~432k ≈ 20k–26k orders/month, 20 ⇒ ~4× that), not the anti-ban control. Since automatic orders are unlimited on every plan it is the ONLY thing bounding order throughput. Lower `AMAZON_ORDER_SYNC_CRON` before raising it (far cheaper than the ~150–300 MB RSS per unit), and raise `API_MEMORY_LIMIT` with it.
  - Per account: 1 concurrent, 3 s between requests, 20/minute. Exponential backoff on 429/captcha/block.
  - **Invariant**: every browser action goes through `schedule(accountId, …)` so two `launchPersistentContext` calls never race the SingletonLock on one user-data-dir.
- **Session reuse**: cookies restored on repeat visits; full login only when the session is not proven valid.
- **Stealth**: `playwright-extra` + `puppeteer-extra-plugin-stealth`.
- **2FA**: TOTP via `otplib` from the stored (mandatory) secret.

### eBay Fulfillment API
- `createShippingFulfillment(accessToken, ebayOrderId, lineItemId, quantity, { trackingNumber, shippingCarrierCode, shippedDate? })` (`EbayFulfillmentService`) marks the order shipped on eBay; called only from `AmazonTrackingProcessorService.handleShipped`. **`lineItemId` is the ORDER LINE ITEM id (`getOrders` → `lineItems[].lineItemId`), never the listing's legacy item id** — the first live conversion (03-15243-67997, 2026-09-30) sent `listings.ebay_item_id` and eBay answered 400 "Invalid line item id", so the paid AQUA number never reached the buyer. Order sync stores it as `orders.ebay_line_item_id` (migration `131`, COALESCE-filled like `ebay_legacy_item_id`); `handleShipped` → `resolveLineItemId` reads the column and, for an order ingested before `131`, makes ONE `getOrder` read and remembers it — BEFORE the conversion, so a missing id can never spend a paid conversion. eBay reporting no line item throws (next tick retries); the listing id is never substituted. `amazon-tracking-line-item.spec.ts` + the `ebay-order-changes.spec.ts` source guard lock it.
- **Read before write (2026-10-01).** `handleShipped` calls `EbayFulfillmentService.fetchShippingFulfillments` (`GET /order/{orderId}/shipping_fulfillment`, budget-governed) right after the line item id is resolved and BEFORE the tracking conversion. `findFulfillmentForLineItem` (`orders/existing-fulfillment.ts`, pure + tested): a fulfillment that names the line item (or lists none) means eBay already has it — its tracking number is recorded as `ebay_tracking_pushed_number`, no conversion is bought and nothing is posted. This covers a POST that timed out after eBay accepted it, a local write that failed after a successful POST, and a seller who marked the order shipped on eBay by hand. A read that fails throws (next tick retries); a 404 on the collection is read as "none" so an unexpected answer cannot hold every push for ever. **The read was verified live on 2026-10-01** (read-only, production): an order with no fulfillment answers HTTP 200 `{ total: 0, fulfillments: [] }`, and the shipped order 03-15243-67997 answers one entry carrying `lineItems[].lineItemId` and the AQUA number we pushed. Not yet exercised: the whole shipped transition with this read in front of it.
- Tracking number + carrier are sent only together.
- What it sends is decided by tracking conversion: a real Aquiline number, or the real Amazon number when the seller chose no conversion — never a fabricated number, and never the raw number when a conversion was expected (`mayPushToEbay`). POST-only: eBay cannot update it afterwards.

### Buyer Auto-Messaging (settings-driven, optional)

Automated, template-driven buyer messages on four order lifecycle events, configured per store in Store Settings (global + per-eBay-store, same resolution as `amazonTaxRate`/`autoFulfillEnabled`). Per event: enable/disable + template choice. This subsystem is outbound-only — it has no inbox or reply. ("No inbox" is scoped to auto-messaging only: the seller's actual inbox — reading and replying to real buyer/eBay conversations — is the separate "eBay Messages" feature below.)

- **Events**: `order_received` (genuine insert; skipped for already-fulfilled orders), `shipped` (tracking shipped transition — eBay already notifies on ship, so opt-in with a distinct default tone), `delivered`, `feedback_request` (delayed job, N days after delivered).
- **Config**: `store_settings.buyer_messaging` JSONB (`{ enabled, events: { [event]: { enabled, template: { kind: 'system'|'custom', id }, delayDays? } } }`), store row overrides global. Migration `054` (column + `buyer_message_templates` + `buyer_message_log`).
- **Only TRACKED orders are messaged (operator decision, 2026-10-03)** — an order linked to one of the seller's listings (`orders.listing_id IS NOT NULL`). A store connected with sales made through another tool must not have its buyers messaged by SellerHill too. `OrderSyncService` does not enqueue `order_received` for an untracked sale, and `BuyerMessageProcessor` re-checks at fire time (`skipped` / `order_untracked`, no provider call) — the processor is the gate, because tracking events can also reach an untracked order linked to Amazon by hand.
- **The config is the ORDER's store's, Store > Global (fixed 2026-10-03).** Both producers used to enqueue `storeId: null`, so the worker always read the GLOBAL row and a store's own messaging choice (incl. "off") was ignored. They now pass the order's `ebay_account_id` (`store_settings.store_id` is the eBay account id), and the processor falls back to `ebayAccountId` for an old job with `storeId: null`. A store row that never saved a messaging config (created by another drawer) inherits the global one in `getResolvedSettings` — before, its NULL switched messaging OFF for that store. `store-settings-resolution.spec.ts` + `buyer-message.processor.spec.ts`.
- **Opt-in is per user only** (master toggle + per-event) — there is NO env master switch. The only related setting is `BUYER_MESSAGING_FEEDBACK_DEFAULT_DELAY_DAYS` (default 3, panel-tunable).
- **Templates are one unified, DB-backed, per-user space** (migration `066`). `buyer_message_templates.is_default` marks the four starter templates; `buyer_message_system_defaults` (PK `event_type`) holds their source copy, so default wording is a data change.
  - **Lazy seeding, once**: `BuyerMessageTemplateRepository.ensureSeeded(userId)` (advisory-lock guarded) inserts the four defaults the first time `GET /v1/buyer-messaging/templates` runs for a user with zero templates — never again, so a deleted starter does not reappear. `066` backfilled existing users.
  - **A default is an ordinary template** — editable/deletable/renamable; `isDefault` only drives a badge and "Reset to default" (`POST /v1/buyer-messaging/templates/:id/reset`, restores the `body` from `buyer_message_system_defaults`, only when `is_default`).
  - **`BuyerMessageTemplateKind.SYSTEM` is legacy-only** — old configs still resolve (body read from `buyer_message_system_defaults` by event type); new saves always write `CUSTOM` pointing at a real row.
- **What the buyer reads (2026-10-02, migration `135`, operator request).** The greeting is `{{buyer_name}}` — the buyer's FIRST name (`greetingName`: `orders.buyer_name`, then the ship-to name; a lower-case first name or an all-capitals full name is re-cased, no usable name → "there"). `{{buyer_username}}` still renders in an old template but is no longer offered as a chip: an eBay username in a greeting ("Hi sadsd_42") reads as spam. **`{{tracking_number}}` is `orders.ebay_tracking_pushed_number` — the number eBay RECEIVED — and nothing else; the Amazon number is never rendered into a message** (it was, until this date: any template using the token would have handed the buyer the supplier's own number). `{{carrier}}` is the carrier that number went out under (`formatCarrierForBuyer`: `AQUILINE` → "Aquiline", `Amazon_Logistics` → "Amazon Logistics"; unknown when the fulfillment was found already on eBay). A line whose tracking number, carrier or estimated delivery resolved empty is DROPPED by `renderTemplate`, so a buyer never reads a dangling "Tracking number:" label. eBay member messages are plain text — default bodies carry no markdown. `135` rewrites the four defaults, moves every seller's still-unedited starter template to the new wording and swaps the username token for the name token in the rest. `buyer-message.processor.spec.ts` source-greps the tracking rule.
- **The `buyer_message_log` INSERT never parsed until 2026-10-02.** Its status parameter was used twice (`$7` as the enum column and in `$7='sent'`), Postgres answered "inconsistent types deduced for parameter $7", and the catch logged it at debug — so the idempotency ledger stayed EMPTY in production while messages went out, and the order timeline showed no message rows. Both uses are now `$7::buyer_message_status`, and a non-unique-violation write failure logs at warn. A parameter used twice in one statement needs an explicit cast on every use; `PREPARE` the statement against a real Postgres.
- **Pipeline**: lifecycle seam → `buyer-message` queue → `BuyerMessageProcessor` (idempotency via `buyer_message_log` partial-unique `(ebay_order_id, event_type) WHERE status='sent'`; config re-checked at fire time → `skipped`; a suspended sender's message is parked hourly and skipped after 30 days; `redactForLog` strips tokens; fail-soft `enqueue` never breaks order/tracking flows) → `BuyerMessagingProvider` port (`EbayMessageApiProvider`, eBay REST Message API `sendMessage`, token via `EbayService.getAccountAccessToken`).
- **Triggers**: `OrderSyncService` genuine insert (`order_received`); `AmazonTrackingProcessorService` transitions (`shipped`, `delivered` + delayed `feedback_request`).
- **FE**: `BuyerMessagingSection` (master toggle + 4 event cards with template picker, delayDays, tooltips) inside `StoreSettingsDrawer` step 2 — no nested Save, the drawer's Save persists both. Template management = two sibling drawers from the settings hub's Store Configuration card: `BuyerMessageTemplatesDrawer` (list, event filter, full body preview, delete via `ConfirmModal`) and `BuyerMessageTemplateDrawer` (create/edit, placeholder chips, preview, event type locked after create, "Reset to default" when `isDefault`). RTK Query: `storeSettingsApi` + `buyerMessagingApi`. i18n under `storeSettings:storeSettings.messaging.*`.
- **Key files**: `apps/api/src/modules/buyer-messaging/` (`buyer-messaging.module.ts`, `buyer-message.service.ts`, `buyer-message.processor.ts`, `buyer-message-queue.service.ts`, `buyer-message.provider.ts`, `buyer-message-template.repository.ts`, `buyer-message.controller.ts`, `buyer-message-helpers.ts` + `.spec.ts`, `buyer-messaging.constants.ts`); shared `packages/shared/src/domain/buyer-messaging/`, `packages/shared/src/schemas/buyer-messaging/`. The queue is in `ADMIN_QUEUE_NAMES` / `OBSERVED_QUEUE_NAMES`.
- **Resolved (2026-09-29)**: the provider's `sendMessage` field shape was a `// TODO(confirm)` guess against an endpoint that turned out not to exist — see "eBay Messages" below for what actually replaced it (`EbayMessageClient.sendMessage`, real Message API, `otherPartyUsername` + a `LISTING` reference).

### eBay Messages (inbox + NEW_MESSAGE webhook, 2026-09-29)

The seller's actual eBay inbox — reading and replying to real buyer↔seller and eBay-to-seller conversations — at `/:locale/messages`. Unrelated in mechanism to Buyer Auto-Messaging above (that subsystem only ever sends; this one reads eBay's own conversations), but it fixed the one bug auto-messaging always carried: `EbayMessageApiProvider.sendMessage` had been built against a made-up endpoint shape and had never actually delivered a message. It now calls the real client this feature introduced.

- **Trading was the wrong door, and the quotas say why.** eBay's older `GetMemberMessages`/`AddMemberMessageAAQToPartner` are Trading calls, metered PER METHOD against the ~5,000/day pools the rest of this file already treats as scarce (see "eBay API call budget"). The REST **Commerce Message API** (`commerce.message`) and the **Commerce Notification API** (`commerce.notification`) are separate, much larger pools — measured against the production keyset (admin eBay Limits tab, 2026-09-29): **500,000/day** and **10,000/day** respectively. `EbayApiResource.MESSAGE` / `.NOTIFICATION` are the two new budget resources; every Message API call acquires `MESSAGE`, every Notification API call acquires `NOTIFICATION`, at `EbayCallPriority.INTERACTIVE` for a seller reading/replying to a thread and `BACKGROUND` for the webhook bootstrap and the buyer-auto-messaging provider — same interactive/background split as every other eBay resource.
- **Two new OAuth scopes, granted together, and a store connected before 2026-09-29 does not have them.** `commerce.message` (read/reply) and `commerce.notification.subscription` (manage the webhook subscription) are added to `EBAY_OAUTH_CONSTANTS.DEFAULT_SCOPES`. `ebay_accounts.granted_scopes` (migration `125`, `TEXT[]`) records the scopes the app REQUESTED at that consent (`oauthService.getScopes()`), not a value eBay returns — written at connect/reconnect from the OAuth service, never a hardcoded list — and `EbayAccountPublicDto.messagingEnabled` is derived from it via `hasMessagingScopes()` (`packages/shared`). A pre-existing store's `granted_scopes` is empty, so `messagingEnabled` is `false` until the seller reconnects; the Messages page shows a reconnect prompt instead of calling the Message API for an account it knows will 403.
  - **A same-owner callback is a RE-CONSENT, whatever the row's status.** The reconnect prompt sends a seller whose store is still ACTIVE through eBay consent; `handleCallback` used to refuse any non-disconnected row with `accountAlreadyConnected`, so that button dead-ended. Now, when the existing `(seller_id, marketplace_id)` row belongs to the calling user, the callback runs the reactivate `UPDATE` (tokens, expiry, `ACTIVE`, `disconnected_at = NULL`, `ebay_username`, `store_name`, `granted_scopes`) and re-subscribes. `accountAlreadyConnected` (409) is only for a row of ANOTHER user that is not disconnected; another user's disconnected row is still `storeOwnedByAnotherAccount`. The subscribe after the write is fire-and-forget (`void`), so the redirect back from eBay never waits on the Notification API. `ebay-messaging-lifecycle.guard.spec.ts` locks both. The Action Center's `EBAY_ACCOUNT_MESSAGING_SCOPE_MISSING` item (INFO — nothing is broken, a capability just isn't turned on yet) surfaces exactly this gap and links to `/stores`.
- **Nothing about a message's CONTENT is ever stored locally — every list/thread read hits eBay live, with that store's own token.** The one thing kept in our own tables is the unread COUNT: `ebay_accounts.unread_message_count` is incremented by the NEW_MESSAGE webhook, decremented by −1 on `POST …/conversations/:id/read` (a best guess pending the next recount — archiving/deleting a conversation deliberately leaves the counter alone, since whether it was unread isn't known from that call alone), and recounted from eBay whenever the sidebar badge is read, nothing keeps that store's counter current (the webhook is disabled, OR the store has no `message_subscription_id`), AND the stored value is older than 15 minutes (`UNREAD_STALE_MS`) — at `EbayCallPriority.BACKGROUND`; the open Messages page's own recount (`GET /ebay/messages/unread-breakdown?ebayAccountId=`, polled every 2 min while the page is open, INTERACTIVE) counts UNREAD conversations of BOTH types from eBay (two calls), stores the sum into that same column — so it also corrects the sidebar badge — and returns it split `{ total, members, ebay }`; the folder rail's per-type "Unread N" badges (and the "(N)" in the compact type/folder switches below `xl`) read that split. The old `POST refresh-unread` endpoint remains but the web no longer calls it. So a deployment with no `EBAY_NOTIFICATION_*` env, or a store whose subscription failed, still gets a roughly-live badge, just by polling instead of pushing. **The badge counts CONVERSATIONS, like the recount:** the webhook adds +1 only for the first unread message of a conversation since the store's last recount (`received_at > unread_message_synced_at`); a further one is stored as `counted_same_conversation` and not incremented. Nothing about a message body reaches our DB except the webhook's own diagnostic `payload` column, and that is never read back into the UI.
- **Destination + subscription lifecycle**, because the eBay Notification API models this as two separate objects: one APPLICATION-level "destination" (our webhook URL, created ONCE for the whole app) and one NEW_MESSAGE "subscription" PER STORE (tied to that seller's own user token, since the subscription is what tells eBay whose messages to deliver).
  - The destination is created lazily — on `OnApplicationBootstrap`, 20 seconds after boot (`BOOTSTRAP_DELAY_MS`, so it never competes with startup), or on-demand before the first subscribe if the delayed bootstrap hasn't run yet. Its id is cached in memory and in `ebay_notification_destinations` (one row per `(environment, endpoint_url)`) so a restart never re-creates it. eBay's own `195021` ("destination already exists for this endpoint") is treated as success — the existing destination is looked up and adopted, not treated as an error.
  - The GET challenge that proves we own the endpoint is `sha256(challengeCode + verificationToken + endpointUrl)` — the exact same `computeChallengeResponse` helper the account-deletion webhook already uses (`ebay-account-deletion.helpers.ts`), reused rather than re-implemented, because the concatenation order is exactly the kind of thing that silently drifts between two copies.
  - A subscription is created (or reconciled) on every eBay OAuth connect AND reconnect, with the store's own freshly-issued user token. `195012` ("subscription for this topic already exists for this user") is not an error either — the existing subscription is listed and adopted (`stampSubscription`) so the row's `message_subscription_id` always points at something real on eBay's side. `195011` ("token lacks the scope this topic requires") means the two scopes above were not actually granted despite `granted_scopes` saying otherwise — reported as `scope_missing`, logged, and left for the next reconnect to fix; it never throws into the OAuth callback.
  - On disconnect, the subscription is deleted from eBay's side (best-effort) **BEFORE** `ebay_accounts`' tokens are nulled — the ordering is load-bearing: attempt it after and there is no credential left to call eBay with, and the subscription would keep silently delivering a stranger's future messages to an endpoint that has nothing left to do with them.
  - Every step in this lifecycle is fail-soft: `subscribeAccount`/`unsubscribeAccount` never throw, so a Notification API outage degrades to "no live badge push for this store" (the 15-minute polling fallback above still works), never a broken connect/disconnect.
- **The receiver** (`EbayNotificationWebhookController`, `GET/POST /v1/ebay/notifications`) follows the same shape as every other eBay-facing webhook in this file: public (no `JwtAuthGuard` — eBay calls with no SellerHill session). The GET challenge skips all three named trackers (`@SkipThrottle({ short: true, medium: true, long: true })` — a bare `@SkipThrottle()` only skips the unnamed `default` tracker, so with this app's named trackers it is a no-op; eBay validates once); the POST carries its own generous `@Throttle` on all three named trackers (600/min short+medium, 36,000/h long) — well above eBay's retry burst, but a ceiling on a public route where every request costs a capture row. A POST while notifications are disabled is **503** before the body is read. The raw body is captured into `ebay_notification_raw_captures` **before** any decision is made, so a future envelope-shape change shows up as `parsed_ok = false` rather than as silence. Signature verification is ECDSA (not the account-deletion webhook's HMAC): the `X-EBAY-SIGNATURE` header carries a key id (`kid`); the `kid` must match `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{8,100}$` before any lookup, the matching public key is fetched from eBay's Notification API and cached in memory for an hour per `kid`, and a `kid` eBay could not resolve is negative-cached for 10 minutes (no second eBay call inside the window — a caller replaying made-up kids cannot turn each request into a Notification API call). A missing/malformed header, a malformed or unknown `kid`, or a signature that does not verify against the raw bytes is **412** — the raw capture is recorded, nothing else (a request with no parseable signature header is captured truncated to 1 KB; a signed one up to 20 KB) — never 200, which would tell eBay the destination silently accepted something it could not actually authenticate. A verified-but-unrecognised body is **400**. A verified, well-formed NEW_MESSAGE delivery is booked through `recordDelivery` (idempotent on eBay's own `notification_id` via `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, so a retried delivery — eBay resends up to a few times — can never double-count the unread bump) and answered **204**.
- **Module layout**: `apps/api/src/modules/ebay/notifications/` owns the webhook receiver, the destination/subscription/signature-cache service (`EbayNotificationService`), the eBay HTTP client and the pure envelope/signature helpers (all Jest-covered). `apps/api/src/modules/ebay-messages/` owns the seller-facing half: `EbayMessagesService` (list/thread/reply/read/bulk-status/unread-count, all scoped to an ACTIVE store the caller owns and that holds the messaging scopes), `EbayMessageClient` (the real Commerce Message API client — `commerce/message/v1`, budget-governed), and the controller. Neither module reaches into the other's tables directly; `EbayMessagesService` depends on `EbayNotificationService` only to check `isEnabled()` for the 15-minute-fallback decision.
- **Web**: `/:locale/messages` (`features/messages/`), gated by `EbayAccountGuard` like every other data page. Folders mirror eBay's own vocabulary rather than inventing a new one: `MessagesFolder` (`packages/shared`, ALL/UNREAD/ARCHIVE) is a grouping over eBay's own `EbayConversationStatus`, each folder sending one `conversation_status` to eBay — ALL → `ACTIVE` (so archived/deleted never appear there), UNREAD → `UNREAD`, ARCHIVE → `ARCHIVE`; eBay does the filtering, only the demo fixtures filter client-side — crossed with eBay's own `EbayConversationType` (FROM_MEMBERS/FROM_EBAY), which is REQUIRED on every read (never inferred, never defaulted). The two-dimensional folder rail renders at `breakpoints.xl` and above; narrower viewports get a compact stand-in — a `TabNav` for the type and a `SegmentedControl` for the folder — instead of losing the filter entirely. Attachments render only when their URL starts with `https://`; marking a conversation unread clears it from the once-per-id mark-read guard, so reopening it marks it read again. eBay errors map to seller keys: a 403 about scope/permission/authorization → `messagingScopeMissing` (409), any other 4xx → `messagingRejected` (400), 429/5xx/transport → `messagingUnavailable` (502). In demo mode the page's own bookkeeping write (`…/read`) answers silently, without the read-only notice. **A row shows the photo of the item the buyer is writing about**: `EbayConversationDto.imageUrl` is resolved server-side from `referenceId` (eBay item id) against the store's own `listings` → `products.image_urls[0]` in one query per page (fail-soft; an item that is not one of our listings, or a thread with no item, keeps the initial-letter avatar alone); the buyer's initial becomes a small badge on the photo's corner, and an unread conversation is marked by a dot, a solid badge, semibold text and its count pill. **The inbox fits the viewport** (`AppRouteMeta.fitsViewport` on `/messages`, see the upstream layout note) and only the list and thread scroll. Every write (reply, mark read/unread, archive, delete) is sent with the URL-state `type`, never a row's own `type`, so a write can never disagree with the folder it was issued from.
- **Buyer auto-messaging never sends to a placeholder or through a store without the scopes.** The processor keeps `buyerUsername || 'there'` for TEMPLATE rendering only and addresses the raw `orders.buyer_username`; an order with none is logged `skipped` / `no_buyer_username`, and a store whose `granted_scopes` lacks the messaging scopes is logged `skipped` / `messaging_scope_missing` — both with no provider call and no BullMQ retry. The provider truncates by code point (`Array.from`), never splitting an emoji.
- **Operator steps to go live**: confirm both `commerce.message` and `commerce.notification.subscription` are enabled on the production AND sandbox keysets before deploying — a missing grant makes eBay reject every consent URL → set `EBAY_NOTIFICATION_VERIFICATION_TOKEN` and `EBAY_NOTIFICATION_ALERT_EMAIL` (nothing is pasted into the developer portal: the API sends the token to eBay itself in `createDestination`) → deploy → have each seller reconnect their eBay store (the Messages page's Reconnect button re-consents an active store in place; a pre-existing store's `granted_scopes` has neither new scope, and there is no other path that backfills them) → open `/messages`. Until both env vars are set and valid, `EbayNotificationService.isEnabled()` is false and the whole notification half is a no-op (destination bootstrap skipped, `subscribeAccount`/`unsubscribeAccount` return `'disabled'`/no-op) — the Message API side (list/reply/read) works regardless, since it does not depend on the webhook at all, just on the store having the `commerce.message` scope and on the 15-minute polling fallback for its unread badge. An `EBAY_NOTIFICATIONS_DISABLED` admin warning (parallel to `SCRAPER_NO_PROXIES` and friends) flags a deployment still running without them — it is derived from those two env values only, not from any store's subscription state.

### Key Files
- **Backend orders**: `apps/api/src/modules/orders/` — OrdersService, OrderSyncService (owns `recomputeProfit`, `maybeEnqueueAutoFulfill`, `adoptUntrackedOrdersForListing`), EbayFulfillmentService, OrderSyncQueueService, OrderSyncProcessorService, AutoFulfillQueueService. Pure: `profit-calculation.ts` (`computeNetProfit`, `deriveCostCaptureStatus`, `estimateProvisionalNetProfit`, `deriveProfitBasis`), `ebay-order-financials.ts`, with specs.
- **Backend Amazon**: `apps/api/src/modules/amazon/` — AmazonAccountsService (`assertCanEnable`, `assertTwoFactorSecret`, proxy field encryption), AmazonScrapingService, AmazonOrderParserService, AmazonTrackingQueueService, AmazonTrackingProcessorService, AmazonRateLimiter, BrowserStateManager, BrowserProfileGcService (`browser-profile-gc.ts`), AmazonVerifyProcessorService (`amazon-verify-helpers.ts`), `amazon-auth-state.ts`, `address-match.ts`; auto cost-capture: AmazonOrderSyncService / queue / scheduler / processor, `order-matcher.ts`, `pick-best-match.ts`; A2: AmazonCheckoutService, AutoFulfillProcessor, `auto-fulfill-helpers.ts`; tracking conversion: see its key-files line above.
- **Backend products**: `apps/api/src/modules/products/` — ProductsService (price/image lookup, `decrementStock`).
- **Product refresh pipeline** (provider = `product.dataProvider`, default scraper, Keepa rollback): `product-source.service.ts`, `scraper.client.ts`, `scraper-refresh.ts`, `keepa.service.ts`, `refresh-scheduler.service.ts`, `refresh-processor.service.ts`, `keepa-usage.service.ts`, `product-sync.service.ts` — see "Product Refresh Pipeline".
- **Sale-driven stock sync**: `apps/api/src/modules/orders/stock-sync-queue.service.ts` (producer) + `apps/api/src/modules/listings/stock-sync-processor.service.ts` (consumer) + `ProductSyncService.syncListingsForProduct()` + `ListingStrategyService.calculateQuantity()`.
- **Frontend orders**: `apps/web/src/features/orders/` — list, detail, carousel, `shared/` (fulfillment-state, tracking-problem mappers).
- **Frontend Amazon**: `apps/web/src/features/amazon/` — `LinkAmazonModal` + `api/amazon.api.ts`. Account management lives in `apps/web/src/features/settings/drawers/` (`AmazonAccountDrawer`, `AmazonAccountsDrawer`).
- **Shared**: `packages/shared/src/domain/orders/`, `packages/shared/src/domain/amazon/`, `packages/shared/src/schemas/orders/`, `packages/shared/src/schemas/amazon/`.
## Architectural Rules

1. **Type Safety** — Types in `packages/shared/src/domain/`. No `any`. No duplicates.
2. **Validation** — Zod schemas in `packages/shared/src/schemas/`. Backend DTOs use `class-validator` but implement shared interfaces.
3. **UI & Styling** — Emotion with semantic tokens. No inline styles. No hardcoded colors (`#fff`, `rgba(...)`, etc.), spacing (`16px`, etc.), or magic numbers. Everything must be a theme token via `tkn()`. If a new color/spacing/value is needed, add it to `packages/ui/src/theme/` (designTokens, themes, tkn paths) first. Use `Icon` component, no inline SVGs.
4. **Forms** — Use `ModernTextInput` (the `@repo/ui` export of the `TextInput` molecule). Inputs must use `React.forwardRef` and `value={value ?? ''}`.
5. **Loading & Errors** — Loading state is business logic and is computed in the container (`.container.tsx`). `useLoading(flag)` shows the global blocking overlay and takes **mutation flags only** (save/delete/bulk) — never an initial `useQuery` `isLoading`. An initial fetch passes `isLoading` to the component, which renders the page's own `EmptyState` loading variant (see "Page-level loading is not the global overlay"); components never render spinners. The only other exception is a Button atom's `isLoading` prop for its inline spinner. `showMessage` from `UIContext` for errors.
6. **Container/Component Split (strict, enforced by ESLint + PreToolUse hook)** — Every feature component is split into 4 files:
   - `[Name].component.tsx` — JSX markup only. Allowed hooks: `useTranslation`, `useTheme`. Forbidden: `useState`, `useEffect`, `useMemo`, `useCallback`, `useReducer`, `useRef`, RTK Query hooks (`useQuery`/`useMutation`/`useLazyQuery`), `useSelector`, `useDispatch`, `useNavigate`, formatters (`formatCurrency`, etc.), event handlers. Forbidden: `styled(...)`.
   - `[Name].container.tsx` — All logic. Forbidden: `styled(...)`. JSX is allowed only as `<Component .../>` return.
   - `[Name].style.ts` — All `styled(...)` calls.
   - `[Name].types.ts` — `interface`/`type`/`enum` declarations only.

   **Atoms/Molecules** (`packages/ui/src/{atoms,molecules}/`) follow the same rules. **Stateful** atoms/molecules (Select, Dropdown, Tooltip, etc.) MUST split into `.container.tsx` + `.component.tsx` like features. **Stateless** atoms/molecules (Button, Badge) stay as `.component.tsx` + `.style.ts` + `.types.ts`.

   **Exempt:** `apps/web/src/features/landing/**`, `apps/web/src/**/api/*.ts(x)`, `apps/web/src/app/store.ts`, `apps/api/**`, `*.config.{ts,js,mjs,cjs}`.

   Enforced by `design-system/styled-only-in-style-files`, `design-system/types-only-in-types-files`, `design-system/logic-only-in-container`, `design-system/no-styled-in-container` ESLint rules, and by `.claude/hooks/pretooluse-frontend-rules.js` (Write/Edit/MultiEdit).
7. **Shared Utilities** — Reusable formatting and locale utilities (e.g., `formatCurrency`, `formatCompactNumber`, `formatDate`, `getLocaleConfig`) live in `packages/ui/src/utils/`. Import from `@repo/ui`. Never define them locally in component or container files.
8. **Design System Only (no custom UI primitives)** — All UI primitives (inputs, selects, checkboxes, buttons, modals, dropdowns, toggles, date pickers, etc.) MUST come from `packages/ui` (atoms or molecules). Never use native HTML elements (`<select>`, `<input>`, `<button>`, etc.) or build custom form controls directly in feature code. If a needed component doesn't exist in the design system, create it there first as an atom/molecule, then use it. This ensures consistency and reusability across all screens.
9. **MessageModal for success/error/info/warning messages** — All informational and error messages MUST use the `MessageModal` molecule via `showMessage` from `UIContext`. Never use native `alert()`/`confirm()`, create custom modal implementations, or bypass this pattern. `MessageModal` provides consistent styling with type-appropriate icons (success=check-circle, error=x-circle, warning=alert-triangle, info=info) and proper button handling.
10. **No hardcoded status/constant strings** — All status values, type discriminators, and constant strings MUST be defined as enums in `packages/shared/src/domain/`. Never use string literals like `'active'`, `'draft'`, `'custom'`, `'predefined'`, etc. directly in code. Use the corresponding enum (e.g., `ListingStatus.ACTIVE`, `TemplateType.CUSTOM`, `PolicyType.PAYMENT`, `OrderStatus.SHIPPED`, `EbayAccountStatus.ACTIVE`). If a new constant is needed, create or extend an enum in the shared package first.
11. **No hardcoded UI strings — all text via i18n** — Every user-visible string (labels, buttons, messages, placeholders, tooltips, table headers, empty states, counts, etc.) MUST use i18n `t()` with keys defined under `packages/shared/src/i18n/resources/<locale>/`. Never write raw text like `<Text>Clear</Text>` or `{listings.length} results` directly in JSX. The only exceptions are: (a) universal symbols like `—` (em dash) used as empty-value placeholders, (b) numeric/format values produced by formatters (`formatCurrency`, `formatDate`, etc.), and (c) string literals passed to `t()` as keys. If a new string is needed, add the key to **every** locale folder (all 16 in `SUPPORTED_LOCALES` — see "Supported languages") first, then use `t('namespace.key')` in the component. The one exception is the `admin` and `legal` namespaces, which exist for `en` + `tr` only.
12. **Keep CLAUDE.md up to date** — When changes are made that affect project conventions, architecture, commands, patterns, or rules, CLAUDE.md MUST be updated to reflect the current state. This includes new packages, changed file paths, updated tooling, new architectural patterns, or modified workflows. Do not let CLAUDE.md become stale.
13. **Mobile-Web Responsiveness** — Every new page, feature, or component must render correctly down to a 360px-wide viewport. Use `theme.breakpoints.*` (`sm`/`md`/`lg`/`xl` and their `*Below` complements) via `tkn('breakpoints.*')` — never a hardcoded `@media` literal or a raw `window.innerWidth` comparison (for the rare JS-side, non-CSS threshold, use an exported constant like `SIDEBAR_MOBILE_BREAKPOINT_PX` — never a second inline magic number). Follow the codebase's existing patterns:
    - Multi-column layouts collapse to a single column at `breakpoints.md`/`mdBelow` (see `SettingsHubPage.style.ts` `TwoColGrid`, `ListingDetailPage.style.ts` `SectionGrid`).
    - Card grids use `DataTable.style.ts`'s `GridContainer` `minmax(min(100%, ...), 1fr)` pattern, never a fixed `repeat(N, 1fr)`.
    - Wide/tabular content that can't reflow gets its own local `overflow-x: auto` container (see `Table.style.ts` `OverflowWrapper`, `ActionCenterPage.style.ts` `Toolbar`) — never left to overflow the page.
    - A horizontal image+content card must stack to `flex-direction: column` below `breakpoints.sm`/`smBelow` once fixed-width children can't coexist with reflowing content at that width (see `OrderCard.style.ts`).
    - Overlays follow `Drawer`/`Modal`'s existing mobile behavior: `Drawer` goes full-width below `breakpoints.md`; a multi-button `Modal` footer stacks to full-width buttons below `breakpoints.md`, keeping the same visual order as desktop.
    - Popover-style menus (`Dropdown`, `Select`) render as a bottom sheet below 640px width, not a shrunk floating popover — see `Select`'s `isMobile`/`createPortal`/`Overlay`+`BottomSheet` pattern, mirrored by `Dropdown`. Reuse that pattern for any new popover/menu atom rather than a third implementation.
    - Fixed bottom action bars reserve `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` in their bottom padding (see `MobileActionBar` in `ListingDetailPage.style.ts`/`OrderDetailsPage.style.ts`).
    - `apps/web/src/index.css` carries a global `overflow-x: hidden` safety net on `html`/`body` — do not rely on it instead of giving wide content its own scroll container; it exists to catch accidents, not as a first line of defense.
    - Verify at a 375px-wide viewport (browser devtools device emulation) before calling a UI change done.

## Environment Setup

1. `pnpm docker:up` (PostgreSQL on :5432, Redis on :6379, plus pgAdmin, the Amazon scraper service and Loki/Promtail/Grafana)
2. Copy `apps/api/.env.example` to `apps/api/.env` and fill in values
3. Copy `apps/web/.env.example` to `apps/web/.env` (defaults work for local dev)
4. `pnpm dev`

API: `http://localhost:3000` (Swagger at `/api/docs`). Web: Vite dev server (default :5173).

## System Requirements & Deployment (local / test / prod)

SellerHill runs the same code in every environment — only config (env vars) and capacity differ. Three runtimes: **local** (developer machine), **test** (Coolify on a VPS), **prod**. There is no environment-specific code path.

### Shared stack (all environments)

| Component | Purpose | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **PostgreSQL 16** | primary DB (`pg` driver, no ORM) | schema in `docker/postgres/init.sql` + `apps/api/migrations/`; auto-run on API boot. Must apply to a stock (non-pgvector) Postgres — see "Migrations" |
| **Redis 7** | BullMQ job queues | `order-sync`, `stock-sync`, `amazon-*`, `auto-fulfill`, `keepa-refresh`, etc. |
| **Node 20+ / pnpm 9+** | api + web + packages | monorepo workspace |
| **Playwright Chromium** | Amazon order scraping + A2 checkout | headless; needs OS libs (see below) |
| **Amazon scraper service** | product data provider (`services/amazon-scraper`, Python) | reached over `SCRAPER_SERVICE_URL` + `SCRAPER_SERVICE_SECRET`; needs proxies — see "Scraper provider" |
| **(A2) Self-service proxy** | Amazon anti-ban for buyer accounts | Optional, per Amazon buyer account, user-supplied from the Amazon Account drawer (migration `080`). Not platform-provided — no admin provisioning, no env fallback. |

**Playwright/Chromium OS deps** (Linux servers, not needed on local Docker which bundles them): `libnss3 libatk1.0-0 libatk-bridge2.0-0 libcups2 libxkbcommon0 libxcomposite1 libxdamage1 libxfixes3 libxrandr2 libgbm1 libpango-1.0-0 libcairo2 libasound2`. Install once: `npx playwright install --with-deps chromium`.

### Local dev

- **Machine:** your PC. **8 GB RAM minimum** (Chromium for scraping is the heaviest single process).
- **Services:** `pnpm docker:up` runs everything in Docker — nothing else to install.
- **A2 here:** runs bare-IP by default (no proxy required to enable auto-fulfill). To exercise scraping/checkout through a proxy, enter one in the Amazon Account drawer's Proxy section for a real Amazon buyer account, then use **dry-run mode** on the account.
- No GPU needed — Playwright is CPU-bound and the LLM is a hosted API call.

### Test (Coolify / VPS)

- **Current Hostinger VPS:** 4 vCPU, 16 GB RAM, 200 GB disk, **no GPU** — runs api + web + scraper + postgres + redis + Playwright. Adequate for test.
- Postgres + Redis run as Coolify resources/containers; api + web as Coolify services behind the Coolify reverse proxy.
- A proxy for A2 dry-run/live checkout is configured per Amazon buyer account from its Account drawer — nothing is provisioned at the platform/admin level.
- Add real Amazon buyer accounts (encrypted at rest via `AMAZON_ENCRYPTION_KEY`).
- 16 GB is comfortable for a handful of accounts; idle-eviction (`BROWSER_CONTEXT_IDLE_TTL_MS`) bounds resident Chromium so even ~10–20 accounts won't pile up.

### Prod

- **PostgreSQL 16** (managed recommended) + **Redis 7**, both with persistence + backups.
- **API + Web** behind a reverse proxy (Coolify / Nginx / Caddy) with TLS.
- **CPU:** 4+ vCPU (Playwright is CPU-bound; the per-account rate limiter caps concurrency, but headroom matters).
- **RAM — the key dimension, driven by `AMAZON_GLOBAL_CONCURRENCY`, not by account count:** ~150–300 MB per resident Chromium context + ~1–1.5 GB base (api + web + Postgres + Redis). Idle-eviction bounds resident contexts by **active** concurrency, not total account count. **Minimum 16 GB; 32 GB for multi-tenant scale.** Set OOM/alerting on memory. **Production defaults are `AMAZON_GLOBAL_CONCURRENCY=20` and `API_MEMORY_LIMIT=12G` (2026-09-29, `docker-compose.production.yml`; the host has 128 GB)** — raised from 5 / 6G because automatic orders became unlimited, so the pool is the only thing bounding order throughput. Raise the two together (~300 MB per unit of concurrency); test stays at 5 / 4G.
- **Disk:** every large consumer is TTL-bounded, but size for the steady state. At ~500 accounts: Postgres 60–100 GB in year 1 (mostly `products.raw_keepa_data`; append-only logs held by the `data-retention` job), `.browser-state/profiles/` bounded by `BrowserProfileGcService`, `fulfillment-evidence/` ~15 GB at the 7-day TTL, Loki ~10–20 GB at 7 days, `apps/api/logs/` a few GB gzipped. **Budget 1 TB NVMe at 500 accounts**; 50 GB only covers a handful.
- **Buyer-account proxying is optional and self-service** — the platform provisions and pays for nothing there. (The scraper's own proxy list is a separate, mandatory, operator-configured setting — see "Scraper provider".)
- **GPU:** not required and not planned.

### Secrets / env checklist (per environment)

- `AMAZON_ENCRYPTION_KEY` — 32-byte hex (AES-256-GCM for buyer-account credentials and eBay tokens). **Required wherever amazon_accounts are used.**
- JWT/auth secrets (access + refresh-cookie signing).
- `KEEPA_API_KEY` (Keepa product provider / rollback).
- `SCRAPER_SERVICE_URL` + `SCRAPER_SERVICE_SECRET` (scraper provider).
- eBay app credentials.
- `CORS_ORIGINS` / `COOKIE_DOMAIN` / `COOKIE_SAMESITE` (prod web origin). `main.ts` resolves CORS as `CORS_ORIGINS` → legacy `CORS_ORIGIN` → `FRONTEND_URL` → `http://localhost:5173`, and the Coolify compose files default `${CORS_ORIGINS:-${FRONTEND_URL}}` as well. Keep the fallback in code: a Coolify env row left **blank** reaches `process.env` as `''` and can bypass the compose-level default, which once rejected every real browser origin.
- A2 tunables (all optional, defaults safe): `AUTO_FULFILL_*`, `BROWSER_CONTEXT_*`, `FULFILLMENT_EVIDENCE_*`, Keepa refresh tunables.

**Env vs admin panel.** The list above is the complete set of things that MUST be env: connection bootstrap, crypto keys, provider credentials, process identity. Operational tuning (refresh cadence + kill switch, AI content toggle, quota enforcement, SMTP host/user/password, order-matching tolerances, evidence retention, admin warning thresholds, scraper proxies) is edited at `/admin` → Settings and stored in `platform_settings`; env values there are only the fallback when no override row exists. Prefer the panel — it needs no redeploy and is audit-logged.

**Compose files.** `docker-compose.yml` = local dev services (Postgres/Redis/pgAdmin/scraper/Loki/Promtail/Grafana — no LLM service; see "Provider/config"). `docker-compose.test.yml` = Coolify test/sandbox (eBay sandbox endpoints, debug logging, concurrency 1). `docker-compose.production.yml` = Coolify production (eBay production endpoints). The two Coolify files build the same images from the same code and differ only in configuration; both mount `browser_state` + `fulfillment_evidence` + `api_logs` volumes, define a `/api/v1/health/live` healthcheck (the API is URI-versioned, so `/api/health/live` is a 404 — that path once left the production container permanently `unhealthy` while it served fine), and carry memory limits (`API_MEMORY_LIMIT`, `SCRAPER_MEMORY_LIMIT`) sized for resident Chromium contexts.

### A2 operational checklist (before enabling real-money auto-fulfill in any environment)

1. (Optional) If the account should route through a proxy, configure host/port/credentials in its Amazon Account drawer and confirm it's reachable — auto-fulfill does not require one, but a misconfigured *enabled* proxy blocks with `proxy_required` rather than silently running bare-IP.
2. ≥1 Amazon buyer account with `auto_fulfill_enabled=true` + `auto_fulfill_cap_total` set.
3. **Run `auto_fulfill_dry_run=true` first** — confirms the full checkout flow reaches the Amazon review step and the cap behaves, **without charging**. Inspect `fulfillment-evidence/{ebayOrderId}/dry_run_review-*.png`.
4. **Tune the Playwright selectors** in `AmazonCheckoutService` against live Amazon DOM during dry-run (selectors are best-effort and DOM-drift is the main fragility). Keep selectors in the `CHECKOUT_SELECTORS` constant.
5. Only then flip `auto_fulfill_dry_run=false` on a low-value test order and watch the placed/blocked status + evidence.
6. Monitor: BullMQ queue depth (`auto-fulfill`, `amazon-tracking`), `orders.auto_fulfill_status` (watch `blocked`/`failed`), memory (Chromium).

### A2 enablement status & gating (read this before touching auto-fulfill)

> **Dry-run selector tuning is DONE (2026-07-30, commit `40906aa`).** Dry-run walks the **full** Amazon checkout to the Place Order button and stops there, against a real logged-in buyer session. Do not describe the dry-run gate as open. Real placements have happened since (see the next-but-one paragraphs).

The live dry-run runs produced most of the A2 hardening documented above, and are why the diagnostic split (`review_unreadable` ≠ `cap`, `no_asin` ≠ `out_of_stock`) exists:
