- **Authentication must be proven, not assumed** — signed-out sessions under Unified Auth's `/ax/claim/*` used to pass as ACTIVE, so checkout no-opped every step and failed at the review total, misreported as `cap`. Origin of `probeAmazonAuth` / `decideAmazonAuth` and of `review_unreadable`. See "Amazon authentication must be positively PROVEN".
- **Amazon's create-account form is never filled** — filling it when Amazon did not recognise an email was one submit away from creating an unintended account under the customer's address.
- **Wrong-recipient guards** (ship-to must be the eBay BUYER) and the street+zip address matcher come from live observations (two addresses under one zip; Amazon prefilling a ZIP from the session's delivery location). See "Automated Amazon Fulfillment (A2)" step 5.

Selectors remain the standing fragility: `CHECKOUT_SELECTORS` is written against Amazon's DOM, which Amazon rotates and which differs by account/region. **Re-run dry-run after a suspected DOM drift, or before onboarding a buyer account in a new region** — it is the cheap way to prove the selectors still resolve, and `dry_run` also simulates the post-purchase bookkeeping so you can see what a placement does to an order without money leaving.

**The first REAL placements are DONE (read from production, 2026-10-02).** Three orders (15-15230-74978, 08-15244-32564, 11-15241-61922; 2026-10-01/02) carry the full `auto_fulfill_events` trail — re-check, cart, address, payment, review total, cap and loss checks, `submit_claimed`, `place_order_clicked`, `confirmation_detected`, `order_id_detected`, `placed` — and are `placed` + `linked` with a real Amazon order id; the two that have shipped were converted (Aquiline) and pushed to eBay. A fourth (10-15236-50953, 2026-09-30) is `placed` with no click stamp or events, i.e. from before migration `132`. The two earlier orders were blocked on `address` and linked by hand. Order 03-15243-67997 also has `delivered_at` set, so delivery detection by polling (verification step 6) has been observed. Do not describe live auto-fulfill as unproven. The onboarding step for a NEW buyer account stays: flip `auto_fulfill_dry_run=false` on ONE account, set a tight `auto_fulfill_cap_total` (e.g. `$10`), let one low-value matched order through, and watch `auto_fulfill_status` reach `placed` with a real `amazon_order_id` and a trusted (`linked`) `net_profit`. Only then widen caps and enable further accounts.

**Standing prerequisite (enforced in code, not convention):** a real Amazon buyer account (email + password + 2FA secret) with `auto_fulfill_enabled=true` and `auto_fulfill_cap_total` set. TOTP is answered from the stored secret — fulfillment must never require a human to type a code. A proxy is **not** a prerequisite (the 2026-07-30 dry-run used the since-retired platform pool; see "Amazon Scraping — Anti-Ban Strategy").

**How to guide the operator:**
- **Wants to test the flow** → dry-run is the tool, and it is known-working. Set `auto_fulfill_dry_run=true`, let one matched order through, read `orders.auto_fulfill_status` (expect `dry_run`) plus `fulfillment-evidence/{ebayOrderId}/`. A blocked reason other than `cap` means that step's selectors need a live-DOM patch.
- **Wants to go live** → the remaining gate above, one account and a tight cap first.
- **Reports auto-fulfill "not working"** → check the blocked reason before anything else, and trust the taxonomy: `review_unreadable` is a selector break, `cap` is the spend guard actually firing, `no_asin` is a delisted product (fix or end the listing — it is not stock that returns), `proxy_required` is that account's OWN configured proxy failing to apply (check the account's proxy fields).
- **Never remove the dry-run step from onboarding docs.** The review-step hard cap is only as good as `readReviewGrandTotal`; if that selector misses, the check fails closed — safe, but it means auto-fulfill is silently not running.

### Observability — log tracing (Loki/Promtail/Grafana)

A free, self-hosted log-tracing stack, present in **every** environment (local `docker-compose.yml` and both Coolify compose files) so a request/job can be traced end-to-end by `correlationId` — the id stamped onto every `apps/api` log line by `RequestIdMiddleware` → AsyncLocalStorage → Winston's `correlationFormat` (see `apps/api/src/common/observability/`).

- **File-tailing, not container-log scraping**: locally `apps/api` runs on the host via `pnpm dev:api`, so there is no API container stdout to scrape. Winston writes JSON logs to `apps/api/logs/*.log` in every environment (`logger.config.ts`, two `winston-daily-rotate-file` transports; level from `LOG_LEVEL`) and Promtail tails that directory. Local dev bind-mounts the host folder (`./apps/api/logs`); the Coolify files share a named `api_logs` volume between `api` and `promtail`. One `docker/promtail/promtail-config.yml` is reused everywhere via `-config.expand-env=true` + an `ENVIRONMENT` env var set per compose file (`development`/`test`/`production`).
- **Label design**: only `level` plus the static `job`/`environment` labels are indexed in Loki. `correlationId`/`queueName`/`jobId`/`context` are parsed by Promtail's `json` stage into queryable-but-unindexed fields — never put a high-cardinality id into a Loki label.
- **Local dev**: `pnpm docker:up` starts `sellerhill_loki` (`:3100`), `sellerhill_promtail`, `sellerhill_grafana` (`http://localhost:3001`, `admin`/`admin123` — local-dev credentials only). Grafana auto-provisions the Loki datasource and a starter "SellerHill API Logs" dashboard (`docker/grafana/provisioning/`).
- **Trace a request**: grab the `X-Request-ID` response header (or read it from a log line), then in Grafana → Explore → Loki: `{job="sellerhill-api"} | json | correlationId="<id>"`.
- **Coolify (test/production)**: same three services on the `coolify` network. `GRAFANA_ADMIN_PASSWORD` is a **required** Coolify env var. Test publishes no Grafana port; production binds it to the VPS loopback only (`127.0.0.1:3001`) for an SSH tunnel (see "Production access for agents").
- **Config is baked into the image in the two Coolify compose files, never bind-mounted.** Each of `docker/{loki,promtail,grafana}/` has its own tiny `Dockerfile` (`FROM <upstream>` + `COPY <config>`) and the compose service uses `build: { context: ./docker/<service> }`. Do not reintroduce a bind mount there — a missing bind source makes Docker create a directory that survives Coolify's `git reset --hard` and shadows the real file (`runc create failed: ... not a directory`). Local `docker-compose.yml` keeps bind mounts on purpose (config edits stay live).
- **Retention**: Loki filesystem storage with compactor-based retention, 7 days (`docker/loki/loki-config.yml`, `limits_config.retention_period`).
- Key files: `docker/loki/{Dockerfile,loki-config.yml}`, `docker/promtail/{Dockerfile,promtail-config.yml}`, `docker/grafana/{Dockerfile,provisioning/{datasources,dashboards}/}`, `apps/api/src/common/config/logger.config.ts`.

### Backups & monitoring (prod)

- **Postgres:** daily logical backup (pg_dump) + point-in-time if managed.
- **`fulfillment-evidence/`:** ephemeral, TTL-cleaned — not backed up.
- **`.browser-state/profiles/`:** ephemeral session profiles — not backed up (re-login on loss). Disk-bounded by `BrowserProfileGcService`; watch its nightly summary line for orphan/dormant removals, since each one means an account will re-authenticate.
- **Alert on:** API OOM / restart, BullMQ dead-letter growth, spike in `auto_fulfill_status='blocked'`.

## Production access for agents — READ ONLY (operator rule, 2026-09-29)

Agents debug production by SSH-ing into the VPS and reading. **Nothing is ever written.** The operator no longer tests locally; production is where behaviour is checked, so this is how an agent answers "what is production actually doing".

- **How**: `ssh sellerhill-prod "<command>"` (alias in the agent machine's `~/.ssh/config`, dedicated key `~/.ssh/sellerhill_prod`, comment `claude-code@sellerhill-dev` — revoke by deleting that line from the server's `authorized_keys`). Postgres, Redis and Loki are Coolify resources on the internal `coolify` network with no published port, so they are reached with `docker exec` from inside the VPS, never by tunnelling a port out. Grafana alone is on `127.0.0.1:3001` for a browser tunnel (`ssh -L 3001:127.0.0.1:3001 sellerhill-prod`).
- **Database**: connect as **`claude_ro`** (`pg_read_all_data`, `default_transaction_read_only = on`, `statement_timeout = 30s`, `CONNECTION LIMIT 3`). Its password lives only on the agent machine in `~/.sellerhill/prod-db.env`. Never use the application's own DB user.
- **Allowed**: `SELECT`s, `redis-cli` reads (`LLEN`, `ZCARD`, `HGETALL`, `KEYS` on a narrow pattern — never an unbounded `KEYS *`), `docker ps` / `docker logs` / `docker inspect`, Loki queries, reading files in the containers, and **eBay API GET calls** run from inside the production `api` container (credentials and seller tokens are already there; nothing secret leaves the server).
- **Forbidden without the operator's explicit OK, every time**: any DB write, any Redis write (`DEL`, `FLUSH*`, queue clean/obliterate), `docker restart|stop|rm|exec` of anything that mutates, and **every eBay POST/PUT/DELETE** — a write to eBay is a change to a real seller's live store.
- **Never start the NestJS app or a worker on production data** — not locally pointed at production Postgres/Redis (the local API runs boot-time migrations and joins the same BullMQ queues, so it would apply unmerged migrations and steal real jobs, including auto-fulfill purchases), and not inside the production container (a second app context double-fires every scheduler). Ad-hoc eBay calls use `curl` / `node -e` or the existing probe scripts (`dist/scripts/ebay-*-probe.js`), which do not bootstrap Nest.
- **The local `.env` stays on eBay sandbox + Stripe test.** Real-API checks go through the SSH path above, not by switching local credentials.
- **eBay quota is shared with every seller** — calls made while debugging draw from the same per-application daily pool (Taxonomy 5,000/day is the tightest). Make single calls, never loops.

## Figma Redesign — Per-Theme Tokens

**Dark mode is disabled app-wide for phase 1.** `packages/ui/src/context/ThemeContext.tsx`'s `ThemeProvider` hardcodes `themeMode` to `'light'` — no `localStorage` read, no `prefers-color-scheme` detection, `toggleTheme`/`setThemeMode` are no-ops, and there is no theme-toggle control anywhere. Reason: dark mode was never tested end-to-end, and OS-preference detection dropped visitors into an unverified theme on their first screen. `darkTheme` and every per-theme token below are untouched and still correct; re-enable by restoring localStorage/system-preference detection in the provider and adding a toggle once dark mode is actually verified.

Redesign tokens (from the figma Make redesign, https://sweet-yang-69529706.figma.site/):
- **Primary is per-theme**: light `#2563eb` (blue-600), dark `#4f6ef7`. Do NOT expect them to match. `colors.border.focus` (and the dark sidebar `accent`) must track `brand.primary`.
- **Sidebar background is per-theme**: light deep blue `#0c1f52`, dark near-black `#0d0f18`.
- **Borders are alpha-based**: `#00000014` (light) / `#ffffff12` (dark) — not solid hex.
- **`accent` token category** (emerald `#10b981`) is for "Active" status badges and success emphasis. Distinct from `semantic.success` (system success states).
- **Font**: **Manrope** for headings and figures (since the 2026-10-01 glass redesign; was Inter) + **Lexend** for body/UI (`typographyTokens.fontFamily.heading`/`body`, both with Noto Devanagari/Arabic/SC fallbacks), **JetBrains Mono** for codes. Lexend is drawn for reading fluency (wide apertures, tall x-height) which suits dense tables/forms; Manrope gives titles and money real weight at 700/800 with tabular numerals. Loaded via Google Fonts in `apps/web/index.html`; tokens in `packages/ui/src/theme/designTokens.ts`; base `body` rule in `apps/web/src/index.css`.
  - **Do not change the app's families without a concrete defect** (rendering, legibility, a missing glyph). A single-family Source Sans 3 swap (`fb00bc9`) was reverted, and unifying on the landing's typefaces was tried on 2026-09-03 and rejected on sight by the operator — marketing faces read heavy and wide at 14px across dense tables. The landing sells, the app is read all day; do not re-propose it as a "brand consistency" cleanup.
- **Body is 14px, not 16.** `typographyTokens.fontSize.base` (0.875rem) is the primary reading size and the value-text size for every form control (TextInput/Select/SearchField/MessageComposer). `fontSize.md` (15px) is the `h4` heading step. Heading leading is tight (`lineHeight.display` 1.1 / `tight` 1.2 / `snug` 1.3); body is `normal` 1.47.
- **Text ink** (light): primary `#27272a` (soft neutral charcoal — deliberately not slate-900, which users found harsh), secondary `#475569`. Dark `text.primary` is `#e2e8f0`.
- **Weights**: `fontWeight.semibold` is **500** and `bold` is **600** (only 400/500/600/700 are loaded). Headings, card titles and row labels use `semibold`. No component hardcodes a weight — change the token, never a call site.
- **Radii**: progressive scale — sm 6px (badges/checkboxes/table cells), md 8px (buttons/inputs/selects), lg 12px (cards/dialogs), xl 16px (modals), 2xl 20px (hero). Tokens in `radiusTokens`; `controlTokens.radius` matches `radiusTokens.md`. **`radiusPxTokens`** (`theme.radiusPx`, adds `xs: 4`) is the pixel mirror for SVG/canvas consumers such as recharts, which cannot take rem — use it instead of hardcoding a chart radius, and keep both scales in sync.
- **Sidebar nav (2026-09-29)**: five static groups, each under an `S.NavLabelWrapper` label separated by one `S.NavDivider`, hand-authored in `AppLayout.component.tsx` — **Genel Bakış/Overview** (Dashboard, Action Center — kept together on purpose: the dashboard says what happened, Action Center says what is waiting), **Satışlar/Sales** (Orders, Messages, Returns — buyer-facing), **Envanter/Inventory** (eBay Listings, Listing Jobs, Drafts, Revision History — catalog management), **Keşfet/Discover** (Best Sellers — Amazon-side product sourcing, not catalog management, so it does not share the Inventory group), **Yapılandırma/Configuration** (Billing, Settings). **Never an accordion** — the seller explicitly asked for a plain, non-collapsible grouping; growing the item count is a reason to add another flat group, not to nest one. `routeMeta.ts`'s `section` field is documentary (`NavSection` union mirrors the five groups); the sidebar is not derived from it.
- **Settings hub**: full-width 2-col grid; header icons on section cards; account rows keep row icons.

Redesign spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-03-figma-site-refactor-design.md`.

## Frontend Architecture Notes (2026-07)

### Typography scale (use `<Text variant>` — never raw font-size in feature CSS)
All values come from `packages/ui/src/theme/designTokens.ts` + `atoms/Text/Text.style.ts`; change the token, never a call site.

| Variant | Size | Use for |
|---|---|---|
| `h1` | 26px / bold(600), Manrope | Page titles (`PageHeader`) |
| `h2` | 19px / bold(600), Manrope | Rare large section titles |
| `h3` | 17px / semibold(500) | Drawer titles, major section |
| `h4` | 15px / semibold(500) | Card titles (`SettingsCard`, `QuickActionCard`) |
| `h5` | 13px / semibold(500) | Small section labels |
| `body` | 14px / regular | Primary UI text, control text, row labels |
| `body-sm` | 13px / regular | Table cells, secondary denser text, drawer subtitles |
| `body-xs` | 10px / regular | Micro meta |
| `caption` / `overline` | 11px / 10px | Meta, helper, chips |
| `mono` | 11px | Codes / IDs only |
| `metric` | 19px / bold(600), Manrope / tabular-nums | KPI figures (period cards, detail-page headline). Headings are for titles — do **not** repurpose `h1`/`h2` for numbers |
| `metric-sm` | 17px / bold(600), Manrope / tabular-nums | Secondary KPI figures (net profit under a headline metric) |

**`numeric` prop** — any figure rendered in a column (money, counts, percentages) must set `<Text numeric>` so digits are tabular and stack down the column. `metric` / `metric-sm` already enable it. A money cell in `body`/`body-sm` without `numeric` visibly jitters row to row.

**KPI card pattern** — label is `caption` + `text.secondary`, figure is `metric`. Never give the label the same weight as its own number (they read as two competing headings).

**Title → subtitle gap**: `PageHeader` uses `spacing.xs` between title and subtitle. Title is always `Text variant="h1" weight="semibold"`; subtitle is `body-sm` secondary. **Do not** invent ad-hoc page titles.

**Page shell (mandatory alignment)**:
- Outer gutter is **only** `AppLayout` `ContentInner` (responsive padding). Feature pages must **not** add their own outer `padding`.
- Root of every authenticated page: `PageContainer` from `@repo/ui` (or `PageContainerWithMobileBar` for detail pages with sticky mobile action bars). Gap between title and sections = `spacing.lg`.
- Pattern: `<PageContainer><PageHeader title={…} subtitle={…} />…</PageContainer>`. Export as `Container = PageContainer` in feature `*.style.ts` if preferred.
- Never double-pad (ContentInner + page Container) — that misaligns titles across screens.

### Form controls (must stay aligned)
**`controlTokens.height` in `designTokens.ts` is the single source of truth.** `packages/ui/src/styles/formControl.ts` and `Button` derive from it — nothing may re-declare a height literal (a drifted copy once made a labeled TextInput never match the Button beside it).

| Size | Compact (no floating label) | Labeled (floating label) |
|---|---|---|
| small | 2.5rem | 3.25rem |
| medium | 2.75rem | 3.5rem |
| large | 3rem | 4rem |

- **TextInput**, **Select**, **SearchField**, **Textarea** share the same heights, surface treatment and `border.control` idle border. Hover does not alter the border. Focus/open uses a clean **brand.primary** border with no halo or box-shadow; field validation uses the shared `ValidationMessage` (outlined triangle-info glyph + semantic error text).
- **`colors.border.focus` must always equal `colors.brand.primary`** in both themes, or focused controls ring in different colours.
- **Floating labels are mandatory** for form fields (drawers, settings, auth). Do **not** place an external `<Text>` label above a TextInput/Select — use the `label` prop.
- **A long unbroken value must not deform the field.** `TextInput`'s inner `<input>` and its `FieldWrapper` set `box-sizing: border-box` (there is no global reset; `content-box` made the input overflow its `overflow: hidden` wrapper, which then scrolled on focus and dragged the floating label left). `min-width: 0` on both is kept as defence. `FloatingLabel` is bounded on both `left` and `right` (icon-aware) so a long label truncates with ellipsis. Keep field **labels short** — the long explanation goes in an `InfoMessage` below.
- Toolbar/filter rows may use compact controls with `placeholder` only (no label) so Search + Select share one height. **Every list-page filter row uses `size="small"`** (orders is the reference; listings, drafts, products, jobs, revision history and returns were on `medium` until 2026-10-01, which is why the "All stores" select looked like a different component from page to page).
- Button `medium` = 2.75rem (matches compact medium); Button `large` = 3.5rem (matches **medium labeled**, i.e. the auth-form pairing of labeled input above primary submit). Form primary actions in drawers use `medium` not `large`.
- Every interactive atom carries a `:focus-visible` ring in `brand.primary`. Checkbox / Radio / Toggle hide their real `<input>`, so they mirror focus onto the visible box with `input:focus-visible + &` — a plain sibling selector, **never** an Emotion component selector (those need the babel plugin and crash at runtime).

### Card grids (list surfaces)
`DataTable`'s grid derives its column count from **`gridMinItemWidth`** (the narrowest track a card can survive in) via `auto-fill`, optionally capped by **`gridMaxColumns`** — never a fixed column count, which crushed wide horizontal cards. Current settings: Orders `26rem`/max 2 · Listings `24rem`/max 2 · Jobs `20rem` · Products `19rem` · Invoice history `18rem`. A card that lays out horizontally (thumbnail beside content) must declare a wide minimum; a compact tile can go narrow.

Inside a card, a fixed `repeat(N, 1fr)` stat strip is the usual failure mode — use `repeat(auto-fit, minmax(…, 1fr))` so cells reflow instead of truncating.

**Pagination has two variants and picking the wrong one looks broken.** `TablePagination` renders `footer` (a divider only) when `Table` places it inside its own bordered surface, and **`detached`** (own border + `radius.lg` + `sm` elevation) when `DataTable` renders it as a sibling of the table card or the card grid; `DataTable` spaces it with `S.PaginationSlot`. The rows-per-page Select always includes the caller's current page size (orders defaults to 20, which is not in the default `[10, 25, 50, 100]` — without that the control renders blank).

### Density, radius and elevation (one scale, no per-page drift)
- **Card tier radius is `lg` (12px)** — `Card`, `SettingsCard`, `QuickActionCard`, `Table` container, `FilterBar`. Controls are `md` (8px), badges/checkboxes are `sm` (6px), modals `xl`.
- **Card content inset is 18px (`spacing.md+`)** whether you use `<Card padding="lg">` or `<CardBody>` — the two APIs resolve to the same value on purpose.
- **Table rhythm**: `Th` 11px vertical (`spacing.sm-md`), `Td` 7px vertical (`spacing.sm`), `Tr` min-height 2.5rem → ~38px rows. Header stays taller than data so chrome reads lighter.
- **`TableColumn.align` is honoured** by `Th`/`Td`/`ThContent`. Money and count columns are `align: 'right'` + `<Text numeric>`; text columns stay left.
- **Row states are their own token group, `colors.table.*`** (`rowZebra`, `rowHover`, `rowSelected`, `rowSelectedHover`, `rowSelectedAccent`) — never `background.*` or `semanticTint.info`, where zebra/hover/selected collapsed into indistinguishable shades. The stripe is neutral because selection is the chromatic state; the two must never both be blue. Selection also draws a left accent bar (`inset` box-shadow on the first `td` — `border-collapse: collapse` makes a `tr` border unreliable) so it does not depend on hue alone, and it wins by doubled-`&` specificity rather than an important flag, which is what makes a **selected + hovered** row expressible. A sticky column paints its own background, so `Td`'s sticky branch must mirror every one of these states.
- **Never hand-roll a Card.** Extend it — `styled(Card)` with layout-only CSS — so a change to the card language reaches every page.

### Overlay and breakpoint tokens
- **`theme.zIndex`** (`zIndexTokens`): `sticky 100 · scrim 990 · sidebar 1000 · dropdown 1100 · assistant 1200 · overlay 9000 · drawer 9100 · modal 9200 · toast 9400 · loading 9600 · tooltip 9800`. Never hardcode a z-index, and never feed a non-z-index token into one (the loading overlay once resolved to `"4rem"`, which the browser dropped, rendering it behind drawers).
- **`theme.breakpoints`** (`breakpointTokens`): `sm 30rem · md 48rem · lg 64rem · xl 80rem`, plus `*Below` max-width complements.
- **One drawer shell:** every `Drawer` is 32rem (full-width below `md`) with the same header/footer surfaces, soft body canvas, typography and spacing from `packages/ui/src/molecules/Drawer`. The legacy `size="sm|md|lg"` prop remains source-compatible but does not change panel geometry. Dense flows must make their internal layout responsive/scrollable inside the canonical width — never widen or narrow the surrounding drawer for one feature.

### Accessibility floors held by the token layer
- `text.tertiary` must clear WCAG AA (4.5:1) on `surface.primary`, `surface.secondary` **and** `background.tertiary` in **both** themes (it is used 60+ times).
- Dashboard period-band gradients are **theme-identical** (the bands are always-dark by design, like the landing hero) and dark enough that `periodForeground` clears 5.3:1 and `periodForegroundMuted` clears 4.7:1 on every stop.

### Container logic extraction
God containers are forbidden. Extract feature hooks under `features/<feature>/hooks/`:
- Example: `listings/all/hooks/useListingsFilters.ts`, `useListingsColumns.tsx`.
- Container orchestrates data + hooks and returns only `<Component .../>`.
- `useForm` lives in container or `useXxxForm` hook — never in `.component.tsx`.

### Loading UX
- `useLoading(...)` is for **blocking mutations** only (save/delete/bulk), not initial page fetches.
- Initial list load → empty state / table message / skeleton, not the full-app overlay. See "Page-level loading is not the global overlay" below.

### Routing
- Route-level `React.lazy` + `Suspense` in `App.tsx` for app pages. Landing stays eager.

### Domain vs design system
- Keep domain composites (`ListingCard`, `ConnectEbayPrompt`) generic where possible; prefer app-level wrappers if they grow domain-specific. Do not add more product/domain molecules to `@repo/ui` without review.
- **`@repo/ui` does NOT depend on `@repo/shared`.** Its tsconfig `rootDir` is `packages/ui/src` and the root path mapping resolves `@repo/shared` to *source*, so a single import breaks the build with `TS6059`. When a design-system component needs domain knowledge, inject it (context/prop) instead of importing shared.

### Marketplace links are environment-scoped (never hardcode a host)
Sandbox and production eBay are separate sites with separate item id spaces: `ebay.com/itm/<sandbox id>` is always a dead link. Item URLs are built by `buildEbayItemUrl(itemId, environment)` / `buildAmazonProductUrl(asin)` in `packages/shared/src/domain/ebay/ebay.urls.ts` (with the `EbayEnvironment` enum + `resolveEbayEnvironment`).
- The app resolves the environment **once** in `apps/web/src/main.tsx` from `VITE_EBAY_ENVIRONMENT` and passes the builders to `MarketplaceProvider` (`@repo/ui`). `IdBadge` and the listing-detail "open on eBay" action both consume them via `useMarketplaceContext()`, so header actions and row badges can never disagree.
- `VITE_EBAY_ENVIRONMENT` **must mirror the API's `EBAY_ENVIRONMENT`**; it is a Vite build-time arg, wired in `Dockerfile.web` and both Coolify compose files (`sandbox` in test, `production` in production). Missing/unknown value → production (safe default). Every `VITE_*` value a compose file passes must also be declared as `ARG`/`ENV` in `Dockerfile.web`, or it is silently dropped (this once hid the Google sign-in button in every container build).
- Amazon has no sandbox; `AmazonCheckoutService`'s Playwright navigation to `amazon.com/dp/<asin>` is a real purchase flow, not a UI link, and stays as-is.

### Every list endpoint is server-paginated (mandatory)
`GET /listings`, `GET /listings/jobs` and `GET /listings/products` all return `{ items, total, page, limit }`. (The jobs page polls every 5s; an unbounded array re-downloaded the whole job history on every poll.)

- `ListingJobsQueryDto` — `page`, `limit` (default 20, clamped 100), `search` (job-id prefix), `status`.
- `UserProductsQueryDto` — `page`, `limit`, `search` (title / ASIN / brand).
- Product counts use `COUNT(DISTINCT p.id)` to match the `SELECT DISTINCT` page query — a product with several listings must count once.
- Every filter control resets `page` to 1; otherwise a narrowed result set leaves the user on an empty page.
- **Never add a list endpoint that returns a bare array.** If a UI shows a table or grid over it, it needs `page`/`limit` on day one.

### Listings list (server-side — mandatory)
- `GET /listings` returns `PaginatedListingsDto` `{ items, total, page, limit, categories }` — **never** the full catalog for table UIs. The FE always sends `page`/`limit` (bare `/listings` falls back to server defaults).
- Query: `ListingsQueryDto` — `page`, `limit`, `search`, `status`, `category`, `ebayAccountId` (store filter), `sortBy`/`sortOrder`, numeric range mins/maxes. Deprecated `stockPreset` still accepted for API compat; **FE does not send it** — use advanced `quantityMin`/`quantityMax` only (avoid dual stock filters).
- `lastSaleAt` on list rows: `MAX(orders.order_date)` correlated subquery via `orders.listing_id` (cheap with index). Sort keys `lastSale` / `lastSaleAt`.
- FE: `useGetListingsQuery(query)`; ListingsAll URL params via `useListingsFilters` (`q`, `page`, `limit`, `sort`, `dir`, `store` → `ebayAccountId`, `status` default **active**).
- Status filter options: **all / active / inactive** only on operational list chrome. **Drafts** are a dedicated view (`?status=draft`), reached from the sidebar's Drafts entry and the overview's other-actions rail; not mixed into carousel or default "all" (API excludes `draft` when status is omitted). **No status column** on the all table by default.
- **Draft create**: `CreateListingsRequest.asDraft` → queue worker prepares product/pricing, inserts `listings` with `status=draft` and **no** eBay publish (`ebay_item_id` nullable). Publish later via `POST /listings/:id/publish` or bulk `POST /listings/bulk-publish`. Shared detail page for drafts + live listings.
- Default visible columns: product, price, quantity, sold, **lastSale**, profit, createdAt — **not** source/listingId/status (ASIN + eBay id live inside product cell).
- Product cell: transparent image (no gray plate), title 2-line clamp, ASIN/eBay label rows; selection column is narrow (`Table` `$selection` + `colgroup`; `@repo/ui` must be **rebuilt** after Table style changes — package loads from `dist/`).
- Overview/Dashboard: small pages (`limit: 12` / `50`). Client-side filter/sort/slice of the full catalog is **forbidden**.

### Dashboard panel (Sellerboard-style)

**Toolbar**: underline tabs (shared `TabNav`, icon + label). There is no store filter on the page since 2026-10-04 — the dashboard shows the top bar's active store (see "One active store"). The rail is underline-styled, not a pill group, so page navigation reads differently from the in-card `SegmentedControl`.

**URL state** (all in the query string, shareable): `?tab=cards|chart|pnl`, `?period=today|thisWeek|thisMonth|thisYear`, `?granularity=day|week|month` (`?store=` is the active store, written by the provider). Owned by `hooks/useDashboardUrlState.ts`; defaults are omitted from the URL. Every value is a **shared enum** (`DashboardTab`, `DashboardPeriodKey`, `DashboardChartGranularity`, `DashboardChartSeries`, `DashboardPnlGroup`, `DashboardValueFormat` in `packages/shared/src/domain/dashboard/`) — no string literals.

- **Cards tab**: 4 period cards (gradient band + headline sales, net profit w/ margin, trend chips, orders/units · refunds · gross profit · ROI grid, expandable "More details" with payout/COGS/tax/shipping/fees/AOV/refund-rate) over two carousel sections (`ListingCarousel` + `OrderCarousel` in `Card`s, "view all" in the card header). Selecting a card refilters both carousels (listings via `soldFrom`/`soldTo`, orders via `dateFrom`/`dateTo`). Skeleton cards render during the first load — **never** `useLoading` for the initial fetch.
  - **The two carousels render at EQUAL height side by side.** `CarouselWrapper` and `CarouselViewport` (in BOTH `ListingCarousel.style.ts` and `OrderCarousel.style.ts`) carry `flex: 1 1 auto; min-height: 0` and the viewport is `display: flex`, so where the parent gives a height (the `SectionsRow` grid) the shorter card stretches to match (`ListingCard`/`OrderCard` `Wrapper` are `height: 100%`, footers `margin-top: auto`). Where the parent is content-height (listings overview, `align-items: start`) it is a no-op.
- **Chart tab**: ComposedChart — net-profit **bar** (gradient fill) on the currency axis, sales **line** on the currency axis, units/refunds **lines** on the count axis (dual axis: currency left, count right), custom themed tooltip, **click-toggleable legend chips**, and a `SegmentedControl` for granularity (30 days / 12 weeks / 12 months). Right rail is a grouped P&L summary (Revenue · Costs · Profit · Ratios) with emphasized gross/net totals.
- **P&L tab**: metrics × 12-month matrix, grouped sections, sticky metric column + header, current-period column highlighted, **heat-map toggle** (per-row relative intensity via an opacity overlay — never a hardcoded rgba) and **client-side CSV export** (the matrix is already in memory; no API round-trip).
- **View all** → `/listings/all?soldFrom&soldTo&from=dashboard` or `/orders?dateFrom&dateTo&from=dashboard`; back returns to `/dashboard`.
- API: `GET /v1/dashboard?chartGranularity=&ebayAccountId=` → `{ metrics, chart: { granularity, points, summary }, history: { months } }`.
- **Bucket keys are anchored on Postgres' `CURRENT_DATE`** (`getAnchorDate()`) and emitted as `to_char(...,'YYYY-MM-DD')` text, never a `date` column: node-pg parses `date` into a *local-midnight* Date, so an API process in UTC+3 talking to a UTC database generated keys that never matched and rendered an empty chart/P&L. Do not reintroduce `toISOString()` on a pg `date`.
- **Cost/ratio aggregates**: `periodSelect()` also sums `purchase_price`, `transaction_fee`, `ad_fee`, `amazon_shipping`, `amazon_tax`; `roi = profitConfirmed / costOfGoods` and `refundRate = refunds / (orders + refunds)`. Costs are display-only (already inside `ebay_earnings`) and render with a `−` prefix.
- **Tiered profit** (per period): headline `netProfit` is **confirmed-only** (`cost_capture_status = 'linked'`, trusted Amazon costs). `profitProvisional` (product-only costs) and `revenueUncosted` (pending/failed — revenue only, no profit) surface as separate chips with tooltips, so users never confuse an unknown cost with a real zero. **Untracked orders (no SellerHill listing) are excluded from every figure** and only counted, rendered as the neutral "N untracked orders excluded" chip — see "The dashboard is scoped to tracked orders" under "Net Profit Formula".
- **Theme tokens**: `colors.dashboard.period*Gradient` (band backgrounds), `periodForeground`/`periodForegroundMuted` (fixed light ink on the always-dark bands — **never** `text.inverse`, which flips per theme), `series{Profit,Sales,Units,Refunds}`, `heat{Positive,Negative}`.
- **Files**: `apps/web/src/features/dashboard/` — `DashboardPage/` (shell), `components/{PeriodCard,CardsPanel,ChartPanel,PnlPanel}/`, `hooks/{useDashboardUrlState,useDashboardFormatters}.ts`, `utils/{metricRows,chartSeries,pnlExport,periodRanges,emptyMetrics}.ts`, `api/dashboardApi.ts`, `dashboard.types.ts`. `utils/metricRows.ts` is the single source of truth for row order/labels/formats shared by the chart summary and the P&L matrix.

### Action Center — pending actions (`/:locale/actions`, 2026-08-12)

**The dashboard answers "what happened"; the Action Center answers "what is waiting on ME".** A blocked auto-purchase, a revoked store token, an Amazon-cancelled order or an exhausted plan slot would otherwise only be discoverable by opening the right list with the right filter already in mind. `GET /v1/action-center` is the single surface that collects them.

- **Sidebar entry sits directly under Dashboard, with a live count badge** (`AppLayout`, polled from the layout container; `ACTION_CENTER_POLL_INTERVAL_MS` = 2 min, matched to the cron cadences behind the signals — faster polling buys nothing). Badge renders **nothing at 0**, red when any item is critical, amber otherwise; collapsed rail degrades the count to a presence dot (`NavBadge`/`NavBadgeDot` in `AppShell.style.ts` — deliberately not the `Badge` atom, which washes out on the always-dark sidebar).
- **Rules that keep the page believed** (all enforced in `action-center.helpers.ts`, Jest-covered):
  - **An item exists only if the seller can act on it.** Anything the platform resolves by itself (a queued job, a pending refresh) belongs on the dashboard. A page that lists things you cannot fix stops being read.
  - **`count === 0` items and empty groups are dropped**, so the badge equals the number of things actually waiting.
  - **Counts are counts of CONDITIONS, not rows.** The badge must read "6 things need you", not "431 orders"; per-item row counts live inside the row. Quota/trial items are therefore `count: 1` with the real numbers in `context`.
  - **Severity is consequence-based, not subsystem-based** (`CRITICAL` money at risk · `WARNING` automation degraded · `INFO` opportunity). A group rolls up to its worst item.
  - Ordering is severity → count → key. The key tiebreak is not cosmetic: the page polls, and a list that reshuffles every 2 minutes is unreadable.
- **The API sends codes, never sentences** — same split as `ListingFailureCode` (the backend has no locale). All text is resolved from `actionCenter.*` on the web side. Breakdown chips **reuse the owning domain's existing vocabulary** (`orders:orders.autoFulfill.reason.*`, `listings:listings.jobs.failure.*`) rather than minting a second copy that would drift. An unmapped code drops its chip instead of printing a raw enum.
- **Probes are individually fail-soft.** Five concurrent probes (orders / connections / listings / plan / setup); one that throws contributes no items and logs at warn. This endpoint is polled from the shell, so a hard failure would break navigation chrome everywhere.
- **Plan pressure reads through `BillingService.getSummary`, not the billing tables** — a second implementation of the quota arithmetic would eventually disagree with the gate that actually refuses the work. With `BILLING_ENFORCEMENT_ENABLED=false` the whole group is omitted — warning about a limit that is not enforced is a false alarm.
- **Counts equal the linked list by construction**, because the order probes count by `buildOrderStageSql` (see "Order stages") and every order row links to `/orders?stage=<stage>` — the same CASE the list filters on — rather than hand-written predicates (`action-center-stage-links.guard.spec.ts`). The single deliberate narrowing is `ORDER_AWAITING_PURCHASE`'s 12 h grace window, which is self-clearing. That item is also the one order probe scoped to TRACKED orders (`o.listing_id IS NOT NULL`), and its link therefore carries no `tracking=all` (it lands on the list's default tracked view): an untracked sale is `ORDER_UNTRACKED`'s job, and since `133` an untracked unshipped sale reads `to_purchase` — a store fulfilled by another tool would otherwise raise "N orders waiting for you to buy".
- **Setup is a staged onboarding checklist, visible from the seller's very first session** (pure `buildSetupItems`):
  - **No eBay account → show ONLY `setup_no_ebay_store`.** Every other setup item links to `/settings?drawer=...`, and the Settings hub is behind the same `EbayAccountGuard` — showing them earlier would link to a page that bounces back to "connect eBay". `ebayCount` is "any row exists", matching `EbayAccountGuard`'s own predicate (not `status = active`); a disconnected/revoked account is `ebay_account_disconnected`'s job.
  - **Once eBay exists, the remaining gaps show together**: `setup_no_amazon_account` (no `amazon_accounts` row), `setup_no_store_settings` (no `store_settings` row with `is_global = TRUE`), `setup_no_listing_settings_group` (no `listing_settings_groups` row).
  - **`setup_auto_fulfill_off` is evaluated only once an Amazon account exists** (otherwise it double-reports `setup_no_amazon_account`). "Ready" requires an `AmazonAccountStatus.ACTIVE` account with auto-fulfill enabled and a cap set.
  - All setup items are `severity: INFO`, `count: 1`.
- **Signals**: `ActionCenterItemKey` (`packages/shared/src/domain/action-center/action-center.types.ts`) is the canonical list — orders (Amazon-cancelled · blocked/failed automation w/ reason breakdown · purchase not confirmed (`ORDER_PURCHASE_UNKNOWN`, CRITICAL — its own item, because the instruction is the opposite of a blocked order) · awaiting manual purchase past a 12 h grace · untracked sales · tracking problem · tracking conversion held · eBay-cancelled sale with an Amazon order still open · past eBay's ship-by date (`ORDER_LATE_TO_SHIP`) · return waiting on the seller), connections (eBay store disconnected/revoked · eBay messaging scope missing · Amazon account needs attention), listings (job failures by code · source unavailable · active at qty 0 · unpublished drafts), plan (listing / AO / conversion quota ≥80% — the AO warning can no longer fire since `125` set AO to unlimited, and the Best Sellers allowance is not yet an Action Center signal —, past due, suspended, trial ending), setup (as above).
- **The page is per STORE, and the store is mandatory (operator decision, 2026-10-03).** `GET /v1/action-center?ebayAccountId=` narrows every per-store probe (orders, returns, eBay connections, listings/jobs) with one predicate `($n::uuid IS NULL OR <alias>.ebay_account_id = $n::uuid)`; blank = every store (no web caller since 2026-10-04: the page, the switcher and the sidebar badge all use the per-store call). The page shows the top bar's ACTIVE store (since 2026-10-04 it has no picker of its own); the switcher shows every store's own count (one RTK query per store, `getActionCenterByStore`, shared with the page and the sidebar badge). Plan, setup and Amazon buyer-account items are account-wide (`accountWide: true`), ignore the filter and carry an "applies to your whole account" caption; connection items list their stores (`stores: { id, label }[]`); links into store-filtered lists carry `store=<id>`. `action-center-stage-links.guard.spec.ts` counts one store predicate per query per alias.
- **Adding a signal is three edits**: a value in `ActionCenterItemKey`, a probe entry in `action-center.service.ts`, an i18n block in every locale's `actionCenter.json`. The page renders whatever it is given.
- Files: `apps/api/src/modules/action-center/` (`.service.ts` probes, `.helpers.ts` + `.spec.ts` pure assembly, `.controller.ts`, `.module.ts` — imports `BillingModule`, nothing imports it back), `packages/shared/src/domain/action-center/`, `apps/web/src/features/action-center/` (`ActionCenterPage/` 4-file split, `actionCenterPresentation.ts` for icon/badge/namespace maps, `api/actionCenterApi.ts`), `packages/shared/src/i18n/resources/<locale>/actionCenter.json`. Gated by `EbayAccountGuard` like every other data page.

### Listings UX chrome (mobile-first SaaS)
- Prefer **no** dense PageHeader action button clusters (Create / Export / End) on list/detail. Use overview QuickActions, SettingsCard rows, drawers, DataTable toolbar download, or a single mobile **Manage** sheet.
- Product images on cards/detail/table: **`background: transparent`** (match `ListingCard`).
- Avoid duplicate facts: ASIN/eBay once; economics once; do not triple KPI + facts + economics.

### Listing detail (`apps/web/src/features/listings/detail/`)
Every section card (Performance, eBay Policies, Automation, Product Content, Danger Zone) is a `SettingsCard` with `SettingsInfoRow`/`SettingsActionRow` rows, matching the Settings hub's visual language (`body-sm` secondary labels, `body` semibold+`numeric` values). **No card header carries an icon** — icons live on the rows (`Meta`, a local icon+label+value helper mirroring `SettingsInfoRow`).
- Layout: hero (gallery + record facts + one economics strip; status badge pinned to the hero card's top-right corner via `position: absolute`) → `SectionGrid`: **Performance** | a `SectionColumnStack` holding **eBay Policies** + **Automation** stacked in one column (so the short policies card doesn't stretch to Performance's height) → **Product Content** full width → **Danger Zone**.
- **eBay Policies is read-only** — no edit pencil. Reassigning a listing's payment/shipping/return policy is not pushed to eBay's offer (see "Deferred"), so editing it here would silently lie to the seller.
- **Automation card**: two summary rows (strategy group name; active-override summary, e.g. "Pause sales, Fixed price" or "Off") + an always-visible static `InfoMessage` ("changes apply on the next sync") that never names the internal provider. Both rows' pencil opens one drawer containing the strategy-group `ModernSelect` **and** all automation overrides, saved as one `updateListing` call, because a group swap and an override both only take effect on the next refresh/stock-sync cycle. Each toggle lives in its own bordered `AutomationBlock`; custom-margin's two inputs are always stacked vertically (`MarginFields`).
- **Title has its own small drawer**, triggered by a pencil next to the hero product title. Uses a `Textarea` (via RHF `Controller`) because titles run up to 80 chars.
- Automation UI maps to DB overrides (migration `029`):
  - **Pause sales** → `disable_ordering` (force qty 0)
  - **Fixed price** → `lock_price` + `disable_repricing` (+ optional `price_override`)
  - **Fixed quantity** → `lock_quantity` (+ optional `quantity_override`)
  - **Custom margin** → `margin_*_override` only when fixed price is off
- Worker: `product-sync.service.ts` applies overrides on refresh fan-out.
- **Product Content**: description / features / specs each render **only when that section has data** — no placeholder text, no icons on its sub-headings. An empty Amazon description is normal (most modern ASINs carry bullets + A+ content instead). Specs come from `products.specs` (see "Persistence (migration `060`)").
- **Ending and deleting live in a Danger Zone card at the foot of the page, at every width** — two `SettingsActionRow`s (`block` + `trash`, the latter `variant="danger"`), not a PageHeader button cluster. `canEnd` is `status === ACTIVE`; `canDelete` is `status === DRAFT`.
  - **Only a DRAFT can be deleted — anywhere.** eBay has no delete for a listing, only end, and dropping our row for a live listing would orphan its orders for good. The bulk menu offers Delete only in the draft view, and `ListingsService.deleteListings` selects `status = 'draft'` only and makes no eBay call, so a direct API call cannot delete a live row either. Drafts keep it because they have no eBay footprint and would otherwise block their own ASIN (`isAsinListed` counts DRAFT). An ended (INACTIVE) listing does not block re-listing.
  - **The mobile Manage sheet does not offer end/delete** — two routes to one irreversible action is how the wrong one gets tapped. It keeps only the non-destructive shortcut (publish a draft / edit automation) plus Cancel.
  - **Copy is singular**: `modals.{end,delete}OneTitle|OneDescription` and `notifications.{end,delete}OneSuccessTitle|OneSuccess`, next to the existing `publishOne*` keys; delete copy is draft wording.
  - **A Dialog primary button can never be red — `variant: 'danger'` is silently collapsed to `primary`** by `resolveFilledVariant` (`Dialog.component.tsx`), by design ("Dialog actions always use brand blue… icon disc may still use semantic type color"). Do not pass it expecting red.
  - **`showMessage`'s `headerKey` is not displayed anywhere** — `GlobalMessageModal` passes only `typeTitles`, so every dialog is headed by its type title. The field is still required; give it the truthful key, but do not expect it on screen.
- Listing money is formatted with `ListingDto.currency`, resolved by the API from `listings.ebay_account_id → ebay_accounts.marketplace_id → EBAY_MARKETPLACE_CONFIG`. UI language controls locale/separators only and must never select the currency. Legacy rows without a resolvable account fall back to the US marketplace currency.
- The updated-at row shows the revisions action only when `GET /listings/:id/revisions?limit=1` reports `total > 0`; an untouched listing has no empty details action.
- **A revision carries the AMAZON stock beside the eBay quantity (migration `134`, 2026-10-01).** The drawer used to print only the quantity formula's result ("Stok 1 → 1, değişmedi"), and the operator read it as eBay stock and asked for Amazon's — both are shown now: "Amazon stoğu 24 → 19" above "eBay stoğu 5 → 5". `listing_revisions.previous_source_stock` / `_status` / `new_source_stock` / `_status`: `new_*` is `products.stock` + `stock_status` at the check (already updated when the fan-out runs), `previous_*` is read at INSERT time from the listing's latest earlier revision (a `LATERAL` in both writers, `recordRevisions` and `recordUnchangedChecks`) — the row before IS the last observation, nothing is threaded through the pipeline. Rows older than `134` carry NULLs and the drawer shows the new value alone. Rendered through `formatSourceStock` so a lower bound reads `20+`. The cross-listing Revision History page shows the same column and card row; `ListingRevisionsDrawer` is ONE component used by both the listing detail and that page (the subject header is the only difference).

### Orders list + order detail — the premium pass (2026-10-01, trial screens)

The operator asked for the app to read as premium and trustworthy; the orders list and the order detail were redesigned first as the trial, and the rules below are what the pass settled on. If the look is approved, the same rules roll out screen by screen (dashboard, listings, returns…).

- **Diagnosis, so it is not repeated**: the screens read as a template because of four things — a brand-blue icon on EVERY label (icon soup), grey boxes nested inside white cards (KPI strip, stat strip), tracked uppercase micro-labels (`NET KÂR`, `SATIŞ`), and three layers of chrome before the first row (tab rail + white filter card + table toolbar). Money also printed `$9,8` beside `$24,99`. None of this was a token problem.
- **Icons carry meaning or are absent.** Row labels in the orders cards and detail cards have NO leading icon; the label column is the only ornament. Icons remain only where they ARE the signal: the stage badge, the timeline markers, the phone glyph under an address.
- **One surface per card.** No tinted box inside a white card. The order card's money row sits under a hairline on `surface.secondary`; the detail hero's receipt is the card's own right half, split by one hairline.
- **No uppercase tracked labels in feature code** — labels are sentence-case `body-sm`/`caption` secondary. (The `Table` header's uppercase is the design system's and stays.)
- **Money is always two decimals on these pages** (`formatCurrency(value, locale, currency, 2)` in both containers). `formatCurrency`'s default of 0 minimum fraction digits is unchanged elsewhere — switch the default only with a sweep of every money surface.
- **Orders list** (`OrdersAllPage`): title → `TabNav` rail with **count pills** (`TabNavItem.count`, a new optional prop on the atom; never bake "(5)" into a label) → one filter row on the canvas (search, three selects, Clear, `ViewToggle`, export) → the table. `DataTable` is rendered with `hideViewToggle` and without `onDownload`, so it draws no toolbar of its own. **Table is the default view on a desk, cards below `md`** (`useIsMobile`). Seven columns: order no + date · product · buyer · stage (+ reason / Amazon id / tracking) · sale · cost · profit (+ **margin**, `formatPercent`, or the Estimated badge). The product column has no `width` so it absorbs the remainder under `table-layout: fixed`; the profit column must never be pushed off the right edge. No `sortable` flags — the page never wires `onSort` and a dead sort affordance is worse than none.
- **Order card** (`OrderCard`, also the dashboard carousel): image · title · stage badge + qualifying chips on one row · a `<dl>` of order no / buyer / date (/ quantity when > 1 / ASIN) · the money row. No "Details →" footer — the whole card is the button.
- **Order detail**: the PAGE TITLE IS THE ORDER NUMBER (a detail page is named after its record), the subtitle is the order date, and the stage badge sits in the header's `actions` slot. The hero is one card with two halves: the product (image, title, a `<dl>` of buyer / quantity / SKU / ASIN / eBay ID) and **the receipt** — net profit at `display` size in the profit colour, margin + ROI under it, then four dotted-leader lines (sale → order earnings → −Amazon cost → net profit). This replaced the five-equal-KPI grey strip; it is the page's one loud element, so nothing else on the page competes with it. The three section cards keep the shared row-rhythm mechanism (`ROW` = `controls.height.small`) with icon-less `Meta` / `Money` rows; totals render `metric-sm`.
- `formatPercent(ratio, locale, fractionDigits)` lives in `packages/ui/src/utils/format.ts` beside the other formatters (`0.253 → "25.3%"`, locale-aware).

**Round 2 — the light-glass language (2026-10-01, operator chose "açık cam / Apple-Stripe", whole shell, new display face).** The restrained round 1 read as "no difference", so the shell and every card-tier surface now carry one explicit visual language. Everything is tokens + one helper; a screen opts in by using the atoms.
- **The canvas is lit, not painted.** `background.gradient` (light) is the AURORA: three radial colour lights — brand blue top-left, amber top-right, teal at the foot — over `#e9f0fb`, each ≤ 0.55 alpha and never overlapping into lavender. It sits on `LayoutWrapper` (viewport-fixed), so panes blur it as the page scrolls under them.
- **Every card-tier surface is a frosted pane**: `glassSurface(theme)` / `glassSurfaceStrong(theme)` (`packages/ui/src/styles/glass.ts`, exported from `@repo/ui`) = sheen gradient over `colors.glass.surface` (52 % white) + `backdrop-filter: blur(1.5rem) saturate(1.5)` + a white `glass.edge` hairline + `shadows.glass` (inset top highlight, hairline contact shadow, long brand-tinted drop; `shadows.glassHover` on lift). Applied in `Card` (all variants but `flat`), `SettingsCard`, `Table`'s container (rows are transparent, `table.rowZebra`/`rowHover` are alpha washes, no vertical cell rules), the detached `TablePagination`, and `OrderCard`. **Drawers and modals are the PANEL (round 5, 2026-10-01): `glass.panel` (94 % white) + the blur, and the drawer BODY is a cool slate canvas, `glass.panelCanvas`** — the round-4 version (a 76 % pane with transparent header/body/footer and white cards straight on it) read as "faint, not premium" to the operator: white on near-white has no edge. Now the header and footer sit on the panel, the body is the slate canvas, and every card inside it (`glassSurface`, the drawer `FormCard`, the revisions cards) lifts off that canvas the way page cards lift off the aurora. `glass.surfaceStrong` (0.84) stays for a pane-on-a-pane (the order receipt, table heads); `Modal`'s container is on `glass.panel` too. The scrims add a `blur(0.25rem)`; the drawer `FormCard` (`drawerSurfaces.style.ts`) is a `glassSurface` pane at `radius.lg`, and the tinted boxes inside drawers (buyer-messaging master card, price-calculator breakdown, template-type toggle) sit on `glass.tint`. **Inputs and menus stay opaque**, and `backdrop-filter` makes a pane the containing block of a `position: fixed` child — safe today because `Select`, `Dropdown` and `Tooltip` portal to `body`; a new fixed-positioned element inside a drawer must portal too.
- **The top bar is frosted** (`colors.glass.header` + blur, `glass.edge` hairline, no shadow); the **sidebar is lit** by `sidebar.glow`/`glowAlt` radial lights and the selected nav row is `sidebar.accentGradient` + `sidebar.accentGlow`; the **primary `Button` is `brand.gradient`** over `brand.primary`. The footer is transparent.
- **Display type is Manrope** (`fontFamily.heading`, loaded in `apps/web/index.html` at 500–800): `h1` (now 26px), `h2`, `metric`, `metric-sm` and `display` render at `fontWeight.bold`; `h3`–`h5` stay semibold. Body/UI stays Lexend — the 2026-09-03 "marketing faces in dense tables" rejection was about BODY text and still holds.
- **One headline figure per page may use gradient ink**: `colors.glass.profitGradient` / `lossGradient` clipped to the glyphs (`S.HeadlineFigure` on the order detail, `-webkit-text-fill-color: transparent`). Nothing else on a page gets gradient text.
- The product shot in the order hero floats on a `glass.glowBlue` radial behind it; the receipt pane adds `glass.glowMint`. These are the only per-feature lights — a new page takes its atmosphere from the canvas and the atoms, not from its own gradients.
- Dark-theme values exist for every new token (`glass.*`, `brand.gradient`, `sidebar.glow*`) so the type stays total; dark mode itself is still disabled.
- **The aurora MOVES (2026-10-01, operator: "give it some life, without overdoing it").** `AppShell.style.ts` `Aurora` + three `AuroraLight`s (`$light` blue / sky / mint, tokens `colors.glass.aurora{Blue,Sky,Mint}`), rendered as the first child of `LayoutWrapper` in BOTH shells (`AppLayout`, `OperatorLayout`): 70 vw radial lights that drift about a tenth of the viewport over 32 / 40 / 46 s, alternating, transform-only (composited, no `filter`), at `z-index: -1` inside the wrapper's own stacking context (`isolation: isolate`), frozen under `prefers-reduced-motion`. The lights carry most of the colour now and the static `background.gradient` is a half-strength base — raise the moving tokens, not the static alphas, to add life; raise the static ones to add tint. Keyframes objects are interpolated directly (`animation-name: ${() => keyframesObj}`), never by `.name`, or Emotion does not register the rule. **The brand-blue light is never the brightest of the three on purpose** (`auroraBlue` 0.17, sky 0.19, mint 0.17, and its drift grows only to `scale(1.06)`): it is the one that lands on the top-centre of the content, over the page title, and the operator asked for exactly that light to be turned down (2026-10-01) — retune it before the other two. The whole set was then cut by a third again ("gözümü alıyor" — the brightness hurt), so a further step down is the likelier next request than a step up.
- **The aurora's intensity is the four alphas in `background.gradient`** (light theme, `themes.ts`: brand blue 0.11 / sky 0.08 / teal 0.08 / mid-blue 0.05 over `#f3f6fc → #f7f9fd` — halved when the moving lights took over the colour since 2026-10-01, then cut by a third again the same day because the brightness hurt the operator's eyes — 0.80/0.66/0.66/0.38 read as tiring, 0.50/0.38/0.38/0.20 over `#e8eff9` was still "too blue" to the operator, who asked for the canvas to move toward white). **The top-right light is SKY (`#0ea5e9`), never amber** — the operator rejected the yellow on sight; the two top lights read as one cool wash with a temperature shift. The panes (`glass.surface` 50 %, `header` 52 %) and the sidebar glows move with it. Change those numbers, nothing else, to retune the atmosphere.
- **Badges are rectangles with `radius.sm` corners, never pills (operator decision, 2026-10-01).** `Badge`'s `isPill` prop is accepted and ignored; `TabNav`'s count pill is `radius.sm` too. Sizes carry real padding and 11/13/13 px text (`xs`/`sm`/`md`) — a 10 px capsule beside 14 px body text read as an afterthought. **`BadgeVariant` carries four extra hues — `teal`, `sky`, `orange`, `navy` (tokens `colors.badge.*`, ink / tint / hairline per hue, light + dark) — and two FILLED variants, `solidNavy` (sidebar navy, white ink) and `solidAmber`**, because the operator asked for every order stage to have its own colour and four semantic tones cannot give twelve stages twelve looks. `primary` and `info` are the SAME hue (brand blue = `semantic.info`); do not count them as two colours. `order-stage.ts` maps each stage to a distinct variant and `order-stage.test.ts` asserts the twelve are all different. The same "one hue per state" rule holds for the return buckets (`return-presentation.ts`: grey unconfirmed · red overdue · amber due · orange escalated · sky in progress · green closed) and for `StatusBadge`'s status map (jobs: pending amber · processing blue · completed green · failed red · cancelled grey; listings: active green · draft navy · inactive grey; accounts: active green · verifying sky · error red).
- **Detail-page section cards are content-high** (`SectionGrid` is `align-items: start` and resets `SettingsCard`'s `height: 100%`): the customer card holds four address lines and a phone, and stretching it to the receipt cards' height left a void under them.

**Round 3 — the whole app (2026-10-01, operator: "apply it to every screen; cards are the default, not the table").** The orders rules above now hold on every seller screen, and the rollout settled five more:
- **Every `DataTable` opens on CARDS, at every width** (`DataTable.container.tsx`: `defaultViewMode ?? 'grid'`; the orders page and the revision history start on `'grid'` too). A card carries the photo and reads the way a seller thinks about a sale or a listing; the table is one `ViewToggle` away for scanning figures. Do not pass `defaultViewMode="table"` to a new list.
- **Filter rows sit on the canvas, never in a card of their own** — `FilterBar` in the listings, drafts, products, jobs, job detail, revision history, returns and messages pages is a plain flex column; the result count is plain caption text, not a tinted chip. The first surface on a list page is the first row of data.
- **One card shape for every list card** — `ListingCard` (domain-ui, also the dashboard and Best Sellers), the listing-jobs `JobCard` and `ReturnCard` mirror `OrderCard`: the title block, a `<dl>` of label / value rows with NO icon, then the figures under one hairline on `glass.tint` (`StatsGrid` / `Facts`), sentence-case labels, no "Details →" footer (the whole card is the button). The anatomy inside the top block is "Card anatomy" below. `ListingCardMetaItem.icon`, `ProductTableCellMetaRow.icon` and `ListingCard`'s `showDetailAction` are accepted and ignored; `SettingsInfoRow.icon` is optional and the listing detail's fact rows omit it. The listing detail's KPI strip and the job detail's stat strip are hairline-separated rows on the pane, not tinted boxes.
- **Hand-rolled controls were folded back into the atoms, and new ones must not appear** (operator request — one atomic design system, tokens only): the Amazon link modal's native `<select>` is a `ModernSelect`; the dashboard and Messages store filters are the same `Select size="small"` (no icon) as the list pages' store filter — a filter that holds a value is a `Select`, never a `Dropdown` with a button trigger (`Dropdown` is a menu of actions); the period card's "show more" and the listings advanced-filter header are `Button variant="text"`; the blacklist scope tag is a `Badge`. `styled.button` / `styled.select` in a feature `.style.ts` is the smell to look for.
- **A `StatusBadge` never prints the raw enum.** `getEbayAccountStatusLabel` (`features/ebay/utils/`) and `getAmazonAccountStatusLabel` (`features/amazon/utils/`) resolve the store and buyer-account statuses through the existing `ebay.accounts.status*` / `amazon.accounts.status*` keys; the stores page and both account cards use them (they showed "Active" in Turkish).
- Money on the listing detail and the listing cards is two decimals, like the orders pages.

**Round 4 — card anatomy, the operator console, drawers (2026-10-01).**
- **Card anatomy (the operator's final call, 2026-10-01): a TITLE ROW first — the title on the left, ONE line cut with an ellipsis (the full text is on the tooltip), the badges on the RIGHT of the same row — then the photo on the LEFT with the facts beside it, then the figures row** (`OrderCard`, `ListingCard` horizontal, `ReturnCard`; the vertical `ListingCard` keeps the photo above). A photo-on-the-right variant was tried the same day and rejected on sight ("the previous one was prettier"); what survived from it is the full-width title row and the right-aligned badges. There is NO stacking breakpoint: below `sm` the photo shrinks (6.5rem → 5rem) and the badges may wrap under the title, still right-aligned (`margin-left: auto`). The title lives in a `TitleSlot` (a flex host whose child gets `min-width: 0`) so the tooltip wrapper lets it shrink. `ListingCard`'s selection checkbox sits at the start of the title row, in flow. Keep the three cards' anatomy identical; a new list card copies `OrderCard`. **Listing card update (2026-10-04, operator request after reviewing a Stitch redesign): the horizontal photo is 9rem (7rem below `sm`) so the card is taller and reads less like a strip; the figures row ends with a `Detay ›` hint (`ListingCardProps.detailLabel`, only while the card has an `onClick` — the mapper passes `translation:common.details`; Best Sellers passes none) because nothing else told the seller the card opens; the stock cell is `Stok (eBay/AMZ)` with the Amazon figure beside the eBay quantity (`ListingCardStat.secondary`, `formatSourceStock`, so a lower bound stays `20+`); the listings grid's `gridMinItemWidth` is 27rem.** The listings filter row shows every applied filter (category, inactive status, tracking, each advanced range) as a removable chip (`activeFilterChips` in `useListingsFilters`, `listings.filters.removeFilter`) — search stays in its own box and the default Active status is not a chip. No "Add listing" button on the page header (operator decision).
- **The Messages inbox FITS the viewport; nothing else does (2026-10-01, operator: "it keeps moving, the competitor's is fixed").** `AppRouteMeta.fitsViewport` (set on `/messages` only) makes `AppLayout` pass `$fitsViewport` to `ContentInner`, which is now a flex column and drops its automatic minimum height (`min-height: 0`) on that route; the page's `Container` is `flex: 1 1 0%; min-height: 0` and the `Shell` fills what the header and toolbar leave (`min-height: 12rem` floor). Only the conversation list and the thread scroll, each inside its own pane — rail, list header, thread header and composer never move, and the content area has no scrollbar at any viewport. Do NOT give `ContentInner` `min-height: 0` unconditionally: on a content-high page the footer would then sit over the overflowing content. **And `ContentInner` is `display: flex` ONLY on a fit-viewport route** — as a flex column on every page it made the page container's height definite, so a `SettingsCard` (`height: 100%`) asked for the whole page and its `overflow: hidden` siblings were squeezed: the order hero rendered as a 120 px strip and the timeline card gained a void (2026-10-01). Every other route gets a plain block child, as before. Measure with Playwright (`main.scrollHeight - main.clientHeight` must be 0 on `/messages`) rather than by eye.
- **The Messages rail mirrors eBay's own Messages page (operator request, 2026-10-01; the competitor does the same).** One `Inbox` group in eBay's order — From members · Unread from members · From eBay · Unread from eBay — then `Archive` and `Deleted` groups split by type (eBay's API needs a conversation TYPE on every read, so there is no combined "all types" entry; `MessagesFolder.DELETED` → `conversation_status=DELETE` is the new folder, in `folderToStatus`, the demo filter and all 16 locales: `messages.folders.{inbox,unreadMembers,unreadEbay,deleted}`, `messages.list.emptyDeleted`). In Archive and Deleted the archive action reads "move back to the inbox". The inbox shows the top bar's active store (no switcher of its own since 2026-10-04), so above `xl` nothing stands between the title and the inbox; below `xl` the compact type/folder switches keep their own row (`S.Toolbar` is hidden at `xl`). On a fit-viewport route `ContentInner`'s bottom gutter shrinks to `spacing.md` so the panes get the height.
- **Carousels swipe on a phone** (`useSwipeNavigation`, `apps/web/src/hooks/`): the dashboard/overview carousels' arrows are hover-only and the dots are tiny, so a horizontal touch drag over `CarouselViewport` (`touch-action: pan-y`) now moves to the next/previous slide; a mostly-vertical drag stays the page's scroll. Reuse the hook for any new slide/pager surface instead of a second gesture implementation.
- **A sortable table header is ONE button — the column name sorts too** (`Table`'s `S.SortButton`, label + chevron, `aria-sort`), not a 16 px chevron beside a dead label.
- **The operator console (`/:locale/admin`) is the same product**: its row lists (warnings, queues, quota bands, aspect coverage/defaults) sit on `S.ListPane` (a `Card` with hairline rows, `AdminPage.style.ts`), never bare on the canvas; KPI tiles are `SummaryCard`s; the settings accordion header is the **`DisclosureButton` molecule** (`packages/ui/src/molecules/DisclosureButton/`: full-width row, title in the page's own ink, `meta` slot for the count/changed badge, rotating chevron, `aria-expanded`) — the hand-rolled `styled.button` header and the dead `SettingsCategory*` styles are gone. Use `DisclosureButton` for any section that expands in place; `SettingsActionRow` is for a row that navigates. The console cannot be reached in demo mode (the demo user is a `CUSTOMER`), so it is verified with a Playwright run that routes `**/api/v1/**` to fixtures (`page.route`, an admin user on `/auth/refresh` + `/auth/me`, minimal DTOs per tab) — rebuild that script from the DTOs in `packages/shared/src/domain/admin/` rather than adding an admin fixture to the demo.

### Order detail (`apps/web/src/features/orders/details/`)
Same `SettingsCard` pattern as listing detail — same font sizes/weights, no header icons (row icons instead). Net Profit Analysis / Customer / eBay Sale Summary / Amazon Order Summary are each a `SettingsCard`. Two row shapes: `Meta` (icon+label left, value right, single line) and `MetaBlock` (icon+label line, value stacked **below**, left-aligned) for content that doesn't fit a right-aligned single line, such as a shipping address.
- **The order's path is a TIMELINE card under the hero (2026-10-01, operator request — "the stage is written as text at the top, competitors show every step").** `OrderDto.timeline` (single-order read only) is built by the shared pure `buildOrderTimeline` (`packages/shared/src/domain/orders/order-timeline.ts`, Jest-covered in `apps/api/src/modules/orders/order-timeline.spec.ts`): five main steps — received → bought on Amazon → shipped by Amazon → tracking on eBay → delivered — each `done | current | attention | upcoming | skipped` with the time it happened and, where one belongs to it, the Amazon order number or the tracking number eBay received.
  - **It adds no status.** The step the order is standing on is decided by `OrderStage`; that row renders the stage's own `meaning`, its `action` and the automatic-purchase reason (`OrderTimelineNote.STAGE`), which is why the hero no longer prints those three as loose sentences above the product title. Every other row is evidence columns (`order_date`, `amazon_linked_at`, `auto_fulfill_submitted_at`, `shipped_detected_at`, `ebay_tracking_pushed_at`, `delivered_at`).
  - **A step this platform did not do is `skipped` ("SellerHill holds no Amazon order for this sale"), never "still to come"** — an order shipped by hand or by another tool must not show an upcoming purchase. A sale cancelled on eBay keeps only what happened and ends with a `sale_cancelled` step (attention only while a real Amazon order is still open).
  - **Buyer-message rows appear only for what `buyer_message_log` proves** (sent, or tried and failed), under the step they belong to. A switched-off event is not a step: four "switched off" rows on every order were rejected as noise. The log read is fail-soft.
  - **`orders.delivered_at` (migration `133`)** is stamped by the tracking processor on the COMPLETED transition — the DETECTION time, not the carrier's. Orders completed before `133` show the step done with no date, never a guess.
  - Codes only on the wire (`OrderTimelineStepKey` / `OrderTimelineNote`); copy is `orders.timeline.{title,step.*,note.*}` in all 16 locales (`order-timeline-i18n.guard.spec.ts`). Web: `features/orders/shared/OrderTimeline/` (presentational) + `shared/order-timeline.ts` (`toOrderTimelineRows`, `orderTimelineIcon`, Vitest-covered); the row in focus wears its STAGE's icon so the badge and the timeline never look like two statuses. Below `md` each row stacks name → time → description. The action buttons stay in the Amazon card (one place per action). Demo mode builds the same timeline through `demoOrderTimeline`.
- **The buyer's phone sits UNDER the ship-to address** (phone icon + `CopyableText`, `S.AddressPhoneRow`), the way eBay's own order page prints it, formatted by `formatPhoneNumber` (`@repo/ui`, `PhoneInput/phone.utils.ts`, Vitest-covered in `apps/web/src/utils/formatPhoneNumber.test.ts`): `+1 843-408-1812` from the bare digits eBay hands over, parsed against the ship-to country. The Contact block carries the e-mail only.
- **Cancellation and refund rows come from eBay's own report (2026-09-30).** The eBay card shows `ebayCancelledAt` under the eBay status and, only when `ebayRefundedAmount` is not null, a "Refunded to the buyer" group (amount, date, and a note that eBay's figure leaves out the tax it collected). A cancelled order with a real Amazon order still open gets `orders.detail.cancelledAmazonOpenAction` as the hero's action line — the one case where the finished `cancelled` stage still needs the seller.
- **Ship-by date and multi-item notice (2026-10-01).** `OrderDto.shipByDate` (`orders.ebay_ship_by_date`, eBay's `lineItemFulfillmentInstructions.shipByDate`) is a row on the eBay card and, while the seller still has to act (`orderStageHasDeadline`: to purchase, blocked, not confirmed, Amazon cancelled), a line in the hero that turns red inside 24 hours — so an Amazon cancellation says how long the seller has. `OrderDto.lineItemCount > 1` renders `orders.detail.multiItemNotice`: the platform tracks only the first item and never buys such an order automatically.
- **`adFee` is the settings group's configured FIXED fee — an estimate, never a charge eBay reported.** eBay's own `ebayMarketplaceFee` already contains its per-order fixed portion, so the row renders only while `ebayMarketplaceFee` is null (nothing reported yet); otherwise the selling-cost rows would not add up to `ebayEarnings`. The eBay card uses eBay's own line names (Subtotal / Shipping / Sales tax / Order total / Transaction fees / Order earnings), the Amazon card Amazon's (see "Amazon order-details parser").

### One active store (top bar, 2026-10-04)

The seller works on ONE eBay store at a time, chosen in ONE place: the `StoreSwitcher` in the top bar beside the language menu (`layouts/AppLayout/StoreSwitcher/`, the app's own `Dropdown`; one store = a plain label). Every store-scoped screen shows that store and every action goes to it — the Easync / Shopify / Seller Central pattern, operator decision. Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-04-active-store-context-design.md`.
- **One rule, `resolveActiveStoreId`** (`features/ebay/utils/activeStore.ts`, Vitest): `?store=` on a store-scoped page when it names one of the seller's stores → this browser's remembered choice (`localStorage` `sellerhill.activeStore.<userId>`, try/catch) → the first store. A foreign or stale id is never sent to the API.
- **`ActiveStoreProvider`** (`components/`) wraps `AppLayout` in `App.tsx`; pages read `useActiveStore()` (`features/ebay/hooks/useActiveStore.ts`) and NEVER `?store=` or a store filter of their own (`features/ebay/active-store.guard.test.ts`). Store-scoped routes are `AppRouteMeta.storeScoped` (dashboard, actions, listings*, orders*, messages, returns); on them the URL mirrors the choice so a link opens the same store. Store-independent pages (billing, settings, stores, profile, Best Sellers, onboarding) show no store name; the switcher stays in the top bar.
- **A switch keeps the VIEW, drops what belonged to the old store**: `searchForStoreSwitch` removes only `page`, `r`, `c`, `drawer`, `asins` and keeps the rest (dashboard tab/period, the drafts view, the messages folder, list filters, a search) — comparing two stores' charts or drafts is the point of a switcher. Pages whose page number is local state reset it by render-time adjustment. **A manual switch on a record page (`/orders/123`, `/listings/abc`, `/listings/jobs/j1`) goes to that record's list** (`recordListPath`) — the record belongs to the store just left. The remembered choice is keyed on the user the auth slice already holds (`selectCurrentUser`), never `/auth/me`, which can answer after the store list and let the first store overwrite it. `ActiveStoreProvider.test.tsx` covers the deep link, the record page and the remembered store. Store-scoped queries `skip` while `activeStoreId` is null, so no all-stores request ever fires first.
- **A record of another store makes that store active, ONCE per record** (`useFollowRecordStore`, keeping the page's own params; a later manual switch is never undone): order, listing, job and return detail. Every production order/listing/return/job row carries `ebay_account_id` (checked 2026-10-04); demo ORDERS carry none, so only demo listings exercise it.
- **Switcher options carry each store's own pending-action count** (`storeOwnItemCount`, account-wide items left out); the sidebar Action Center and Messages badges show the ACTIVE store (`getActionCenterByStore`, shared with the switcher; `EbayUnreadCountDto.byAccount`).
- **Drawers**: Add Listings and Import existing listings have no store field — they add to the active store, named in the subtitle (`listings.actions.addingToStore` / `listings.existingImport.importingTo`), and a switch drops the other store's policies. The settings hub's store-settings drawers open on the active store (`resolveSettingsScope`); "all stores" is an explicit `?scope=global`.
- **No "All stores" view anywhere** and no per-row store labels (removed with `useStoreLabel` / `resolveRecordStoreLabel`). The API is unchanged; the web no longer makes the blank-store Action Center call (`getActionCenterByStore` gives the page, the switcher counts and the badge). No toast on a switch (no toast provider is mounted); the switcher's label is the signal.
- Known gap: the demo `/dashboard` and messages answers are not narrowed per store.

### Multi-store (`ebay_account_id`)
- **Create/import always names a store:** both Add Listings and Existing eBay Import drawers send the ACTIVE store (top bar; see "One active store"). `ebayAccountId` travels through request → persisted listing job → BullMQ batch → processor → `EbayBulkService.createListings`; these flows must never publish through an arbitrary `LIMIT 1` active account. It is enforced at enqueue (`400` when absent), not defaulted.
- Migration `030`: `listings.ebay_account_id` (backfill from user's eBay account).
- Filter: listings and orders both take `?ebayAccountId=`; `OrderDto.ebayAccountId` exposes the order's store to the frontend.

**Currency is resolved from the connected eBay store's marketplace — NEVER from the UI language.** `getLocaleConfig(language)` (`packages/ui/src/utils/format.ts`) returns `{ locale }` only — it once also returned a language-keyed currency, which rendered eBay-USD amounts as TRY for Turkish-language sessions. Money gets its currency from domain data:
- **A listing** carries its own resolved `ListingDto.currency` — `useListingsColumns` formats each row with `listing.currency`, not a page-wide value.
- **An order, the dashboard, and anywhere else without a per-row currency field** resolve it via `apps/web/src/utils/resolveStoreCurrency.ts`: given the user's connected eBay accounts (`useGetEbayAccountsQuery`) and an optional `ebayAccountId` (an active store filter, or the order's own `ebayAccountId`), it returns that account's marketplace currency, falling back to the first connected account, then to eBay US.
- **Amazon-sourced money** (the Products page) is always `'USD'` — only Amazon US exists — never derived from the eBay store or the UI language.
- **A billing plan's price** or an **admin/platform cost** carries its own `currency` field from its own domain.

### Listing settings group — content policy
